# RUNNER-SPEC v2.95: entries, calls, sessions, subscriptions and operation of a place (lane runner-spec-1020)

**Tree:** `62f15988b85ca4afdb6d37e0ff324d9072274615` (detached), 18 crates. The checker was rebuilt there (BUILD SUCCEEDED). Every v2.2 probe was checked and run on it. **v2.3** adds R0's built figures (runner-build-1033, landed bf7cf64f, measured there and at 71b306f0) and three probes run at 62f15988 (p19 split and few-registration variants).

**Store:** written against **STORE-SPEC v2.7** ([`STORE-SPEC.md`](STORE-SPEC.md) beside this file, written by store-spec-1001; CHANGES-v2.4 to v2.7: G1–G4, H1–H5, I1–I5, J1). The landed store is S1 (603a2274); S1.1 (`look`, `changes`, Shape reads) is being built.

**Review answered:** runner-spec-review-1028/ADVICE.md (findings 1–7 and every "Model and law gaps" line).

Evidence marks:
- **RUN**: run or checked here;
- **READ**: taken from a cited file;
- **ESTIMATE**: computed from RUN or READ parts;
- **GUESS**: an estimate with no measured part.

**v2.11** writes the R2 contract that WIRE-SPEC v2 (wire-spec-1059, after wire-attack-1060) needs from this spec. It covers the publisher-to-owner event and its accounting, normalized rows (F10), `complete` (F11), `DONE_MAX` and the outcome reserve (F12), Cover-only retention (F4), and gone-key slots (F9). It **corrects** 1059's reserve: the slot table alone does not bound the outcome rows owed to a session. It adds five laws. R2-g, R2-h and R2-i (admission, launch identity, resume) are not in v2.11. The models were not re-run; the new owner bound is **UNTESTED** until its R1 case. The v2.10 text is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.10.md.

**v2.8** applies Sam's gavel 11 (duplex owned halves, option A; net-proposal-999 §3 as amended by net-proposal-amend-1053): a session is one owned task group per connection. Text only, no model re-run: the models' session is one sequential actor, and the owner task is that actor. The v2.7 text is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.7.md.

**v2.6** rules on L3's oracle (runner-build-1033, followups.md 09:50). v2.5's oracle, a harness `Sender` carried in H, cannot be built: `handles` makes H from the Db alone (rule 12). The law now counts the body's `TRY_SEND` events through the CONC-10 event sink, on every backend. Text only; no model behaviour changed, so no model was re-run. The v2.5 text is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.5.md.

**v2.5** answers review 1042 (runner-build-review-1042/ATTACK.md): one send received two Outcomes in the built runner, and no check observed it. Rule 5 now defines a send and its one Outcome. The calls and subscription models gain exactly-once checks (`models/calls25_model.py`, `models/subs25_model.py`), and every model was re-run. The v2.4 text is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.4.md.

The v2.2 and v2.3 texts are RUNNER-SPEC-v2.2.md and RUNNER-SPEC-v2.3.md. **v2.4** rules on R1's five gaps (runner-build-1033, followups.md 2026-11-16 "R1 gaps") against STORE-SPEC v2.9 and REPLAY-SPEC v2.1, by reading; no model behaviour changed, so no model was re-run. Models are in `models/`, their outputs in `models/evidence/`. Probes are in `programs/`, their outcomes in `probes.txt`. The v2 and v2.1 change tables are in CHANGES-HISTORY.md, and the v2.1 text is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.1.md.

## CHANGES-v2.95 (recorded code resolves through a release's declared `Replay` export; lane release-recipe-v5-exports-spec-3521)

§7 step 3: recorded-code mode resolves its code from a verified installed v5 release (launch.md "Release recipe and host assembly", "Exports" and "Recorded-code replay") and outside a launch of the writer's own image never from a serving image, a granted `main`, an entry revision or an image digest alone. `tape check` also requires equal replay directory commitments. L30 gains the `digest_inferred_code` mutant. Text only; not yet built.

## CHANGES-v2.94 (call credit replaces q; lane s4-call-window-spec-3519)

Text only. The built wire still speaks `hirsel.4` with `q` until the held implementation lane lands (DECISIONS 2026-11-28, "S4 call window (3516)"). §3 "Call credit" replaces `Welcome.q`, the slot table and every bound derived from `1+q`, with no compatibility path. A connection gets cumulative grants in calls and bytes. Each grant is reserved in the shared pool P and bounded by a per-Caller share of the place window W, by the Caller's and the place's stream rows, and by the session ceiling. The window, the share policy, the stream caps and the ceiling are operator configuration. Unconfigured, they are 4,096 calls and 768 KiB (S1 of 2026-11-27), max-min fair, 256 streams per Caller and 16 MiB. `Call` becomes a bounded list. `Hello` gains a want, and `Welcome` gains `epoch`, `grant` and `frame_max`. The new frames are `Window` (tag 12) and `Credit` (tag 13). A lone call is a one-item list, so there is one wire form for one call or many, with no scalar or batch mode. Store §1.8 rows and the durable format are unchanged. A successor position on a stream settles its predecessor, and no client acknowledgement exists. The subprotocol becomes `hirsel.5`, and `hirsel.4` is retired. Rows that do not fit in an update's first frame continue in outcome-only Batches at its version.

The owner inbox capacity `C` = 5 is now a fixed constant, so `Drained`, repair and L24 are unchanged. §3 states the lone-call path, the stalled-reader peak bound with its proof, both crash cuts with their replay rules, the two stream-lease cost numbers, and the implementation's acceptance gate. L1, L6+L7, L9, L19 and L58 are **PLANNED** where they change. L72–L76 are new and **PLANNED**. The CHANGES history and §8.1's model evidence keep `q`, the parameter those models ran with, and re-running them over credit is **PLANNED** with L9.

**Reply recovery is chosen per entry (Sam's ruling, 2026-11-28 ~17:45Z).** Every transact registration now names its reply recovery, and none is the default. `runner.transact` and `runner.transact_via` are deleted. Their replacements are `runner.transact_recovered`, `runner.transact_unrecovered`, `runner.transact_via_recovered` and `runner.transact_via_unrecovered`.
- The kind stays `Transact`, and `recovery` is an attribute of the registration (§1.2 "Reply recovery").
- A recovered entry behaves as `transact` did.
- An unrecovered entry keeps durable-before-reply but has no admission marker and no recovery row. Its calls carry connection-local ids and are sent once.
- `recovery` is in `Described`, the contract, the release digest and the Want and entry key.
- The `Call` and `Batch` frames gain local lists, under the same `hirsel.5` revision.
- An unrecovered call writes no `CallRecord`. A recovered call keeps H2's record until lane 3523 removes per-call recording from the serving path, and 3523 also owns §7's text. This ruling makes no Store format change.

**DECIDED here; reversible** (beyond 3516's surface):
- the `Window` frame and the `Hello` want, because without a demand signal idle connections would hold share;
- forfeit by raising the spent counter, which is race-free on an ordered connection;
- the `Credit` frame carrying the share;
- `frame_max` in `Welcome`;
- `CALLS_MAX` = 256;
- FIFO raises and max-min shares;
- the 30 s close for credit that is not forfeited.

## CHANGES-v2.93 (transaction adapters and HTTP streams)

A package registers a wrapped transaction with `transact_via(r, definition, descriptor, run, n, s)`. The entry key uses the identity of `definition` and the wire schemas of `A` and `Result[R, E]`. The CALL record carries that definition's revision and the usual bounds and launch identity. Serving, resuming, trigger dispatch and replay all invoke `run(mut tx, handles, caller, take arg, descriptor)`. The registry rebuilds the inert descriptor at placement. A wrapper can carry its handler's `StaticFn` in that descriptor and mint a concrete opaque scoped proof over a local owner after checking the caller in the transaction. OWN-19 excludes the proof from owned fields. Ordinary `transact` uses the same execution path.

A raw route mounted in `HttpPlaceConfig.raw` can use `http_stream_route(handler, body_max, stream_bytes)`. A matched explicit cap applies to both Content-Length and the cumulative decoded chunk sizes. Other requests inherit the listener cap. `http_fill_chunk` fills a reusable nonempty buffer to capacity or EOF and clears it on a body fault. `http_stream_response` returns a stream spec for setting headers before promotion. Its producer retains any captured snapshot while producing chunks and can open a cursor on that snapshot. A producer returns at EOF. Peer disconnect, listener stop, lease end and the optional exclusive wall-clock expiry end the stream and release its producer state. Expiry fences the response head and data writes.

## CHANGES-v2.92 (adopting a restored child)

§14.5 specifies the first launch of a fresh group incarnation from a verified restore. STORE §15.8 L-A11 is its implementation gate.

## CHANGES-v2.91 (a parked head asks for its sync; lane runner-http-retry-hang-3266)

H3 gains one rule. A head whose store's syncs wake it (`SyncWake`) waits for a held group's sync on its inbox instead of in `durable`. Before it parks there with a group still unsynced it calls `store.request_sync`, which ends the Directory journal's open group at once instead of at its gather deadline (STORE-SPEC L94). The deadline is the journal worker's own and wakes nobody else: an armed group with no further arrival synced only when the deadline passed, which under a program-made clock is only at `clock.advance_to` (STORE-SPEC L94), so `http_invisible_retry_keeps_one_effect` hung at Y's newer-stream send, and under a real clock every idle release waited up to `group_delay_us` for nothing. Back-to-back commits still share a group; a head that goes idle gives up gathering beyond that point. No wire, API or format change. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.90 (claim authority, Deposed and the Bucket L70 (d) half; lane store-claim-auth-origin-3048)

`Ended` gains `Deposed(store.Claim)` and `HostReply` gains `PlaceDeposed(epoch, holder)` (driver tag 13). `PlaceRead.claim` is `PlaceClaim { epoch, holder, attempt }`, and a failed claim or ORIGIN read is an error, never an absent claim. A managed launch takes its Bucket `ClaimAuth` only from the move envelope, and an envelope carrying `Adopt` is refused (§14.6 "Claim authority"). L70 (d)'s objects@1 half is built as `activation_fate_survives_bucket_crash` (`runner_activation_fate_bucket_crash_laws.hirsel`): a sim bucket cuts the install of r2 after each request in turn, applied and dropped, until both cuts reach past the install, and every reopen through an uncut address recovers r1 with all six rows unchanged or r2 with all six settled. The measured sweep saw 55 cuts recover r1 and 1 recover r2 over 28 request sites. The Directory half waives the trust teardown of the install handle it poisons on purpose (`store.allow_unverified_teardown`).

## CHANGES-v2.89 (one WebSocket subprotocol and one activation format; lane legacy-l2-retired-ws-2970)

Standalone and routed WebSocket upgrades require `hirsel.4`. L58 applies the same HTTP 400 refusal to retired tokens, unknown tokens and missing offers. Persistent activation opens use the current fenced format.

## CHANGES-v2.88 (typed stream time refusal; lane schema-runner-streamfuture-2954)

`Refusal` appends `StreamFuture(u64)` at tag 8. The payload is the store's keyed time in milliseconds. HTTP 400 carries `{"refused":"stream_future"}` and `Hirsel-Now` with the same time as an unsigned decimal integer.

## CHANGES-v2.87 (a place's charge is what it can use; lane place-budget-overhead-2890)

`operate_bytes` drops its three round figures and derives each term from declared limits and the tasks and channels the part makes (§12.1 "What operate_bytes charges"). The head's 2 MiB own term becomes its wake relays, alarms, inbox, 256 KiB of state and the largest transact or fenced `n` it runs, with a 2 MiB floor only while an `attempt` mint lets triggered attempts spawn from it. The serving task's 2 MiB supervisor intercept becomes its own allocations (pending tokens, allocation slots, the serve inbox, the acceptor, refusal tasks, `n_service`, 6,711 B per owner), still capped by H. The 1 MiB keep becomes 64 KiB plus the Template's `n_place` when `operate` registered the place in its own task. The three `n_place` terms stay: the head, the publisher and the serving task each replay the place into their own Registry. A small dialed place now fits 4 MiB (L-2890 in platform's local laws). No wire or format change. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.86 (helper units for local places; lane local-shards-2854)

`local` starts its places on helper units. See §14.11 for the per-shard admission rule, the complete status image and the factory seed contract. The three admission and progress laws run together under `test_local_units_run_together`.

## CHANGES-v2.85 (the catalog store is sized for max_places; lane local-4000-commit-2844)

`local` opened its catalog store at `root/host` with the session store's `Options`. A host whose seeded stores are small (the lash swarm's 1 MiB `resident_limit`) filled its catalog after about 2,000 creates, because every session adds catalog rows. The refusal surfaced as `Failed("commit")`: `platform.decided` folded every `Ok` outcome that was not `Committed` into `Missing("commit")`.

| # | Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The catalog's options | **CHANGED** | §14.9 "Shape". The catalog store's `resident_limit` is the session store's plus `max_places` times the largest resident charge one session adds to the catalog (`local.session_catalog_bytes`): its `sessions` and `keys` rows at a `store.ROW_KEY_LIMIT` key, and the five rows `platform.undeployed_group_bytes` prices for its group. Each row is priced by `store.resident_row_bytes`, Store's own charge. `host_bytes` prices the catalog's writer at these options. |
| 2 | A refused catalog transaction | **CHANGED** | §14.9 "Ends". `platform.decided` reports a refused or failed catalog transaction as `Store(Full(capacity))`, `Store(Conflict)` or `Store(Drift)`; `local` answers it `Failed` with the store, the capacity and its figures ("catalog store: resident needs N bytes, has M"). |
| 3 | Law | **NEW: `local_creates_to_max_places`** | §14.9 laws. |

Open: a deleted session's catalog rows (`retired`, request rows) are not counted against `max_places`; a host that creates and deletes past `max_places` sessions is not covered by this sizing.

## CHANGES-v2.84 (the in-process transport as built; lane platform-local-scale-2815, slice 1)

Slice 1 built §14.11 "Transport". Where v2.83's text and the build differ, the build was checked against the TCP path and the text is corrected here.

| # | Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What a dial carries | **CHANGED** | §14.11 "Transport". A session's connections carry the WebSocket frame bytes themselves (`Client[Bytes]`), not a `Wire { kind, data }` message. Masking, frame decoding, `max_frame` (1009) and close frames are the socket path's code, and the reader's refill and the writer's put are the only transport branches (`std.wsock` `In`/`Out`). `Listen = Tcp(Addr) \| InProcess(Inbox[std.wsock.Dial])`; a host holds a `std.wsock.Dialer`. |
| 2 | Refusal codes | **CHANGED** | §14.11 "Transport". A dial refused for the session limit or while draining is answered HTTP 503, the code transport admission answers a socket (§9 "excess sessions"), not a close code. |
| 3 | Deadlines | **STATED** | §14.11 "Transport". A `conn` receive has no deadline, so reader-side idle probing and the message deadline are TCP-only. The owner's Hello, auth-expiry and close alarms run on both. A dialing place's idle vote has no accept loop to park. |
| 4 | Where the dial connection is made | **STATED** | §14.11 "Transport". `local` makes it when it sizes the place (so `operate_bytes` counts each session's dial room) and hands it to the place's task; a lease gets the `Dialer` once the place is Ready (`local.dialer(lease)`), and `client.client_dial`, `wire_client.connect_dial` and `fixture.client_dial` open a session through it. |
| 5 | `Status` is not `cross` | **CORRECTED** | §14.11 "What crosses". `store.Attention` holds `IndexReport`s with `String`s, so `runner.Status` is not plain. Slice 2 settles the mirror's form. |
| 6 | `local_dial_parity` | **CHANGED** | §14.11 "Laws". The arms are the runner fixture's `start` (TCP) and `start_dialed`, and each arm is checked against hand-derived outcomes. |
| 7 | `std.ingress_policy` | **MOVED** | The trusted-ingress source policy (`Prefix`, `source`, `trusted`) moved from `std.wsock` to its own module, unchanged. |
| 8 | A TCP client's refusal code | **FIXED** | §9 "excess sessions". `std.websocket.client` reported every non-101 answer as 400, so a TCP client saw a full place's 503 as 400 while a dial saw 503. A 4xx or 5xx answer now fails with the server's status; any other answer that is not a valid 101 is still 400. `local_dial_parity`'s TCP arm was red on this. |

## CHANGES-v2.83 (in-process places on every core, reached without sockets; lane platform-local-scale-2815)

Text first; the build follows in slices in the same lane, and each slice's state is named in §14.11 "Slices". The evidence is Atropia's lash_swarm per-agent ladder (hirsel-briefs/atropia-perf-1126): 500 in-process places in one process peaked at 92.7% CPU, and each place held a loopback listener, an accept task, a clock provider and about 6 FDs (62,058 FDs at 10,000 places).

| # | Item | Change | Where |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The in-process transport | **NEW** | §14.11 "Transport". An in-process place takes its sessions through a dial handle, over typed connections that carry the same frames a WebSocket binary message carries. The checks run in the same owner and in the same order as over TCP: session limit, drain admission, Hello deadline and binding, message bound, credits and close codes. |
| 2 | No listener per in-process place | **CHANGED** | §14.9, §14.11 "External access". `PlaceConfig.listen` is `Listen = Tcp(Addr) \| InProcess(Dials)`. `local` sets `InProcess`, so its places bind nothing, and `Status.addr` is `None`. A host program reaches the outside through its own front, which is one listener per host (the harness gateway's HTTP front is one), and that front reaches places by dialing. |
| 3 | Shards | **NEW** | §14.11 "Shards". `local` runs places on `Config.units` helper units. Each place starts on the unit with the most room. A place stays on that unit until it ends, and it is placed again at every start. A unit receives a fixed share of `place_bytes`. |
| 4 | Factory and seed | **CHANGED** | §14.11 "What crosses". `make` runs in the place's unit. The seed is `T: cross`, and the factory's `copy` makes one seed per unit. |
| 5 | Attack surface | **NEW** | §14.11 "Co-located places". |
| 6 | Laws | **NEW: `local_dial_parity`, `local_stale_dial`, `local_units_run_together`** | §14.11 "Laws". |

**Decisions, each reversible with no stored format.** The pool size is a Config field, not derived from cores. Placement is not sticky. A unit's share is `place_bytes / units`. External access belongs to the host's front, not to a per-place lazy listener. The `/wholehog` end state of each decision is argued in §14.11.

## CHANGES-v2.82 (self-editing amended after the soundness review; lane self-edit-build-1c-2808)

Text only; the build follows in the same lane. It applies review self-edit-gate-review-2801 §3 items 1, 2 and 4 to 10, the Q5 rescan and the Q11 correction, and the F2 settlement check from astra 2807. F3 (which claims of the settler bound NEW's serving code) is pending a ruling and its three sentences are unchanged.

- **§14.3 "Preparing".** `HostImport` gains the approved fates, a flag that the request is an editor's, and NEW's entry list. While it validates PREPARED, OLD computes the migration fact (the 0x0082 payloads and the 0x0083 (id, kind) list) and the settlement it applies, and returns them on `PlaceImported` beside the digest. OLD refuses the import when an editor's PREPARED without fates carries a 0x0085 frame or ships a range of `store.triggers.pending`, when a rewrite names any other table, or, with fates, when the pending rows PREPARED applies differ from the ones OLD derives from its own rows at the base. The host reads both facts only from that answer.
- **§14.6.** The prepare scan's last sentence no longer says the host fails the deploy `Unsettled`: it holds (v2.81). The scan runs again at the final catch-up round's base, inside the pause; a held row that the approved fates do not settle aborts the export and holds D. "Who supplies a fate" names `hirsel decide`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->.
- **launch.md.** The editor's bindings are an allow list with `Dir` bound by (device, inode) and re-checked at every admission, at clearance and at each serving start (F1). SetEditor (ask 9) and `hirsel editor`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> set and revoke the editor; revocation fails the editor's open requests (F5). The settler reads migration and runs from OLD's `PlaceImported` report (F2). Decide-approved fates cover only the shown held groups (F4). The deploy row gains `settled` (F6). An editor tree with Foreign or Exec is `Denied` (F7). Each group has a set of allowed package roots (F8). `hirsel decide`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> decides any held request, and `Decision` gains `tree` and `root` (F9). The F10 text fixes, the harness turn-boundary rule.
- **Decisions, each reversible with no stored format:** the protected-directory list replaces "contained by any directory the launcher's user owns", which the rule's own ownership requirement makes uncheckable; OLD, not the host, derives the expected settlement, because only OLD holds the paused head's rows; `hirsel deploy` keeps deciding the request it began, and `hirsel decide`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> is the one client for any other.

## CHANGES-v2.81 (self-editing: the editor principal, the law gate and the settler; lane self-edit-gate-spec-2794)

Text only; not built. launch.md gains "Self-editing: the editor, the law gate and the settler". It adds an editor principal (a second UID on its own socket) that may only submit gated source, a `hirsel test` admission gate bound to the published root H, a settler that cuts over an editor's source alone only when the gate is green and the grant diff, the Foreign and Exec approval text, the migration (PREPARED's catalog delta and replacement inventory) and the held runs are all empty, and source sent by content as a manifest with per-group blob presence. §14.6: a deploy whose trigger report is unsettled no longer fails `Unsettled`. The host holds it in AwaitDecision, and the operator's Decide supplies its fates. The operator class 13 `Unsettled` is replaced by 13 `Red`. A standalone install still refuses `Unsettled` and exits 1. Only the operator decides; the "any caller the gateway lets decide" route is deleted. Reversible: the spec only.

## CHANGES-v2.80 (the head steps registered projectors; lane store-tail-2-3-2665, Store 2.3)

Built: §12.5. The head's upkeep gains a projector job, job 4 after build, sweep and retention, before scrub (Scrub and Walk become jobs 5 and 6): on an idle turn it runs one `project_step` of the next registered projector that has work, round-robin (STORE-SPEC v2.130 §1.14 "Driving"). A step that commits releases the head's snapshot. No projector steps while an export lease is held. A refused projector turns off by itself, and `Poisoned` ends the head. `Status.upkeep` gains `projected`, the projector steps that had work. Law `test_head_steps_projector`. No wire or format change. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.79 (the head sweeps Log retention; lane store-log-retain-1-6-2668, Store 1.6)

Built: §12.5 job 3, Retention. On an idle turn, after the build and expiry sweep jobs and before scrub, the head runs one retention sweep slice, `retain_step(mut db, 1024)` (STORE-SPEC v2.128 §14.4a), while a Log is over its declared `Keep(n)`. The sweep is a data commit, so the head seals it as a group with no calls and releases it once durable, as it does an expiry sweep. It runs while an export lease is held, since catch-up replays data commits. Scrub and Walk become jobs 4 and 5. No wire or format change. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.78 (store operator commands and upkeep counts; lane store-ops-1-4-2664, Store 1.4, with STORE-SPEC v2.127)

Built. (1) `Status.upkeep: UpkeepCounts {swept, tours, scrub_faults, walks, walk_findings}` beside `Status.store` (§12.1, §12.5); the head publishes it with the attention mark, whenever either changes, and `StoreAttention` carries it. (2) Launch word 20 is the store operation: 0 serves, 1 `hirsel store rehearse`, 2 `hirsel store verify --release`, 3 `hirsel store repair --release` (`LaunchEnv.store_op: StoreOp`, flags `--hirsel-dry-run`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, `--hirsel-store-verify`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, `--hirsel-store-repair`<!-- doc-ref: external -->). `operate` emits the operation's header and runs `operate_dry_run`, `operate_store_verify` or `operate_store_repair` (STORE-SPEC §15.6) instead of serving. (3) `OpenError` gains `Operator(OperatorRefusal)`, with `OperatorRefusal = Unverified(problems) | ExportLeased`, the refusals of verify and repair. (4) A dry-run's backfill line names its cause. (5) The driver's `hirsel store rehearse` on a store held by a writer exits 75, as backup does; before, it failed as an error. No law is new here: the store laws cover verify and repair. No law reads `Status.upkeep` yet; the counts are the head's v2.76 and v2.77 tallies, which `test_head_builds_demoted_index` drives, now published. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.77 (the head sweeps expired rows; lane store-ttl-4-1-2666, Store 4.1)

Built: §12.5 gains job 2, Sweep. On an idle turn the head runs one `sweep_expired` of at most 1,024 rows and 1 MiB (STORE-SPEC v2.125), seals its commit as a group with no calls and releases it once durable. It runs while an export lease is held, since catch-up replays data commits. No timer wakes a quiet head for expiry. Scrub and Walk become jobs 3 and 4. No wire, API or format change. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.76 (the head drives the store's upkeep; lane store-head-driver-1-3-2618, Store 1.3)

Built: §12.5. The head owns the Db, so it runs the store's upkeep in bounded slices when it has nothing else to do: index build chunks, scrub slices and index-walker slices (STORE-SPEC v2.123), on idle turns only. Jobs that commit stay off while an export lease is held, because catch-up refuses a catalog change on OLD. No wire, API or format change. Reversible: code and text only.

## CHANGES-v2.75 (the store section of Status; lane store-status-1-8-2619, with STORE-SPEC v2.120)

Built: `Status.store: store.Attention` (§12.1 "Status"), what the store reports to its operator (OPTIMAL-STORE-ENDSTATE §II.3, plan item Store 1.8).
- **H1. One writer.** The head reads `store.attention_mark(db)` at the top of each turn. When it moved since the last report, the head reads `store.attention(db)` and sends it to `operate` as a life event, and `operate`, still the only publisher of the root, republishes `Status` at the current phase. The watch channel gets no send, since the phase did not change (§12.1's six sends stand).
- **H2. Freshness.** The section is as of the head's last turn. Index states, drift and demotions change only in the head's own commits, so they are current after the turn that made them. A reclaim failure is counted by the journal task, and shows at the head's next turn; nothing wakes an idle head for it.
- **H3. Refusals keep the last answer.** An `attention` that cannot allocate, or a full life inbox, keeps the published section and retries at the next turn; it never ends the head.
- **H4. Not on the wire.** `GET /hirsel/status` and `PlaceReady` are unchanged. The section is in-process, as `Status` is.
- **Laws.** No new law (plan item 1.8). The causing cases read the counters: `test_index_drift_counted` (drift, demotion, states), L-IX's `test_index_build_law` (each state, and the first chunk's cursor), and `bucket_test_m2_reclaim_retry` (failures and pending). The idle law reads `Status.store` of a running place: the trigger index `due`, all Public, no drift or failure.

## CHANGES-v2.74 (build slice F4 of the activation fate; lane run-activation-fate-f4-2590)

Built: `hirsel dev` releases every held run on each install it runs (§14.6 "Who supplies a fate"), and core/std/api/runner.md's "Held runs" section. The four build slices of v2.62's activation fate are now all built. The dev case is the driver unit test `package_dev_releases_held_runs` (`native::tests::dev_laws`, Cranelift). Its fixture is `dev_place.hirsel`. On main before this change, the case's install was refused `Unsettled` and dev reopened OLD. Decisions where the spec was silent, each reversible with no stored format:
- **G1. Dev's Release travels on dev's own install launch, not in a run argument.** Dev installs with `--hirsel-install-predecessor=<hex>`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, and the runtime accepts that word only beside `--hirsel-dev-store`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, so that launch is dev's alone. The runner maps it to `InstallFrom(Fates(all: Some(Release), entries: []))`. The fate goes through the same `fate.resolve` and `fate.settle` as a deploy's or a hand install's, in the activation's one commit, and there is no second mechanism. launch.md's earlier wording, "Dev's install passes `--hirsel-held=release`<!-- doc-ref: external -->", is replaced: dev passes no `--hirsel-held`<!-- doc-ref: external --> word.
- **G2. Release applies to every store dev supervises,** including one supplied with `--store DIR`. That store is the one dev installs into, and a run in flight across a rebuild is the case §14.6 names. An operator who wants another fate uses a standalone install outside dev.
- **G3. Dev refuses `--hirsel-held*`<!-- doc-ref: external --> in the program arguments,** as it already refuses `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and `--hirsel-activation=`<!-- doc-ref: external -->: dev chooses the activation and its fate.
- **G4. The dev case reads the settled row at the activation commit.** The fault shim's `hold-after` mode freezes dev's install child after its commit and before it replies. The case then reads the held row offline: `Waiting`, not parked, `cancel` false, stamped with NEW's identity digest, attempt 1, due 7. Dev then recovers through a plain reopen of NEW, which serves, and runs no second install. NEW's head running attempt n + 1 on NEW's code is L70 (a)'s claim, and the dev case does not repeat it. `operate`'s listener serves the wire and `/hirsel/status` only, so a plain HTTP read could not observe the run under dev.
- **G5. core/std/API.md has no per-module rows.** Module pages are reached through the generated API-INDEX.md, which already lists api/runner.md. So F4 extends that page and adds no API.md row.
- **G6. The dev fixture tracks main's runner API.** On main before this change, `dev_place.hirsel` wrote four type arguments for `PlaceConfig` (v2.71 removed `B`; main's 6e4a3147 fixed the count alongside this lane). Its wire also had no `bind` mint (`BindNoGrant`, CHANGES-v2.71 B1), so both dev laws were red. The fixture now has three type arguments, its own grant type (`Run(TriggerContext) | Bound`), a `bind` mint and an `attempt` mint. At this version `package_dev_s1_persistence_and_fencing` is red on main for a separate reason that this change does not touch: in one of its later dev launches, linking a memoized unit fails with `core@1/heap/finish_prepared_push: Malformed("annex does not match body")`. It is red the same way with this change's runner and dev.rs reverted, so it rides along.

Engines: on this tree, L70 `activation_settles_held` ((a) to (c)), `activation_fate_survives_crash` ((d)), `activation_fate_hot_deploy` ((e)) and L40 `trigger_scan_classes` passed on Cranelift and on LLVM. The dev case ran on Cranelift, the driver unit test's engine. L49's and L54's marks come from F3's runs, which this change does not touch: L49 on Cranelift, and L54 on Cranelift and LLVM (lane activation-fate-f3-2483). `hirsel test` has no evaluator backend.
## CHANGES-v2.73 (a start `local` refuses answers its waiting routes; lane runner-start-full-2588)

A start that `local` refused before any place task ran, because its charge did not fit the room left in `place_bytes`, was reported to the host as `Spawn("Full")`, but no route waiting on the group was ever answered. A route only got an answer from a child's end, and a refused start has no child, so the route waited through every backoff retry. census-bisect-fix-2566 found it: the five local laws hung for 120 s.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.73 says |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Routes on a refused start | **STATED: answered as a failed start's are** | §14.9 "Ends". A serving start that the launcher refuses before a place task runs is reported `Spawn` to the host. Once the host has applied that refusal, the routes waiting on the group are refused, unless the host started the group again at once. This is v2.32's rule for a failed start, with the refusal in place of the end. |
| 2 | The answer | **STATED: typed** | A start whose charge does not fit `place_bytes`, or whose place `operate`'s sizing refuses, is answered `Full`, the refusal §14.2 names for a host with no room. A start that ends with `StartError::Full` is now `Full` too, not `Failed("Full")`. Every other refused start is `Failed` with its cause, for example `workdir`. A route that arrives while the group waits on backoff waits for the next retry and is answered by it. |
| 3 | Law | **NEW: `local_start_full`** | §14.9 laws. |

Reversible: item 1 adds answers where there were none. Item 2 changes one refusal from `Failed("Full")` to `Full`; the gateway already maps `Full` to its own `Full`.

## CHANGES-v2.72 (build slice F3 of the activation fate; lane run-activation-fate-f3-2483)

Built: the prepare launch's fourth argument (the hex of the value-codec `Fates`, launch words 23 and 24), `PrepareConfig.fates`, TRIGGERS as `TriggerReport { rows, unmatched }` with each row's `hold` and `fate`, Begin's `held`, error class 13 `Unsettled`, `InstallRefusal.InstallUnsettled`, `hirsel deploy --held`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> and `--held-entry`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->, L40 (d), L49's and L54's v2.62 cases, L70 (d) on a disk store and L70 (e), and the `operator_wire` vectors. Laws run on Cranelift and LLVM; `hirsel test` has no evaluator backend. Decisions where the spec was silent, each reversible with no stored format:
- **E1. The prepare scan settles with the install's code.** `fate.settle_read` is `fate.settle` over a reader and snapshot, and the prepare child calls it on `store.staged_reader` and `staged_snapshot`. Prepare and install therefore agree on which rows are held, on each row's fate and on `unmatched`. A store with no trigger tables reports no rows and no `unmatched` path.
- **E2. The fates are opaque bytes to the launcher.** The launcher passes Begin's `held` bytes as the prepare argument and never decodes them. The place decodes them with `fate.decode`, which also refuses entries that are unsorted or repeated. Bytes that do not decode end the prepare child before it opens the export: exit 1 from `launch_prepare_args`, or `Missing("prepare fates")` on the launch-word path.
- **E3. The platform reads `held` from D's recorded Begin** (`deploy_held`). A drift retry whose request row cannot be read prepares with `Fates(all: None, entries: [])`, so it can only fail `Unsettled`, never settle a row by a default.
- **E4. `Unsettled` is decided by the host from the report.** When a prepare child exits 0 and its report has a `held` group with no fate or an `unmatched` path, the host aborts OLD's export once and fails D `Unsettled` with the report in its preview. The child writes TRIGGERS and no PREPARED.
- **E5. The hosted install child keeps no fates (replaces D2's "until F3").** An install child has no deploy request. A hosted install that finds a held run is refused `InstallUnsettled` (tag 10, after main's `InstallPredecessor`, no payload), and the operator recovers with a hand install that names the fates (`--hirsel-held`<!-- doc-ref: external -->), as launch.md states.
- **E6. Paths print as text.** The deploy JSON prints a trigger row's `path` and each `unmatched` path as UTF-8 text, or as hex when the path is not UTF-8, the same rule `fate.push_report` uses. `--held-entry PATH=FATE`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> takes that text and splits at the last `=`. The client refuses a repeated path, or a second `--held`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->, before any I/O, and refuses either option with `--status`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> or with a grant decision.
- **E7. L70 (d) cuts the install's own I/O.** A clean close syncs everything, so the law scripts an `Eio` at each filesystem operation of the install in turn, then crashes the sim filesystem. It must see recoveries on both sides of the MANIFEST rename. **The objects@1 half stays UNTESTED.** The settle rows are staged in the activation's one commit, and on objects@1 that commit's CHECKPOINT head write publishes the activation and binding atomically (STORE `bucket_test_m2_checkpoint_advance`). The runner has no objects@1 crash harness in `[tests.sources]` yet.
- **E8. L70 (e) drives the hot path below the host.** It runs `begin_export_copy`, `prepare_export`, `validate_prepared`, `reserve_import`, `import_trees`, `keep_import` and `cutover_store` on (a)'s store, with (a)'s fates. PREPARED carries four rewrites (A, B, C, F). The cutover is the one commit after the export, and at that commit the rows are those that (a) states.
- **E9. The private runner notice is renamed `TriggerNotice`,** so that `TriggerReport` names the public prepare report.

## CHANGES-v2.71 (build of v2.69's mints and v2.70's bind mint; lane runner-grant-v269-build-2509)

v2.71 records what the build of v2.69 and v2.70 settled. v2.67's single grant task, `wire_bind`, `WireSeed`, `PlaceConfig`'s `B`, `law_bind_only` and the checker refusal `wire_bind_nonshareable` are deleted with no shim. Each present `Mint` (`attempt`, `call`, `claim`, `bind`) has its own seed, task, `mint_bytes` and `mint_steps`, and its mint runs as `isolate` around `bound steps`, so a mint failure fails only its own request.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.71 says |
|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | Open refusal order | **STATED** | `check_grant_lists` runs on the config before the store opens, in this order: a `wire` with no `bind` mint is `BindNoGrant`; a `TriggerConfig` listing a granted entry with no `attempt` mint is `TriggerNoGrant(path)`; a `CallMint.entries` element naming no registered granted entry of that path and argument schema is `CallNotGranted(path)`. The path is the PROG-9 definition identity, e.g. `runner@1/runner_calls/mn_run`. |
| B2 | The head term | **STATED** | `place_head_bytes` adds, per present mint, its task record under `mint_bytes` and its one-slot inbox. A mint whose task record does not fit is `Full` at sizing, before the head starts. |
| B3 | HTTP and claims | **STATED** | `operate`'s HTTP front gets the `bind` mint's inbox, through `BindGrant`, and no claim mint's inbox: `operate` serves no HTTP keyed route. A front outside `operate` passes its own `BindGrant` (`grant_binds_of`). |
| B4 | `BindGrant` | **STATED** | A session or HTTP front holds `BindGrant[G]`: the place's `Wire[G]` and a sender to the `bind` mint. Each Hello and each HTTP bind asks that mint for a fresh `G`, which the binding task owns and drops when the bind ends. A failed bind mint is Close 1013 or, over HTTP, 503 with `Retry-After: 5` for `OutOfBytes` and 500 for a fault or `OutOfTime`; never 1008 or 401. |
| B5 | Session-owner bind term | **CHANGED** | v2.70 sized a session owner as `WIRE_OWNER_FLOOR + n_granted` and left the Hello's bind child (4096 + `wire.bind_bytes`) out, so a JWT binding on a default place overran its owner. The owner now has a separate bind term, `bind_owner_bytes` = 4096 + `bind_bytes`, or 0 with no wire, apart from `n_granted` and the room term. `room_serving_bytes`, `serve_wire` and `serve_connection` take it; `serving_bytes` (no wire) passes 0. jwtbind's `jwt_default_place` binds a JWT on `wire_defaults` with `n_granted` 0. |
| B6 | L36 case (3) | **CHANGED** | A rejected credential's bind still mints one `G` from the `bind` mint (v2.70 mints per Hello, before the binding judges). It causes no call or attempt mint and no granted run. |
| B7 | Stored-stream position after a granted call | **CHANGED: positions are stored calls only; client fix** | The SDK, `runner.client` and `runner.fixture`'s client advanced on every answered position, so a stored call after a compute or granted one went out at p > latest + 1, which S7 rejects, and ended `Abandoned`. A client now knows each entry's kind from its Key (the SDK from the generated `kind` beside `want`) and advances only on a stored call's reply: a transact `Returned` or `Failed`, or `Failed(Gone)` (§1.3 Positions, STORE-SPEC §1.8 P1). A granted call whose attempt may have been delivered ends `LocallyUnknown` and is never re-sent. The wire, S7 and F6 are unchanged. `client_at` takes an optional `Resume` (stream and next position) in place of `first_p`, and `resume_at` reads it back, so a reconnect continues its stream. Laws: `test_client_positions_stored`, and the SDK's `positions_move_only_on_stored_calls` and `granted_lost_attempt_is_not_resent`. |

Reversible: B1 to B4 restate built behaviour. B5 adds a sizing term, so a place sized exactly at v2.70's sum may need a larger `--limit`. B7 changes client code, generated bindings and spec text only, with no wire byte, store format, recovery format or admit rule, so it reverts cleanly.

## CHANGES-v2.70 (J-D4: a route for a `Secret` into a binding, and HMAC JWTs; lane jwt-jd4-design-2500)

v2.69 gave claim functions and granted bodies a route to a `Secret` on paper only. v2.70 checks that route and closes J-D4. http.md K-2's worked examples were written as real Hirsel and type-checked and run on main 973c2391. The check used a scratch package outside the repo, with that tree's `runner` and `store` copied in. v2.69's `Mint` is not built, so the package declared it as a stand-in, with a mint task that runs the mint as `isolate` around `bound steps`. Seven cases passed:
- Standard Webhooks' published vector binds the message id as the key. Several signature entries pass. A wrong tag is refused, and the ±300 s tolerance holds at 1614265630999 and fails at 1614265631000.
- GitHub's documented delivery passes. The same body resent under a new delivery id is a new key. An uppercase hex signature is refused.
- A `verify`-use key gets `Denied` from `hmac_sha256` and `jws_hmac_sign`. A 26-byte `mac` key gets `ShortKey`.
- A bind mint's `G`, holding a `latest.handle` and a `secret.share` holder, crosses `isolate` and a channel. A binding over that `G` verifies a Python-signed HS256 JWT through `std.jwt.hmac_key`, accepts it up to `exp + leeway` and refuses another key's tag and an `RS256` header.
- RFC 7515 A.1 verifies through `verify_jws` with a `verify`-use key, and a token with one changed signature character is refused.

The check found three spec defects (F1 to F3). F4 to F8 are the design. Everything except the RUN check above is **PLANNED**: this version changes spec text only.

| # | Finding | Decision | What v2.70 says | Evidence |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | §12.1's `Mint` cannot be written | **FIXED: `mint_bytes`, `mint_steps`** | `bytes` is a reserved word (LANG-2: `bound bytes`, `try_spawn(.., bytes n, ..)`), so `Mint { .., bytes: u64, .. }` does not parse. The fields are `mint_bytes` and `mint_steps`, like `bind_bytes` and `claim_bytes`. §12.1, rule 13, L36 and http.md K-2 now name the new fields. CHANGES-v2.69 keeps its own wording. | RUN: refused by the parser on 973c2391 |
| F2 | K-2's `fn claim_hook(..) waits` is refused | **FIXED: no `waits`** | LANG-8 refuses `waits` on a function whose body waits for nothing. Both worked examples drop it. A function that does not wait fits `Claim.f`'s `StaticFn[fn(G, ClaimRequest) -> Result[Claimed, ()] waits]`. §15.2's `jwtbind.bind` drops it too: the built one does not wait. | RUN: refused with `waits` and accepted without it; `github_claim()` builds a `runner.Claim` |
| F3 | Standard Webhooks' grant line delivers the wrong bytes | **FIXED: a file of the decoded key** | `env:WEBHOOK_KEY` delivers the variable's bytes as they are. A key held as `whsec_…` therefore fails every signature: the run exited 1, while the base64-decoded bytes exited 0. The key is binary, and an environment variable cannot hold NUL. So the line is `--grant 'Secret:hook=file:/run/secrets/webhook verify'`, and the file holds the decoded bytes that follow `whsec_`. A GitHub secret is its own text, so `env:` works for it. | RUN: `grantcheck` under `hirsel run` |
| F4 | `Wire.bind` has no route to a `Secret` | **CHANGED: the binding takes the place's `G` from a `bind` mint** | `Grants` gains `bind: Option[Mint[S, H, (), G]]`. Each bind (a Hello, or an HTTP request on a place with a `Wire`) asks the `bind` mint task for a fresh `G`, as a claim does, and calls the binding with a view of it. `Wire[G]` holds only the binding and its bounds: `runner.wire(bind, bind_bytes, bind_steps)`, where `bind: StaticFn[fn(G, Bytes) -> Result[BoundCaller, BindRefusal] waits]`. `runner.wire_bind` is deleted, and `PlaceConfig` and `operate` lose `B`. The mint passes in whatever the binding needs: a changing key set as a `latest.handle` of the application's `Latest`, a sealed key as a `secret.share` holder, and a plain value as a copy. All three never wait and never fail. So the binding, the claim function, granted calls and granted attempts all get their authority one way. K-2's "the claim function is `Wire.bind` for a keyed binding" now holds by type: both take `G`, so an application can call one function from both (K-2's `Idempotency-Key` example). The `G` is dropped when the bind ends. A session keeps no `G` after Welcome, so `bind.mint_bytes` scales with concurrent binds, not with sessions. The fold couples the binding to the place's one `G` type. Every mint that builds a `G` must fill the binding's fields, and every granted body's `G` carries them, unless the place makes those fields an `Option` that only the `bind` mint fills. | Type-checked and run in scratch (above): the mint's `G` and the binding over it |
| F5 | Bind mint refusals | **STATED** | A `Wire` on a place with no `bind` mint refuses the open with `OpenError.BindNoGrant`, as a keyed binding with no `claim` mint gives `KeyedNoGrant`. A failed bind mint is never a credential failure, and the binding does not run. On WebSocket every mint failure is Close 1013 before Welcome. Over HTTP, `Error(Capacity)` or an exhausted `mint_bytes` is 503 with `Retry-After: 5`, and a fault or an exhausted `mint_steps` is B-7's 500 `{"fault": ..}`, as for the claim mint. The SDK already recovers from 1013 and 503 (sdk.md), so no new close code is needed. The askers are bounded by admitted Hellos and HTTP requests (`max_sessions`, `socket.pending_max`). | PLANNED |
| F6 | The cost of the fold | **MEASURED** | Each bind adds one send and one receive to one serial mint task, next to the bind child it already spawns. v2.60's `wire_bind` read a `Latest` with no queue. The mint itself copies a handle and shares a holder. J9 measured the landed fold on one CPU with a release LLVM build, 120 interleaved pairs of 2,000 ES256 JWT binds: direct 50.413 µs/bind (19,836 binds/s), minted 50.748 µs/bind (19,705 binds/s), paired mint cost median 0.400 µs/bind (mean 0.411, p99 2.185). The guess of at most 10 µs per bind holds. The mint task does not limit the bind rate here: the minted rate is 0.66% below direct while each bind spends about 50 µs verifying the JWT. A multi-task mint is additive if a cheaper or much hotter binding ever makes the serial mint the bottleneck; a second seed route is not the answer. | RUN: `bench/runner/mint_cost.py`, 120 interleaved pairs on CPU 16 under `core/tools/timing_shield.py` |
| F7 | Rotating a sealed key | **NARROWED: J-D11, OPEN** | A mint's seed lives as long as the place, so a sealed key is replaced by a redeploy. systemd credentials and Cloudflare Workers secrets work the same way: a new value means a new unit start or a new deployment. For overlap the seed holds both keys, named by `kid` (§15.2 `Mac`). Rotation in place, as Envoy SDS and the Vault agent do it, would need a host-pushed holder that a mint can share, which is J-D11. | READ: §15.11 |
| F8 | HMAC JWTs | **CHANGED: HS256, HS384 and HS512 admitted, verify-only, with a symmetric source** | `jwtbind.Source` gains `Mac(List[MacKey])`, holding 1 to 4 keys by `kid`. `verifier` refuses with a `ConfigError` any key that can sign, any key below an allowed algorithm's minimum, an HS algorithm with a `Static` or `Remote` source, an asymmetric algorithm with `Mac`, and an empty or duplicate `kid` list (§15.2). The reason is in §15.2 "Keys". | RUN for the primitives (above); PLANNED for `jwtbind` |

Reversible: `Grants.bind`, `Wire[G]`, `BindNoGrant` and `Source.Mac` are config and API only, and no stored format depends on them. Reverting the fold restores `wire_bind` and `B`, and changes every call site of `runner.wire`.

## CHANGES-v2.69 (review 2486's findings on the place grants; lane runner-grant-amend-2488)

Review runner-grant-place-review-2486 (AMENDS NEEDED) found that v2.67's wire calls and trigger attempts share one grant task, one allowance and one serial queue. It also found two config states the old types could not express, and stale text. v2.69 makes each mint a **bulkhead**: its own seed, allowance, step bound and task, and a mint that cannot wait. Each finding's law is in L36 or L-claim. All are **PLANNED**. Spec text only; nothing here is built.

| # | Finding | Decision | What v2.69 says | Departs from 2486? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| F1 | Wire calls starve the attempt mint; a mint fault ends the grant task | **CHANGED: one `Mint` per use, each its own task and allowance** | `Grants` holds up to three `Mint`s: `attempt`, `call` and `claim`. Each has its own seed (all three of type `S`), `bytes` and `steps`, and each is served by its own **mint task**. A live `G` stays charged to the `bytes` of the mint that made it (MEM-20), so no use can exhaust another use's allowance. Each mint runs as `isolate` around `bound steps steps`, so a step overrun is caught once its bound has ended. An `Error(Capacity)` from the mint, or an exhausted allowance, gives `Failed(OutOfBytes)`; a fault gives `Failed(Raised)`; an exhausted step bound gives `Failed(OutOfTime(steps))`. Each fails only its own request, and the mint task keeps serving. Law `mint_allowance_apart`. | Yes. 2486 proposed two figures for one task. One task cannot keep two allowances: MEM-20 hands a bound's charges to its task when the bound ends, and a sent `G` keeps the record it was made under. So two allowances need two tasks, and an owned seed has one owner, so each mint carries its own seed. A seed that holds a `Sender` or a `Secret` is duplicated by the application with `channel.sender` or `secret.share`, which never wait and never fail. |
| F2 | A listed granted trigger with no `attempt` mint is consumed `Gone` | **CHANGED: refused at open** | A `TriggerConfig` that lists a granted entry while `grants` has no `attempt` mint refuses with `OpenError.TriggerNoGrant(path)`. The check runs on the config alone, before the store opens, so it refuses before any start commits. It runs in the serving start, the install and the prepare child alike, since all three take the same `PlaceConfig`. So the prepare scan never classes such a row as runnable. "With no `attempt` mint the run is `Gone`" is deleted. Law `trigger_no_grant_refuses`. | No |
| F3 | One serial queue, a `call` mint that may wait, keyed claims that mint before authentication | **CHANGED: no mint waits, and uses do not share a queue** | A mint is `fn(S, H, I) -> Result[G, Capacity]`, not `waits`, and it takes its input by view: `isolate`'s body takes no owned state (FAIL-16, F reserved), so the `attempt` mint calls `share_trigger_context` (non-waiting) to put a context in its `G`. Every mint in the tree (notes, harness, platform, runner laws) copies senders, clocks and handles and waits for nothing. `isolate`'s body cannot wait (FAIL-16), so a waiting mint could not be contained. A `G` that needs a waited-for resource gets it in the body, which runs in the request's own task. Attempts, calls and claims each queue only at their own mint task, so no use waits behind another. A keyed claim's pre-authentication mint comes from `claim`, under `claim.steps` and inside `claim.bytes`. The number of claims waiting for a mint is at most the place's admitted HTTP requests, which `max_sessions` and `socket.pending_max` bound (http.md "No per-Caller rate"). Laws `mint_waits` (checker) and `mint_step_bound`. | Yes. 2486 kept `attempt` waiting. That would leave the attempt mint unable to run inside `isolate`, so its fault would end its task, which is F1(b) again. `attempt_not_behind_calls` stays withdrawn (2486's correction): separate tasks make it true by construction. |
| F4 | `call: Some` exposes every `Granted` entry, and keyed claims force `call: Some` | **CHANGED: exposure is a list; claims have their own mint** | `Grants.call` is a `CallMint`: a `Mint` and `entries: triggers.Entries`, the granted entries that a wire call may start. Matching is by path and argument schema, as `trigger_key` matches trigger rows. A wire call to a granted entry outside the list is `Refused(NotAllowed)` at the session, before anything is reserved and before any mint. A list element that names no registered granted entry refuses the open with `CallNotGranted(path)`. No durable row can name it, so a typo must not silently hide the entry. Keyed claims use `Grants.claim`, so a webhook place exposes no granted entry to the wire. A `G` that holds a `TriggerContext` is made only by `attempt`, since `TriggerContext` is opaque (`share_trigger_context` is its only maker). A place whose trigger bodies need one lists those entries for triggers and not for `call`. Law `call_mint_scope`. | 2486's first option, taken with both halves: a list and a separate claim mint |
| F5 | Ownership, budget, drain and claim status of the grant task | **STATED** | The head spawns one mint task per present `Mint`, after `schema` (rule 12: H exists only from then), whatever the trigger configuration. Each task's `bytes`, its record and its inbox are a term of `operate_bytes`, so `--limit` counts them. The head ends them at `Finish`, after S's sessions and its own trigger drive have ended and before `store.close`. So a call or attempt admitted before admission closes always finds its mint, and `Failed(Gone)` from a mint is unreachable before `Forced`. A keyed claim whose mint fails gets 503 with `Retry-After: 5` (capacity) or B-7's 500 `{"fault": ..}` (fault or step bound), records nothing and never runs the claim function. It is never 401: the credential was not judged. A call whose deadline or session expiry passes while it waits for its mint never runs its body. Law `grants_without_triggers`. | No |
| F6 | Replay and charging unstated | **STATED** (rule 13, §12.1) | A mint is application code and is never recorded. A granted wire call records nothing, so no replay re-mints. Attempt n + 1 mints again from the top. A mint's steps are its own `steps`, not the entry's `s`, and its `G`'s bytes are its mint's `bytes` for the `G`'s life, not the entry's `n`. No law. | No |
| F7 | L36 `bind_granted` cannot be built as written | **CHANGED: L36 rewritten** | Case (1)'s trigger is written by a second, transact, call. `mint_per_call` is added. The v2.24 and v2.28 statement text is replaced. The RUN column now says only what main has run (`granted_copied`, `law_bind_only`), and every v2.67 and v2.69 case is PLANNED. | No |
| F8 | Stale text | **FIXED**, and J-D4 ruled | A7, rule 13's verb ("mints none" becomes "issues none"), §3 "Binding", §12.3's `PlaceConfig` parameters, §15.1's signatures, §15.4 "Asymmetric keys only", J-D4, runner.md, http.md H9 L-claim. **J-D4:** a mint over an owned seed that holds a `Secret` can give each `G` a holder from `secret.share` (TLS-5: it admits nothing, never fails, never waits). So a K-2 claim function and a granted body now have a route to a `Secret`, by reading. Neither http.md's worked examples nor such a mint has been checked, so J-D4 stays OPEN for them until a check runs. `Wire.bind` still has no route: its seed is plain or a `Latest`, and neither holds a `Secret`. | Rules on 2486's question |

Reversible: the `Mint`, `CallMint` and `Grants` types are config only, and no stored format depends on them. `TriggerNoGrant` and `CallNotGranted` are open refusals. Each mint task's charge is a new `operate_bytes` term, so a place sized exactly at v2.67's sum may need a larger `--limit`.

## CHANGES-v2.68 (build slice F2 of the activation fate; lane run-activation-fate-f2-2448)

Built: `fate.hirsel` (module `runner.fate`: `HeldFate`, `EntryFate`, `Fates`, `HeldBy`, the shared resolution `fate.resolve`, the held class `fate.is_held`, and `fate.settle`), the settle rewrite through `triggers.settle` and `store.stage_rewrite`, the standalone install's `--hirsel-held*`<!-- doc-ref: external --> options and `OpenError.Unsettled`, and L70 (a) to (c) (`runner_activation_fate_laws.hirsel`, `activation_settles_held`). Decisions where the spec was silent, each reversible with no stored format:
- **D1. Fates travel on the selection.** `Selection` is `Current(u64) | Reopen | Install(Fates) | InstallAt(u64, Fates)`. `launch_selection` reads `--hirsel-held=`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and `--hirsel-held-entry=`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and exits 1 on a bad fate, a repeated `--hirsel-held`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, a repeated entry path, an entry with an empty path, or any held option without `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external -->. An entry option splits at its last `=`, since a fate never holds one; entries are kept sorted by path.
- **D2. The hosted install child settles with no fates until F3.** It runs `InstallAt(version, Fates(all: None, entries: []))`, so a hosted install that finds a held run is refused `Unsettled`, which reaches the host as `InstallConfig` (the `InstallRefusal` wire is unchanged; F3 adds its class).
- **D3. The standalone install scans the staged catalog.** `store.staged_install_snapshot` gives the install's staged rows, the counterpart of the export's `staged_snapshot`, so a replaced `store.triggers.pending` is scanned in its staged range (B1); its rewrite edits the staged range (F1.5). A held row is the scan's: not `cancelling`, and `triggers.held_on_start` against NEW's `trigger_stamp`.
- **D4. The install's report.** The `Unsettled` stderr line is followed by one line per held group, keyed (path, schema, hold) with its fate, count and earliest due, and one line per unmatched path. It has no kind column (only granted rows are held) and no other class; the TRIGGERS `TriggerReport` stays F3's.
- **D5. An in-memory store has no activation** (`!store.is_disk`), so its install settles nothing and ignores the fates.
- **D6. L70 (b)'s fence** is checked by holding attempt 1's `Attempt` across the install and calling `trigger_effect` with it: refused `StaleAttempt`, and the row its step 0 wrote is unchanged. L70 (a)'s "C's step rows" is not checked separately: C has no step in the law, and the settle writes no `store.steps` row. **L70 (d) stays UNTESTED**: the settle is staged into the one activation commit that the existing crash laws already cover, but no crash law yet holds a held row.

## CHANGES-v2.67 (the place grants: one grant source for triggers and wire calls, so `wire_bind` places expose granted entries; lane runner-wire-bind-granted-2464)

`runner.wire_bind` had no grant half. A place that binds its sessions through it could expose no `runner.granted` entry over the wire, and a place whose triggers hold a movable `G` (a `TriggerContext`, a `Sender`) could not use `runner.wire`'s plain copied grant at all. The workaround was to drop `wire_bind` and pass the capability through pure transact and query code. v2.67 moves the grant source to the place.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.67 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Where `G` comes from | **CHANGED: `PlaceConfig.grants`** | `Grants[G, H, S]` holds the place's grant seed `S` and two optional mints over it: `attempt(S, H, take TriggerContext)` for a granted trigger attempt (v2.27's `TriggerConfig.grant`, moved) and `call(S, H)` for a wire call to a `Granted` entry and for a keyed HTTP claim (http.md K-2). `bytes` is the grant task's allowance (v2.27's `grant_bytes`). One grant task owns `S` for the place's life and runs both mints in arrival order, so a waiting mint delays the next one. `TriggerConfig` keeps `entries` and `author` and loses its type parameters. | like kinds alike |
| 2 | `Wire` | **CHANGED: binding only, `Wire[B]`** | `Wire` loses its grant half and `G`. `runner.wire(seed, bind, ..)` takes a plain seed `B` copied into each bind, `runner.wire_bind(seed, bind, ..)` a `Latest[B]` (v2.60). Both require `bind`, so `None` stays the only "no binding". The grant half, `WireGrant`, its `duplicate` and the checker case `wire_granted_nonplain` are deleted. | one source |
| 3 | Wire calls to `Granted` | **CHANGED: minted per call** | A wire call to a `Granted` entry runs only when `grants.call` is `Some`, with or without a `Wire`. The entry's task asks the grant task for a fresh `G` and then runs the body with it and its Caller. Each call gets its own `G`: none is shared (no `Arc[G]`), none is consumed by another call. With no `call` mint the call is `Refused(NotAllowed)` before anything is reserved, as v2.28's bind-only wire was. A mint error is `Failed(OutOfBytes)`. Transact, query and compute entries never cause a mint and never receive `G`. | R6 |
| 4 | Keyed HTTP claims | **CHANGED: minted per claim** | A claim function runs with a `G` from `grants.call`. A place with a keyed or retired binding and no `call` mint refuses its open with `KeyedNoGrant(op)` (was `KeyedNoWire`). | http.md K-2 |
| 5 | Rule 13 and D9 | **AMENDED** | D9 (a) "a copied `G: plain + send` per call" becomes "a fresh `G` per call from the place's `call` mint". A plain grant is the mint that returns a copy of a plain seed. "A movable `G` stays trigger-only" is withdrawn: a movable `G` reaches a wire call only as the value minted for that call. D9 (b), (c) and (d) stay dropped. The runner still authenticates nothing and makes no access decision. The mint is application code, and it receives no Caller and no credential. | R6 substance kept |
| 6 | Laws | **CHANGED: L36** | L36 `bind_only` becomes `bind_granted`, §8. | — |

Reversible: `Grants` is one config field, and `Wire` is opaque. Moving the mints back changes type spellings and the place's config only; no stored format depends on them.

## CHANGES-v2.66 (recheck 2430's amends to the activation fate; lane run-activation-fate-amend2-2444, with STORE-SPEC v2.103)

Recheck activation-fate-recheck-2430 rated v2.64 sound with amends. v2.66 applies its B1–B6. Text only; not built. No ruling changes.
- **B1.** When NEW's migration replaces `store.triggers.pending`, the scan classifies the rows of the staged replacement range, and the settle calls `stage_rewrite`, which on a replaced table edits the staged range and writes no 0x0085 rewrite (§14.6 "One commit", STORE v2.103 §3.5a). This replaces v2.64's A3 sentence, under which the scan saw settled rows. The store change is its own slice, F1.5, after F1 ("Not built" under CHANGES-v2.62).
- **B2.** STORE v2.103 §3.5a places the 0x0085 frame last, in role 6, and reads OLD's key check from the roots the export lease captured.
- **B3.** `HeldBy = Cut | Revision(same_code: bool) | Request(same_code: bool)`: a rollback to the code that started a `Revision`-parked run is the one case where Release is exact. The fate stays one per entry path; `hold` informs it and never selects it (§14.6, launch.md).
- **B4.** L40 (d) gains A6's vacuous case: an `entries` element for the unchanged entry's path is not unmatched and rewrites nothing.
- **B5.** The reason A5 declines `PreparedMeta.rewrites` is now that the loss fails safe, not compatibility. The count is still not taken.
- **B6.** The F1 transition cost names the real refusal, and a second transition cost is added: F3 changes the prepare launch, so a host built after F3 cannot prepare a NEW built before it (CHANGES-v2.62 "Not reversible").

## CHANGES-v2.65 (spec drift from the dev/add design; lane run-dev-add-s10-text-2421)

Text only; no rule changes. It records what the runner already does (`runner-identity.hirsel`, `runner-wire-protocol.hirsel`).
- **One identity.** `ReleaseIdentity` has five fields: `revision`, `n_place`, `exported` (each entry as `described`, `args_fp`, `result_fp`, `revision`), `bindings` (the HTTP route bindings, `HttpDescribe`) and `slots` (each export's query slot schemas). v2.42's three-field list was drift (§6 "One identity", §2 rule 10, "Restart and rebuild").
- **No `Upgrade` frame.** The frame grammar has none. An old client that holds a gone entry is answered `Placed(Gone)` for that want, and the TypeScript SDK client then enters `Incompatible` before it sends its pending call (§6 "Gone keys", "Matching an old client", §12 preview).
- **`hirsel dev`.** Its store is persistent and dev-owned, and the SDK client restores sessions across a swap (launch.md "Dry run and dev loop"). §14.6's dev line says so.
## CHANGES-v2.64 (review 2409's amends to the activation fate; lane run-activation-fate-amend-2425, with STORE-SPEC v2.102)

Review activation-fate-review-2409 rated v2.62's §14.6–14.7 and STORE v2.101 §3.5a sound with amends. v2.64 applies its A1–A9. Text only; not built. The standing ruling of 2393 is kept: there is no default fate, and `Unsettled` is refused.
- **A1.** L40 (d)'s second case resolves `Release` from `all`: the first report's `held` groups have `fate` `None`, the second's have `Release`, and `unmatched` is [`no.such`] (§14.7).
- **A2.** L40 (a)/(b) and the `held` class sentence here and in launch.md no longer describe parking: an activation that holds a run settles it or is refused (§14.6, §14.7, launch.md "Preparing the pending trigger report").
- **A3.** When NEW's migration replaces `store.triggers.pending`, the fate is applied to each row as it is staged for the replacement range, and no rewrite is written for that table (§14.6 "One commit").
- **A4.** The prepare child stages each rewrite with `stage_rewrite` on its `StagedMigration`, which makes all four checks against the staged catalog; OLD's M1–M3 check only the frame's order, the table and key, and `VALUE_LIMIT` (STORE v2.102 §3.5a, L51 (e″)).
- **A5.** A rewrite list that does not fit its frame fails prepare `Limit(triggers)` (§14.6, STORE §3.5a). The optional `PreparedMeta.rewrites` count is NOT TAKEN. (v2.66) It would guard only a synced PREPARED that loses exactly its last frame, and that loss fails safe: the file activates with its held rows unsettled, NEW's head parks them as before v2.62, the next activation settles them, and no fate is misapplied. Every other frame class is count-covered (`meta.changes`, `meta.ranges`, inventory rows), so taking the count is the /wholehog end-state; it is declined because the format is pre-1.0 and the count can be added later.
- **A6.** An `entries` element is unmatched only when its path is the entry path of no pending row and of no entry OLD or NEW registers. A typo is still refused; a real path whose last held run ended between the report and Begin is vacuous (§14.6, launch.md).
- **A7.** Each `held` group is also keyed by `hold`, so the operator sees a R-2393-1 re-hold: `Request(same_code: true)` is a run that NEW's own code held (§14.6, launch.md, L40 (d)).
- **A8.** STORE v2.102 §3.5a: a rewrite on a Tiered table is a row of the same activation cut, as for any other table.
- **Build.** F2 builds the `hold` key's resolution, A3's staged-fate path and A6's unmatched rule; F3 carries `hold` on TRIGGERS. L40 (b)'s fates (`all: Some(Cancel)`) and `HeldBy`'s shape are this lane's decisions, not the review's text.
- **A9.** L70: each row has its own entry path; (d) also crashes around objects@1's head write; a refused activation "publishes no cut" rather than "writes nothing", since an objects@1 install's claim has already deposed a running writer (§6 "Genesis and explicit install").

## CHANGES-v2.63 (review 2385's amends to §15; lane jwt-caller-amend-2400)

Review jwt-caller-review-2385 rated §15 sound with amends. v2.63 applies its A1–A7. Text only; not built.
- **A1.** `Issuer.max_life_s` (1..=86400, default 3600, GUESS), checked at bind: a token whose `exp` lies further ahead is `Rejected`. `stale_ms` gains an upper bound of 7 d. §15.5 states how long a removed key reaches (§15.2, §15.3 step 3).
- **A2.** `runner.wire` keeps its v2.57 contract: a plain `G` copied into each bind, with no `Latest` and no `Arc`. Only a `runner.wire_bind` seed is a `Latest[B]`. R6, rule 13 and D9 (c) stand; J-D2 loses "one seed shape" (§15.1 (a)).
- **A3.** Staleness is measured on `host.mono_ns`: `Seed.good_until_mono_ns` replaces `good_until_ms` (§15.2, §15.3 step 1, §15.7).
- **A4.** `Issuer.typ`: when set, the header's `typ` must match (RFC 7515 §4.1.9) (§15.3 step 3).
- **A5.** The binding's error is `BindRefusal`, because `runner` already has `Refusal`. `Issuer` is `shareable + send`, so `Seed` can hold it.
- **A6.** `Seed.confirmed_until_mono_ns`: an unknown `kid` is `Rejected` only while the set is confirmed, and `Unavailable` after (§15.3).
- **A7.** A `Secret` is a resource, so it is never in a value that is copied or published (a plain seed, or a `Latest` of a shareable value). (v2.69) A K-2 claim function gets its `G` from the place's `claim` mint, and a mint over an owned seed can hand each `G` a `secret.share` holder. (v2.70) The binding gets its `G` the same way, from the `bind` mint, and `wire_bind`'s seed is deleted. http.md K-2's worked examples and the bind route were type-checked and run (CHANGES-v2.70). J-D4 is closed.
- **J-D7.** "A number is never reused for another `iss`" is an operator rule that nothing enforces.

## CHANGES-v2.62 (the activation fate: an activation settles every held run in its own commit; lane run-activation-fate-spec-2393, with STORE-SPEC v2.101)

This writes ENDSTATE `storage-runs` "one activation commit selects ... the fate of open runs" and steps law 9's "the activation's fate for open runs decides whether it is released on the new code or cancelled". It is option B of the platform truth audit (research-1124/platform-audit/REPORT.md, "Decision for the lead"). The whole contract is in §14.6 "The activation fate". Changes elsewhere:
- **§6.** The standalone install's cut and the prepared cutover's cut also carry one activation rewrite per held trigger row.
- **§14.6.** `PrepareConfig` gains `fates`, and the prepare scan resolves each `held` row's fate. An unsettled report writes no `PREPARED`.
- **launch.md.** Begin gains `held` (a 6th field), the error classes gain `13 Unsettled`, `hirsel deploy` gains `--held`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> and `--held-entry`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->, and the install gains `--hirsel-held`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and `--hirsel-held-entry`<!-- doc-ref: external -->. The prepare launch gains a 4th argument, and TRIGGERS carries `fate` and `unmatched`.
- **STORE-SPEC v2.101.** Activation rewrites (§3.5a): PREPARED kind 0x0085 and `store.stage_rewrite`.
- **Laws.** L40 (d), L49's fates case, L54's `Unsettled` case and the new L70 `activation_settles_held` (§14.7).
- **Residual R-2393-1.** A released run whose new body diverges from its recorded steps is held again by steps law 4 and settled by the next activation or by the program (§14.6).

**Build slices.** F1, F1.5, F2 (v2.68), F3 (v2.72) and F4 (v2.74) are all built:
- **F1, store.** Kind 0x0085 and `stage_rewrite`, with the STORE L51 (e″) case. It lands refusing a rewrite on a table the staging replaces.
- **F1.5, store (v2.66, B1).** `stage_rewrite` on a table the staging replaces edits the row in the staged replacement range and writes no 0x0085 row (STORE v2.103 §3.5a), with an L51 (e″) case. It follows F1 and must land before any release replaces `store.triggers.pending`; none does today. Built (activation-fate-f15-2447), replacing F1's refusal.
- **F2, runner.** `Fates` and the shared resolution, the settle rewrite, the install's `--hirsel-held*`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and `Unsettled`, and L70 (a) to (d). (v2.68: built, with L70 (a) to (c). v2.72: (d) on a disk store; its objects@1 half UNTESTED, CHANGES-v2.72 E7. v2.90: the objects@1 half built, CHANGES-v2.90.)
- **F3, platform.** The prepare argument, TRIGGERS `fate` and `unmatched`, Begin `held`, the `Unsettled` class, `hirsel deploy --held`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->, `--held-entry`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->, L40 (d), L49, L54, L70 (e) and an `operator_wire` vector. (v2.72: built.)
- **F4.** `hirsel dev` passes `Release`, and core/std/api/runner.md. (v2.74: built, with the dev case in §14.7.)

**Not reversible.** Nothing. Every format here is pre-1.0 (Begin, PREPARED 0x0085, TRIGGERS, the prepare arguments), and "no default fate" can become a default later without rewriting a row. One transition cost: OLD applies the activation rewrites, so a deploy from an OLD built before F1 with a held run fails its import (`ImportMalformed` at validation, before anything is reserved; v2.66). Its operator uses a standalone install with `--hirsel-held`<!-- doc-ref: external --> once. A deploy with no held run writes no 0x0085 frame and is unaffected. A second transition cost (v2.66): F3 adds the prepare launch's fourth argument and changes TRIGGERS to `TriggerReport`, and a place parses its prepare arguments with its own code (`runner.launch_prepare_args` and the runtime's `prepare_path` both require exactly four arguments today). So a host built after F3 cannot hot-deploy a NEW built before F3: its prepare child refuses the five-argument launch, and the host fails the deploy with OLD serving on. Its operator rebuilds NEW, or installs it standalone once.

J2 implements `BindRefusal`, the `Latest` seed for `wire_bind`, and the runner refusal responses. The JWT binding and client recovery have their own build slices (§15.9).

Not reversible: the Caller mapping (J-D7). Everything else is reversible (§15.8).

## CHANGES-v2.58 (rooms: the ephemeral lane, as built; lanes hirsel4-cutover-2276 and hirsel4-rooms-2301)

Added rooms: the `Room` entry kind (Describe kind 4), its admission view returning `Grant`, the frames `Set`, `Send` and `Live` (tags 9–11) and the relay. The contract is §1.1 and §4 "Rooms" (rewritten in v2.59). A Room's `Described.result` is the codec of `(value schema, slot list, state schema, message schema, peers, state, message)`. Its `result_fp` covers the room's types and bounds, so a stale client fails at Hello. The client half is docs/spec/sdk.md "Rooms". The bench reference server (bench/wire) has no rooms and closes a session that sends a room frame with 1007.

## CHANGES-v2.57 (a bound session expires; lane wire-bind-expiry-2288)

A bound session had no expiry: the binding returned only a Caller, so a session kept working on an expired credential until its socket closed. The fix:
- **The binding's result.** A binding returns `Result[BoundCaller, ()]`, `BoundCaller { caller: Caller, expires_at_ms: Option[u64] }`. `expires_at_ms` is the binding's exclusive expiry in Unix milliseconds (`host.wall_ms`); `None` never expires. The application computes it after validating the credential, including any skew policy and the checked conversion of ticket seconds. A binding whose expiry the place's `Clock` has reached when ingress accepts it (`wall_ms >= expires_at_ms`) is refused like any other: WS `Close` 1008 before Welcome, HTTP 401, nothing recorded. The anonymous path has no expiry. `runner.bind_credential` takes the front's `Clock` and applies the same rule. Every binding was cut over; there is no `Caller`-only form.
- **Enforcement.** The session owner latches expiry the first time it reads `wall_ms >= expires_at_ms` (equality expires) and never unlatches, so a wall clock set back cannot revive the session. It reads the clock at each admission point (a client frame, a queued call or deferred input taken from the owner's queues) and before the first byte of each new application frame it writes (a Batch, Done or Reset, a room message, an SSE event or a private HTTP stream chunk). A finite binding arms one cancellable `std.clock.wall_alarm` at `expires_at_ms` so an idle session expires without traffic; the alarm is cancelled when the session ends. An expired session admits nothing, writes no further application frame, releases its joins, rooms and subscriptions, and closes. A frame whose first byte has left completes (ruling 73); a Close cannot overtake it, so the close code is best-effort on a blocked socket.
- **Closing.** An established WS session that expires closes with private code **4401 `AuthExpired`**. An SSE stream with its head written ends with the final event `event: expired`, which nothing else writes; a stream that ends at `lease_end` or a stop has no final event, so a client tells the two apart by the last event, and a re-request with the expired credential gets 401 either way (http.md B-9). A raw private HTTP stream ends its body, and its next request gets 401. A call admitted before expiry may commit; its reply stays byte-identical and is retrievable by the same Caller after a fresh bind.
- **Replay.** The expiry is transport state. It never enters a store key, a transaction, `CallInfo` or a recorded call, so a replay of a session that crossed expiry gives the same store state with no wall time recorded.
- **Laws** L62 `bind_expiry` and L63 `sse_expiry_terminal` (§12).
- **Open.** WS sessions (bind, idle, late delivery) and one-shot HTTP (refused at bind) enforce this today. SSE and private HTTP streams do not yet carry the expiry from `prepare` into the stream loop (so L63 is OPEN), rooms are not on main, and the replay case waits on the helper-unit host tape; each is fixed forward against this text.

Reversible: one field of the binding's result and the owner's latch; the close code is part of the SDK contract (docs/spec/sdk.md).

## CHANGES-v2.56 (the store term is Store's plan; prices from recipes; lane admission-plans-2113, with STORE-SPEC v2.76)

Text only, not yet built. RUNNER v2.54 derived X itself, from `service_bytes([])` and `follower_starter_bytes()`. L60 pinned a root of 49,616,253 that restated Store's figures, and when Store's starter charge moved from 1,034 to 1,098 the literal fell 64 bytes short (49,616,317). Platform priced a store with `disk_store_bytes`, a Runner copy of the old manifest maximum, 16,777,476. That copy is now 164 bytes below Store's 16,777,640, and it ignores the journal worker, the channels and the cut's working bound. Both are one defect: a caller restating a provider's price. The fix:
- **The store term.** The head's store term is `store.planned_bytes(plan)`, where `plan = store.planned(kind, share, p.sizes)` and share = root limit − `operate_bytes` − the keep (v2.87: 64 KiB plus the Template's `n_place` when registered in-task). Runner computes no X, and no max(T, L + …).
