- **Deleted from Runner.** `disk_store_bytes`, and every byte literal that prices a Store, a channel, a task or a box. Runner's own terms derive from its own types through MEM-33's `bytes[T]` and `charge.*`, in §12.1 "Store sizes" and in the relay and wire budgets. The relay's `RELAY_*` record and item sizes and the wire protocol's frame records are `bytes` of their record types. The counts stay Runner's: positions per caller, sends, inbox slots.
- **Platform.** `host_bytes` asks Store for one writer's price, `store.writer_bytes(store.writer_plan(Directory, options))`, and keeps its owner count of two stores (the catalog and the one a create seeds). (v2.85) The catalog's writer is priced at the catalog's own options, §14.9 "Shape".
- **Laws.** L60's literal roots are rewritten: each case adds Runner's own hand-derived terms to the `planned_bytes` that Store publishes. Store's figures are pinned only by Store's own laws (STORE §1.1, §12.10 L85).

Reversible: the spec only.

## CHANGES-v2.55.3 (sim@1 backing)

`Sim(SimBacking)` carries the S3 simulator client's sendable `Requests`, the ordered `SimLevels`, the world's `LevelSync` and `op_ms`. The task that starts the client keeps its task-affine `Workers` alive for the place. The head attaches the `Requests` and opens through `store.sim_authority`, using the same split as the Store journal. Sim backing uses the Bucket sizing rules with the weak level reservation. Its release gate waits for the strongest level (STORE §12.4).

## CHANGES-v2.55.2 (waiting Reader calls in the projection)

One `bound steps s` encloses the projection, including its waiting Reader calls. Reader failures arrive as typed Store errors. Cover matching, row conversion and allocation run in non-waiting isolates inside that step bound. A step stop in either part reaches the same bound and ends only the subscriptions being projected. An allocation failure inside the granted byte scope is `Failed(OutOfBytes)`. The projected query's read set stays indexed. [FAIL-16](../../../docs/spec/failure.md#fail-16-isolation) · [core.hirsel:324](../../core/core.hirsel#L324)

## CHANGES-v2.55.1 (v2.55's byte outcomes by source, and three corrections; lane runner-query-budget-2070)

Text only. Corrects v2.55 in place. Reversible: the spec only.
- **Bytes.** Exhaustion inside the granted `n` gives `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))`. A `bound bytes` scope catches nothing after entry (MEM-25). The non-waiting isolates catch allocation failures in cover matching, row conversion and allocation, and the Reader returns failures as typed Store errors (v2.55.2). `Refused(Budget)` applies when the entry cannot obtain `n` or a subscriber's ledger refuses its charge (§3 "Admission per subscriber" and L28 B1b).
- **Decode.** A run's decode error is `Refused(Malformed(..))`, as `query_attempt` maps it. It is not `Raised`.
- **Read sets.** Closed by STORE v2.73. A run stopped at `s`, raised, or refused carries the reads it made before the early end. Runner indexes them as the subscription's ended entry (§3 "Ended entries").
- **No bound current.** That no step bound is current around a view's evaluation is the caller's contract, as v2.54 "No bound" states for `operate`. Under MEM-30 a task spawned under a bound runs under what that bound has left. So `operate`, `platform.serve` and §14.9's actor must start the head, and through it the publisher, with no step bound current, on every backing. Under a bound with less than `s` left, `look(.., Some(s), ..)` would be `Stopped` before the body ran.
- **Words.** "Conversion" (G4, L28) and "projection" name the same phase.

## CHANGES-v2.55 (a query's run and its projection each get the entry's step bound; lane runner-query-budget-2070)

Text only, not yet built. v2.47 said "Row conversion shares the query's remaining step and byte budgets". That cannot be built. m3-s3-slots-2010 found two reasons:
- A retry-capable `look` needs a finite `steps`, and each attempt reserves that allowance from the bound current around the call (STORE §13.3 "Retry-capable calls are bounded", "Enclosing step bounds"). `query_projection` called `look(.., None, ..)` inside `adapt_query`'s `bound steps s`. Passing `Some(s)` there reserves s a second time, so every query is `Stopped`.
- No operation reads what a bound has left (MEM-30: "Nothing can name a bound").

Projection could not move into `look`'s body either: `cover_rows`, `rerun_rows` and `covered_all_pages` wait on the publisher's Reader and append to the publisher's `Log[Row]`, and a body is a plain non-`waits` `fn(Tx, E)` with an inert E (STORE §13.3 "Bodies carry no authority and no sink"). Only the encode can move into the body. Reversible: the spec only.

| Rule | v2.55 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Two phases | A query view's evaluation has two phases. The **run** decodes A, calls the body and encodes R, all in `look`'s body, under `look(r, b, Some(s), env, query_attempt)`. Each attempt gets its own bound of `s`, and no step bound is current around the call. The **projection** matches the covers and enumerates the slot rows from the Reader. It runs after `Seen`, under its own `bound steps s`, inside the same `bound bytes n` as the run. Each phase gets the entry's registered `s`. Nothing carries a remainder from one phase to the other | §4 "Query budgets", rule 4, §1.1 |
| Overrun | A run stopped at `s` and a projection stopped at `s` each produce `Failed(OutOfTime(s))`. A projection allocation that fails inside `n` produces `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))` through a local isolate or a typed Reader error (v2.55.2). The outcome ends only the subscriptions whose rows were being converted: a view's live subscribers for a rerun, a Cover-only round or a first run, and only the joiner for a join image. A session's other subscriptions, other views, the round and the publisher go on | §4 "Query budgets", §3 "Ended entries" |
| Ended entry | A projection overrun's ended entry is the read set of the run whose answer was being projected. For a G2 rerun or a G3 first run, that is the new run's complete read set, returned with `Seen`. For a Cover-only round or a join, it is the view's current read set | §3 "Ended entries" |
| CoverSet reuse | A Cover-only round and a join to a live view run only the projection, under the same `bound bytes n` and `bound steps s`. A rerun's projection uses the same bounds, so all three paths have one rule. `project_changes` and `view_image_rows` already run this way | §4 "Query budgets" |
| Tiered | On a store with a Tiered collection, the Reader reads a projection makes are retry-capable. Every one of their attempts is charged to the projection's bound (STORE §13.3 "Enclosing step bounds"). The attempt count and each attempt's steps depend on the root and the projection, not on the cache (STORE §13.0, §13.3 "Cost"). So on one build, whether a projection stops at `s` is the same warm or cold | §4 "Query budgets" |
| Worst case | Per view evaluation, at most (a + 1)·s steps: a run attempts plus one projection, where a is 1 with no Tiered collection. Before v2.55 the figure was s for both phases together | §4 "Query budgets", §1.1 |
| Laws | L8 gains `query_budget_split`'s cases | §8 L8 |

**Why the split (option a, as refined here), and not a remainder handoff (b).** The /wholehog end-state is the split: one registered figure per entry, applied to each phase the same way, with no new core operation and no second figure.
- **(b) is ruled out.** A remainder handoff needs one of two things:
  - a core operation that reads the current bound, which MEM-30 forbids ("Nothing can name a bound") and STORE §13.0 Q1 rules out ("No core or compiler change"), so it would overturn Sam's M3 Q1;
  - a step count in `Looked.Seen`, which makes steps a program-visible value, against §13.0 Q1.
- **Retries leave no single remainder.** Under retry, F27 charges every attempt to the enclosing bound, so the quantity to hand over has no single definition. It could be what the deciding attempt left, or s less the sum of all attempts.
- **`flow.stage` is not a handoff.** MEM-30 has it copy the owner's remainder into a stage, and a spawned task gets "what that bound has left". Both are copies into another task: the owner's bound is not reduced and no steps return. A spawned projection would also have to move the publisher's Reader and `Log[Row]` across tasks, and the double reservation around `look(Some(s))` would still remain.
- **A separately declared projection allowance** (from Options, or a third figure in each `query` registration) was weighed. It adds a parameter that only one kind has. Deriving the allowance from page limits was also weighed. It cannot bound the slot codecs' `via` code, which projection runs.
- **Encode moves into the run.** The encode goes into `look`'s body so a query matches compute and transact: "the steps of decode and encode are the entry's" (v2.7 item 3).

**BUILD (v2.55).** The changes, by file:
- `core/programs/runner/runner-query.hirsel`:
  - `QueryAttempt` gains `value: std.codec.Codec[R]`.
  - `query_attempt` calls `encode_query_value` inside its `isolate`, after the body returns, and returns `Result[Answer[Bytes], Outcome]`. An encode error stays `Failed(Raised(5))`, and an encode allocation failure stays `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))`. An encode step stop is the attempt's `Stopped`, because the encode runs inside `look`'s scope.
  - `query_projection` calls `store.look(read_handle, newest, Some(steps), attempt, query_attempt)`.
  - On `Seen`, it runs cover matching and then `rerun_rows` or `cover_rows` under one `bound steps steps`. The projection uses non-waiting isolates for cover matching, conversion and allocation. A step stop during or between Reader calls becomes `Faulted(Failed(OutOfTime(steps)), reads)`. An allocation failure in an isolate becomes `Faulted(Failed(OutOfBytes(room)), reads)`. A typed `store.Error` keeps `projection_error` (v2.55.2).
  - `adapt_query` keeps `bound bytes n` and drops its `bound steps steps`. Its outer `Error`, `n` not granted at entry, stays `Refused(Budget)`.
- `core/programs/runner/runner-subscriptions.hirsel`: `Faulted` carries its reads to the ended entry (`advance_view`'s `EndRead`, `at_base_generation`'s `retain_initial_failure`). `project_changes` keeps its `bound bytes allowance { bound steps steps { .. } }`, and its `Stopped` leaves the view's current read set as the ended entry. Its projection helpers isolate allocation and conversion between waiting Reader calls. Byte-scope admission still returns `Refused(Budget)` (v2.55.2).
- `core/programs/runner/runner-shared-views.hirsel`: `view_image_rows` keeps its bounds and shares the projection helpers. A step or allocation overrun returns `Faulted` with the view's read set, so a join or repair preserves the ended entry (v2.55.2).
- `core/programs/runner/runner_retired_laws.hirsel`: `law_projection_isolated`'s harness gains L8's `query_budget_split` cases.
- No core, MEM-30 or STORE change. `look`'s signature is STORE §13.3's.

## CHANGES-v2.54 (who provisions a Runner follower, and how B23 admits it; with STORE-SPEC v2.69 B, lane runner-follower-service-2055)

Text only, not yet built. STORE v2.69 B moved every follower's apply onto one worker that a `FollowerService` provisions, and `follower(..)` now takes a `FollowerSlot` and `expect` instead of `Tasks` and `Options`. The Runner's one follower, §14.3's objects@1 capture, said neither who starts that service nor how its bytes enter B23's root-limit admission (v2.52), so an undersized limit would pass `place_store` and then fail at the capture. Reversible: the spec only.

| Rule | v2.54 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Who | The Runner opens one kind of follower: the objects@1 capture of a refused start (§14.3 "The probe's read", "Leaving no record", and §6's `NotCurrent(Active(v, current))` cause). The head that took the refused open starts that capture's `FollowerService` itself, in its own task, and nothing else in the Runner starts one. The host, the launcher, the fixture, §14.9's in-process places and every log@1, `Memory`, `Temp` and `Disk` place open no follower and start no service | §14.3 "The capture's service" |
| Lifetime | From the refused open to the report. The head starts the service, takes the one capture, drops the Follower, its owner and its `Followers` source, calls `close_follower_service`, and only then sends `PlaceCurrent` and ends. A start captures at most once, so a start has at most one service, which is "one service per process owner" for a place child, whose head is its process's only store owner | §14.3 "The capture's service" |
| No bound | On a `Bucket` backing, `operate` is entered with no step bound current: `runner.main`, `platform.serve` and §14.9's actor call it from tasks with none, and a caller that enters it under `bound steps` breaks its contract (MEM-30: no code can name a bound, so this is the caller's contract, as STORE §12.5 states for `follower_service`). The head is `operate`'s child, so its service starts outside every bound | §12.1 "Tasks", §14.3 |
| B23 | Only a `Bucket` backing makes a plan. On it, `place_store` subtracts X = `service_bytes([])` + `follower_starter_bytes()` from the share before sizing, then makes the plan from the exact `Options` `sized_reservation` returned, and the head's store term is max(T, L + `service_bytes([plan])` + `follower_starter_bytes()`), which is T + X because F = T − L. A `Shortage` from `follower_plan`, or `None` from `service_bytes`, is `Full` with the required figure. Every launch mode of a `Bucket` backing (serving, install, prepare) uses this one share, so one backing has one set of sized figures. Other backings make no plan, have X = 0 and a store term of T, and their figures are unchanged. L60's existing Bucket figures move by X | §12.1 "Store sizes", §14.3 |
| Fresh deadline | The capture's `fresh` runs with the deadline `BucketBacking.op_ms` from now, the backing's per-operation budget, which every other Bucket request of the place already uses | §14.3 "The probe's read" |
| Failed reads | A capture whose service, slot or follower is refused (`Full(Allocation(c))`, `Retired` (STORE v2.71), `Missing("follower_kind")`) is a failed read: `PlaceCurrent` with no read, retried under the ordinary backoff | §14.3 "The probe's read" |
| Place followers | Place code is offered no follower in v2.54. When it is, it is provisioned the same way: one service per head, started by the head with no bound current, held for the head's life, and sized in `place_store` by `service_bytes` over every plan it serves | §14.3 "The capture's service" |
| Laws | L60 gains the Bucket capture cases (a refused start reports its read and its room after `close_follower_service` is its room before its client started; a root at the floor is admitted and one byte below is refused at `place_store` naming T + X; install and prepare size the same T). The host half of STORE L54 (`cutover_survives_host_loss`) reads each objects@1 witness through the capture's service | §8 L60, §14.3 "Across sessions" |

**What STORE must change. Both items were closed by STORE v2.70 (store-spec-d1d2-2058), and these two bullets are amended in place to its rulings.**
- **D1, the Authority after a refusal.** §14.3 moves the refused open's Authority into `follower`, so the capture's transport is the client that T already counts. A second client would also work (`secret.share` gives a second holder of the sealed key), but it would start a second set of lanes, resolve and connect again, and copy the credentials' strings outside any figure the Runner admits. STORE §1.1 writes `open(tasks, a: Authority, ..)`, which lends `a`, but the build's `open_authority(tasks, take authority, ..)` consumes it, so after a refusal the head has no transport left. This gap predates v2.69 B: the old `follower(tasks, a, c, o)` also needed an Authority. **STORE v2.70 ruled (§1.1):** `open(tasks, take a, c, r, o) -> Result[Db, Refusal]`, and **every** failed open hands its Authority back whole in `Refusal.authority`, with the attempt's tasks joined and its other reservations refunded. This is not the Option proposed here: the build's Client is whole at every failure, so `None` never happened, and a rule keyed to the claim would have split `Lost`, `Remote`, `Undecided`, `Corrupt` and `Full` by where each arose. So the client lanes L, which T already counts, are the capture's transport after any refusal, which is the F = T − L that STORE §1.1 assumes ("a follower's transport is the authority's").
- **D2, what the starter pays outside `bytes`.** `follower_service(tasks, bytes)` reserves `bytes` from the starter, and MEM-24 also charges the starter the service task's 256 + its result's size + 32. STORE §12.5 also charges `follower_owner`'s endpoint and each request's capacity-one reply channel to the caller. STORE publishes none of these figures, so `place_store` cannot admit them exactly. **STORE v2.70 added (§1.1, §12.5 "The starter's charges")** `pub fn follower_starter_bytes() -> u64` = 288 + max(I, P, K). Its terms are the service task's 288, the owner endpoint's 0, and the largest single transient: the startup reply channel (I = 128), a slot request's box and reply channel (P = 810), or one follower request's box and reply channel (K = 664). That is **1,098** at the current build's types. The list above left out P and the boxes. STORE makes the sum exact with two rules: a plan holds no heap storage, and a request's storage is refunded before its call returns. The capture makes one request at a time (`fresh`), and the head drops the endpoint before the close.

**BUILD (v2.54).** `runner-budget.hirsel` deducts X from the Bucket share, builds the plan from the returned Options, and admits T + X as the store term. `runner-managed-head.hirsel` passes the serving selection and identity checks to the open before it can claim. On `NotCurrent`, it uses the returned Authority for the capture in `runner-identity.hirsel` and closes the service before returning the report. `PlaceRead` carries the claim pair and distinguishes a missing witness row from a row with a different prepared digest. `platform-recovery.hirsel` records each accepted Bucket claim against its serving generation. Preparing pins OLD's claim in the deploy row. Absence under OLD's claim keeps the attempt Undecided and selects OLD's next probe. A Directory read still decides absence under LOCK. Cranelift checks these paths in L60's `operate_derived_budgets` and L44's `cutover_survives_host_loss`.

## CHANGES-v2.53 (one slot-image rule for every slot kind; lane runner-slot-union-spec-2050)

Text only, not yet built. Closes the gap B27 (w3a-b27-cell-log-slots-2049) stopped on: v2.46 said a slot's image is the union of its covers, bound to one collection, yet G2 sent a tail cover's `window_changes` "and nothing else for the slot" and let a rerun "switch Logs". A slot that has both a fixed range and a tail lost the fixed range's changes, and a trim deleted rows the fixed range still covered. Reversible: the spec only.

| Rule | v2.53 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| One rule | A slot's rows from (registration a, snapshot a) to (registration b, snapshot b) are the keyed difference of its two images, each key once. The image is the keys present in the slot's fixed spans together with the keys in the widest tail's window. Table, Cell and Log slots, Cover-only hits, reruns, Subscribe, join and repair all use it | §3 "Slot images" |
| Fast path | `window_changes(a, b, l, t)` is that difference only for a slot whose registrations have no fixed span and the same widest t on both sides. Otherwise it is not used | §3 "Slot images", §1.1, G2 |
| Cost | A Cover-only round looks only at the changed and trimmed keys inside the cover plus the windows' symmetric difference (at most 2T). Membership comes by arithmetic from the registration and the two descriptors, so no rows are retained (L20) | §3 "Slot images" |
| One collection | A slot never changes its Log. Moving a query to another Log moves its coverage between two slots, in one group. A cover that names a collection other than its slot's bound one is `Failed(Raised(5))`. "Switches Logs" is struck from G2 | §1.1, G2 |
| Bounds items | They are matched against read-set entries only, Points and Spans. They are never matched against a CoverSet, even though a tail cover's `Span(id, id + 1)` holds `id` | §1.1, G2 step 2 |
| Laws | L20 gains the mixed-slot trim, the append after it and the two-tail case; L59 gains the grow and shrink cases, the move between slots, the cover of another Log and the bounds-only fence. Each has a hand-derived image | §8 |

With STORE-SPEC v2.68, whose §14.9 points here for the cover semantics.

## CHANGES-v2.52 (W3a B23)

`platform.serve` is the place entry point. It runs the same lifecycle and root-limit budget admission as `runner.operate`. The standalone WebSocket accept loop is `runner.serve_wire`.

## CHANGES-v2.51 (generic entries by PROG-9's instance descriptors; lane generic-descriptor-spec-2041)

Rule 2 no longer says PROG-9 refuses a generic function: `code.describe` now describes an instance of a generic function at closed type arguments, and such a `StaticFn` registers like any other. The key (rule 3) is unchanged in form; its identity bytes name the instance, so two instances are two keys. Identity bytes of functions without type parameters, and so every key registered today, are unchanged. The SDK's stub name is the final component of `D`, which ends at the first `[` or `(` (sdk.md §4), so two instances of one generic registered in one place are `DuplicateStubName`. Text only, not yet built.

## CHANGES-v2.50 (Bucket backing; with STORE-SPEC v2.66, lane bucket-backing-t1-2022)

`Backing` gains `Bucket(BucketBacking)`: a Net, a `std.object.Bucket` place (endpoint, region, bucket, prefix, style), `std.cred.Credentials` whose secret is sealed (a `Secret` grant, never plaintext), lane configuration, durability level and per-operation budget. The head task starts the object client and opens the place through `store.open_authority`, the path every objects@1 store takes, so §12.2's endpoint check runs at every open. A Bucket backing is persistent: it installs and deploys like `Disk`, its store size kind is `Bucket(lanes)`, and a deposed head stops on `Fenced` (§ operation). Placed installs still need `Disk` scratch.

## CHANGES-v2.49 (Changes.bounds in G2; with STORE-SPEC v2.61, lane w3b-spec-fix-2003)

G2 reads `Changes{rows, trimmed, bounds}`. A bounds item (a Log's id, one per Log whose (first, next) differs between `a` and `b`) hits a read set's `Point(id)`, never a CoverSet, and adds no slot row. `changes_page` clips spans to the page range. Text only.

## CHANGES-v2.48 (staged state held apart; Tiered L60 refusal; lane w3a-spec-fix-1997, with STORE-SPEC v2.60)

Text only, not yet built. Fixes forward v2.46's pipelining paragraph and finishes L60 for STORE-SPEC v2.60. Reversible: the spec only.

| Rule | v2.48 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Staged state | Staged memory is the publisher's own charge, never a ledger's; a view's last value, CoverSet, read set, reservations, admissions and `Ended` are held with the staged bytes and installed only at the matching Release, after admission is re-run against the ledgers; the head's `Staged` is a `try_send` and never waits | §4 G1 "Pipelining" |
| L60 Tiered | The Tiered floor is STORE-SPEC v2.60 §13.8's resident floor; below it, open on a Tiered catalog is `Missing("cache_bytes")` before any reservation or claim | L60 |

## CHANGES-v2.47 (inert entry state; a failed publisher read resets and fences; the recorded stop; lane m3-spec-fix-1994, with STORE-SPEC v2.59)

Text only, not yet built. Closes M3-O1 and M3-O2 of STORE-SPEC v2.59 §13.5, for astra 1990 findings 5, 9, 11 and 13. Reversible: the spec only; the build is `m3-tiered-spec-1981/BUILD-ITEMS.md`.

| Rule | v2.47 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| H is inert (#5) | Store bodies are plain fns with an `inert` env (STORE v2.59 §13.3, D-M3-2). H, the place's Tables and Indexes, is `shareable + inert + send`; every bound that said `shareable + send` adds `inert`. Each adapter's env is (the entry's `StaticFn`, H, Caller, the argument bytes), and it decodes A inside every attempt. A query's projection and the publisher's group are built from the value `look` returns, after it returns `Seen`, never from a cell the body captured | rules 2, 4, 12, §1.1 |
| Tx cursors | `range`, `range_prefix` and `log_range` return a `TxRows`, stepped by `next_in(tx, c)` (STORE v2.59 §13.4) | §1.4 |
| Reads take a Reader (#13) | The publisher reads through one `Reader` the head makes at open and moves to it; a granted run's `trigger_snapshot` returns its snapshot with a Reader | §1.1 "publisher_never_blocks", §4, §12.1 |
| G4: a failed read (#9) | A round, Subscribe or repair holds its rows, CoverSet and read set privately. On `Reclaimed`, `Remote` or `Io` the staged work is discarded, every affected session is fenced before R = b and Reset, each owed `Told` is delivered once, and subscriptions start again from a fresh durable base. A failure at an unchanged base retires the failed instance by a publisher-made `Reset`. A failure during a `Staged` pass only discards it | §4 G4, L56 |
| Liveness | The publisher's waits are its `receive` on `PubIn` and bounded pager I/O on its own Reader; neither waits on an owner | §1.1, L23 |
| Re-execution (#11) | The record's stop kind decides first: a step mismatch where either side is `OutOfSteps` is `Unknown(Residency)` | §7 |
## CHANGES-v2.46 (astra w3a-check-astra-1995's findings on v2.45; Cell and Log slots and the tail cover, W3B-O1; lane w3a-spec-fix-1997, with STORE-SPEC v2.60)

Text only, not yet built. Each row closes the counterexample of the astra finding it names (`w3a-check-astra-1995/FINDINGS.md`); re-traces are in `w3a-spec-fix-1997/TRACES.md`. Sam's DX Q1–Q6 rulings are kept. Reversible: the spec only.

| Finding | v2.46 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| #2 value-only content | Every group is content, a value-only group included: the instance check, the oldest-component fence, retirement, supersession and `Dropped` apply to it, and a reused sub id for another view is a new instance | §3 "Which units are checked", "Subscription instances", owner on `Dropped`; L28 |
| #3 frozen shared view | Due suppresses delivery only: every live view re-runs on its hits even when all subscribers are due, and advances a validated base; join and repair read only an image validated through the current base; an ended entry stays indexed until its last drop (DX Q4) in a retention record that a new view under the same key cannot replace | §3 "Ended entries", "Repair", "Joining", "Leaving"; G3; L28, L55 |
| #7 lost value | A bounced group of any kind marks its subscriber instance due; a due subscriber gets no delta until its full `complete`; repair, join and reconnect carry the full R and every slot image; a view encodes its delta once for synchronized subscribers and its full image once for those owed a `complete` | §3 "Repair", "One group per round, two encodings", "Groups"; L57 |
| #13 shared budget | A view is evaluated once; each subscriber's change is reserved on its own ledger, a refusal ends only that subscriber (fenced, its old read set retained), and only a common evaluation failure ends the view | §3 "Admission per subscriber"; G2; L55 |
| #10 bounds | `changes` and cover images are enumerated in pages; the whole encoded Batch is counted per session, group headers included, and no group or round is sent in part; the one-complete figure is 220,964 B, not 220,976 B | §3 "Frame bounds"; G2 step 1 |
| #11 slot operand | A slot is a name and a static selector `fn(H) -> Table` (or Cell, Log); Describe and the key use names and schemas only; the publisher binds selectors to the open's H and checks each cover's StoreId and table id against its bound slot | §1.1 "Slot binding"; §3 "Rows" |
| #12 cutover | Every shipped client moves to the current subprotocol. B-11 returns B-9's completing group envelope | §3 "The subprotocol", http.md B-9 and B-11, L58 |
| #9 SDK | The accessor contract: pending until complete, invalidate on reset or disconnect, release a previous Caller's data, stage a whole Batch before notifying, decode an empty `complete` value | sdk.md; L59 |
| Pipelining (Sam 2026-11-23) | The publisher may diff and encode a round at `Staged(v, snapshot)` while the commit's fdatasync or S3 PUT runs; the flush, fences and R still wait for the Release after `durable`; a failed `durable` discards the staged bytes | invariant 7, H3, G1, `PubIn`, L61 |
| #14 Tiered sizes | L60 covers both residencies with STORE-SPEC v2.60 §1.1's one equation | L60 |
| W3B-O1 | `slot_cell`, `slot_log`, `cover_cell`, `cover_log`, `cover_tail`; G2 reads `Changes{rows, trimmed, bounds}` (STORE v2.61 §14.8): a `Trimmed` hits by overlap, a fixed-range cover-only hit sends span ∩ cover deletions, a tail cover's rows are `window_changes`. Refined by astra 1998: Log bounds are a read dependency whose change re-runs and fences but sends no rows (#2); a re-run diffs old and new registrations by full logical key, with the window [n − min(t, n − f), n) (#4); a slot registers its collection's id, codec and Readers and publishes Cell and Log values through AG3 (#11) | §1.1, §3 Rows, §4 G2 |

## CHANGES-v2.45 (one schema declaration; computed values; shared views and one fence barrier; wire hirsel.3; derived budgets; lane w3a-dx-wire-spec-1982, with STORE-SPEC v2.57)

Text only, not yet built. Sam's 2026-11-23 Store DX rulings Q1 and Q3–Q6 (DECISIONS.md, "2026-11-23 Sam `$gavel` batch") and the two later additions: computed-value live queries and projectors. Lane decisions with their reversals: `w3a-dx-wire-spec-1982/DECISIONS.md`. Reversible: the spec only; the build is `w3a-dx-wire-spec-1982/BUILD-ITEMS.md`.

| Rule | v2.45 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Q1: one declaration | `schema(mut d: store.Declare) -> Result[H, store.Error]` replaces `handles`, and `on_migrate(mut m: store.Migration)` replaces `migrate`. The head calls `schema` once, in mode `Migrate` (genesis, install, prepare) or `Open` (every other open), and shares H, which is `shareable + send`, with the publisher. `store.Schema` and `store.shape` are deleted | rule 12, §1.1, §1.4, §6, §12.1 |
| Computed values | A query body returns `Answer[R]{value, covers}`: a typed value R plus a `CoverSet` of named slots. Slots are registered with a name and a selector `fn(H) -> Table` (v2.46); the slot id is its u16 registration index. A query's key fingerprints R's schema and its slot list | rules 2–3, §1.1 |
| Q4: shared views | Subscriptions with one view key `(entry key, args bytes, None)` for `query` or `(entry key, args bytes, Some(Caller bytes))` for `query_as` share one view: one run, one read set, one encoded group per round. Each subscriber is still charged in full | §3 "Shared views", §4 G2, G3 |
| Q4: one barrier | A round computes every hit view (ended entries included), takes the union of their sessions, publishes every fence, then R = b once, then offers. The barrier was already per round (v2.19); v2.45 states it over views and forbids a per-view advance | §3 "The release check", §4 G2 step 3 |
| Value elision | A re-run whose encoded R equals the view's last value sends an empty `value` (unchanged); a read-set hit still fences | §4 G2 |
| Wire | Subprotocol `hirsel.3`. `Batch.rows` becomes `groups: List[Group{sub, value, rows: List[Row{slot, key, value}]}]`. The publisher encodes a group's bytes after `sub` once per view and splices them per session | rule 10, §3 "Frames" |
| Q3 | `Fault` gains `Conflict(String)` at tag 4: a transact body that returned `Ok` after a unique `Conflict` | §1.1, §1.3 |
| Describe v2 | `Exported` gains `slots` and `value`; `Describe` gains `tables` (diagnostic, not in the identity) | §5, §6 |
| Q6a | `Backing.Disk` loses its `store.Options`: the head sizes the store with `store.sized(kind, share)`, share = root limit − `operate_bytes` − 1 MiB. `WireConfig.n_place` and `Disagrees("session.n_place")` are deleted | §12.1 |
| Laws | L55 `shared_view_once`, L56 `global_fence_barrier`, L57 `value_elided`, L58, L59 `slot_rows_atomic`, L60 `derived_budgets`; L5, L12, L16, L17, L20, L28 and L38 restated in place | §8 |

## CHANGES-v2.44 (the owner writes; a writer child only while a send would block; lane wire-rss-1968, Sam 2026-11-23)

Built. Every wire session kept a writer child for its life. A parked child holds its task record and its touched stack pages, so 1,000 idle sessions held 1,000 writers that had nothing to write. The owner now writes each frame itself without waiting and starts a writer child only when a send would block. The child finishes that one frame, hands the Writer back and exits.

| Rule | v2.44 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| §3 Session | The steady task group is the owner and the reader. The owner owns the `WriteHalf`. It applies the release check, stages the unit and tries each send once, without waiting. When a send would block, it spawns the writer child with the Writer and the rest of that frame. The child writes the rest under the frame's deadline and close rules, returns the Writer with `Sent` (or `WriteClosed` for a Close) and exits. The owner joins it on that event. The owner still has at most one frame staged or at the child, and starts the next only after `Sent` or `Dropped` | §3 Session: Owner, Writer, Ending; the check; the transport row |
| A refused writer spawn | The child is spawned with `try_spawn` under the owner's existing allowance, which already held a writer for the session's life. A refused spawn ends the session with 1001, as a write error does. Part of the frame may already be on the wire, so no Close frame can follow it | §3 Session, Ending |

Reversible: before 1.0. Budgets: unchanged. The writer child's record and `writer_bytes()` stay in the owner's allowance and the per-session peak, because a session whose client stops reading still has one. Laws: `law_write_spawned_on_block` (a small frame is written inline with no child; an unread socket makes a later frame start the child; once the client reads, the child returns the Writer with `Sent` and exits, and the next frame is inline again) and `law_write_capacity_ends` (a spawn refused `Capacity` ends with 1001), both in `session-laws.hirsel`. `law_writer_one_frame` is removed with the handoff channel it tested.

## CHANGES-v2.43 (one Registry for every wire session; lane wire-rss-1968, Sam 2026-11-23)

Built. Each wire session ran `place` at its Hello under `bound bytes n_place` and kept the Registry for its life, so 1,000 checkbox sessions held 1,000 equal Registries (about 3.8 MB at 1,000 clients, wire-rss-1968 profile). The serving task now runs `place` once, and every session plans against that one Registry.

| Rule | v2.43 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Rule 10 | The head, the publisher and the serving task each run `place` under `bound bytes n_place`. The serving task's Registry is immutable. It moves into the session allocation pool, which plans every session's Hello, call and Subscribe against it. A session holds no Registry: at Hello it records, per entry, whether the Registry has the key or lists it as gone, and the `Granted` launch it plans for a call. A lone connection (`serve_connection`) is given its allocator by its caller | rule 10, §1.1, §3 "The owner holds" |
| D7 | Ruled **(c)**: one Registry per serving task, shared by its sessions through the allocation pool. Compute calls still run in the pool's bounded, isolated allocation step, so no compute request waits behind another session's longer than before. (a)'s per-session account is replaced by one `n_place` in the serving budget | Decisions table, D7 |
| Budgets | `n_place` leaves the per-session peak and service job and is counted once in `serving_bytes`. The per-session `Exceeds(n_place, …)` refusal is removed: `n_place` is bounded by the serving budget like `n_pool` | §3 the 16 MiB per-session ceiling and its pool table; api/runner.md "Sizing `n_place`" |

Reversible: before 1.0. Laws: `law_serving_budget` repinned by hand (10,000 owners: 67,108,864 overhead + 655,360,000 floors + 1,048,576 `n_place` + 402,653,184 `n_pool` = 1,126,170,624); `law_start_refusals` keeps only the `Disagrees` half, because the per-session `n_place` bound it tested is gone.
## CHANGES-v2.42 (a store opens on its release identity; per-build steps and peaks in L4 and L7; lane store-runner-soften-1974, with STORE-SPEC v2.55)

Text only, not yet built. Sam's 2026-11-23 Spec Q1 and Q5 (DECISIONS.md, "2026-11-23 Sam `$gavel` batch"). Reversible: the spec only; the M2 build implements it (`store-runner-soften-1974/BUILD-ITEMS.md`).

| Rule | v2.42 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Q5: what binds a store | The **release identity** `{ revision, n_place, exported }`, with the entry's `code.revision` from the launch metadata, replaces the encoded `Describe` as what a store open compares. Its digest is SHA-256 over `u8 18 ‖ "hirsel.identity.v1" ‖` its codec bytes. The executable digest and target are diagnostics. Cranelift, LLVM, other optimisation levels and recording builds of one program open one store. The schema is covered by the entry's revision, which reaches `migrate` and `handles` (PROG-9), so the runner's API does not change | §6 "One identity", "Restart and rebuild", rule 10 |
| Q5: one key | The identity digest is the only key for the store open, the log@1 post-open comparison, the host's match of a store to a record, the no-record mark and the trigger stamp. `release` stays the exported-list digest that clients compare and never decides an open | §6, §14.3 "What serves", "Leaving no record", §14.6 |
| Q5: assembly records | v2.38's `Describe.assembly` and `--hirsel-assembly`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> are deleted (never built). They existed to make one store activation name one record; under Q5 two records can share an identity, and the host picks between OLD and NEW by the deploy's decided outcome (Installed: NEW; failed: OLD), a function of its own durable facts with no operator choice. The v2.51 decision against `Select` and the pending choice stands | §14.3 "What serves" |
| Q5: allowance | A launch with an explicit `--hirsel-n-place`<!-- doc-ref: external --> runs at it in every mode; one without runs at `PlaceConfig.n_place`. This replaces v2.40's rule keyed on the assembly name. The hand install is `./place --hirsel-n-place=<bytes> --hirsel-install` | §6 "One n_place", §14.3 |
| Q5: trigger stamp | A granted trigger's revision stamp is the identity digest, not the image digest, so a rebuild by another backend no longer holds a started run, and a change to `handles` still does | §14.6 |
| Q5: L4 | `peak_retained_bytes` after 10⁴ malformed frames equals it after 10, on one build; no figure is pinned | §8 L4, §9 |
| Q1: L7 | `L7/deadline_hung` compares the counts at bounds `s` and `s + 2` on one build instead of a hand-counted prologue `e` | §8 L6+L7 |

## CHANGES-v2.41 (the private prefix's order; a reported refusal survives host loss; the mark's way back to Unmatched; lane store-m2-spec-1970, with STORE-SPEC v2.54)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.40 check (m2-v253-astra-1967) found that L54's hosted allowance was over the per-session ceiling (item 1, broken, blocking), that the documented hand install put `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> first and so ran unnamed (item 2, broken, blocking), that a refusal a start reported was lost with the host (item 3, new), and that launch.md had no way from `Adopted` back to `Unmatched` (item 4, new).

| Rule | v2.41 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| L54's allowances | 524,288 bytes configured (`PlaceConfig.n_place`) and 1,048,576 bytes hosted, the allowance of the existing serving fixture (`runner_calls.hirsel` `wire_defaults(65536, n)` at 1 MiB). With `wire_defaults(65536, 1048576)` and one session, `session_problem`'s peak is 15,771,732 bytes, under the 16,777,216-byte ceiling; at 33,554,432 it is 80,783,444, so OLD could never serve. The configured value is used only by unnamed installs, which return before `session_problem` | STORE L54; §14.3 "Across sessions" |
| The private prefix | The native launcher reads its private options only from the leading run of recognised private options; the first other argument ends the prefix, and it and every later argument are `Args`. `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and `--hirsel-activation`<!-- doc-ref: external --> are `Args`, so they come last. The hand install is `./place --hirsel-assembly=<OLD-hex> --hirsel-n-place=1048576 --hirsel-install`; with `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> first it is a bare install at `PlaceConfig.n_place` | §6 "A named launch runs at the launch's allowance"; §14.3 "Leaving no record"; launch.md "Describe launch", "Place activation" |
| A start's last answer survives host loss | The launcher keeps a serving child's last answer before its end (a `PlaceCurrent` with any read it carries, a refusal, or `PlaceStoreMismatch`) with the unacknowledged end, and lists it, with the selection the child was launched with, in the inventory. The host applies it by the ordinary rules before the end and before acknowledging. Applying it a second time changes nothing | §14.5 "The control session", "Genesis", "Failing and backoff" |
| `Adopted` back to `Unmatched` | Stated in launch.md as §14.3 already had it: adoption clears the cause; a later no-match read sets a new cause at the activation it read; the first accepted `PlaceOpened` clears the mark; a relaunch that dies before reporting leaves `Adopted` and contributes its exit status | launch.md "When the host starts" |
| The exit status in `last` | `exit status <n>` for an exit and `killed by signal <n>` for a signal, the only two forms | §14.5 "Failing and backoff" |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. Build items: `--hirsel-assembly=<hex>`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> joins the prefix loop in `crates/hirsel-core-rt/src/entry.rs` (it is not recognised today, so it ends the prefix), with v2.40's refusal of a name without `--hirsel-n-place`<!-- doc-ref: external --> (exit 103, `not started:`); the launcher's child entry keeps the last answer beside `install_reply`, set where `drain_endpoint` relays it, carried in that child's `Inventory` entry, and dropped at the end's `Acknowledge`; the inventory's `ChildState` carries the serving selection (activation and assembly) from its `Start`, which replaces v2.40's item "the host keeps each outstanding start's selection with its generation" (the host keeps it in memory for a live session and takes it from the inventory after a restart); L54 and `cutover_survives_host_loss` cases run through the native entry point.

## CHANGES-v2.40 (a named launch runs at the launch's allowance; the mark's cause in Status; a start's own cause; lane store-m2-spec-1966, with STORE-SPEC v2.53)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.52 check (m2-v252-astra-1965) found that the documented hand install could never match a candidate (item 1, broken, blocking), that a retry ending before it reported replaced the mark's cause with an exit status (item 2, broken), that an Undecided probe could be told of a false `Describe` mismatch (item 3, new), and that the cause's headline claimed more than the host tested (item 4, new).

| Rule | v2.40 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| A named launch's allowance | A launch whose `--hirsel-assembly`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> names a record runs at the launch's `--hirsel-n-place`<!-- doc-ref: external --> in every mode, as a hosted launch does; `PlaceConfig.n_place` is not read. The native launcher refuses the name without an explicit allowance, as it refuses a malformed private launch option. Keyed on the name, not on install | §6 |
| The mark's two states | `GroupRow.recovery` is `Unmatched(attempt, a, assembly, n_place)` or `Adopted(attempt, a, record)`. Adoption sets `Adopted`; a later read that matches nothing sets `Unmatched` again; `PlaceOpened` clears either. Replaces v2.39's `unmatched` | §14.3 "What serves", "Leaving no record" |
| `Status.failing[].last` | Stated once and formatted from the mark when `Status` is answered: an `Unmatched` mark's cause, then `; last start: ` and the start's own refusal or exit status; otherwise the start's own outcome alone. Covers ends with no report and ends recovered from the inventory | §14.5 "Failing and backoff" |
| A start's own cause | Compares the refusal's activation with the activation this start was launched to open (a probe's intent, otherwise its generation's selection) and names the record it ran: `NotCurrent: the store is at activation v; this start requested w`, or `NotCurrent: the store's Describe at activation v is not that of assembly <hex>, which this start ran`. Replaces v2.39's comparison with the group's recorded activation | §14.5 "Genesis" |
| The headline | `No recovery candidate matches the store at activation a`, with the read's assembly and allowance and each candidate's name and allowance. Replaces v2.39's "No retained assembly matches" | §14.3 "What serves" |

Reversible: every item before 1.0. Build items: the native launcher's refusal (`crates/hirsel-core-rt/src/entry.rs` refuses malformed private options with `EINVAL`; this one joins them); `operate` and `operate_bytes` read the launch allowance when `assembly` is set; the catalog's `recovery` field (re-created under the no-compatibility rule); the host keeps each outstanding start's selection with its generation.

## CHANGES-v2.39 (a group left with no record recovers through its retries; the no-record cause; lane store-m2-spec-1962, with STORE-SPEC v2.52)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.51 delta check (m2-v251-astra-1961) found that a group left with no record had no way out (item 3, broken), and that its cause named the wrong fact (item 5, new). After a deploy decided with nothing selected, the group kept OLD at its old activation and every retry was refused there. A correct hand install of OLD's record at a later activation changed nothing: the host never read the store again, the ordinary `PlaceCurrent` changed nothing, and a hot deploy needs a live OLD. The cause said "under another image" (or "the host selected w") when both launches ran the same executable.

| Rule | v2.39 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| The no-record mark | The transaction that selects nothing writes `unmatched` in the group's row: the deploy attempt whose OLD and NEW are the candidates, the read's activation and the read's assembly name (named, none, or undecodable). It is durable, so a host restart keeps both the recovery and the cause. New field of `GroupRow` | §14.3 "What serves" |
| The retry reads | A serving start of a marked group is refused at its recorded activation, as before, and then takes the probe's read without a witness row: store ID, activation, image and `current`, from one capture (objects@1 follower, log@1 under its own `LOCK`). The objects@1 capture now also reads `current`, so every read can name the assembly | §14.3 "The probe's read", "Leaving no record" |
| Adoption | When the read's image equals the `Describe` digest of the mark's OLD or NEW, and that record is still a root of the group, one transaction makes it current at the read's activation, writes it into the activation index there and applies "Roots" with it selected. It re-checks the incarnation, the same mark, no retiring mark, no open deploy and the reporting generation. The deploy's terminal outcome and its index entry at `v` are untouched. The start's end relaunches at once; `PlaceOpened` clears the mark. While nothing matches, the selection stays and the mark takes the new read | §14.3 "Leaving no record", §14.5 |
| The cause | `No retained assembly matches the store at activation a`, with the read's assembly (`names assembly <hex>`, `names no assembly`, or `does not decode`) and the names of the adoption's candidates, the mark's OLD and NEW that the group still roots. It comes from the mark, so a retry refused at the stale version, or one whose read fails, keeps it. A refused start of an unmarked group names both activations, or, when they are equal, says the store's `Describe` there is not the recorded record's. Replaces the strings of CHANGES-v2.32 row 4 | §14.3, §14.5 "Genesis", "Failing and backoff" |

Not changed: no operator request chooses an assembly (no `Select`), and genesis adoption is still one-shot. A healthy group whose store is moved by hand, even under its own record, stays failing until it is retired (a deploy needs a live OLD): only a decided no-record deploy marks a group.

Reversible: every item before 1.0. `GroupRow` gains `unmatched`; the catalog is built, so a catalog written before this change is re-created under the no-compatibility rule (open item for the build lane, with v2.38's `Describe` field).

## CHANGES-v2.38 (`Describe` names its assembly record; lane store-m2-spec-1960, with STORE-SPEC v2.51)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.50 delta check (m2-v250-astra-1959) traced three bugs to one design: two distinct assembly records could match one `Describe` digest, and v2.37 resolved that by a pending choice and an operator `Select`. v2.38 removes the ambiguity: `Describe` gains the 32-byte name of the assembly record the launch runs, so the store's activation names exactly one record or none. **v2.38 withdraws v2.37's pending choice, `Select`, the `select` CLI verb and the two-record failing cause.**

| Rule | v2.38 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| The assembly in `Describe` | Launch metadata gains `assembly`, the name of the assembly record the launcher runs (empty when the launcher has none), and `Describe` carries it after `n_place`. A launch of another record is refused `NotCurrent` like another `n_place`, and OLD installs NEW's exact `Describe`, so the record travels with the activation | §6, launch.md "Immutable launch identity", "Describe launch" |
| Matching | The read's image equals the `Describe` digest of at most one of OLD's and NEW's records (or both, when they are one record). No index lookup, no pending choice, no operator request | §14.3 "What serves" |
| Installed at `v` | A decided Installed outcome records NEW's record for activation `v` in the same transaction, whatever the read's activation, so a refused probe's read and an opening probe write the same catalog; the read's activation then picks what is current | §14.3 "The outcome", "What serves" |

## CHANGES-v2.37 (two matching assemblies, one root rule, the log@1 read; lane store-m2-spec-1954, with STORE-SPEC v2.50)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.49 delta check (m2-v249-astra-1953) found that two assembly records could both match the store's `Describe` (1a), that "Across sessions" contradicted "Roots" (1b), and that a hosted log@1 deploy stayed Undecided after another install (3).

| Rule | v2.37 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| Matching | Identical records collapse first. Two distinct records with the read's image select the one the host's activation index names for that activation; with none named, nothing is selected and the group is failing until an operator `Select` | §14.3 "What serves", launch.md "The operator socket" |
| Roots | Stated once in "Roots"; "Across sessions" refers to it. A failed deploy drops NEW only when OLD was selected; with nothing selected OLD stays current and NEW retained | §14.3 |
| The log@1 read | A log@1 probe refused at the post-open comparison keeps `LOCK`, checks the store ID and reads the activation, `current` and `releases[v]` from its recovered state, with no application handles, and reports them as the objects@1 read does. An absent row there is failed. The pending row records both `Describe` digests on every backend | §6, §14.3 "Preparing", "The probe's read" |

## CHANGES-v2.36 (an unknown install is decided after the activation moved on; lane store-m2-spec-1951, with STORE-SPEC v2.49)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.48 delta check (m2-v248-astra-1950 #1) found that the Undecided path decided only by an open at `v`: once a standalone install moved the store to `v + 1`, or took `v` with another `n_place`, every probe was refused and retried forever.

| Rule | v2.36 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| OLD's pair | On objects@1 `PlaceImported` carries OLD's (epoch, holder) (`store.claim_of`), and the pending row records it with OLD's and NEW's `Describe` digests | §14.3 "Preparing" |
| The probe's read | A probe of an Undecided deploy names the attempt's `v` apart from its selection. Refused `NotCurrent` on objects@1, it takes STORE §12.7's follower read of one capture and reports it in `PlaceCurrent`: store ID, activation, image, pair and `releases[v]` | §14.3, §14.9 "Ends" |
| Outcome apart from selection | Row `v` decides the attempt even when the head is past `v`; an absent row under OLD's pair stays Undecided. What serves follows the head's activation and image, matched to OLD's or NEW's `Describe`; no match leaves the group failing with that cause. Installed at `v` never rolls a group back from a later activation | §14.3, §14.5 "Genesis" |
| Roots | The selected assembly is current; the other is retained when the deploy installed or NEW was selected; a failed deploy's NEW is no root only when OLD serves | §14.3 "Across sessions", launch.md "Collect" |

log@1 is unchanged (open item in STORE CHANGES-v2.49).

## CHANGES-v2.35 (an unknown install is reconciled by its attempt witness; lane store-m2-spec-1949, with STORE-SPEC v2.48)

Text only, not yet built. The v2.47 delta check (m2-delta-astra-1948 #3) found that the deploy compared the attempt's prepared digest with `ReleaseRecord.digest`, which holds the executable image digest.

| Rule | v2.35 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| The release row | `ReleaseRecord` is `{ release, digest, prepared }`: `digest` stays the executable image digest, and `prepared` is the attempt's validated PREPARED digest, written by the install from the import, empty for genesis and a standalone install | §6 |
| Deciding an unknown install | The probe or candidate that opens at `v` (the exported main head + 1 the host recorded) reports `releases[v].prepared`; only equality with the attempt's prepared digest proves the install, and an open at `v` with another `prepared` proves it was not this attempt's | §14.3 |

## CHANGES-v2.34 (Store M2 on objects@1; lane store-m2-spec-1940, with STORE-SPEC v2.47)

Text only, not yet built. STORE-SPEC v2.47 §12 gives a place a second authority, objects@1, where a claim and a conditional head write replace the OFD lock. These rows give each runner rule its objects@1 reading. log@1 places, and §14.9's in-process places (always log@1), are unchanged.

| Rule | v2.34 | Where |
|---|---|---|
| A fenced store stops the run | The head registers `store.on_unhealthy` on its own inbox; when the health leaves `Ok` the head ends abnormally as for a failed `durable`, with exit 1 and no clean report. An idle place learns of a fence from the store's idle head poll | §2.1 "Poison and fence", §12.1, §14.4 |
| "Under `LOCK`" | Reads "holding the claim; the conditional head write decides". The activation, image and store-ID checks move into `store.open` (`o.activation`, `o.image`, `o.expect`) so a rejected launch never deposes the serving holder | §0 rule 6, §6, §14.5 |
| A second opener | Deposes rather than being refused `Held`; the launcher's one serving launch per group is the guard | §0 rule 6, §14.5 |
| An unknown install | `Store(Fenced(e))` or `Store(Undecided)` at the activation head write is unknown: OLD ends abnormally and the deploy takes the Undecided path, whose probe is the qualified read. `Refused` only before the head write | §6, §14.3 |
| `Kept` and zero-write `Committed` (reexec-gate-1939) | One case for re-execution: compare reply bytes and writes; the label is not observable and no marker is added | §7 |

## CHANGES-v2.33 (the platform host publishes submitted source; lane source-publish-1901)

`launcher.Input.Source` used to be refused `Unsupported` by the host and was not handled by the launcher. It now carries a `launcher.Tree`, and the launcher publishes it with the normal checker before admitting the publication (launch.md "Publish and assemble" step 1).

The current `TREE_BYTES` is 5 MiB. The source-by-content design stores manifests in request rows and gives the launcher up to 4 MiB of file content plus 1 MiB for the other encoded fields (launch.md "Source by content", "Limits").

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.33 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What a source is | **NEW: `launcher.Tree`** | A package path, the grants and `--dir` bindings, and the files. v2.33 capped the codec bytes at 4 MiB so a request row could hold the input twice. Paths are plain and relative, with no repeats and no file that is also a directory. A tree that breaks these rules is `Invalid`. | brief; launch.md "What an untrusted author submits" |
| 2 | Who compiles | **THE LAUNCHER, as a child `hirsel build --emit recipe --code-store DIR --frozen`** | The child runs on fresh scratch under the launcher's compiler, package and code stores and inherits the toolchain memory limit, so an out-of-memory or crashing compile ends the child and never the launcher. Its H lands in the launcher's code store and the attempt goes on as `Publication(H)`: one path, admission sees only publications. A compile is cancelled with its attempt. | launch.md "Authority" |
| 3 | Failures | **NEW classes `Uncompiled` (10) and `Exhausted` (12)** | `launcher.Failure` gains `Uncompiled(diagnostics)` and `Exhausted(text)`. The host maps them to operator classes 10 and 12; tag 10 was `Unsupported`, whose only producer is gone. Diagnostics are cut at 64 KiB. | brief |
| 4 | Untrusted authors | **REFUSED BEFORE `Admit`** | `admission_refusal` refuses, as `Denied`, any input but a source, any approved Foreign or Exec, a tree grant starting `Foreign` or `Exec`, and a recording group (FFI-1, EXEC-1). | launch.md |
| 5 | Dependencies | **ONLY WHAT THE HOST HOLDS** | `--frozen` needs the tree's `hirsel.lock` and never fetches; a dependency missing from the host's package store refuses the compile as `Uncompiled`. | launch.md "Authority" |
| 6 | Laws | **NEW: L53 `untrusted_source_admission`; Rust law `source_publishes_with_the_normal_checker`** | §14 L53 row. The Rust law's oracle is the CLI's `hirsel build --emit recipe --code-store DIR` of the same files under separate stores. | brief |

Reversible: items 1, 2, 4, 5 and 6. Item 3 changes the operator class numbering (tag 10 renamed, tag 12 added). No build ever produced tag 10 for a client, so no stored or relayed answer changes meaning.

## CHANGES-v2.32 (a crash inside the genesis activation window recovers without an operator; lane activation-crash-1892)

A group's genesis start installs the store's first activation under `LOCK` (§12.1 `Activating`) before the host records that activation from the start's `PlaceOpened` (§14.5). A crash between the two leaves the store at activation 1 and the group's row at 0. Every later start selects `Current(0)`, which the runner refuses `NotCurrent(Active(1))` because the store is not empty (§12.1). The host dropped that answer, so the group failed on backoff until someone deployed again. Since v2.31 every harness session is a `local` group whose first route runs genesis, so one crash at that moment left a conversation dead.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.32 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The genesis window | **NEW: the host adopts the store's first activation** | §14.5 "Genesis". A serving start of a group whose recorded activation is 0, answered `PlaceCurrent(v)` with `v` ≥ 1, records `v` in one transaction that re-checks the incarnation, the recorded 0 and that the group is not retiring. The start's end then relaunches at `v` at once, outside backoff. The open at `v` is still the proof: that start compares the store's `Describe` with its own before handles, and only its `PlaceOpened` records the store ID. | brief; p6-retire-directory-1886 "Open reds and gaps" |
| 2 | Where the fix lives | **HOST, not runner** | `Current(0)` still expects an empty store (§12.1), and the runner's crash suite (`runner_crash`, genesis case) still requires it to refuse an activated store. Treating `Current(0)` as `Reopen` would also have healed the window, but it would weaken an exact selection that the cutover's discard argument relies on, and it would weaken a crash law. | host rules: crash suites are not weakened |
| 3 | One-shot | **ADOPT ONLY FROM 0** | A group with a recorded activation never adopts, so the relaunch outside backoff happens at most once per incarnation. A store another image activated refuses the start at `v` too. That start counts as unsuccessful, and the group stays failing on backoff (§14.5). | §14.5 "Failing and backoff" |
| 4 | The failing cause | **NEW: the start's refusal survives its end** | A `PlaceCurrent` that the host does not act on is kept as the start's cause, and its end reports that cause in `Status.failing[].last` (`NotCurrent: the store is at activation v under another image`, or `…, the host selected w`) in place of the exit status. The cause is text, like every other `last`. | brief: "a typed, operator-visible park with a cause" |
| 5 | Routes during a failed start (§14.9) | **CHANGED: refused only if the host does not start again at once** | `local` used to refuse the routes waiting on a group as soon as its start failed, before the host had applied the failure. A route now waits while the host starts the group again at once: the first failure's retry, and the relaunch of item 1. It is refused when the host leaves the group on backoff. | the gateway's `reach` does not retry a refused route |
| 6 | Laws | **NEW: `activation_window_recovers`, `local_genesis_recovers`** | §14.7 and §14.9. | brief |

Reversible: every item. None changes a durable format: `GroupRow.activation` already exists, and the cause and the relaunch mark are the host's memory. One behaviour changes: a group added at activation 0 over a store that the same image already activated (a kept store re-added under its name) is now adopted on its first start. Before, it failed forever.

## CHANGES-v2.31 (the platform host runs in-process places; lane p6-retire-directory-1886)

The harness gateway runs one place per conversation in its own process, and §14.8 row 9 retires the directory once the platform host covers PD1 to PD4. The host could only launch processes. v2.31 gives it an in-process launcher, so the gateway's sessions become host groups.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.31 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | In-process places | **NEW: `local`** | §14.9. `local` is the platform host with a launcher that runs each group's place as a task in the host's process, on a disk store under the host's root. It carries out the host's requests itself; the wake row, the generation fence and the clean report are the host's own (§14.1 to §14.5). | §14.8 row 9 |
| 2 | Unload on request | **NEW: `platform.unload`** | §14.4 "Unload". An operator can ask a live group's serving child to stop by the idle stop's drain; the row then goes `Clean` or `Dirty` by the clean-report rule, so an unload never loses a wake. | the gateway closes a session |
| 3 | The store's identity in `Status` | **NEW: `Status.store_id`, `Status.retain_from`** | §12.1. `operate` publishes the store's identity and retention start at activation, so an in-process launcher can report `PlaceOpened` as a process child does through its control socket. | §14.9 |
| 4 | Directory laws | **PORTED: PD1 to PD4 as `local_*`** | §14.9 laws. Same properties, stated against host groups. | §14.8 row 9 |
| 5 | The harness gateway | **MOVED: sessions are host groups** | The gateway (the harness, now in [lash-hirsel](https://github.com/SamGalanakis/lash-hirsel)) runs each session as a `local` group. Its catalog keeps only key and id; the wake row, due wakes and retries are the host's. A create seeds the store, adds the group and then commits the row; a delete removes the row and then the group; a start removes groups no row names and adds a group for each row without one. | v2.30 row 6 |
| 6 | Reads during a run | **NEW: the host sets `max_sessions`** | §14.9. `local` sets every place's wire session count, as it sets the idle time, and the gateway sets 4: a front's following stream holds one session while a run is busy, so a page, a status read, a message or a decision needs another, and none waits for the run. | h-ts-client-1887: a mid-run page took 1,056 ms with 1 session, 3 ms with 4 |
| 7 | The directory | **DELETED: §13, `places`** | The place directory, its laws PD1 to PD4 (now `local_*`, §14.9) and its §8 rows are gone, with no compatibility path. CHANGES rows of v2.21 to v2.30 that cite §13 refer to the v2.30 text (git `8e515e1b^`). | §14.8 row 9 |

Reversible: rows 1 to 3 and 6 are additive. Rows 5 and 7 are reversible only by restoring the directory from git, which the gateway would then have to move back onto; nothing outside the gateway and the host's harness used it.

## CHANGES-v2.30 (the directory lists names at run time and may leave unloading to the place; lane h-gateway-1864)

The harness gateway creates and deletes one session place per conversation. The directory took every name it would ever run at construction, and its idle sweep stopped an unleased place even while a run was active. An agent run is not a lease, so a sweep would cut a model call in flight.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.30 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Names at run time | **NEW: `add`, `remove`, `max_names`** | §13.1. `add` lists one more name (a listed name is `Duplicate`, `max_names` listed names are `Full`). `remove` unlists a name with no instance (a loaded one is `Loaded`, so it is closed first). A removed name leaves its slot unlisted, so the slot numbers live place tasks report under never shift. The directory reserves its own bytes for `max_names` names up front. | the gateway's create and delete |
| 2 | Who unloads | **NEW: `idle_ms: None`** | §13.2 Idle. With `None` the directory never unloads by its sweep; a place unloads only by its own §14.4 idle stop, which waits for no sessions, no busy work, no active trigger and no trigger due within its idle time. | an active run is not a lease |
| 3 | Shared services | **NEW: the seed `T`, `seeded_factory`** | §13.1. The directory owns one `seed: T`; a `seeded_factory`'s `make` sees it at each start. `Factory`, `Listed`, `Directory` and `Opener` take `T`. | the gateway's session services |
| 4 | A place's own name | **NEW: `Supplied.name`** | §13.1. `make` gets the listed name it is starting, so a place can name itself to shared services (a subagent tool keys its child by its parent's name). | the gateway's subagent tool |
| 5 | The test client | **NEW: `fixture.client_at`, `fixture.entry_keys`** | §12.3. A client of a place the fixture did not start, by address, on a fresh stream or resuming an existing one (`Resume`, read back with `resume_at`, v2.71 B7), and the entry keys a `place` registers. | the gateway's laws and subagent tool |
| 6 | The platform host | **UNCHANGED: §14.8 row 9** | The gateway runs its sessions in a directory because the platform host cannot yet run in-process places. When P6 retires the directory, the gateway moves to the platform host with it. | orchestrator, 2026-11-23 |

Reversible: both are additive. A directory built with every name and `Some(idle_ms)` behaves as in v2.29.

## CHANGES-v2.29 (a granted run reads a fenced snapshot; the harness is one session per place; lane harness-core-1806)

A granted run had no read of its store. It could change rows only through `trigger_call`, so a worker that has to build a large outside request, such as a model call over a conversation, had to receive the request as a trigger argument or a step result. That copies the body into the journal on every round. The harness ([lash-hirsel](https://github.com/SamGalanakis/lash-hirsel)) needs the worker to read the entries itself, off the write lock.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.29 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Reads from a granted run | **NEW: `trigger_snapshot`** | §12.1 "What a granted run may read". The head answers `Snap(attempt)` after the release gate and the attempt fence, with `store.snapshot(db)`. A fenced-out attempt gets `StaleAttempt`, a cancelled one `Cancelled`, and a closing head `Closed`; none of them gets a snapshot. A run replays deterministically only over rows that its fenced calls sealed and that no transaction rewrites. | harness steer: the advance transaction seals only a cutoff |
| 2 | Laws | **NEW: L47** | `trigger_snapshot_fenced`, §8. | — |
| 3 | The harness's place shape | **RULED: one Place is one session** | The harness keeps no conversation table and no conversation key. Its seven tables are one singleton session row, entries, inbox, runs, effects, waits and views. A subagent is an Outside tool that calls another session's place. A fork is a new place seeded from an exported prefix. Nothing in this spec changes for it: a session place uses triggers, attempt entries and `trigger_snapshot` like any place. | Sam, harness rescope |

Reversible: `trigger_snapshot` is additive. Removing it removes one `TriggerAction` arm, and no stored format depends on it.

## CHANGES-v2.28 (a bind-only wire: a place whose trigger grant is movable binds its sessions; lane wire-bind-auth-1803)

`PlaceConfig.wire` was a `Wire[G]` over the registry's grant `G`, and `runner.wire` needs `G: plain + send`. A place whose triggers carry a `TriggerContext`, a `Sender` or an owning `Secret` has a movable `G`, so it could have no wire, and every Hello took the anonymous path. An authenticated subject can come only from the binding: a field the caller supplies cannot authenticate itself.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.28 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The shape | **CHANGED: one `Wire[G, B]` whose granted half is optional** | `Wire[G, B]` holds the binding's seed `B` and, optionally, the plain grant `G` that wire calls to `Granted` entries copy. `PlaceConfig[G, H, S, B]` and `WireSession[G, H, B]` carry `B`. `runner.wire(grant, bind, ..)` keeps its v2.24 contract: `G: plain + send`, both halves, the binding seeded with a copy of `G`, so it makes a `Wire[G, G]`. `runner.wire_bind(seed, bind, ..)`, with `B: plain + send` and any `send` `G`, makes the binding alone. Its `bind` is required, so no `Wire` has neither half and `None` stays the only "no wire". One type, not two parallel ones: the session, ingress and every place treat both alike, and only the granted path asks whether the half is there. | like kinds alike |
| 2 | Wire calls to `Granted` | **KEPT for `runner.wire`; refused for `runner.wire_bind`** | Each wire call to a `Granted` entry copies the grant half (v2.24 item 1). With no grant half, or with `wire: None`, it is `Refused(NotAllowed)` before anything is reserved. A movable `G` still never reaches the wire (§10 D9). | R6 |
| 3 | The binding | **CHANGED: seeded by `B`** | §3 "Binding" calls `bind(copy of B, credential)`; with `runner.wire`, `B` is `G`. Its bounds, its refusals (1008, 401), the issuer-1 refusal of a bound Caller and the anonymous path with no binding are unchanged. | §3 |
| 4 | Laws | **CHANGED: L36** | L36 adds `bind_only` and the checker refusal `wire_bind_nonplain`, §8. | — |

This reverses v2.24's decision-log entry "where the binding's authority comes from", which rejected a second authority type because no place needed one; the signup example's movable trigger grant is that place. Reversible: `Wire` is opaque, and dropping `B` again changes only type spellings.

## CHANGES-v2.27.1 (the place fixture is built; lane clock-fixture-1738)

§12.3's fixture is built as the module `fixture` (fixture.hirsel). The text now states the signatures as built. `test_fixture_granted` in runner_calls.hirsel **RUN**s it natively: a Caller-bearing transact, a granted entry with `wire` set, and the same entry `Refused(NotAllowed)` without it. The evaluator path still waits on W8a. **UNTESTED**: `subscribe`, `seen_at`, `status`, every `Deadline` path and dropping a `Fixture` without `end`.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.27.1 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The test-side handle | **DECIDED (brief clock-fixture-1738)** | `Caller` is renamed `Client` and `caller` is renamed `client`, so the name no longer collides with `runner.Caller`, the principal that entries receive | §12.3 |
| 2 | Typed entries | **AS BUILT** | `transact_entry` takes the registry's Caller-bearing signature `fn(mut store.Tx, H, Caller, take A) -> Result[R, E]`. `granted_entry` is in the API block. Every key comes from `runner.attempt_key` over the codecs that registration fingerprints | §12.3 |
| 3 | Not `send` | **AS BUILT** | `Fixture` and `Client` own task handles, which are not `send` (CONC-2), as `places.Directory` does | §12.3 |
| 4 | The client's credential | **DECIDED here; reversible** | `client` takes the Hello token. Without one, a place whose `wire` binds a credential gives the session no Caller | §12.3 |
| 5 | Query entries | **AS BUILT** | A query `Callee` is `Callee[A, ()]`. Its described result encodes the ordered key and current value schemas, and its rows arrive in Batches. | §12.3 |
| 6 | Start's allowance and an idle client at end | **AS BUILT** | `start` returns `Full` when the root allowance cannot hold the place (a Memory or Temp place needs about 1.1 GiB). A client left open at `end` makes the result `Forced` at the grace deadline | §12.3 |

## CHANGES-v2.27 (amendments to v2.26 from astra's attack, platform-spec-attack-1748/REPORT.md; lane platform-spec-amend-1755)

This part answers findings 8, 3 and 6 of the v2.26 review. P1's idle stop, startup stop and child report are built. The native `core-runner-r1` checks cover L44, the pre-Ready stop and the child part of L42.

| # | Finding | Decision | What v2.27 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 3: removing the last trigger entry strands committed rows | **ADOPTED** | The head drives triggers when its entry list is non-empty **or** its store holds a branch-0 trigger row. With an empty list a row's cancel, hold and release run as usual, and a row that reaches its entry is consumed `Gone`. The no-drive path is kept only for a store with no row, which an empty list can never fill, since `triggers.write` needs a listed entry | §12.1, §14.4, L40 |
| 2 | 6: a started granted run is held only when its stamp differs | **ADOPTED** | `held` is a granted row that is parked (and not cancelling), or whose attempt is at least 1 **and whose stamp differs from NEW's launch image digest**. A started run under an equal digest is classified by its entry like any row, so with its entry removed it is `gone`. A parked `Request` hold stays `held` under an equal digest; a parked row whose cancel is requested is `cancelling`. Idle and the clean report use `triggers.next_pending`'s eligibility | §14.4, §14.6, launch.md, L40 |
| 3 | 8: idle reports can be stale when the stop is decided | **ADOPTED, with the confirmation made concrete** | The idle interval starts when both parts report idle and resets on every busy message. At its end `operate` asks S and then H to confirm from their own state; each that confirms parks (S's acceptor stays parked, H admits no request, trigger start or export) until `operate` stops the place or releases it. Busy from either releases both and resets the interval. An idle stop therefore starts its drain with admission already closed. The idle time reaches the serving process through the wake launch, not `runner.main`'s `None` | §12.1, §14.4, L44 |

**Second part (findings 1, 2, 4, 5, 7, 9, 10).** Findings 1, 2 and 10 are one problem: what the host may conclude from the launcher's reports. They get one mechanism, the control session of §14.5, instead of three patches.

| # | Finding | Decision | What v2.27 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 4 | 1: a host restart has two meanings | **REPLACED by one rule: the control session** | The launcher's events reach the host as one ordered stream per session. `Hello` ends the old session (its unacted requests are discarded, never acted on later) and is answered with an **inventory** taken in the launcher's one state loop: children by (group, incarnation, gen, role), accepted launches not yet started, and readable cold listeners. A host restart adopts matching live children and probes only unmatched `Dirty` rows, after pending deploys. A launcher loss fences the old launcher with an OS lock, empties the child cgroups under delegation, then answers `Hello` with an empty inventory; cold backlogs are lost and addresses are bound again. The report's text needed three separate guarantees (inventory, handoff, fencing); the session gives all three from one ordering | §14.5, launch.md "The launcher" |
| 5 | 2: a generation fences reports, not starts | **ADOPTED, through the session** | One outstanding serving launch per group (two in a cutover), keyed and idempotent by (group, incarnation, gen, role), with every concurrent cause coalesced into it; both host and launcher enforce it. `Dirty(g)` names the authoritative child; a cutover's NEW is a candidate at the next counter value and becomes authoritative only when OLD's head answers `Installed(v)`. On `Refused` or a final `Drift` the candidate is discarded, and it cannot have changed the store, since its selection `v` was never installed (`NotCurrent`). With no answer, the candidate's open at `v` commits, and otherwise a probe decides after the pending record is reconciled. An install child takes a generation too, and its end always leaves `Dirty`. An idle stop closes admission at its confirmation (§12.1 step 1). `incarnation` is new per added group, so no identity is reused after removal. A launch lost with its session never runs | §14.1, §14.3 |
| 6 | 4: an eligible wake can wait forever | **ADOPTED, narrowed** | Queued wakes are retried on every capacity-enabling change (a row leaving `Dirty`, a raised `max_places` or tenant quota, a retirement), each independently, one per group. The timer follows the index's minimum. A queued overdue row leaves the timer. **Only reconstructable causes are queued** (a due time, an arrival); an operator or deploy request that finds no slot is refused `Full`, which is retryable, instead of being persisted. So a host restart rebuilds the queue from the due index and listener readiness and loses nothing. The host's own group takes no slot and no idle stop | §14.2, §14.3 |
| 7 | 5: a failing group has no removal path | **ADOPTED** | Retirement of a `Dirty` group: a durable retiring mark, the launcher's `Retired` once no process of the group remains, then one transaction that removes the rows, releases the slot and records the store's disposition (`keep` or `delete`). Never a fabricated `Clean`. Backoff covers every unsuccessful start, post-start crashes included, and arrivals while failing are coalesced into the next retry. Liveness is stated conditionally | §14.3, §14.5 |
| 8 | 10: L46's host-side delivery | **ADOPTED (stream order)** | The launcher samples the listener after the exit, sends `End`, then starts its level watch, so any `Arrived` follows the `End` in the stream; the host applies a group's events in stream order, and each inventory repeats readable listeners. L46's connection arrives after admission closes, and also just after the exit sample | §14.3, L46 |
| 9 | 7: collection's root read and unlink are in two processes | **ADOPTED (a collection fence)** | Collection is begin, root read, collect, end, in that order; while the fence is held the launcher releases no reservation, so every root is in the host's snapshot or still reserved. A new session drops a fence without deleting | launch.md "The launcher" |
