| 10 | 9: child-mode authority | **ADOPTED** | The launcher passes the listener, private endpoint and wake report only to the serving child. The control socket stays with the host. Prepare opens the staged export. | launch.md "Child modes" |

## CHANGES-v2.26 (cold places under the platform host: the wake row, the idle stop, the clean report and the deploy trigger scan; lane platform-host-spec-1741)

Agent Platform 2027 (ratified 2026-11-22, Topics 2 and 3) runs places cold by default under a Hirsel platform host (docs/spec/launch.md "Platform host and launcher"). §13.5 said an unloaded place is not woken for a trigger. That rule is reopened for platform places in §14, which records the implementation status.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.26 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Waking a cold place | **RATIFIED (T3-Q1 = A)** | One generation-fenced row per place in the host catalog, `Dirty(gen) \| Clean(Option[next_due])`; the host wakes a `Clean(Some(t))` place at wall time t | §14.1, §14.3 |
| 2 | Capacity | **RATIFIED** | Checked before `Dirty` is written; `dirty` ≤ `max_places` in every committed state; lowering below it is refused | §14.2 |
| 3 | Only a clean stop writes `Clean` | **RATIFIED** | `Ended` becomes `Stopped(Option[u64]) \| Unstarted \| Forced(u64)`; `runner.main --hirsel-wake` reports `next_due`; any other end leaves `Dirty` and a probe | §14.4, §14.5 |
| 4 | Idle stop | **DECIDED here; reversible** | `PlaceConfig.idle`; idle needs no session, no worker, no active run and no trigger due within the idle time | §14.4 |
| 8 | A wake of a live group, and an arrival during a drain | **DECIDED here; reversible** | A wake of a live group changes nothing; the launcher checks the listener before it reports a child's end, so an arrival during a drain relaunches at `gen + 1` instead of writing `Clean` (L46) | §14.3 |
| 9 | One listener per group, and `Arrived` | **DECIDED here; outward-facing; reversible until P4 lands** | Each group has one TCP listener that the launcher holds for its life; clients find it in the host's routing table, and the host never proxies. Changing to a shared front listener later changes every client's address | launch.md "Routing" |
| 5 | Deploy trigger scan | **RATIFIED** | Runs in the prepare child after the staged migration, with the head's own dispatch code; exact, or `Limit(triggers)` | §14.6, launch.md |
| 6 | Image-digest retry guard | **RATIFIED: kept** | Started granted runs keep the launch image-digest stamp; the scan reports them `held` | §14.6 |
| 7 | The §13 directory | **DECIDED here; reversible until P6 lands** | Superseded by the platform host; deleted once the host's harness covers PD1 to PD4 | §14.8 row 9 |

## CHANGES-v2.25.1 (an owner stages every Update already queued in one delivery job; lane checkbox-repair-1670)

At width 8 the 64-client checkbox sent 3,944 to 12,937 whole-board repairs, about 125 KB each, for 12,800 toggles; width 1 sent 0, and the Tokio twin sent 0. The repair rule (§3 "Repair") was not at fault: it repairs a sub only after its owner's 1+q-slot inbox was full. The owner was. It staged one publisher Update per delivery job, and each job is one trip through the session's shared allocation pool, which spawns and joins a task. Under one worker the owner ran between publisher rounds. Under 4 and 8 workers the publisher ran rounds while owners were still staging, so the inbox filled, subs went due, and every repair, a whole board, slowed the owner further. A rule that held only because the scheduler ran the owner after each round would depend on an order that CONC-10 and SEM-8 do not give, so the owner now takes its backlog instead.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.25.1 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | An owner's queued Updates | **DECIDED here; reversible** | When an owner takes a publisher Update, it also takes, with `try_receive` and in inbox order, each Update already queued behind it, up to its inbox's capacity C (1+q) in all, and stages them in order in one delivery job. Each take counts toward the owner's wake (§3) like any take. The first event of another kind ends the run; the owner keeps it and handles it next, before it takes again. Each Update is staged as before: its own outcomes, generations, supersession and `budget` check, in order. | brief; law `owner_takes_queued` (L24) |

**Decision log.**
- **Item 1.** The other places a fix could go were the publisher and the inbox. Publisher-side coalescing per session would make the publisher hold rows for a full inbox, against ruling C (§3 item 4: "The publisher holds no subscription rows for a full inbox"). A bigger inbox would add a second bound beside 1+q and still storm at a larger burst. Taking the backlog keeps one account: the C events were already charged to the session, and the staged output stays under `budget`. /wholehog: one job per run of queued Updates is the clean end state; nothing else changes. Reversible: the take is owner-local.
- **Measured** (lane checkbox-repair-1670 REPORT.md, the same CPUs and host for each pair): width 4 on CPUs 0-3, 1,486 repairs before and 15 after; width 8 on 0-3 and 24-27, 914 and 6; width 8 on 0-3 and 8-11, 3,944 and 192. Width 1 sends 0 before and after. Every client converged to the same checksum. The repairs that remain are not attributed: they move with host load, from 6 to 192 across the pairs, and a `Sent` between Updates still ends a take run.

## CHANGES-v2.25 (two query kinds: `query` does not depend on the Caller, `query_as` does; lane runner-query-caller-1608)

Sam approved this lane on 2026-11-22 (gavel thread). It comes from the storage-engine and zero-unsafe audit. v2.19 item 2 gave every query body the Caller (`fn(Tx, H, Caller, take A)`), but the built runner never passed it: `query` took `fn(Tx, H, take A)`, so no query could decide access by Caller. v2.25 keeps both forms as two registration kinds, so the body's type says whether its result depends on who asked.

| # | Item | Ruling | Detail |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The two kinds | **CHANGED: `query` and `query_as`** | `query(mut r, f, n, s)` takes `fn(Tx, H, take A) -> Result[Cover, Capacity]`. The body has no Caller, so its result is a function of the snapshot and the argument alone. `query_as(mut r, f, n, s)` takes `fn(Tx, H, Caller, take A) -> Result[Cover, Capacity]`. Its body receives the Caller of the session that subscribed (rule 2). The kind is the body's type, so registering a Caller-reading body with `query`, or a caller-free body with `query_as`, is a type error, not a runtime check. **Why two names and not one `query` that always passes the Caller (v2.19's text):** a caller-free body needs no Caller parameter it must ignore, and a reader of `place` sees which queries are per-caller. **Why `query_as` and not an overload:** Hirsel has no overloading; the name reads "query as the Caller". |
| 2 | What the Caller is | **The subscribing session's, at every run** | A subscription's input holds its key, argument and Caller. The session copies its bound Caller (§3 "Binding") into each Subscribe it forwards. The publisher runs the query with that Caller at subscribe (G3), at every rerun after a read-set hit (G2), and at repair (§3 "Repair"), because each of those runs reads the same input. Results are never shared between sessions: each (session, sub) runs its own query and keeps its own Cover and read set (L5's `shared_cache` is a mutant). So nothing is keyed by kind, and a `query` result is not shared either. The kind decides only whether the body sees the Caller. |
| 3 | What is unchanged | **KEPT** | The key (identity ‖ fp(A) ‖ fp(unit)), `Described.kind` (`Query` for both, because a client subscribes to both the same way), the wire frames, and every existing `query` registration. Each existing query (checkbox `board`, `wire/admission` `read`, `runner_cover` `span`, `runner_calls` `everything`) reads no Caller, so it stays `query`. |
| 4 | Charge | **CHANGED** | A Subscribe's input charge adds the rounded capacity of the Caller's subject to the key's and argument's (§3 "Admission"). |
| 5 | Law | **ADDED: L38 `query_as_caller`** | See §8. |

## CHANGES-v2.24.1 (http.md Part B binds keyed claims through application code; lane http-keyed-noauth-1510)

http.md Part B no longer has verifiers. A keyed binding's principal and key come from an application claim function that is `Wire.bind`'s mechanism (§3 "Binding") with the request's named headers and body as input, run with a copy of the wire grant (http.md K-1, K-2). So v2.17 M3's namespace registry has no place in the runner: `RegisterError.NamespaceTwice` and `OpenError.NamespaceChanged` are deleted, with no replacement. Keyed open refusals (`KeyedOpChanged`, `KeyedNoWire`, `KeyedOpDropped`) are http.md's, as before. Nothing else in this spec changes.

## CHANGES-v2.24 (ruling R6: wire-callable `Granted` entries copy a `plain + send` grant per call, and the runner does no authentication or authorization; lane lang-spec-r2r7-1493)

Sam ratified R6 on 2026-11-21 (gavel ledger, R6 Option A): "require `G: plain + send` and copy `G` per call; NO auth baked into Hirsel or `runner`", and "make it abundantly clear that we do not bake auth into hirsel." Dropped, and not to be proposed again: a shareable `Arc[G]` capability, a badge maker, or any authentication or policy in the runner (D3).

**No authentication, no authorization.** Hirsel and `runner` contain neither. The runner checks no token, signature, password or key, mints no credential, keeps no allow or deny list, and decides no access by Caller. It has no badge maker, no Caller-based policy, no per-caller grant and no `Arc[G]`. Authentication is the application's binding function (item 2). Authorization is the application's entry bodies, which receive the Caller and decide in their own code (v2.19, D8). Rule 13 states this normatively.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.24 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Wire-callable `Granted` | **NEW: a copied plain grant** | A place makes its `Granted` entries callable over the wire only by giving `PlaceConfig.wire` a `Wire[G]`, which `runner.wire` makes and which requires `G: plain + send`. Each wire call to a `Granted` entry runs with its own copy of that `G` (`let` copies a plain value, OWN-2), so no call consumes it and every call sees it. `plain + send` admits `Dir`, `Net`, `Clock`, `Random` and records of them. A `Secret` is a resource and is never in a wire grant (v2.63). With `wire: None`, a wire call to a `Granted` entry is `Refused(NotAllowed)`, as before. A movable `G` (not plain) stays trigger-only: `PlaceConfig.triggers` makes one per attempt and moves it into that attempt, and it never reaches the wire (§10 D9). | R6 |
| 2 | Who binds the Caller | **CHANGED: the application, or a fresh anonymous Caller** | The runner does not authenticate a Hello token or a Bearer header. The Hello's `credential` bytes, or the HTTP `Authorization` value, go to the application's binding, `Wire.bind`, a function from `(G, credential)` to `Ok(Caller)` or `Error(())`. An `Error` refuses the Hello with `Close` 1008, or the HTTP request with 401, and records nothing. With no binding, every session gets a fresh anonymous Caller (issuer 1) whose 16-byte subject the runner draws from `Random` at Hello, so no two sessions share a Caller and a saved reply never crosses sessions. Dedup across a reconnect therefore needs an application binding: the anonymous Caller of one connection is never seen again. | runner-granted-1496 boundary (1)–(3) |
| 3 | What is deleted | **DELETED, with no compatibility path** | Minting: `anon:` principals, the Welcome bearer, `POST /.hirsel/principal`, the new-principals-per-IP limit and its refusals, and the SHA-256 derivation of an anonymous subject from a token. Welcome's `principal: Bytes` becomes `caller: Caller`, which is not secret. Hello's `token` becomes `credential`, passed to the binding unread. | item 2 |
| 4 | What is unchanged | **KEPT** | `Caller{issuer, subject}`, `caller_key`, the stream key `caller_key ‖ u32-BE(stream)`, Caller-keyed reply isolation (L32) and every transact and query signature. A credential byte is never stored (L31). | runner-granted-1496 boundary (4) |
| 5 | The `Granted` body | **CHANGED: it takes the Caller** | `fn(G, Caller, take A) -> Result[R, Capacity] waits`, like transact and query, so the application can decide a granted call's access in code. A wire call passes its session's Caller; a triggered attempt passes `TriggerConfig.author`. | like kinds alike; D8 |
| 6 | Laws | **CHANGED and NEW** | L29 says "one credential the binding maps to one Caller" in place of "one token". L31's oracle searches for the credential's bytes. New L36 (`granted_copied`) and L37 (`anonymous_fresh`), §8. | — |

**Decision log (each reversible; the brief left them open).**
- **Item 2, where the binding's authority comes from.** The binding takes a copy of the wire grant `G`, so a binding that checks expiry reads `G`'s `Clock`; one that needs a sealed key has no route yet (§15.8 J-D4; v2.63). A second plain authority type beside `G` would add a type parameter to `PlaceConfig` for one function. Reversible: `Wire` is opaque, so its fields may change without touching entries. (Reversed by v2.28: a place with a movable `G` needs that second type, `B`.)
- **Item 2, rejection codes.** 1008 and 401 are the codes a refused credential already used (§3, http.md B-6). A binding's `Error` carries no reason, so no oracle on why a credential failed leaves the place.
- **Item 5.** Without the Caller a granted entry could not decide access, so the ruling's "auth is the application's code" would leave granted calls open to every session. Reversible: the Caller parameter is one adapter argument.
- **http.md Part B's verifiers** (`StandardWebhooks` and other signed verifiers) checked secrets inside the runner. Under R6 they are application code: v2.24.1 records that http.md K-2 now binds keyed claims through an application claim function, and that the verifiers are gone.

## CHANGES-v2.23 (the publisher coalesces as G1 says, and a full head inbox refuses nothing; lane wire-overload-1464)

The W9 wire benchmark held only 20 offers/s at 1,000 sessions, against Rust's 8,000/s (wire-w9-1233). The built runner had two deviations from this text, and both are fixed here. **(1)** G1 says the publisher "drains the inbox in order and takes the newest snapshot, so a slow publisher coalesces". The built publisher instead ran one round, with its fan-out to every owner, per `Released`. At 1,000 sessions, a round's fan-out costs about 38 ms on the single worker. So `PubIn` filled with about 64 one-call Releases, and each answer waited about 64 rounds, or 2.7 s. **(2)** No rule refuses a send because the head's inbox is full. V1's `Overloaded` is for the session's own slot table, and the queue table bounds requests at the head by 1+q per session. Yet the owner `try_send`ed each request to the head and answered `Refused(Overloaded)` when the 64-slot inbox was full. That happened at 500/s once (1) was fixed: a probe build tagged each refusal site, and every refusal came from this one. Text and build together.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.23 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | G1 coalescing | **AS SPECIFIED; built here** | After a `Released`, the publisher takes every message already in `PubIn` with `try_receive`, in order, while each is a `Released` or a `Drained`. It keeps every Release's answers, in order. Then it runs one round at the newest snapshot it took and publishes that round's fences. The first message of any other kind ends the run, and the publisher handles it next, in inbox order. A `Drained` carries only the pass that follows each message, so taking it into the run changes nothing. Every Outcome still travels in its Release, so none is skipped (G1), and V3's order is kept: a snapshot-less answer is sent at the base of the round that follows it. | G1; laws `calls_one_outcome_per_send`, `repair_one_unsent`, `join_waits` |
| 2 | A full head inbox | **DECIDED here; reversible** | The owner offers each forwarded request to the head with `try_send`. If the inbox is full, the request is held in the owner's post queue, in order, and every later request goes behind it. The owner's loop `send`s the queue with a wait before it waits on its own inbox again. A forwarded position is held in the slot table from the moment it is queued, so requests at the head and in the post queue together stay at most 1+q per session (queue table). Its deadline timer runs from that moment too, so a send whose deadline passes while it waits is `Abandoned` (V1), as at a held head. A closed head inbox closes the session with 1011, as before. **No deadlock:** the head never waits on an owner. It answers through the publisher's `PubIn` (V3, V4), and the publisher never waits on an owner (§1.1). So the owner's wait ends when the head takes its next message. Rationale and reversibility: the decision log below. | §3 queue table; law `full_head_waits` |

**Decision log.**
- **Item 2.** There were three options: refuse (as built), make the head inbox bigger, or wait. A refusal tells a client to retry while the place is only busy, and every retry adds load. A bigger inbox still refuses at a larger burst, and it adds a second bound beside the slot table. Waiting keeps one account, the slot table, as the queue table already states, and it treats requests like v2.22's `Joined`: like things alike. /wholehog: waiting is the clean end state, since the slot table is the one bound and a full channel is not an admission decision. Reversible: the post queue is owner-local and puts no byte on the wire.

## CHANGES-v2.22 (one unsent repair per session, a waiting `Joined`, and superseded owner content; lane repair-cap-1459)

repair-storm-1453 found three faults in the width-4 checkbox. The owner kept every whole-board repair it took, so a slow reader went over `budget` and was `Reset`; that fix landed in 36b8a8c4 and is specified here (item 1). The publisher sent a new whole-board repair each time an owner drained, though the last one was still unsent: 4,222 to 9,347 repairs of about 125 KB in a 64-client run (item 2). And 64 clients connecting at once filled the 64-slot `PubIn` with `Joined` and first `Subscribed` messages, and the owner's `try_send` refused them `Refused(Overloaded)` while the place was not overloaded (items 3 and 4). The orchestrator's rulings of 2026-11-20 (DECISIONS.md) accept 1453's wording for items 1 and 2 and its option (b) for item 3. Text and build together; the laws are in admission-laws.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.22 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Content the owner holds that a later `complete` replaces | **AS BUILT (36b8a8c4), clarifies v2.20 item 5 and §3's slow-reader row** | Content the owner still holds for a sub instance that a later `complete` of the same instance replaces is superseded. The owner drops it before it weighs `budget`. Outcomes and their marks stay. The `budget` check weighs only what the client will still see. | ruling 1; law `repair_coalesced` |
| 2 | One unsent repair per session | **RULED (accepted); built here** | A repair is sent only when the owner can take it and has sent the last one: the publisher repairs a due sub only when it has taken the session's progress token since the session's last bounce and its last repair, **and** the session holds no `complete` that the owner has taken and not yet sent. Until then the sub stays due. The publisher counts the `complete`s each session's inbox took (`handed`). The owner counts the `complete`s it takes for its current join, and publishes that count in the session's **taken register** (a `Latest` it makes at each join and hands over in `Joined`) only while no `complete` is queued in its output or held by its writer. The session is caught up when the register is at least `handed`. When the owner publishes a new count, it `try_send`s a `Drained` (no token) so the publisher looks again. The publisher keeps no row bytes, so v2.12.1 item 4 and ruling C still hold. | ruling 2; law `repair_one_unsent` |
| 3 | `Joined` waits | **RULED (b); built here** | The owner `send`s its `Joined` into `PubIn` and may wait, under the same bound as `connect`'s `send` (v2.12 item 3): the publisher never waits on an owner, so the wait is at most one drain. `Subscribed`, `Unsubscribed` and `Drained` stay `try_send`. **No deadlock:** the publisher's only wait is its `receive` on `PubIn` (every send it makes to an owner, a `Told` sender or a register is a `try_send` or a publish), so nothing the publisher does waits on an owner. While its `Joined` waits, the owner holds no sub and no content of that join: the publisher has not registered it, so it owes it nothing, and the owner's own tasks (reader, writer, policy) wait only on the owner, not the other way round. A `PubIn` closed by `Halt` ends the wait with `Closed`; the owner then answers the Subscribe `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`, as it does for an Admit with id 0. | ruling 4; law `join_waits` |
| 4 | The first Subscribe | **DECIDED here; reversible** | The Subscribe that makes an owner join rides in its `Joined` (`first`: sub, generation, input), and the publisher registers it when it takes the Join, after the `Admit`, at that base (G3). The owner reserves the sub's room before it sends, and it counts the sub live at that generation when it sees the `Admit`. A later Subscribe is `Subscribed` with `try_send`, as before. | decision log below; law `join_waits` |
| 5 | Laws | **NEW** | §8: `repair_one_unsent` (L24) and `join_waits` (L23), and `repair_coalesced` (L24) for item 1. | brief |

**Decision log.**
- **Item 4.** With only `Joined` waiting, a burst keeps `PubIn` full of waiting Joins, so the separate `Subscribed` the owner `try_send`s right after its `Admit` would still be refused. Folding the first Subscribe into the Join gives one message per join, which halves what a burst puts into `PubIn` and leaves nothing to refuse. /wholehog: the join and its first Subscribe are one event, so one message is the clean end state; a separate `Subscribed` with a wait would make two kinds of `Subscribed`. Reversible: the Join's shape is internal to the place.
- **Item 2, "sent" rather than "taken".** 1453's wording says "the owner has not yet taken". As built, a `complete` counts until the owner's writer has sent it to the client. The owner takes its inbox quickly, so a count at take time would still let the publisher compute a whole board for every drain while the writer is behind, and item 1 would drop each one unsent. Counting at send keeps at most one whole board in flight per session. Every `complete` counts, a first one or a repair, because the owner cannot tell them apart and like things are treated alike.
- **Item 2, the token is kept.** A token taken while the session is not caught up is remembered until the next bounce or repair. Without that, a drain that comes while the last repair is still in the owner's inbox spends the token, and in a quiet place no other drain comes, so the sub would stay due forever. A bounce still clears it, so a token sent before a bounce never counts after it (v2.20 item 3).
- **Item 3, checked against the code before writing.** The publisher's body (runner.hirsel, `publisher` through `repair` and `settle`) has no `channel.send`; it waits only in `channel.receive(from)`. The owner's waiting send is made in its own loop (`wire_owner_join`), before it waits on its inbox again.

## CHANGES-v2.21 (a directory of prelinked places by name, §13; lane place-dir-1454)

The Lash harness needs to create, find, load and unload places by name inside one process (harness-prereq-1437 REPORT §5, build order item 3). `runner.operate` runs one place; nothing ran several. v2.21 adds the module `places` (package `store`), a directory over `operate`, and one runner field. Text and build together; the four laws are in runner_calls. No language, store-format or core change.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.21 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The API | **NEW: an allowlist of `name → factory`, `open` returns a lease** | §13.1. The caller lists each name with a `Factory`, a `Backing` and a `Selection`. A factory is made only by `place_factory(make)`, whose `make` is a plain fn that builds a `PlaceConfig`, so only prelinked code can be listed (loading code by digest stays out, harness-prereq §6). `open(name)` returns a `Lease` on a Ready instance, starting one if needed. `close(name)` and the idle sweep unload. | brief; harness-prereq §5 |
| 2 | One head per name | **NEW: one directory task owns every name's state** | §13.2. Each name is `Absent`, `Starting`, `Running` or `Closing`. Opens that find `Starting` queue on that start. A failed start answers every queued open with `Start(e)` and the name goes back to `Absent`. | workerd one container per id; Akka buffers during start |
| 3 | Leases, idle and close | **NEW: a lease pins its instance; unload waits for operate to return** | §13.2. A lease holds a share of the instance's pin. The directory unloads a place only when it holds the only share, after seeing that for `idle_ms` (idle) or at once (`close`). Unloading sends `()` on operate's `stop`, so it is operate's drain (§12.1). The name stays `Closing` until the place task's last message, which it sends after `operate` has returned. An open during `Closing` waits and then starts a fresh instance. | workerd strong-ref check; Orleans and Akka buffer during deactivation |
| 4 | Backing | **RULED: volatile backings never idle out** | §13.3. `Memory` and `Temp` places are unloaded only by `close` or directory stop, never by the idle sweep, because an idle timer must not be what loses data. Disk places reopen the same `Dir`, the same options and the activation the last instance ran. | brief |
| 5 | Activation on reopen | **CHANGED: `Status.activation`** | §12.1 and §13.3. `Status` gains `activation: u64`, the activation version the head selected or installed (0 for a memory store). The directory lists the next start of a disk place at `Current(activation)`. | `head_prepare` refuses a different `Current` |
| 6 | Directory stop | **NEW** | §13.4. `stop` sends every live place its own stop at once and returns when each has ended, so it takes as long as the slowest place's §12.1 drain. Opens and closes after it are refused `Stopping`. | brief |
| 7 | Bounds | **NEW: a count and a byte budget, reserved up front; typed refusal** | §13.4. `max_places` caps live instances (Starting, Running and Closing). `place_bytes` is reserved when the directory starts. A start is refused `Full(Count(n))` or `Full(Reserve(room))` when it does not fit; nothing is evicted. `runner.operate_bytes(config)` is what operate spends. | Orleans memory-pressure collection and Akka limit passivation evict; we refuse instead |
| 8 | Release check and triggers across unload | **ARGUED** | §13.5. Every session of an instance ends in its drain, before its publisher halts; the next instance has a new publisher at its own durable base. Due triggers run at the next open (ENDSTATE `storage-triggers`); the directory does not wake an unloaded place. | ENDSTATE.md "after a long outage" |
| 9 | Laws | **NEW: PD1–PD4** | §8 and §13.6: `dir_one_head`, `dir_open_during_close`, `dir_idle_unload`, `dir_reopen_keeps`. | brief |

## CHANGES-v2.20 (a full owner inbox is repaired, not ended; Sam's ruling C on overload-advice-1449/ADVICE.md; lane auth-remove-1423)

A content bounce at a full owner inbox is a scheduling pause, not an end. Before v2.20 it ended the sub with `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`, and the client resubscribed and reran the whole query. Under the width-4 checkbox regime (checkbox-overload-1443) that turned ordinary pauses into terminal events. v2.20 keeps the sub live and owes it one whole replacement. Text and build together; no model has a ledger, so no model was re-run.

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.20 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | A bounced live sub | **RULED: mark for repair** | When `settle`'s `try_send` of a session's content is refused because the owner's inbox is full, every live sub whose rows or `complete` that content carried is marked **due** (one flag per sub; several lost rounds set the same flag). No `Ended` and no `Reset` is made, and the bounced rows are dropped: the publisher keeps no row bytes for them. Ended entries and a pending `Reset` in the bounced content stay owed exactly as before, because they are already bounded ledgers. `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))` stays only for real admission failures: an owner's own full-`PubIn` Subscribe (§3), a refused `Joined` reservation (v2.19.3 item 3) and a draining place (§12.1). | ADVICE "Ruling for C" 1 |
| 2 | A due sub in later rounds | **RULED** | Its read set stays indexed. A read-set hit still fences the session at c in step 3, as any hit does. The sub is not re-run and sends no diff, because its client's image has a hole that a diff would cross. Its terminal decision (a fault or budget end on re-run) waits for the repair. | ADVICE 1 |
| 3 | When it is repaired | **RULED: on owner progress** | At its first Subscribe the owner makes a capacity-1 **progress channel** and hands the receiver over in `Joined`. Each time the owner offers the v2.13 `Drained`, it first `try_send`s `()` on that channel (a full channel already holds a token). Before every `settle`, the publisher takes each joined session's token with `try_receive`. A session whose token it took, or that has no progress channel (the HTTP and one-shot sessions), is **repaired** in that pass: each of its due subs is re-run at the publisher's base with its generation and input (as G3), its read set is replaced, its due flag is cleared, and its full result is queued as a `complete` naming the sub, merged into the session's owed content. A token taken before a bounce never counts after it, because the take precedes the `settle` that bounces. A quiet place repairs within C of the owner's takes (the v2.13 argument, CHANGES-v2.13 item 2), with no commit, timer or client request. | ADVICE 2 |
| 4 | Soundness of the replacement | **ARGUED** | Dropping the old read set at repair is safe: any commit since its last run that hit it fenced the session already, and if none did, the re-run reads the same keys. The replacement is a content unit at the base and passes the writer's release check like any other (§3). A replacing Subscribe, an Unsubscribe, a retirement or the session's end removes the instance and its due flag with it. | ADVICE 3, 4 |
| 5 | The client | **RULED: every `complete` replaces from empty** | A Batch whose `complete` names a sub replaces that sub's cache from an empty image, whether it is the first or a repair. WIRE-SPEC's `complete`, std runner.md and http.md B-9 say so. The load client's bounce resubscribe is deleted. | ADVICE "Cutover" |
| 6 | Limits | **UNCHANGED** | A full owner inbox spends no slow-reader strike. Rows over `budget` still `Reset` with Outcomes, one counted per connection, and a second counted overrun closes 1008. `Dropped` stays exempt. The 30-second write deadline and its 5-second close stay separate. | ADVICE 5 |
| 7 | Laws | **REWRITTEN L23, L24 → `repair_delivered`; L28 gains repair cases** | §8. L23 and L24 now require the same instance to reach its exact current rows after each stall and drain, with no `Ended`, `Reset` or resubscription. | ADVICE "Cutover and falsifiers" |

**Deviations from the ruling, as built.**
- The progress record is a capacity-1 channel per joined session, not a published drain generation. A token holds the same one bit (progress since the last sample). Taking it before `settle` gives the ruling's order: sampled before the failed attempt, so a wake racing the bounce counts for the next pass.
- A session with no progress channel repairs on every pass. Only the HTTP and one-shot sessions join that way, and neither has an owner inbox that fills.
- The runner law has no removed-key or empty-image case: the wired harness has no delete fixture. The client's replace-from-empty is held by `every_complete_replaces_the_image_from_empty` in bench/wire.
- The wire owner's token send is held by the width-4 checkbox regime, not by a law; the fixture owners send it, and `no_token` (fixture owners never send it) deadlocks L23 and L24.

**Prior art (v2.20).**
- **Meteor oplog observer** (oplog_observe_driver.js:138–144, via ADVICE): on dropped work, repoll the query. ADAPTED: Hirsel repolls one sub, and only once its owner drains.
- **Convex sync worker** (worker.rs:497–508, via ADVICE): query transitions start only when consumption wakes the worker. ADOPTED in shape: repair waits on owner progress.
- **Firestore target reset** (remote_store.ts:735–785, via ADVICE): a detected gap unwatches and rewatches one target for a full update. ADAPTED: no client round trip; the server sends the full update on the same id.
- **Retained diffs (option B, the Tokio checkbox reference's merge)**: NOT TAKEN. Merged rows grow with distinct keys and end in a session `Reset`, and its newer-version stamp would let a newer component certify an older row.
- **PostgreSQL logical slots and Kafka offsets**: NOT TAKEN. Exact replay retains unbounded log, and an offset reconstructs no query image.

## CHANGES-v2.19.3 (the release check as built at c199076d; the orchestrator's rulings on its three deviations; lane auth-remove-1423)

| # | Point | Decision | What v2.19.3 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | How a writer gets the round register | **ACCEPTED as built: in the join answer** | `round(mail)` and the `Round` type are deleted, and `connect` loses its `round` argument. The publisher answers each `Joined` with `Admit{who, round}`: the session's id and a handle to the round register. A session has no writer check before it has joined, and it has no content to check before then either. | ruling 1 on c199076d |
| 2 | A publisher that traps leaves the head committing | **FIXED: the register fails closed** | The round register is a resource whose cleanup publishes R = `u64::MAX` from its reserved `Arc`. Only `Halt` disarms it. The publisher holds it as its last-declared slot, so a trap cleans it first (FAIL-8): R = `u64::MAX` is published before any owner channel or the inbox closes. The head treats a refused `Released` as the publisher's end: it commits nothing more, and `place_head` returns `OpenError.PublisherStopped`. A commit the head made before it learned this is never offered, and every writer drops content from E_MAX on. L28 gains `publisher_trap_open`. | ruling 2 on c199076d |
| 3 | Fence and round `Arc`s were allocated per round; a failed allocation left R = `u64::MAX` silently | **FIXED: reserved, and a failure stops the place** | `mailbox` reserves the round register's spare and its `u64::MAX` `Arc`. `Joined` reserves the session's two fence spares and one round spare. A round publishes each new value by writing into a spare that no reader shares (`arc.with_unique`), and the old value becomes a spare. The writer holds at most one share of each, so a spare is always free. The reservation is admitted from the publisher's allowance, which is the only ledger the publisher has. A refused reservation answers the join with id 0, and the owner ends the pending subscription `Refused(Overloaded)`. Only a writer of a session that has already left can hold a round spare beyond its session's reservation. Then the round allocates one more `Arc`, and if that is refused, the publisher stops as in point 2. No path leaves the place running at `u64::MAX` without a word. | ruling 3 on c199076d |

## CHANGES-v2.19.2 (astra's recheck of v2.19.1, astra-recheck-1427/RECHECK.md; lane auth-remove-1423)

astra rechecked 905b048d: 6 closed, 2 open, plus a progress cost. The orchestrator ruled: close both, and replace the strict `Ended` with an indexed partial read log. B2 and M1–M4 are unchanged.

| # | Finding | Decision | What v2.19.2 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | A bounce found at G2 step 4, after R is published, removes a read set with no fence | **FIXED: ended entries (a third mechanism)** | The orchestrator named two fixes: learn the bounce before publishing, or fence every sub whose offer can bounce. v2.19.2 uses a third, from the same invariant: a read set leaves the index only when no content that depended on it can still pass a writer unfenced. A subscription the publisher ends, by fault, refusal, overrun, bounce or `Reset`, stops running but keeps its read set indexed as an **ended entry** until a read-set hit fences the session (the entry then leaves), the owner removes its instance, or the session ends. So no ending needs a fence when it is found, wherever it is found, and v2.19.1's "removal is a hit" fences are deleted. | RECHECK B1 |
| P | Unrelated rounds can reset a quiet session forever | **FIXED: no strict units** | The orchestrator's fix. A deterministic fault depends only on the reads made before it, so a faulted or refused run's partial read log is its ended entry, and its `Ended` gets the ordinary check. The strict rule (R equal to the unit's version) is deleted, in http.md's B-11 and SSE text too. | RECHECK "Other progress cost" |
| M5 | `Retire` can stay owed forever, and resubscriptions starve behind it | **FIXED: retirement rides on `Subscribed` (a third mechanism)** | The orchestrator named two fixes: reserve `Retire`'s slot, or have the publisher wake the owner. v2.19.2 uses a third, whose invariant is: the publisher applies every retirement before it charges any later subscription of the session, and no message waits on a slot. Every `Subscribed` carries the owner's last retired instance, and the publisher ends every subscription of the session at or below it before it registers the new one, in `PubIn` order. A best-effort `Retire` is still offered after each owner event, so a retired instance whose content keeps arriving is ended without a resubscription. A Subscribe is no longer refused while a retirement is owed. Nothing is allocated for it: the number is a field of a message the owner already sends. | RECHECK M5 |
| L28 | B1b can be masked, B1c is underspecified | **RE-AIMED** | B1b forces the budget end through Cover-only conversion, so no read-set re-run fences it. B1c gives the replacement disjoint reads and distinguishable rows, and has a held case (the old unit gated at the writer before its check) and a delayed case (the old `complete` taken after the replacement). New cases, each red on v2.19.1: a step-4 bounce, a fault `Ended` that survives unrelated rounds, and an idle owner's retirement under a full `PubIn`. | RECHECK L28 |

**What it costs, stated.**
- An ended entry stays charged to its session's `budget`, as its subscription was, until its first read-set hit, its owner's removal or the session's end. Its first hit resets the session once, even when its `Ended` was already written.
- Retired instances that no `Subscribed` or `Retire` has reached stay charged and indexed. Their hits fence a session that holds no content of a live instance, so they reset nothing a client keeps.

## CHANGES-v2.19.1 (astra's attack on the release check, astra-release-1425/ATTACK.md; lane auth-remove-1423)

astra attacked v2.19 at bc50b6f9: 3 Blocker, 5 Major, 1 Minor. The orchestrator ruled: fix Blockers 1 and 2, the five Majors and the Minor, each by the smallest fix the attack names; reject Blocker 3. Text only: no model was re-run. Each blocker's trace is a case of L28 that is red on v2.19.

| # | Finding | Decision | What v2.19.1 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| B1 | A queued result can outlive its read set | **FIXED: removal is a hit, strict `Ended`s, and subscription instances** | §3 "The release check". (a) Every removal of a subscription that the publisher makes in round b (a fault, a budget or `Overloaded` end, a publisher-made `Reset`) moves the session's fence to b, as a read-set hit does. (b) A publisher-made `Ended`, whose fault may depend on reads it no longer indexes, is **strict**: it passes only while R still equals its computation version. (c) A removal the owner asks for (`Unsubscribed`, a replacing `Subscribed`, `Retire`) is sent only while its writer holds no unit. The owner tags each `Subscribed` with an **instance** that the publisher echoes on its content, and discards any content of an instance that is no longer current, so an old `complete` that arrives after a replacing Subscribe is not taken as the new one's. The same rules cover SSE `ended` events and B-11 error bodies. | ATTACK B1 |
| B2 | The newest component certifies a mixed Batch | **FIXED: a unit's version is its oldest content component's** | Each content component carries its computation version inside the runner, and a unit is checked at the oldest one. Outcome rows are outside the check and survive a drop. | ATTACK B2 |
| B3 | Coalescing loses intervening hits | **REJECTED** (orchestrator) | Ruling 73 asks for a re-check "against current state", and the publisher computes only at group boundaries. A read-set value that changes and returns within one group leaves a queued unit equal to what a fresh run would produce, so G2 keeps the endpoint diff. | ruling 73 as amended |
| M1 | The deploy exposes its fence one session at a time | **FIXED: one publication of R = `u64::MAX`** | The halt publishes no fences. It publishes R = `u64::MAX` once, from an `Arc` reserved at `mailbox`, before C7 reports the install and before any 1012. A writer that loads it drops every content unit. The ordinary `Halt` publishes nothing. | ATTACK M1; hot-deploy C7 |
| M2 | V3 lacks a dependency version | **FIXED: `seen`** | Every V3 answer carries the newest commit the head had made when it decided, and the head pushes it only behind the Release of the group that holds that commit. It ends an open group early to do so, and never waits on a running worker. | ATTACK M2 |
| M3 | HTTP still has a second release path | **DELETED** | http.md loses row 14b, `Withheld`, `Failed(Policy)`, the scope 403s, `Recheck`, stamps and the `n_policy` terms. A call response no longer joins the publisher: its only reason was to post `Recheck`. SSE events and B-11 reads follow §3. K-5 answers a duplicate with its stored reply unchanged. §6's install no longer checks policy. | ATTACK M3 |
| M4 | L32 needs discarded history | **RE-AIMED** | L32 re-sends the current position across a membership change, then advances and expects `HistoryUnavailable` for the old one. The duplicate's Outcome and the content check are tested apart. | ATTACK M4; STORE AH2 |
| M5 | The owner cannot retire the publisher's subscriptions on Reset | **FIXED: `Retire` and instances** | On `Dropped`, the owner sends `Retire(session, instance)`, kept owed while `PubIn` is full. The publisher drops every subscription of the session up to that instance. Content of a retired instance that is still in transit is discarded on arrival. A publisher-made `Reset` that names no live instance is delivered as its outcomes alone and counts toward nothing. | ATTACK M5 |
| m1 | The fence budget is incomplete | **STATED: every round allocation is reserved** | `Joined` reserves two `Arc[Fence]` and one `Arc[u64]` on the session's empty `budget` ledger. `mailbox` reserves the round register's next `Arc` and its MAX `Arc`. A round's fence step allocates only from these, so it cannot fail, and v2.19's run stop is deleted. | ATTACK m1 |

**What it costs, stated.**
- (v2.19.1 only; v2.19.2 deletes it with the strict rule.) A strict `Ended` taken after another round published is dropped, and its session resets. So a faulting subscription can reset its whole session once per round in which its `Ended` waits. The client resubscribes, and a deterministic fault answers `Ended` again at the new base.
- A removal by the publisher resets every older queued content unit of its session, as a read-set hit does. These removals are faults, budget overruns and full inboxes, which are rare by design.
- An owner-asked removal waits for at most one unit's write.

## CHANGES-v2.19 (ruling C: the store does no authorization; lane auth-remove-1423)

Sam ruled on 2026-11-19 (DECISIONS, "Store authorization is removed; ruling 73 amended") that the store does no authorization. A call or a query gets its authenticated Caller and decides access in its own code. Ruling 73 now reads: "Queued subscription data is re-checked against current state before it is sent; a call's saved reply is delivered as it was committed." v2.19 deletes the authority design of v2.15 to v2.18: the policy, key scopes, the authority register, stamps, rechecks and `Withheld`. It replaces the writer's stamp check with a read-set release check that keeps v2.17's ordering invariant. v2.18 is this file at a59aceac. Text only: no model was re-run. astra's attack reads this text while the code is built.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.19 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | What is deleted | **DELETED, with no compatibility path** | The runner: `policy`, `KeyPolicy`, `public_access`, `RegisterError.PolicyTwice`, `OpenError.NoPolicy` and `PolicyTable`, `Fault.Policy`, `Outcome.Withheld`, `Stamp`, `Recheck`, `Reauthorized`, the writer's `Stale(unit, R)` rule, the owed `Told`'s run scope, and the policy terms of the reserves (`n_p`, `s_p`). The store (STORE v2.30 AN1): `KeyScope`, `narrow`, `narrow_view`, `scope_within`, `span`'s scope check, `Error.OutOfScope`, `authorize`, `Authorized`, `Digest`, `declare_authority`, the authority tree, `seq`, the `Latest[Seq]` register, `AuthorityReader`, `authority_reader`, `dup`, `load`, `catalog_table`, the scope and digest in `Receipt`, `Pending` and `Stored`, and `Stop.Policy`. CALL loses the 32-byte policy revision and the policy's two bounds. `cover` checks no scope. | ruling C; astra-auth-1421/ADVICE.md "Clean removal" |
| 2 | The Caller | **KEPT as an entry parameter** | The `Caller` value, its key, the anonymous subject and the stream key `caller_key ‖ stream` are unchanged (§3). A transact body gets `fn(mut Tx, H, Caller, take A)`, and a query body gets `fn(Tx, H, Caller, take A)` (rule 2). A body that refuses reads its own tables, for example a membership row, and returns its own error, or a query returns an empty Cover. Those reads are ordinary reads. In a transact body they are part of its serializable transaction. In a query they are in the subscription's read set, so a change to them re-runs the query (G2). **Why a parameter and not a `CallInfo` field (the brief's example):** a query runs in a `look` and has no `CallInfo`, so a parameter is the one form both kinds share. Replay already decodes the Caller from the record's stream key (§7), so `CallInfo` and CALL stay unchanged. | rule 2; §7; Convex `ctx.auth` |
| 3 | The release check | **NEW: a per-session fence and a place-wide round register** | §3 "The release check" and §4 G2 step 3. The publisher raises a session's fence to b when commit b hits the read set of one of that session's subscriptions. It publishes every fence of round b, then the round register's b, and only then offers anything of round b. After taking a unit and before its first byte, a writer loads the round register and then its fence. It writes a content unit only if the effective fence is at most the unit's version. A dropped unit becomes a `Reset`, and the client resubscribes and gets a fresh `complete`. | ruling C; ruling 73 as amended |
| 4 | Its ordering proof | **STATED** | §3 "Why the check is ordered". Commit b has one effective point for every writer: the publication of the round register's b. No effect of b that a client can see leaves before that point. | CONC-10; CONC-13 |
| 5 | Replies | **CHANGED: delivered as committed** | `Duplicate(Stored)` answers the stored reply unchanged, with no check. Every `Told`, first delivery or duplicate, passes the writer with no load. A stored reply is 41 + R bytes, not 73 + R. The place store's format word becomes 2, with no reader for 1 (STORE v2.30 AN2). | ruling 73 as amended |
| 6 | Deploy | **STATED: a deploy fences every session** | When a deploy commits, OLD halts its publisher (hot-deploy C7). The halt publishes every session's fence and the round register at `u64::MAX` before OLD sends any 1012 `Close`. So every content unit a writer takes after that point is dropped, and only units accepted before it finish. Outcome units still pass, as committed. No grant or code identity is compared. | deploy-design-1397; hot-deploy-1375 C7 |
| 7 | Laws | **CHANGED** | L28 is re-aimed at the release check. L30 drops the policy's revision and bounds. L32 says that a duplicate discloses only its own saved reply. L33 drops the policy terms. L26 and L27 are deleted (§8). | — |

**What it costs, stated.**
- **The fence is per session, not per subscription.** A read-set hit on any subscription of a session drops every queued content unit of that session older than the hit. This is a sound superset of the brief's per-subscription rule. A unit is one frame, which may carry rows of several subscriptions, and the `Reset` that follows a drop is session-wide anyway (§4 "Reset"). A Cover-only change never moves a fence (G2 step 2's second case), so ordinary data updates never drop units.
- **Progress.** Each drop costs the session one `Reset` and one resubscribe. If a session's read sets are hit faster than one publisher round plus one writer handoff, its subscriptions may keep resetting without completing. Outcome rows are never dropped, so calls always progress. Such a `Reset` never counts toward the protocol-error close (§4 "Reset").
- **Memory.** Each session holds one `Latest[Fence]` root (32 B) and its current `Arc[Fence]`. The place holds one round root and its current `Arc[u64]`. v2.19.1 states who pays for each (§3 queue table).

**Prior art (opened in /tmp/ref-convex-backend).**
- **ADOPTED, replies as committed:** Convex's `check_mutation_status` (`crates/application/src/application_function_runner/mod.rs:2136-2167`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->) returns a retried mutation's stored result before running anything (`:1012-1018`), with no access re-check. Item 5 is the same rule.
- **ADAPTED, access in code with tracked reads:** Convex functions read `ctx.auth` and their own tables, and a query's reads invalidate it. Here the Caller is an entry parameter, and the reads land in the read set (item 2).
- **REPLACED, session ordering:** Convex's sync worker drops every subscription on an identity change (`crates/sync/src/worker.rs:952-964`<!-- doc-ref: historical -->) and orders one client's transitions by its own version. A Hirsel Caller is fixed per session, and a place may have several writers, so v2.19 gives every writer one effective point through the round register (item 4).

## CHANGES-v2.15 (operating a place: lifecycle, release manifest and preview, the place fixture; gap study 1117 items 1, 2 and 5)

v2.15 adds §12 for gap study 1117's items 1, 2 and 5 (`GAPS.md` from gap-study-1117). Item 6, keyset pages, is a store read and is in STORE-SPEC v2.25. Each rule extends a v2.14 rule and reopens none. The slice plan is `SLICES.md` from service-ops-1127. Text only: no model was re-run. `calls25_model.py`'s `drain` scenario is planned with slice O1. v2.14 is this file at 8f15cd57.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.15 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | One lifecycle owner | **NEW** | `runner.operate` owns store open, activation, publisher, listener, drain and close, publishes `Starting(step)`, `Ready` and `Draining` to one `Latest[Status]` root, and supervises the head, the serve supervisor and its acceptor as separate tasks (§12.1). | GAPS #1 |
| 2 | Readiness | **DECIDED: a status route on the place's listener** | `GET /hirsel/status`, a raw route that reads the `Latest[Status]`: 200 in `Ready`, 503 in `Draining`; no connection is accepted before `Ready`. The supervisor-channel option is NOT TAKEN as the outside read, because every outside reader speaks HTTP probes and a channel would need a second outside protocol. | §12.1; http.md §6 |
| 3 | The drain | **EXTENDS V1 and V3** | One absolute deadline D = stop + `grace_ms`, shared with std.http's stop (http.md HTTP-1 "Stopping", added in v2.15). Admission closes at min(stop + `lag_ms`, D). After `Close`, a send the session does not hold and a FIFO entry never admitted are `Refused(Overloaded)`; admitted work runs to its reply; at D each owed send is `Abandoned` and the store is left for recovery. | §12.1; rule 5 |
| 4 | The stop source | **DECIDED** | `host.stopped(p: Process)`: the first SIGTERM or SIGINT, through the replay point. | §12.4; DECISIONS, 2026-11-18 "service-ops host stop source" |
| 5 | Manifest | **NEW; extends D1** | Written by `hirsel build --manifest`, from the preview launch with an empty old list, so there is no second entry list and no second hash. | §12.2; launch.md |
| 6 | Preview | **NEW** | The new executable answers the old entry list with the session's own `Placed` function. Schema readiness is reported as unknown. It is advisory (ENDSTATE: no deploy gate). | §12.2 |
| 7 | Confirmation | **NEW** | `hirsel confirm`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> reads the status route and compares the identity and `release_digest`. | §12.2 |
| 8 | Fixture | **NEW** | The `fixture` module runs `operate` over the wire path, with typed calls, durable replies, `seen_at`, relative bounds on the place's clock and full release. It is native-only until W8a. | §12.3; DECISIONS 2026-11-17 |
| 9 | Laws | **NEW O26–O35** | §8. The service-operations brief uses L26–L35 for these laws. | — |

**Decision log.**
- **Readiness (item 2).** Candidates: (a) a raw HTTP status route that reads `operate`'s `Latest[Status]`; (b) a supervisor channel (a local socket or pipe that the supervisor reads); (c) both, as two equal sources. (c) is NOT TAKEN: two sources for one fact can disagree. (b) is NOT TAKEN: its readers (proxy, supervisor, `confirm`) are outside the process and already speak HTTP probes, and (b) needs a second listener and grant. Its strongest case is that it works before std.http serves the place. That is build order, so it decides the slice order and not the design. (a) is TAKEN.
- **Drain answers (item 3).** Candidates: (a) `Refused(Overloaded)`; (b) a new `Refused(Draining)` with tag 8; (c) close the session at once, with no answers. (c) breaks rule 5: owed sends would get no Outcome. (b) asks the client for the same action as (a) and changes frozen tags. (a) is TAKEN.
- **Forced exit (item 3).** Candidates: (a) cancel and leave the store to recovery; (b) wait without bound for `durable`; (c) call `store.close` after cancelling. (b) has no bound, and (c) waits on journal acks past D. (a) is TAKEN: recovery already covers every cut (L1, H4).

**Prior art (v2.15).** See §11's new rows.

## CHANGES-v2.14 (teardown retires a session's subscriptions without waiting; the retirement mark; ATTACK R4 on HTTP Part A, DECISIONS 2026-11-17 "HTTP Part A closure check")

v2.14 adds what docs/spec/http.md (Part A, 7f28160a; §2 "Publisher retirement", HTTP-15 rows 10, 14 and 14a, Decision row 36, law L9p) needs from this spec. Under the v2.12.1 text, a session tells the publisher it has ended by a message on `PubIn`: `Left` from a WebSocket session's end, or, in an HTTP draft, `Unsubscribed`. That message needs a `PubIn` slot. Either the session's end waits for one (a blocking `send`: under contention, nothing promises it wins one; CONC-10 promises no fairness), or it is dropped (a `try_send`: then the publisher keeps the subscription for good). An HTTP group must end without waiting (http.md "The release bound": "nothing for the publisher"). v2.14 fixes this with v2.13's pattern: state the session owns, which the publisher checks after every message it takes, plus a wake that is safe to drop. Text only; no model has a ledger or a session end, so no model was re-run. v2.13 is this file at 9c701ac2.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.14 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The mark | **ADOPTED (the ruling); representation decided here** | `Join` gains `mark: Option[Receiver[()]]`. A **marked** session makes a `channel.new(1)` of `()` when it joins, keeps the only `Sender` and puts the `Receiver` in its `Joined`. It never sends on the `Sender`, never clones it, and drops it only when it ends. **Who reads it:** only the publisher, by `try_receive` in the sweep (item 2). The `Receiver` moves to the publisher with the `Joined`, and the publisher keeps it in that session's `Joiner`. By CONC-9, `try_receive` on an empty channel returns `Empty` while the `Sender` lives and `Closed` once it is cleaned (tasks.md, `channel.try_receive`). So the mark is open exactly while the session's owner holds it. The sweep reads only `Closed` as the end. The `Ok` arm cannot happen, because no one sends on the mark, and the sweep makes no check for it (rule 11). **Unmarked sessions:** `mark` is `None`. A WebSocket session is unmarked, and its end stays `Left` (item 4). **No new `PubIn` kind and no frame change:** the mark rides in `Joined`. | http.md §2 "Subscription", "Publisher retirement" |
| 2 | The sweep | **ADOPTED; order fixed here** | After **every** message it takes (`Released`, `Joined`, `Subscribed`, `Unsubscribed`, `Left`, `Drained`), the publisher first sweeps, then runs v2.13's retry pass. The one exception is `Halt`, after which it returns. **The sweep:** for each joined marked session, in join order, one `try_receive` on its mark. On `Closed`, the publisher **retires** the session. It removes the session's subscriptions, and with them each one's Cover, read set (its Points and Spans, and their interval-index entries) and charge on the session's `budget` ledger. It drops the session's ledger (its waiting Endeds or Reset) and its waiting `Told`s, which go to no one (rule 5: a send whose session ends has no Outcome). It removes the `Joiner` and drops the mark's `Receiver`. That is everything the publisher holds for a session. It holds no subscription rows for a full inbox (v2.12.1 item 4), so http.md's "held content" is the ledger and the `Told`s. On `Empty`, nothing changes. **Why the sweep comes first:** the retry pass would only `try_send` to a retired session's closed inbox and get `Shut`. **A session that ended before its `Joined` was taken** is retired by the sweep that follows that `Joined`. **Late messages:** a `Subscribed` or `Unsubscribed` naming a session the publisher does not hold, retired or forgotten, changes nothing. A session id is minted once and never reused, so such a message can never reach a later session. | http.md §2 |
| 3 | The wake, and the end order | **ADOPTED (v2.13's `Drained`); the joiner's wake decided here** | A marked session ends in this order: (1) drop its inbox `Receiver` and the mark's `Sender`; (2) `try_send` one `Drained` on `PubIn`. Nothing waits. Taken, the `Drained` is a message, and a sweep follows it. Dropped by a full `PubIn`, it is safe to lose: a full `PubIn` holds a message the publisher will take, and a sweep follows that one. After `Halt`, a closed `PubIn` ignores it. **The order matters:** if the `Drained` went first, the publisher could take it and sweep while the mark is still open, and then nothing would follow. **A failure or a cancellation** ends the task that holds the mark before step 2 runs. Its cleanup still closes the mark, because dropping the `Sender` is mechanical release (FAIL-13). But the session sends no wake, so in a quiet place nothing would retire it. So **the joiner of a task that held a mark offers one `Drained` after its `join` returns, whatever the task's outcome.** CONC-8 has finished that task's cleanup by then, so the mark is closed before the wake. For HTTP, the joiner is the supervisor, which joins every group and connection task (http.md §2). On a normal end, this second `Drained` costs one free `PubIn` slot or nothing. One rule covers every end: after the mark closes, one `Drained` is offered by the first code that runs after the close, which is the session's own end code, or else its task's joiner. A finalizer cannot do it: it runs before the resource's fields drop, so it would offer the wake while the mark is still open (FAIL-11). **No fairness assumption:** no step needs a waiting sender to win a `PubIn` slot. | v2.13 item 2's argument; tasks.md CONC-10 |
| 4 | `Left`, `Unsubscribed`, `Halt`, `Drained` | **NARROWED (/wholehog: one end path per session, no fallback)** | **`Left`:** only an **unmarked** session's end. A marked session never sends `Left`. Its end is its mark, swept, so no session has two end paths. **`Unsubscribed`:** only a **live** session's owner sends it, for its client's `Unsubscribe` of one sub (v2.12 item 3's rule for a full inbox is unchanged). No session sends `Unsubscribed` as part of ending, marked or not. The HTTP draft's teardown `Unsubscribed` and its probe are deleted (http.md Decision row 36). A session's end retires all its subs at once, and the publisher drops a sub only by `Unsubscribed`, a Subscribe that replaces it, `Ended`, `Left` or the sweep. **`Halt`:** the publisher returns without a sweep. It holds nothing after it ends, so everything the sweep would release goes with its task. **`Drained`:** its meaning is unchanged. It carries nothing, and the work after it is the sweep and the retry pass. A joined marked owner keeps v2.13's run wake as well. | http.md Decision row 36; v2.12.1 item 2 |
| 5 | Charge | **STATED** | The mark is one `ch(1, ())`. The session makes it in its own scope and pays for it (http.md HTTP-15 row 10). The `Receiver` keeps its charge when it moves to the publisher, because a channel send never transfers a charge (MEM-20). When the session's task ends before the sweep, MEM-20's hand-over joins that record to the session's spawner (an HTTP group's supervisor, or a read's connection task). The sweep's drop of the `Receiver` refunds it there. What the sweep releases from the session's `budget` ledger (item 2) is the publisher's own `n_publisher` charge, as v2.11 item 1 says. **Lingering marks:** at most one per session the publisher holds (item 6), plus one per `Joined` still queued in `PubIn`, which is at most `PubIn`'s capacity (64 in the reference server). | budgets.md MEM-20; v2.11 item 1 |
| 6 | The bound | **STATED** | Let marked sessions be opened only by holders of slots from a pool of M, where each holder keeps its slot until its task ends (http.md: `streams_max` groups and `reads_max` reads, so M = `streams_max` + `reads_max`). **Then the publisher holds state for at most M + 1 marked sessions.** Right after any sweep, every marked session the publisher still holds had an open mark at that sweep. So its owner was alive and holding a slot: at most M of them. Before the next sweep, the publisher takes one message, and only a `Joined` adds a session: +1. Subscription state for those sessions is bounded per session by its `budget`, as it is for any session (§3). **Cost:** one `try_receive` per held marked session per message taken, which is at most M + 1 recorded events per message. Unmarked sessions cost the sweep nothing. | http.md §2 "So every ended group is retired" |
| 7 | Determinism | **STATED** | Each sweep `try_receive` and its answer is an event in CONC-10's one order, and so is the drop of a mark's `Sender`. So replay makes the same sweeps and gets the same answers, and it retires the same session after the same message. The sweep reads no clock. Its join-order iteration is fixed by the order in which the publisher took the `Joined`s. | tasks.md CONC-10; http.md §5 |
| 8 | Law | **NEW L25 `retired_without_waiting`** | §8, L25. Mutants: `retire_waits_for_slot` (the end `send`s its notice on `PubIn` and waits for a slot); `no_joiner_wake` (only the session's own end code offers a `Drained`, so a failed session in a quiet place is kept); `sweep_skipped_when_quiet` (the sweep runs only after a `Released`, so a quiet place keeps a dead session); `sweep_retires_live` (the sweep reads `Empty` as the end). The end order (item 3) is a rule, not a mutant. On the single-worker scheduler, a `try_send` is not a yield point, so a reversed order is drop-then-run in practice, and no case could tell it apart. **RUN** (built in 4b9aea33; all four mutants CAUGHT). http.md's L9p is the HTTP group's case of the same law. | — |
| 9 | Wire | **No change** | `Join` and `PubIn` are opaque and internal. No frame, tag or field changes. | — |

**Decision log (items 1 and 4).**
- **Candidates.** (a) A mark only on sessions that must end without waiting (HTTP groups and one-shot reads); WebSocket sessions keep `Left`. (b) Every session is marked, and `Left` is deleted. (c) The end is a message on `PubIn` (`Left` or `Unsubscribed`) sent by `try_send`, plus a retry. (d) A reserved `PubIn` slot per session for its end.
- **(c) is NOT TAKEN.** A dropped end message has nothing to retry it. The ending session is gone, and a retry from its spawner is a courier task (v2.13's (c)) with the same slot race.
- **(d) is NOT TAKEN,** for v2.13's (b) reason: `channel` has no reserved capacity.
- **(b) is NOT TAKEN, for its cost.** It is the /wholehog form: one end path for every session, with `Left` deleted. Its strongest case is exactly that "like things alike", and that a WebSocket session's end would stop waiting too. But the sweep is one recorded `try_receive` per marked session per publisher message (item 6). HTTP caps marked sessions at `streams_max` + `reads_max`. WebSocket sessions are capped only per source IP (§3's threat table), so (b) would make every publisher message cost O(open sessions) recorded events. A WebSocket session's `Left` is a `send`, and that wait is bounded: the publisher never waits on a session, so it is one drain. Nothing requires that end to be wait-free.
- **(a) is TAKEN.** Each session has exactly one end path, fixed when it joins: a mark, or `Left`. No session has both, and no fallback runs beside either one. The landed http.md text says the same (§2, "A WebSocket session needs no mark; its end stays `Left`").
- **Reversible:** until H6 or H8 builds it. If the sweep's cost turns out to be negligible (for example, a build keeps closed-mark notices O(ended) instead of scanning), (b) becomes the end state, and `Left` is deleted then.

**Prior art (v2.14).**
- **Erlang/OTP monitors** (`erlang:monitor/2`, the `'DOWN'` message; cited, not opened here): the runtime tells a watcher that a process ended, at most once and without the dying process sending anything. It is **ADAPTED**. Like a monitor, the end is observed, not announced, so a dying session never needs a slot. The weakness: OTP delivers `'DOWN'` into the watcher's unbounded mailbox. Our inboxes are bounded, so we observe a closed channel instead of queuing a notice. The cost is a scan (item 6) instead of a push.
- **Rust `std::sync::mpsc` / Tokio `Sender` drop** (tokio-1.54.1 src/sync/mpsc/chan.rs:242-243, `Tx::drop`, where the last sender's `tx_count` decrement closes the channel; error.rs:88, `TryRecvError::Disconnected`; opened): when the last sender is dropped, `try_recv` returns `Disconnected`. **ADOPTED unchanged** as the mark's representation. A one-slot channel that is never sent on is exactly a drop-observable token. Tokio's `Sender::closed()` (bounded.rs:862) waits for the other direction, so it is NOT TAKEN: the publisher must not wait on any one session.
- **Goose:** **NOT TAKEN.** Channels, cancellation and structured concurrency are libraries, and the language gives only the coroutine state machine (docs/design/coroutines.md:88-92, opened). So there is no end notice to adapt.

## CHANGES-v2.13 (a waiting Ended is delivered without other traffic; runner-build-1033's OPEN GAP, followups.md 2026-11-17 "L23 publisher_never_blocks as built")

(v2.20: a bounce no longer makes an `Ended`; the bounced sub is repaired, CHANGES-v2.20. The wake below is kept and also carries the owner's progress token.) v2.13 closes the liveness gap that runner-build-1033 found while building L23. The publisher only `try_send`s to an owner. A bounced content's subs end `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`, and that Ended waits on the session's ledger. The publisher retries a ledger only after it takes its next `PubIn` message. In a quiet place no message comes, so the client lacks the bounced rows and never gets the Ended. A waiting `Told` was already live, because its send's deadline answers it (`Abandoned`). Text only; no model has a ledger, so no model was re-run. v2.12.1 is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.12.1.md.

| # | Item | Decision | What v2.13 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The wake | **DECIDED here: the owner's run-length wake; reversible until R2 is on the wire** | `PubIn` gains a third internal kind, **`Drained`**, which carries nothing. A **joined** owner takes each event with `try_receive` first. It keeps a **run**: the number of events taken since it last found its inbox empty or last offered a wake. Finding the inbox empty sets the run to 0, and then the owner waits in `receive` as before. When the run reaches C, the owner's inbox capacity (1+q, §3), the owner `try_send`s one `Drained` to the publisher and sets the run to 0. It never waits: a full `PubIn` drops that `Drained`, and a closed one ignores it. The publisher does with `Drained` what it does after every message: it retries every waiting ledger and every waiting `Told`, oldest first (§1.1). An owner that has not joined keeps no run and sends nothing. An owner that keeps up finds its inbox empty before C takes and never offers. The head's inbox loop already has this shape (`try_receive`, then `receive`). | 1033's OPEN GAP; brief runner-spec-v213-1098 |
| 2 | Why it is live | **ARGUED** | A bounce means the owner's inbox held C events. The owner takes all C of them after the bounce, and it cannot find the inbox empty until it has, because other senders only add events. So the run is not reset by an empty inbox during those C takes, and it reaches C within them. The owner then offers a `Drained`, after the bounce and after it has made room. If `PubIn` takes it, the publisher retries the ledger. If `PubIn` is full, it holds at least one message that the publisher takes later, and the retry follows that message. Either way the retry comes after room was made. If the inbox has filled again by then, the retry bounces, and the same argument applies to the new C events. So the ledger is retried after every bounce until a retry finds room. The Ended is then taken. The owner hands it to the client ahead of the session's next content (item 3), and the client resubscribes (WIRE-SPEC D3). **Limit:** if the session's own children keep the inbox full (a client that sends without stopping, so the reader refills each slot as it frees), every retry can bounce. Then the client's own traffic delays its Ended, as it already delays its own V1 rows. Another session cannot cause this. | the owner's loop (§3, v2.8) |
| 3 | Per-session order | **UNCHANGED (v2.12.1 as built)** | A session's waiting ledger (its Ended list, or a Reset that covers them) goes before that session's next content. A ledger that still does not fit bounces that content too, and that content's subs join the ledger. A Subscribe of a sub drops that sub's waiting Ended, because the new subscription's Batch replaces it. So a client never sees content for a sub after that sub's Ended. | 1033 as built |
| 4 | The bound | **STATED** | Per joined session: at most one `Drained` offer per C consecutive takes without an empty inbox, so offers happen only while that owner is C events behind. Each offer is one `try_send`, never a wait, and holds at most one `PubIn` slot until the publisher takes it. The owner pays one `try_receive` decision per take, and a session that never joins pays nothing. The publisher's work per `Drained` is its existing retry pass after every message: one check per joined session (as built), and a send only for a session that owes something. A build may keep the set of owing sessions to make that pass O(owing). The publisher holds no new state: the ledger and the waiting `Told`s are v2.12.1's. **Cost:** while owners are behind, their offers share `PubIn`'s 64 slots with `Subscribed`. So under load a few more Subscribes get `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`, and the head's `send` of a Release may wait up to one drain longer. An owner that keeps up offers nothing. Calls-only sessions never join, so L3's hand-derived `TRY_SEND` counts do not move: neither `calls_runs_once` nor `calls_deadline_hung` subscribes. | from items 1-2 |
| 5 | Determinism | **STATED** | The wake depends on the count of takes, never on a clock. Each `try_send` and each take is already an event in the run's one total order (CONC-10), so a replay makes the same offers and gets the same answers. | docs/spec/tasks.md |
| 6 | Law | **NEW L24 `owed_ended_delivered`** | §8, L24. Mutants: `owed_ended_waits_for_traffic` (no `Drained`: the build as of v2.12.1) and `wake_once` (the owner offers one `Drained` per session, then never again). **UNTESTED** until runner-build-1033's `subs_main` harness has the case. | — |
| 7 | Wire | **No change** | `Drained` is internal to the opaque `PubIn`, like `Left` and `Halt`. No frame, tag or field changes. | — |

**Decision log (item 1).**
- **Candidates.** (a) The run-length wake (item 1). (b) A reserved control slot per session that carries the Ended, so it can never bounce. (c) A courier: on a bounce the publisher spawns a task that does a blocking `send` of the ledger, in the shape of Tokio's `reserve_owned` (tokio-1.54.1 src/sync/mpsc/bounded.rs:1265). (d) A timer that retries.
- **(b) is NOT TAKEN, because it cannot be built on `channel`.** `channel` has `new`, `send`, `try_send`, `receive` and `try_receive` (core/core/channel.hirsel). It has no reserved capacity and no select. The owner waits on one inbox (v2.8). A second, control channel is read only when the owner next wakes on the first one, so a quiet owner would never see it. A slot reserved inside the inbox needs a core change, a held permit as in Tokio's `try_reserve`. One permit covers only one bounce: once it is used, the publisher must re-reserve, and a full inbox leaves it no permit for the next bounce.
- **(c) is NOT TAKEN: more state than (a) for the same liveness.** It does have one advantage over (a): a blocked `send` waits in line with the reader, so the client's own flood cannot starve it. Its costs are a task and a spawn charge per overloaded session, a handoff of the ledger to the courier and back, and a cancel at `Halt`. The publisher also needs a third ledger state, "with the courier", because Endeds that arrive meanwhile must wait for the courier to finish.
- **(d) is NOT TAKEN.** A timer polls whether or not anything is owed. It adds a clock to the publisher, which waits on `PubIn` and nothing else (§1.1), and a period is a latency floor.
- **(a) is TAKEN.** It is the simplest of the four. The owner keeps one counter and makes one `try_send`; the publisher gets one message kind that does nothing new. It is bounded (item 4), and it is replay-deterministic (item 5).
- **/wholehog:** (a) is the clean end state. The publisher still retries at one point, after each message it takes, and (a) guarantees that such a message comes. No timer runs beside it as a fallback, and there is no second retry path. Every waiting item (Ended, Reset, Told) is made live the same way. I recommend (a). The strongest case for (c) is the fairness point above. It matters only against a session that floods itself, which is that session's own cost (item 2).
- **Reversible:** until R2 is on the wire. If the build needs another shape, runner-build-1033 says so in followups.md.

**Prior art (v2.13).**
- **TCP zero-window probe** (RFC 9293 §3.8.6.1; cited, not opened here): when the receiver's window is zero, the sender holds the data and a persist timer probes until a window update arrives. It is ADAPTED: like TCP, the side that made room (here the owner) tells the waiting side (the publisher). The win over TCP is that no timer is needed. In TCP a window update can be lost, which is why the persist timer exists. Here a `Drained` is dropped only when `PubIn` is non-empty, and then the next message makes the same retry.
- **Tokio mpsc** (bounded.rs:924 `try_send`, :1116 `reserve`, :1265 `reserve_owned`; opened): a waiting send, or a permit, is woken by its channel's waker when a slot frees. NOT TAKEN, as (b) and (c) above. Waking a waiter needs either a permit or a task that waits, and the publisher must never wait.
- **Goose**: NOT TAKEN. It has no bounded-channel wake; channels are a library over a `Wait` (docs/design/coroutines.md:88, :961).

## CHANGES-v2.12.1 (runner-build-1033's built R2 wiring, followups.md 2026-11-17 "R2 wiring as built"; the orchestrator ruled each point)

v2.12.1 replaces v2.12's §1.1 wiring with the built wiring, as the orchestrator ruled. CHANGES-v2.12 items 1 and 3 are superseded where they differ. Text only; no model re-run. v2.12 is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.12.md.

| # | Item | Ruling | What v2.12.1 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The signatures | **AS BUILT** | The publisher's inbox is a `Mailbox`: `mailbox(n) -> Result[Mailbox, Capacity]`, and `post(mail) -> Sender[PubIn]` makes a sender to it. `place_head` takes `take mail: Mailbox` in place of a receiver and a sender. `connect` takes `to_publisher: Sender[PubIn]` and no session number. | 1033's built signatures |
| 2 | Two internal kinds | **RULED** | Inside the opaque `PubIn` there are two more kinds. `Left`: a session ended, so the publisher drops its subs. `Halt`: the head stopped serving, so the publisher stops and never outlives its head. Neither kind can be named outside the runner. | orchestrator |
| 3 | When a session joins | **RULED: at its first Subscribe** | A session sends `Joined` and gets its publisher id at its first Subscribe, not at connect. A session that never subscribes holds no publisher subscription state. | orchestrator |
| 4 | The publisher never blocks on a session | **RULED; law `publisher_never_blocks`** | Every publisher send to a session is a `try_send`. If the owner's inbox is closed, the publisher drops that session's subs. If it is full, (v2.20) each live sub whose content did not fit stays live and is marked due for repair, with no `Ended` (CHANGES-v2.20, §3 "Repair"). Ended entries, a pending `Reset` and any `Told` stay on the session's ledger until the inbox takes them. The publisher holds no subscription rows for a full inbox. Not built yet: the built publisher still waits on a full session (1033's known R2-a gap). | orchestrator; W-D3 |

## CHANGES-v2.12 (as built at origin/main e2534995; the orchestrator's PubIn ruling, followups.md 2026-11-17)

v2.12 writes R2-g, R2-h and R2-i as they landed. It does not re-decide them. It also adds the one-inbox publisher that the orchestrator ruled on 2026-11-17 (option (a), runner-build-1033's SPEC GAP). Text only: no model behaviour changed, so no model was re-run. v2.11.4 is RUNNER-SPEC-v2.11.4.md.

| # | Item | Status | What v2.12 says | Basis |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | The publisher's inputs | **RULED (a); signature written here** | The publisher has **one inbox**, `PubIn = Released(Release) \| Joined(Join) \| Subscribed(session, sub, key, arg) \| Unsubscribed(session, sub)`. Nothing else feeds it. The head sends `Released`, and each session sends the other three on its `Sender[PubIn]`. `place_head` takes the inbox's receiver, plus a sender for the head's own `Released`, in place of `sessions: Receiver[Join]`. `connect` takes a `Sender[PubIn]` and a session number (§1.1). | followups.md 2026-11-17 RULED (orchestrator) |
