  | DecodeError | Truncated | Invalid | Utf8 | Duplicate | Trailing | Schema | Value |

  `Bad.at` is the error's byte offset. A retired number is never reused: 9 named `Tape`, which left the language when recording moved outside the program (docs/spec/capabilities.md CAP-9). A kind added to core or std later takes the next unused number, whatever its declaration position. A client reads a number it does not know as a fault, or a decode error, of an unknown kind. That is why these are numbers and not typed mirrors: a new variant in a typed enum would make an old client fail to decode the whole Batch.
- **The store's `info` is `info(mut tx: Tx) -> CallInfo`, its only signature** (STORE-SPEC G2). No `info(Tx)` overload exists, so a query (view Tx, inside `look`) cannot obtain CallInfo.

### 1.2 What each one does

| Declaration | Allocates | Waits | Records | On main |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `registry`, `compute`, `granted`, `describe` | slot closure, map row, Described: 495 B per entry at 100 (RUN R0, bf7cf64f; probe p19 at 62f15988: 420–460 B) | no | nothing | RUN p13, p02, p09, p19 |
| any register function, key already present | nothing | no | nothing: `Error(AlreadyRegistered(key))`, registry unchanged | RUN p13 |
| `transact_recovered`, `transact_unrecovered`, `transact_via_recovered`, `transact_via_unrecovered` (v2.94) | as `compute` | no | nothing | RUN p04, p08, p16 as `transact`; the four names are **PLANNED** with the implementation |
| `query`, `query_as`, `slot`, `cover` | as `compute` | no | nothing | RUN p16; the rest needs S1.1 (`look`) |
| `call` | inside `bound bytes n` only | no | nothing | RUN p01 |
| `start` / `finish` | task stack + `bytes n` | `finish` | nothing | RUN p02 |
| `place_head` | one H, shared with the publisher (v2.45), the gone-key set (§6), one worker record per stream in flight, at most each connection's outstanding call credit in requests (§3 "Call credit") | `schema`, activation, `durable` once per group | admit, then effect + reply (+ record, R5) in one commit | needs S5 |
| `publisher` (its `PubIn` inbox, v2.12) | per view (v2.45): its CoverSet, read set and last value bytes; per subscription: its charge of them (Points included), never a copy of covered rows (v2.11, L20); `Told`s and `Ended`s waiting for a full inbox, never subscription content (v2.12.1); all on its `budget` ledger (§3); a marked session's mark `Receiver`, until the sweep retires it (v2.14) | channel; the sweep's `try_receive`s never wait | nothing | needs S1.1 |

**Reply recovery (v2.94; Sam's ruling 2026-11-28 ~17:45Z).** Each transact registration chooses its reply recovery, and there is no default. The kind is `Transact` either way. The body, its `Tx`, the Caller, rollback on `Error`, durable-before-reply (L61) and the entry's place under `api.call` are the same for both.
- **`Recovered`** (`transact_recovered`, `transact_via_recovered`). The call takes a stream position. The head admits it with its request digest, and the reply bytes commit with the effect at that position (§2 H1–H4; STORE-SPEC §1.8). A resend of the same (stream, p) with the same digest is answered with the recorded reply bytes and runs nothing. A different digest is refused (`RequestMismatch`). Store retention bounds how long that answer is kept, as operator policy (STORE-SPEC §1.8 "Retention").
- **`Unrecovered`** (`transact_unrecovered`, `transact_via_unrecovered`). The call takes no stream position. Store writes no admission marker and no recovery row for it. The head runs it as one isolated transaction and releases its reply at the durable version, like any other (§2 H5). Its wire reference is connection-local (§3 "Frames"). It uses no recovery stream, and it spends one call credit and its bytes. Once dispatch may have happened, the SDK settles a lost reply, a timeout or an abort as `LocallyUnknown`. Nothing resends it on reconnect or restart (docs/spec/sdk.md). No declaration marks an unrecovered entry safe to retry. A fresh explicit call is a new call and may repeat the effect.
- **Identity.** `recovery` is in `Described`, the exported contract, the release digest (§5 "One identity") and the entry key, which is identity ‖ fp(A) ‖ fp(Result[R, E]) ‖ the recovery tag. Changing an entry's recovery changes its key, so a Want from before the change gets `Placed(Gone)` before any call is dispatched. A pending recovered call under the old key follows the gone-key rules (§6) and is never dispatched as unrecovered. There is one wire and manifest revision for this, with no compatibility decoding and no client, server or global switch.
- **HTTP.** An unrecovered entry takes no `Stream` or `Keyed` binding, because both promise recovery. `bind` refuses one with a `BindError` at registration (http.md, binding check 2).
- **Recording.** An unrecovered call writes no `CallRecord`. A recovered call still commits its record with its reply, as H2 and §7 say today. Removing per-call recording from the serving path for both forms is lane 3523's pending change, and so is §7's text. This ruling changes no Store format.

The budget `n` is set per entry by the place at registration, never by the client or the caller (v2.4). Exhaustion after entry gives `Failed(OutOfBytes)`. A budget the place cannot grant gives `Refused(Budget)`, before forwarding.

### 1.3 Outcomes

| Outcome | Meaning | Recorded | Caller action |
|---|---|---|---|
| `Returned(b)` | the entry's answer; for transact, `b` is `Result[R, E]` | effect (unless `Error`) and reply in one commit | final; the same p returns the same bytes |
| `Failed(f)` | a fault or byte exhaustion after entry, `Gone`, or (v2.45) `Conflict(index)`: a transact body returned `Ok` after a unique conflict, and its data writes were discarded | reply-only commit at p | final |
| `Refused(r)` | nothing recorded. `NoEntry`: no entry of the kind asked for under this key, which includes a key registered as another kind (a granted key passed to `call`, a compute key to `start`). Rejected at the session, or (`Overloaded` only) the store refused the commit and the body's effects rolled back | nothing | `Overloaded`, `Deadline`, `Budget`: retry the same p. `RequestMismatch` (v2.11.3/4): p is bound to other bytes, so never re-send these bytes at p. `HistoryUnavailable` (v2.11.4): p is below the stream's latest, and the store cannot say whether it ran these bytes; terminal for this request only, the client retires the stream (W4c). Others: fix the call |
| `Abandoned` | **changed in v2.2: crosses the wire.** This send's deadline passed while it was owed (v2.5: each send has its own deadline). p may still run; its later answer goes only to sends of p still owed then. | whatever finishes | re-send p: the session forwards it again only after the head's first answer, then Duplicate returns the reply, or p runs once |
| `InDoubt` | granted entries only: an outside operation in provider state I (REPLAY-SPEC §3) | — | observe; never retry blindly |

`Refused(Deadline)` is now only for a request that had already expired when it arrived; it was never forwarded.

`Refused(StreamFuture(at))` records nothing. A caller can use `at` to correct the timestamp of an unused stream before retrying.

**The reply bound (v2.11, F12).** A `Returned(b)` row is encoded as `Done{stream, p, outcome}`: 32 B (stream time 8, stream nonce 8, position 8, Outcome tag 4, Bytes count 4) plus |b|. A reply with |b| > `DONE_MAX − 32` is never sent. It is answered `Failed(OutOfBytes(Room{requested: |b|, available: DONE_MAX − 32}))` instead:
- **transact:** the check is inside the entry's transaction, before commit. The transaction aborts with no effect, and the head commits the `Failed` reply-only, as for any `Failed` (H2). On the reply-kept-alone path (`Error(E)`), the over-bound `Error` reply is replaced by the same `Failed`, which is kept alone.
- **compute and granted:** the owner (compute) or the entry's task (granted) checks the encoded reply; nothing is recorded.
- A recorded reply never exceeds `DONE_MAX`, and the same limit applies after reopen. A `Duplicate(r)` therefore fits.

**Sends and their Outcomes (v2.5).** Outcome rows carry `(stream, p)`, not a send identity, so the client counts: it holds n open sends of `(stream, p)`, and each row for `(stream, p)` closes one. Since every send of one position is the same request, rows for that position are interchangeable, except `Abandoned`, which the client matches to the send whose deadline it set to pass first. Example: send A (deadline 2 ms) and send B (50 ms) of p on a held head. A gets `Abandoned` at 2 ms. If the head answers at 10 ms, B gets that answer. If it answers at 60 ms, B got `Abandoned` at 50 ms and the answer goes to no one.

**Positions (v2.71).** A stream position is a call the head admits: a recovered transact call (v2.94), or a call on a gone slot (§6). A compute or granted call carries `(stream, p)` only to label its Outcome row. The session never calls `admit` for it, nothing is recorded, and the stream's latest position does not move, so the client sends the next stored call at the same `p` (STORE-SPEC §1.8 P1). S7 is unchanged.

### 1.4 What an entry body may do with the store

- A transact body reads and writes through `h.t` (one `Table`, v2.45) and reads through an Index. A unique `Conflict` is mapped to a domain error with `?=>`; returning `Ok` after one is `Failed(Conflict(name))`.
- **A body that updates rows it looked up collects them first, then writes.** `IndexRows` is scoped to the Tx, and the Tx cannot be written while it lives (STORE-SPEC J1, OWN-10).
- A query reads only. It may cover any span of a table that one of its slots names.
- **Access is the body's own code (v2.19, ruling C).** The store does no authorization. A body that must refuse a Caller reads its own tables, for example a membership row, and returns its own error, or a `query_as` body returns an empty CoverSet (v2.25: only a `query_as` body has the Caller). Those reads are ordinary. In a transact body they are part of its transaction. In a query they are in the subscription's read set, so a change to them re-runs the query (G2) and fences the session's queued content (§3 "The release check").
- **Tx cursors (v2.47).** `range`, `range_prefix` and `log_range` return a `TxRows`, which the body steps with `store.next_in(tx, c)` (STORE-SPEC v2.59 §13.4). A Cold miss there latches the attempt, and the store reruns the body (§13.3).
- A store error inside a query (`Stale` from a handle older than a drop, which rule 12 makes impossible within an open) has no case in the query's result type. The body faults or maps it, and that subscription gets `Ended`.

## 2. Placements and the call protocol

**P1, same run.**
- A compute entry runs in the session's owner task (v2.8) under `bound` + `isolate` (RUN R0 at bf7cf64f: 2,754.5 instr per call against 312 direct; v2.2's probe figure 1,593 encoded outside the bound).
- A granted entry runs as a task (`bytes n`).
- Compute never runs in the head. A compute body that never returns runs under its step bound `s`: other tasks run at its slice ends, and it is answered `Failed(OutOfTime(s))` at step `s` (D5, v2.7).

**P2, a transact call at a place.** The head task owns the `Db` (own, not send) and its Registry and H. A session validates a request, then sends `Req{stream, p, key, arg, reply_to}`. The head keeps, per stream, `Option[Worker{p, waiters}]` and a FIFO of waiting requests.

- **H1. One worker per stream.** A request for the worker's position joins its waiters **if its request digest equals the worker's** (v2.11.3). Otherwise it is answered `Refused(RequestMismatch)` at once as V3, and it never runs. A request for another position waits in the FIFO. When the worker ends, the next waiting request starts.
- **H2. Start.** The head calls `admit(stream, p)`:
  - `Admitted(c)` or `Abandoned(c)`, where `c` is the position's `Completion` (an abandoned position from a crashed open gets one too): call `store.serve(mut db, take c, plan, record, body)` (STORE v2.29 AM1). `plan` holds the entry's byte and step allowances; `record` is the position's `CallRecord` (R5). `serve` runs the body as one isolated execution over the head's snapshot (v2.19). The reply is written inside the body's bound, and the record and the reply commit together. Its result:
    - `Committed(Receipt)`: the effect, the reply and the record share a commit. The `Receipt` (STORE AM3) gives the head the reply for the first `Told`, with no read-back and no duplicate mark.
    - `Kept(Receipt)`: the body returned `Error(E)`; its writes rolled back, and the reply and record are kept alone in one streams-only commit.
    - `Failed(p, stop)`: the body raised, a bound stopped it, or the reply passed `plan.reply_max`. The head calls `store.conclude(mut db, take p, fault)` with the fixed fault's bytes: `Failed(Raised(n))`, `Failed(OutOfTime(s))` or `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))`. `conclude` writes them with the Pending's record and its CallInfo (STORE v2.29 AM1, AM6), runs no code, and returns a `Receipt` (AM3).
    - `NoRoom(p, c)` (v2.18 B4; ATTACK-4 B7): the store refused the commit before its publish point (resident limit), and `c` is that admission's `Capacity{requested, available}`. Nothing is stored. A stream position drops `p`: the Completion goes with it, and the stream stays `Admitted(p)`, answered `Refused(Overloaded)` as V3. A keyed claim on its third resident refusal (http.md K-8) calls `store.conclude(mut db, take p, Failed(OutOfBytes(Room{requested: c.requested, available: c.available})))` from the claim's reserve, once. The body does not run again. A store `Error` at or after the publish point poisons the head (§2.1 Poison; STORE AM4; ATTACK-4 m1), and nothing is answered before the exit.
    - The head does not wait for the admit ticket: the journal is prefix-ordered, so a durable reply implies a durable admit.
  - `Duplicate(Stored{reply})` (v2.19): the head answers the stored reply unchanged, as it was committed (ruling 73 as amended). Under H1 and H3, any reply the head can see at the stream's latest position is durable.
  - `Rejected(l)`, p > l: the request waits in the FIFO and is retried when a request arrives or a worker ends. The request holds its live session slot until answered. The head retires it when the session departs, before admitting replacement work.
  - `HistoryUnavailable` (p < latest) or `RequestMismatch` (p = latest, another digest): `Refused(HistoryUnavailable)` or `Refused(RequestMismatch)`, as V3; nothing is committed and nothing runs (STORE AH1, AH2; v2.11.4).
  - `StreamFuture(at)`: `Refused(StreamFuture(at))`, as V3. Nothing is committed and nothing runs.
  - A gone key (§6): `Admitted(c)`/`Abandoned(c)` gives `store.conclude(mut db, store.untried(take c, None), Failed(Gone))`; `Duplicate(Stored)` answers it.
- **H3. Gate.** After each group, the head calls `durable` on the group's newest ticket. It collects every worker answer in commit order and pushes one `Release{version: d, snapshot, outcomes}` for the group. Here d is the durable version the group reached. (v2.46) The head may first push `Staged(v, snapshot)` for the group's commit version v, before it calls `durable`; it carries no outcomes and releases nothing (G1 "Pipelining"). (v2.91) A head woken by its store's sync notices may wait for the sync on its inbox instead of in `durable`, taking later work meanwhile. The head closes the group when it finds the inbox empty. The Directory writer submits ready work without a gather deadline.
- **H4. Across opens.** An admitted-unreplied position from a crashed open returns `Abandoned` and re-runs. `p+1` arriving first gets `Rejected(p)` from S7 and waits until p is re-sent.
- **H5. Unrecovered calls (v2.94).** A session sends an unrecovered call as `Req` with its connection-local id in place of (stream, p). The head neither admits it nor checks a digest. It runs the body with `store.run` under the entry's byte and step bounds, as one isolated execution in the current group. The reply is encoded and checked against `DONE_MAX − 32` inside that transaction, before commit. An oversized reply aborts it with no effect and is answered `Failed(OutOfBytes(Room))` (L18's rule, with nothing committed). `info(mut tx)` gives the CallInfo of the commit the run makes (STORE-SPEC §1.7), and with no admission frame that is the only one. A `LocalItem` for a gone key gets `Failed(Gone)` from the session with nothing committed, because there is no position to conclude (§6). A `LocalItem` whose id equals an unanswered local id on its connection closes the connection with 1008. A commit's reply is released by H3 at the group's durable version, in commit order, with the group's other outcomes. An `Error` rolls back, a fault or bound stop commits nothing, and a resident refusal is `Refused(Overloaded)` (V3). Each of these is released in the same group's order, so no unrecovered reply overtakes a commit it may have observed. A store `Error` at or after the publish point poisons the head, as in H2. After a crash, nothing about the call remains apart from its committed effect, if it had one.
- **Throughput.** H1 serialises only within a stream. Different streams interleave inside one group commit.

### 2.1 Delivery of every Outcome (V1–V5, new in v2.2)

| Rule | Who decides | Outcome | Delivered how | Frame version |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **V1** | the session | `Refused(Malformed)`, `Refused(NoEntry)`, `Refused(Budget)`; `Refused(Deadline)` when the request had expired on arrival; **`Abandoned` when a send's own deadline timer fires** while that send is owed (one timer per send, v2.5) | at once, in a Batch with no rows | the session's last sent version. These report no commit, so rule 9's first clause does not apply. |
| **V2** | the session | the head's answer, once per owed send | A session forwards a position at most once while the head holds it; a later send of it with an **equal request digest** is a local join, owed separately. One with a different digest ends the session with `Close` (1008), a protocol error, because rows identify only `(stream, p)` (v2.11.3). Every send holds its own call credit until its row is `Sent`. The send that forwarded a position also holds its credit until the head's answer arrives, even after `Abandoned` (§3 "Call credit", v2.94). When the head's answer arrives, the session sends one row per owed send. With no owed send it sends nothing. | as the head's answer |
| **V3** | the head | `Duplicate(Stored)`, `Refused(RequestMismatch)`, `Refused(HistoryUnavailable)`, `Refused(StreamFuture(at))`, store `Refused` → `Refused(Overloaded)`, and K-5's refusals | in a **snapshot-less Release** on the publisher's one inbox (`Released`, v2.12). It carries, inside the runner, `seen`: the newest commit the head had made when it decided (v2.19.1). The head pushes it right after the Release of the group that holds `seen`. If that group is still open, the head ends it there (H3: `durable` on its newest ticket, then its Release) and then pushes the answer. It never waits on a running worker or a later commit. The publisher sends it in a Batch at its current base, after the round of every snapshot `Released` it took before this one. It waits for no later round. | the publisher's base, which is ≥ `seen` (below) |
| **V4** | the head | everything a commit produced | a Release pushed only after `durable`, carrying the group's durable version d ≥ the commit version | d. Releases therefore leave the head in version order, whatever order workers finish in. |
| **V5** | the head | `Failed(Gone)` | a gone key is forwarded by the session, admitted and replied in a reply-only commit, then delivered as V4 | as V4 |

**Why a commitless answer cannot overtake the commit it reports.**
- **Duplicate(Stored).** The stored reply's commit was released before the worker that wrote it ended (H3). A Duplicate is decided after that, so its Release sits behind that commit's Release in the FIFO. When the publisher sends it, its base is ≥ that commit's version.
- **`RequestMismatch` and `HistoryUnavailable`** report no commit (v2.11.4).
- **After a reopen,** the publisher's first base is the durable version at open, which is ≥ every reply the store holds.
- **Store `Refused`** reports no commit.
- **Every V3 answer, by `seen` (v2.19.1, ATTACK M2).** An answer can reveal state that a commit made: a `RequestMismatch` another digest's admission, a `HistoryUnavailable` a later position, a K-5 quota refusal another claim's admission. The head decides each after every commit up to `seen` and pushes it behind `seen`'s Release, so the publisher offers it after the effective point of every commit it could reveal (§3, "Why the check is ordered"). Ending a group early only shortens it: it holds commits already made, and `durable` on them is the wait H3 takes anyway. Session-local answers (V1: a timer, malformed input) read no store state and need no `seen`.

**What v2.1 did wrong, and what the model found.**
- v2.1 let a commitless outcome ride "the next Release". The review showed that schedule has no next Release when a body hangs. V1 answers the deadline at the session, and V3 sends the head's commitless answers at once.
- **The first v2.2 run then found a real bug:** a Release carrying its own commit version went backwards with two streams ("publisher version went back 2, 4"). V4's group durable version fixes it. Mutant `release_at_commit_version` is CAUGHT.

**The timer is the session's.** It answers a request the head holds (in its FIFO, behind `durable`, behind a slow body that yields). A body that never returns is D5's case, not V1's: its slice ends let the timers run, and its step bound ends it (v2.7, L7/deadline_hung). The head may drop a waiting request whose deadline has passed; that is an optimisation and is not modelled.

**Poison.** If `durable` returns an error, the head answers nothing and the process exits. Clients re-send after reconnecting. Mutant `poison_answers` is CAUGHT.

**Poison and fence (v2.34, STORE v2.47 §12.3).** At open the head registers `store.on_unhealthy(db, inbox sender, StoreUnhealthy)`, so the store's message arrives in its own inbox like any other. When the health leaves `Ok` (`Fenced(e)`, `Undecided`, `Poisoned`, `Remote`), whether or not a call is in flight, the head ends abnormally exactly as for a failed `durable`: it answers nothing more, terminates outstanding run and session work, releases no reply, batch, outgoing call, wake or Position that is not already durable, sends no clean report (§14.4), and the process exits 1. On an idle place this is how a fence reaches the runner: the store's idle head poll sets the health, and its message wakes the head's ordinary receive.

**Liveness premise.** A client re-sends every position it has no final answer for (after `Abandoned`, `Overloaded`, or a reconnect). A client that gives up on p leaves p+1 waiting until its own deadline.

**P3, another process.** The session speaks §3's frames. One pending stored call per stream is the store's rule (S7). How many streams a connection runs at once is set by its call credit (§3 "Call credit"). H1–H4 and V1–V5 hold for any client.

## 3. Sessions, principals and frames

- **Session (v2.8, gavel 11):** one owned task group per connection.
  - **Owner** task: it waits only on its event inbox, which has capacity `C` = 5, a fixed constant (v2.94: no call limit sets it). It also joins a writer child once the child has sent its last event, so that join ends as the child exits (v2.44).
    - Its events are: client frames from the reader; Batches from the publisher (by `try_send`; the publisher never waits on a session); `Sent` and `Ended` from the children; and deadline expiries.
    - It decides every protocol step: V1, call credit, owed sends, and the control frames first in its outgoing queue.
    - It writes the next frame itself, only after `Sent` or `Dropped`, and never waits on the socket: it tries each send once, and a send that would block goes to the writer child (v2.44).
  - **Reader** child: it owns the `ReadHalf` and holds no receive buffer while idle. It waits for readiness with no buffer, checks out an 8192-byte loan charged to itself for one receive (`std.wsock.refill`), and sends decoded frames to the inbox. The shared allocation task parses the loan and decodes application frames before returning both by move. Before it waits for read credit, the reader settles: a loan with unread read-ahead becomes storage of exactly the unread tail, charged to the reader, and a loan with nothing unread is released. When the inbox is full it waits, which stops reading the socket and pushes back on the client. Calls are bounded by call credit before they reach the inbox (§3 "Call credit"), so a full inbox only delays reading.
  - **Writer** (v2.19, v2.44): the owner owns the `WriteHalf` and the pending byte cursor, and holds a handle to the place's round register and one to its session's fence root (§3 "The release check"). Before a unit's first byte, it applies the release check. If the unit passes, the owner stages it whole and writes what the socket takes without waiting. If the rest would block, it spawns the **writer child** with the Writer and that frame's deadline; the child writes the rest, returns the Writer with `Sent` (or `WriteClosed` for a Close frame) and exits, and the owner joins it on that event. A unit it has accepted is finished, whatever commits after. If the unit fails, nothing is written and the owner takes `Dropped` in place of `Sent`. A unit returns to its owner only at `Sent` or `Dropped`. Between that event and the next unit the session holds no unit. The owner sends pending `Unsubscribed` messages there before it sends deferred successors to the publisher (§3, "Ended entries").
  - **The owner on `Dropped` (v2.19, v2.19.1, v2.19.2):** it drops every content component it holds: groups (value-only groups included, v2.46), `complete`s and publisher-made `Ended`s, the dropped unit's included. It keeps every Outcome row, the dropped unit's included, and builds one `Reset` that carries them all (§4 "Reset"). It ends every live instance and records the last instance it numbered as **retired** (v2.19.2). Every later `Subscribed` of the session carries that number, and the publisher ends every subscription of the session at or below it before it registers the new one, so a resubscription is never charged on top of the old instances and waits on nothing. Until a `Subscribed` has carried it, the owner also offers `Retire(session, retired)` by `try_send` after each event it takes, every arrival of a retired instance's content included. A bounced `Retire` is offered again at the next event, and nothing waits on it. Content of a retired instance that arrives later is discarded (§3, "Subscription instances"). A `Reset` made this way never counts toward the protocol-error close (1008). The client resubscribes, and each sub completes afresh at a version at or above the fence (§4 "Resume").
  - **Ending:** the reader ending (end of stream, a reset), a write error, or a writer child the owner cannot spawn (`Capacity`) reports `Ended` and ends the session; the last two end with 1001 and no Close frame, because part of a frame may already be on the wire (v2.44). To end, the owner cancels the reader and any writer child, then joins them. If the owner is itself cancelled, CONC-8 cleans the two handles one at a time; that is bounded, because the children's waits are unshielded.
  - The owner holds:
    - the principal and its stream keys;
    - its call credit counters G, A and R, and each send's credit (§3 "Call credit"). Every send holds its credit from arrival until its Outcome row is `Sent` or the session ends. The send that forwarded a position also holds its credit until the head answers (v2.94);
    - each V1 refusal row, inside its send's credit (v2.94);
    - the deadline timers, which report to the inbox;
    - a sender to the head (its requests), a sender to the publisher's inbox (`Joined`, `Subscribed`, `Unsubscribed`; v2.12), and its own event inbox, where the publisher's content arrives;
    - per Hello entry, whether the serving task's one Registry has its key or lists it as gone (v2.43; the session holds no Registry, rule 10);
    - its subscriptions, which the publisher holds and charges to it.
      - A Subscribe the owner cannot `try_send` because the inbox is full is answered `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`, and nothing is registered.
      - An Unsubscribe that meets a full inbox is applied at the owner, which drops that sub's rows. The publisher keeps the subscription, charged to the session, until a Subscribe of the same sub replaces it or the session ends (v2.12). `Unsubscribed` is only ever this: a live session's one sub. A session's end is `Left` or its mark, never `Unsubscribed` (v2.14).
    - if it is marked (v2.14), the only `Sender` of its retirement mark, paid in its own scope. It drops it, with its inbox `Receiver`, before its ending `Drained`. Its task's joiner offers another `Drained` after `join` (CHANGES-v2.14 item 3).
    - once joined, a run: the events it has taken since it last found its inbox empty or last offered a wake. When the run reaches the inbox's capacity, it offers the publisher one `Drained` by `try_send` (v2.13, §1.1).
- **No authentication in the runner (v2.24, rule 13).** The runner reads no credential. It issues no principal and checks no token, signature or password. Authentication is the application's binding, and authorization is the application's entry bodies.
- **Binding.** A Hello carries `credential: Option[Bytes]`, and an HTTP request carries its `Authorization` value. The binding receives that credential, or empty bytes if absent, and runs once inside `bind_bytes` and `bind_steps`. (v2.70) Each bind first asks the place's `bind` mint for a fresh `G`, and the binding sees a view of it. The `G` is dropped when the bind ends. A changing key set reaches the binding as a `Latest` handle in `G`, read once per bind, and a sealed key as a `secret.share` holder (§15.1). A failed bind mint is not a credential failure: Close 1013, or HTTP 503 or B-7's 500 (§12.1 "Mint tasks"). A `Wire` with no `bind` mint refuses the open with `BindNoGrant`. The runner bounds the application binding and trusts its answer. An `Ok(BoundCaller)` names the Caller and sets its optional exclusive wall-clock expiry. `Rejected`, a fault, an exhausted bound or an already expired result gives WebSocket Close 1008 or HTTP 401. `Unavailable` gives Close 1013 or HTTP 503 with `Retry-After: 5`. A refused bind records nothing. An established session latches expiry on equality, checks it at admission and before each new application frame, and arms a cancellable `std.clock.wall_alarm` while idle. Expiry closes the session with 4401 `AuthExpired` (v2.57).
- **Anonymous (v2.24).** With no binding, every session gets a fresh anonymous Caller: issuer 1, with a 16-byte subject drawn from `Random` at Hello, so it is unguessable and no two sessions share one. Saved replies are keyed by Caller (L32), so they never cross sessions, and a reconnect starts a new Caller with new stream keys: dedup across reconnects needs an application binding. Each HTTP request without a binding is its own anonymous session. A credential sent to a place with no binding is ignored.
- **Caller (v2.15, v2.24).** Ingress binds the `Caller` once, at Hello or at the HTTP request. A Caller is `{issuer, subject}`, not secret, with `subject` at most `SUBJECT_MAX` = 64 B. Its key is `caller_key(c) = u8 issuer ‖ u8 len(subject) ‖ subject`, so no key prefixes another. Issuer numbers form a fixed table, and a number is never reused. Only 1, anonymous, is assigned. For the anonymous issuer, the subject is 16 random bytes (v2.24; the v2.15 derivation from a token is deleted). A binding chooses its own issuer number from the fixed table. The session keeps the Caller, never the credential, after Welcome. Its `Join` carries the Caller, and every `Req` carries it to the head.
- **The release check (v2.19, v2.19.1; ruling 73 as amended: "Queued subscription data is re-checked against current state before it is sent").**
  - **The fence.** When the owner joins the publisher, it makes its session's fence root, a `Latest[Fence]` holding `Fence{prior: 0, latest: 0}` (CONC-13). It keeps one handle for its writer and gives one to the publisher in its `Joined`. The fence's `latest` is the newest commit that hit the read set of any subscription of the session,, an ended entry's included (§3, "Ended entries", below). Its `prior` is the value `latest` had before that.
  - **The round register.** `mailbox(n)` makes the place's round root, a `Latest[u64]` holding 0. The publisher publishes its base version there at start and at the end of each round's fence step (G2 step 3). The publisher answers each `Joined` with `Admit{who, round}` (v2.19.3), so the session's writer gets its handle with the session's id. The register's value R is the newest version whose fences are all published. `u64::MAX` is reserved for the deploy halt.
  - **Which units are checked.** A **content unit** is a frame or SSE event that carries a group, a `complete`, an `Ended` the publisher made, or a B-11 response head with its body. Each **content component** (one sub's group, whether it carries rows, a value or both, a value-only group of a computed-only query included; a `complete`; a publisher-made `Ended`) carries, inside the runner, the version it was computed at and its subscription's instance (below). The wire form carries neither. (v2.46, astra 1995 #2) A group is content whatever it carries: the instance check, the oldest-component fence, retirement, supersession and `Dropped` apply to every group on every transport, and its Outcomes survive a drop as every Outcome does. A unit's **version** is the **oldest** version among its content components (v2.19.1, ATTACK B2), so a newer component never certifies an older one. Every other unit passes with no load: a `Batch` or `Reset` that carries only Outcome rows, a V1 refusal, an `Ended` the owner made itself, a `Close`, and an HTTP call response. A saved reply is delivered as it was committed.
  - **The check.** Before a content unit's first byte, the owner loads R, and then its fence `{prior, latest}`. It copies both values and releases both shares before it writes. If R is `u64::MAX`, the unit is dropped. Otherwise the effective fence is `latest` if `latest ≤ R`, otherwise `prior`, and the unit passes if the effective fence is ≤ its version. A unit that does not pass is dropped.
  - **The publisher's order: one barrier per round (v2.45).** In round b, the publisher computes every hit view (G2 step 2), ended entries included, before it publishes anything. It forms the set of fenced sessions as the union of the subscriber sessions of every view with a read-set hit, deduplicated by a per-round mark on each session (so a session in many hit views is visited once per view and fenced once, O(Σ subscribers of hit views)). It then publishes `{prior: old latest, latest: b}` for each fenced session, then R = b **once**, and only then offers any update of the round (step 4). A Cover-only hit moves no fence. No view advances R or offers before every view's fences are published: a per-view advance would let a writer of session s₂ load R ≥ b while s₂'s fence for b is unpublished (L56).
  - **Why one round is enough (v2.45).** The effective-point argument below uses only the publisher's program order (fences before R = b) and the owner's `Latest` loads before a unit's first byte (R, then its fence; v2.44). It holds for any number of views and sessions in one round, and it needs no total order of events across tasks.
  - **Ended entries (v2.19.2, RECHECK B1).** A read set leaves the index only when no content that depended on it can still pass a writer without a fence that covers it. Each path that removes one keeps that:
    - **The publisher ends a subscription:** a faulted or refused run (`Ended`, in G2 or G3), a read-set overrun, or a projection overrun (§4 "Query budgets", v2.55.1: `Ended(sub, Failed(OutOfBytes(..)))`, `Ended(sub, Failed(OutOfTime(s)))`), a subscriber's ledger refusal (`Ended(sub, Refused(Budget))`), and a publisher-made `Reset` (rows over `budget`, a `look` Error). The subscription stops running and its Cover is dropped, but its read set stays in the index as an **ended entry**, still charged to the session's `budget`. For a faulted or refused run, that is the run's partial read log: the reads it made before it stopped, all already charged. Query code is deterministic, so the fault depends on nothing else. For a projection overrun, it is the read set of the run whose answer was being projected (§4 "Query budgets"). In every other case it is the read set of the subscription's last run. A Cover-only hit on an ended entry does nothing. A read-set hit on one in round c fences the session at c in step 3, as any read-set hit does. (v2.46, DX Q4, astra 1995 #3) The entry stays indexed and charged until its last drop: it leaves only when the owner removes the instance that holds it (below) or the session ends, never at its first hit. A later hit fences the session again, which drops only content older than that hit. So no ending needs a fence when it is found. An ended shared view is unlinked from join lookup at once (§3 "Shared views"), and its **retention record**, which holds the ended read set and the subscriber instances it still protects, is kept apart from the view table: a new live view under the same key is a new view and never replaces or frees that record.
    - **Repair (v2.20; v2.46 per subscriber, astra 1995 #3, #7).** A content bounce at a full owner inbox ends nothing. Each live subscriber instance whose group (rows, a value or both), `complete` or value the bounced content carried is marked **due**, so a bounced value-only group marks its subscriber due as a bounced row does. Due suppresses delivery to that subscriber only: it is sent no later group or diff until its full replacement `complete` has been queued in order behind everything it was already sent, and a second bounce keeps it due. Due never stops the view: every live view processes each round's hits, re-runs on a read-set hit and advances its **validated base** after every successful round, even when all its subscribers are due. A due subscriber's view keeps its read set indexed, so a read-set hit still fences the session in step 3, wherever the bounce was found (at step 4 after R is published, or in the retry pass). Before each `settle`, the publisher takes the session's progress token (§1.1); if it took one, or the session has no progress channel, each due subscriber is repaired from its view's image validated through the current base, with no run of its own: the full current R and every slot's full rows, owed as a `complete` naming it. (Before v2.46 a due sub was re-run alone; with shared views the view is already current.) The writer checks that `complete` like any unit. Removing the instance (below) or ending the session clears the mark. (v2.22) A repair is sent only when the owner can take it and has sent the last one: the publisher repairs a due sub only when it has taken the session's progress token since the session's last bounce and its last repair, and the session holds no `complete` that the owner has taken and not yet sent (the session's taken register is at least the count of `complete`s its inbox took). Until then the sub stays due. Content the owner still holds for a sub instance that a later `complete` of the same instance replaces is superseded: the owner drops it before it weighs `budget`, and Outcomes and their marks stay.
    - **The owner asks for removal.** The owner marks a sub leaving immediately and stops accepting content for it. It keeps the publisher's read set while the writer holds a unit. Once the writer clears, it sends `Unsubscribed` with a waiting send, oldest first. An idle writer sends it immediately. The publisher never waits on the owner. A successor arriving before the removal takes a charged deferred slot. Live and deferred subs together obey `max_subs`; the first successor gets the freed slot and excess requests get the usual per-sub refusal. The owner posts deferred `Subscribed` after the removals. An Unsubscribe of a deferred sub drops its input locally. The publisher discards content for every instance it removes.
    - **The session ends** (`Left`, the sweep): its writer is gone.
  - **Subscription instances.** Each `Subscribe` on a hirsel.5 session uses a strictly increasing sub id. Id 0 may be first. A repeated or lower id closes with 1002. The owner keeps a u64 next-id bound, so u32 exhaustion cannot wrap. The owner numbers publisher subscriptions by instance and drops stale content for removed or retired instances. Outcome rows carry no instance and survive a subscription reset. A publisher `Reset` carries the last instance it took. If no live instance is at or below that mark, only the outcomes are delivered. Otherwise the owner retires its instances as on `Dropped` and accepts later subscriptions with fresh ids.
  - **Admission (v2.19.1, ATTACK m1).** A round's fence step allocates only from reservations, so it cannot fail:
    - At `Joined`, the publisher reserves on the session's `budget` ledger two `Arc[Fence]` (the next one it publishes, and the one the writer may still hold after a publish) and one `Arc[u64]` (the round value that writer may hold). The ledger is empty at `Joined`, so the reservation always fits. The root and its first `Arc` are the owner's, in the session's scope.
    - `mailbox(n)` admits the round root with its first `Arc`, the next `Arc` and the `Arc` holding `u64::MAX`, in its maker's scope.
    - A round allocates one fence `Arc` for each of its H fenced sessions and one round `Arc`, each from a reservation. A replaced `Arc` frees when its last share is released, and a writer holds at most one share of each, for one copy.
    - Each checked take costs two `latest.read`s. An unchecked unit costs none.
    - **As built (v2.19.3).** Every value is published from a spare that no reader shares, found with `arc.with_unique`, and the old value becomes a spare. The publisher keeps a share of each current value, so it never writes into one a reader can reach. A session has its current fence and two spares. The register has its current value, `u64::MAX` and one spare per joined session plus one; spares beyond that are freed when no reader holds them. The publisher has no `budget` ledger, so `Joined`'s reservation is admitted from its allowance. If it is refused, the join is answered with id 0 and the owner ends the subscription it was joining for with `Refused(Overloaded)`. If the only free spare is held by a writer whose session has already left, the round allocates one more `Arc`. If that is refused, the publisher stops (below).
  - **A publisher that stops (v2.19.3).** The round register publishes R = `u64::MAX` when it is cleaned, unless `Halt` disarmed it. The publisher declares it last, so a trap cleans it first, before any owner channel or the inbox closes. A head whose `Released` is refused commits nothing more, and `place_head` returns `OpenError.PublisherStopped`. Every writer that loads after E_MAX drops every content unit, so a commit the publisher never saw is never released unfenced.
  - **Deploy (v2.19.1, ATTACK M1).** When a deploy commits, OLD's publisher halts (hot-deploy C7). The halt publishes R = `u64::MAX` from its reserved `Arc`, one publication, before C7 reports the install and before OLD sends any 1012 `Close`. A writer that loads it drops every content unit. So the deploy has one effective point for every writer, and only units accepted before it finish. Outcome units still pass, as committed. The ordinary `Halt`, when the head stops serving, publishes nothing: every session ends with the place.
  - **Net-zero changes within a group (ATTACK B3, rejected).** Ruling 73 asks for a re-check against current state, and the publisher computes only at group boundaries. So a read-set value that changes and returns within one group leaves a queued unit equal to what a fresh run would produce, and G2 keeps the endpoint diff.
- **Why the check is ordered.** Every `latest.publish` and `latest.read` is an event in CONC-10's single order. Let E_b be the publication of R = b.
  - **One effective point for every writer.** Take a writer that loads R ≥ b. Its load comes after E_b. Each fence of round b was published before E_b, by publisher program order, and the writer loads its fence after R. So it reads `latest ≥ b`. If a later round b' > R overwrote the fence, then `prior ≥ b`, because rounds publish in version order. Either way the effective fence is ≥ b, and a content unit below b for a hit session is dropped. A writer uses `latest` only if `latest ≤ R`, so only after that round's R was published. Otherwise it uses `prior`, which was the `latest` of an earlier round, and that round's R was published before this fence (rounds publish in version order). So a fence acts at a writer only after its round's R is published. No writer acts on a fence of b before E_b, and every writer that loads after E_b acts on it. That holds across writers: two sessions hit by b are never split, with one reset and the other still accepting old content after the first has seen b (ATTACK-3 B1's second case).
  - **No effect of b that a client can see leaves before E_b.** Path by path:
    - Content at a version ≥ b is made in round b or later and offered only after that round's E (step 4).
    - An Outcome that reports b travels in b's Release or a later one (V4). The publisher offers it only after the round of every snapshot `Released` it took before it (V3), so after E_b.
    - A `Duplicate` of b's own position is decided after b's worker ended, so its snapshot-less Release is behind b's in `PubIn` (§2.1).
    - A `Reset` that the check causes needs an effective fence ≥ b, so it comes after E_b.
    - A V3 answer that could reveal b is pushed behind b's Release (`seen`, §2.1), so the publisher offers it after E_b.
  - **Ended entries.** A content component computed at v depends only on the reads its run made at v, a fault's partial log included, because query code is deterministic. Those reads stay indexed until a hit fences the session, the owner removes the instance after discarding all its content while its writer holds none, or the session ends. So a commit after v that changes one of them is a hit while they are indexed, and from its E every writer drops the component.
  - **The deploy.** R = `u64::MAX` is one publication. Every writer that loads after it drops every content unit, and none drops for it before.
    - In a running place, the publisher is the only task that observes main's snapshots. `schema` gets a `Declare` with no snapshot or row operation (rule 12), a body sees only its own Tx or `look`, and the runner calls `follow` nowhere. The head observes b only to answer, and its answers travel through the publisher.
  - **Durable before E_b.** b may be durable for one publisher round before E_b. In that window, nothing of b is visible, so a unit accepted then is ordered before b, and its acceptance is allowed. If the process dies in that window, every session dies with it. Clients reconnect and join at a first base ≥ b.
  - **What the check ensures.** No content unit computed before a commit that changed one of its session's read sets is written after that commit's effective point. No content of a subscription is written after the effective point of a commit made after that subscription left the index. Outcomes are not checked: a reply is the committed answer of a call the Caller made.
- **Shared views (v2.45, DX Q4).** The publisher keeps one **view** per view key, a hash map lookup:
  - the key is `(entry key, args bytes, None)` for a `query` and `(entry key, args bytes, Some(Caller codec bytes))` for a `query_as`, so a `query_as` view is shared only by sessions bound to byte-equal Callers (L38);
  - **why sharing discloses nothing.** The runner runs no access policy (store authorization was deleted on 2026-11-19, D8 (c)): a body decides access in its own code from what it reads and, for `query_as`, from its Caller. A `query` body gets no Caller, so its result is a function of (args, snapshot) alone and is the same for every session that could subscribe it. A `query_as` body's result is a function of (args, Caller, snapshot), and its sharers have byte-equal Callers. So every subscriber of a view is owed exactly the view's image, and a joiner's image at base is the one its own run would compute (query code is deterministic);
  - a view holds the CoverSet, the read set, the last value's encoded bytes, the base version it was computed at, the subscriber set and whether it has ended;
  - a subscriber holds the session, the sub id and owner instance, its due mark and repair state, its owed `complete`, its fence handle, and its `budget` charge.
  - **Charging.** Each subscriber is charged the view's full read set, CoverSet and value bytes on its session's `budget` ledger, exactly as an unshared subscription. Sharing saves the publisher's runs and encodes, never a session's admission room, so a session's admission does not depend on who else watches.
  - **Joining.** A `Subscribed` whose view key names a live, not-ended view joins it with no run only when the view's validated base is the publisher's current base. That always holds after a round ends, because every live view processes every round's hits, all-due views included (§3 "Repair"), so the joiner's `complete` is the view's image at base: the full current value and every slot's full rows, read from the base snapshot through the CoverSet. A key whose view has ended, or none, makes a new view and runs it (G3). An ended view is unlinked from join lookup when it ends and is never joined (v2.46, astra 1995 #3).
  - **Admission per subscriber (v2.46, astra 1995 #13).** A view is evaluated once, under its entry's byte and step bounds. Before the publisher installs a re-run's new read set, CoverSet and value for a subscriber, it reserves the complete change on that subscriber's session ledger. A ledger refusal ends only that subscriber instance with `Ended(sub, Refused(Budget))`: it is fenced in the hit round like every subscriber of the view, and its old read set, already charged, stays as its ended entry under the retention rule (§3 "Ended entries"). The view continues for the subscribers whose reservations succeeded. A join makes the same reservation and the whole-image size check (§3 "Frame bounds") before it attaches, and a refusal ends only the joiner. Only a failure of the evaluation itself (`Failed`, `Refused` from `look`), which is common to the view, ends all its live subscribers. So a session's admission never depends on who else watches, and no subscriber is charged less than its image.
  - **Leaving.** A live view with no subscribers is dropped. An ended view's read set lives on in its retention record, indexed and charged to each subscriber instance it protects, until the last of them drops (§3 "Ended entries").
  - **One group per round, two encodings (v2.46, astra 1995 #7).** A view's subscribers are either **synchronized** (they applied the view's previous image) or owed a `complete` (a joiner, a due subscriber being repaired, a reconnect). The round's common delta, an elided or new value plus the slot diffs, is encoded once and spliced into each synchronized subscriber's frame behind its own `sub`. A subscriber owed a `complete` gets the full-image encoding (the full value and every slot's full rows), also encoded at most once per round per view. A due subscriber gets neither until its repair.
- **Frames** (v2.4; exact text for TS-SDK.md in followups.md, N8 re-issued). A session may use several streams of its principal; the store's stream key is `caller_key ‖ t ‖ n`, with both stream words in big-endian order.

  | Direction | Frame | Fields |
  |---|---|---|
  | client → server | `Hello` | `credential: Option[Bytes]` (v2.24: passed unread to the application's binding), `entries: List[(identity: Bytes, args_fp: u64, result_fp: u64, recovery: Option[Recovery])]` (v2.94: the recovery is part of the key), `want: Want{calls: u32, bytes: u64}` (v2.94) |
  | client → server | `Call` | `calls: List[CallItem]` and `local: List[LocalItem]` (v2.94), 1 to `CALLS_MAX` = 256 items in all, within the connection's credit. `LocalItem` is `id: u64` (connection-local, unique among the connection's unanswered local items), `slot: u32`, `deadline_ms: u32`, `arg: Bytes`, for an unrecovered transact entry only. A `CallItem` on an unrecovered entry, or a `LocalItem` on any other entry, is answered `Refused(Malformed)` (V1) and runs nothing. `CallItem` is `stream: store.StreamId`, `p: u64`, `slot: u32`, `deadline_ms: u32` (relative, read at arrival; 0 or already passed gives `Refused(Deadline)`), `arg: Bytes` |
  | client → server | `Window` | `epoch: u64`, `want: Want`, `spent_calls: u64`, `spent_bytes: u64` (v2.94, §3 "Call credit") |
  | client → server | `Subscribe` / `Unsubscribe` | `sub: u32`, `slot: u32`, `arg: Bytes`, `resume: Option[Bytes]` / `sub: u32`. Rung 1 mints no token. The SDK sends `None`, and a `Some` is unknown, so it gets `Reset` (v2.12, §4). |
  | server → client | `Welcome` | `caller: Caller` (v2.24, not secret; was the bearer `principal`), `epoch: u64`, `grant: Grant{calls: u64, bytes: u64, share_calls: u32, share_bytes: u64}`, `frame_max: u32` (v2.94), `budget: u64`, `executable: Bytes` (the launch digest, empty when `Unavailable`), `target: String`, `n_place: u64`, `now_ms: u64`, `retention_ms: u64` |
  | server → client | `Entry` | `Ready(slot) \| Resolve(slot, writer_args: Bytes) \| Gone`, one per Hello entry |
  | server → client | `Batch` | `version: u64`, `complete: List[u32]` (v2.11, F11), `groups: List[Group]` (v2.45: `Group{sub: u32, value: Bytes, rows: List[Row]}`, `Row{slot: u16, key: Bytes, value: Option[Bytes]}`), `outcomes: List[Done]`, `local: List[LocalDone]` (v2.94: `LocalDone{id: u64, outcome}`, a `LocalItem`'s row; every frame that carries `outcomes` carries `local` beside it). `Abandoned` crosses; `InDoubt` never does. `Done` uses `store.StreamId`. |
  | server → client | `Credit` | `epoch: u64`, `grant: Grant` (v2.94, cumulative, §3 "Call credit") |
  | server → client | `Ended` | `sub: u32`, `Outcome`, for a subscription whose query faulted or ran out of bytes; the others continue |
  | server → client | `Reset` / `Close` | §4 / the threat table |

  The publisher gives the owner one update for a durable group, with its content and all answers still pending at that version.

- **The subprotocol.** The server selects `hirsel.5` (v2.94) if the client offers it anywhere in the list. Otherwise the standalone and routed servers answer HTTP 400 with an empty body and close the connection. This applies to missing, retired and unknown tokens. Frame tags 0–8 keep their existing variant names. `Set`, `Send` and `Live` have tags 9–11, and `Window` and `Credit` have tags 12 and 13. `hirsel.4` is a retired token (L58).
- **Groups (v2.45).** A group is one subscription's content at the Batch's version. `value` is R's codec bytes (`std.codec`, R's value schema in the key, rule 3) at that version, or empty when R is unchanged since the last group the client applied for that sub. (v2.46) The view's elision compares against its own last value, so this is exact only for a synchronized subscriber, one that applied every group the view made since its last `complete`; a subscriber that missed one is due and gets no delta until a `complete` (§3 "Repair", "Shared views"). A type whose encoding is empty for one value is empty for all of them (`()` and empty records), so `value` = empty never hides a change: for `R = ()` the value costs 0 bytes on every group. This holds because R is `codec`, so its codec is derived (codec.md `of`): scalars, String, List, Option, Map and Result encode at least one byte for every value (a width, a length or a tag); `()` encodes none; a record or tuple is the concatenation of its fields; and `via S` encodes as S. By induction on the type, either every value of R encodes to zero bytes or none does. A group in a Batch whose `complete` names its sub always carries the full value (or empty for an empty-encoding R) and the sub's full slot rows. `rows` are the slot diffs: each `Row` names its slot by id, and its `key` and `value` are §3 "Rows". The client applies a Batch whole, values and rows together. A group with an empty `value` and no rows, outside a completing Batch, is not sent: a re-run that changes neither R nor any slot's rows puts nothing on the wire, though its read-set hit still fences (§4 G2).

- **Rows (v2.11, F10; v2.45: per slot).** `Row.slot` is the slot id (rule 3). `Row.key` is the data-tree key, exactly `Change.tree_key`: the table id as u32 **big-endian**, then the key-codec bytes. `Row.value` is the **published value codec**: the table's current value type's codec bytes at the Batch version, without the store's u16 schema prefix. A value stored under an older schema number is decoded with that schema's reader and re-encoded with the current codec, so stripping two bytes is wrong. "Current" is the value type of the slot's Table, fixed for the open (rule 12). `None` means the key left the Cover or was deleted. This **publication adapter** is STORE-SPEC v2.23's `published_value(t, c)` (AG3). It serves both `changes` and the initial rows from `span_changes`. The publisher erases it once per slot when it binds the slot's selector to the open's H (§1.1 "Slot binding"): the bound slot keeps the collection the selector returned, a Table, Cell or Log, with its id, current codec and Readers, and the adapter at its types (v2.11.1; v2.46, astra 1998 #11).
- **Slot images (v2.53; one rule for Table, Cell and Log slots).** A slot's **registration** in a run is the covers its CoverSet holds under the slot's name: fixed spans (`cover`'s key spans, `cover_cell`'s one key, `cover_log`'s sequence ranges) and tail lengths (`cover_tail`). Every cover names the slot's bound collection (§1.1 "Slot binding"). With T the largest tail length, or none, the slot's **image** under registration g at snapshot s, I(g, s), is the set of keys that are either present at s inside one of g's fixed spans or, for a Log slot with a tail, `id ‖ seq` for seq in W(s, T) = [n − min(T, n − f), n), where (f, n) are the Log's bounds at s. Each key carries its published value at s (§3 "Rows"). Every tail of a slot ends at the same n, so the widest one alone decides the window, and two tails give one window.
  - **Rows.** The slot's rows from (g_a, a) to (g_b, b) are the differences between I(g_a, a) and I(g_b, b), in key order, each key once: a deletion (`None`) for each key in I(g_a, a) and not in I(g_b, b); the value at b for each key in I(g_b, b) and not in I(g_a, a); and the value at b for each key in both whose stored value differs between a and b. A key that leaves one cover but stays in another sends nothing. A completing Batch (G3, a join, a repair) sends I(g, s) whole, the difference from the empty image.
  - **Where it applies.** A Cover-only hit (G2) has g_a = g_b, the view's kept registration. A rerun (G2) diffs the view's old registration at a against the rerun's at b, and an absent registration is the empty image. So a change of t, a tail turned into a fixed range, a slot dropped, or coverage moved from one slot to another sends every removal and insertion, even when no Log was written. Coverage moved between slots goes in one group: the removals in one slot, the insertions in the other (L59).
  - **What a Cover-only round reads (cost).** Only these keys are candidates: the round's changed keys inside the slot's covers; each `Trimmed` span intersected with the fixed spans, enumerated from a; and, for a tail, the sequence numbers in W(a, T) △ W(b, T), at most 2T. For each candidate, membership at a and at b is decided by arithmetic, from the registration and the two snapshots' descriptors (a Log row is present at s exactly when f ≤ seq < n), and by the candidate's own `Change`. No other key can change its membership or value. So the round costs the candidates and no enumeration of either image, and the view keeps no rows (L20). A rerun with g_a ≠ g_b enumerates both images in pages (G2 step 1).
  - **The fast path.** When neither g_a nor g_b holds a fixed span and both have the same T, the rows equal `window_changes(a, b, l, T)` (STORE-SPEC §14.9), and the publisher may compute them that way. `window_changes` is not used for any other slot.
  - Example: fixed range [85, 95) and a tail of 10 on Log L, at a = (0, 100). I = [85, 95) ∪ [90, 100) = [85, 100). `trim_before(92)` gives b = (92, 100): the fixed range keeps [92, 95), W = [92, 100), so I = [92, 100), and the rows are seven deletions, 85 … 91, with 90 and 91 once each even though the trimmed span and the window both drop them. `window_changes` alone would send only 90 and 91.
- **Where an outcome row lives (v2.11, R2-a).** Rows are made in three places, and each is reported to the owner:
  - The publisher delivers head answers (V2, V4, V5) and commitless answers (V3). If the owner's inbox is full, its answers remain pending until the next delivery attempt at the publisher's current base. The owner's `Drained` wakes it to retry (v2.13).
  - The owner makes V1 rows, `Abandoned` rows, and compute replies itself. A granted entry's task reports its reply to the inbox.
  - The owner keeps each row in its outgoing queue until it hands a frame to the writer. The writer holds the encoded bytes while the owner retains the units and refusal count through `Sent` or session end.
  - The owner builds every `Batch`, `Ended` and `Reset`. A `Reset` and its continuation frames carry every row the owner holds, including those in the publisher update that caused the reset and those in a unit the writer dropped. Rows held by the publisher reach the owner first: the publisher drains its pending `Told`s for that session before it signals a reset.
  - Every row lies inside its send's credit: a Done row is at most `DONE_MAX` B plus its prefix, and a V1 refusal is at most 48 B (`Refused(Budget(Room))`: stream 16, p 8, Outcome tag 4, Refusal tag 4, Room 16). So the rows a session holds are bounded by its outstanding credit (§3 "Call credit"). A frame carries the rows that fit, and the rest continue.
- **Frame bounds (v2.11, F12).** At the defaults (`max_frame` 256 KiB, `budget` 1 MiB) the per-frame outcome reserve is `DONE_FRAME_RESERVE` = 5·8,192 + 5·48 = 41,200 B. It is a fixed constant (v2.94: it no longer comes from q), and rows beyond it continue in outcome-only Batches. A Batch's envelope is 24 + 4·|complete| B, plus (v2.45) 12 B per group (`sub` 4, `value` count 4, `rows` count 4) and 2 B per row for its slot. Values and rows get `max_frame − reserve − envelope`; with one completing sub whose one group carries the value and its rows that is 262,144 − 41,200 − (28 + 12) = 220,904 B before row metadata, which holds the full checkbox board (179,768 B).
  - **The whole Batch is counted (v2.46, astra 1995 #10).** For each session the publisher counts the entire encoded Batch it would offer: the envelope, every group's 12-byte header, every slot id, key and value encoding, every value, the `complete` list and the outcome reserve. It counts groups in order and stops at the first one that would pass `max_frame` or the session's remaining `budget`; it never sends a prefix of a group or of a round. Two value-only groups that each fit their own bounds but not one frame together are therefore caught, not sent.
  - A Subscribe whose initial value and rows exceed that is `Ended(sub, Refused(Budget(Room{requested: value and row bytes, available: the rows allowance})))`. A view's value bytes are bounded by min(the entry's byte bound `n`, the rows allowance), checked when the value is encoded (v2.45). Initial images of several subs that each fit go in separate Batches at the same version (G3).
  - A G2 round whose content for a session exceeds the allowance, or `budget`, becomes `Reset(version, outcomes)`, the round being atomic. Reset always fits: its outcomes take the reserve, and the rest continue in outcome-only Batches at its version (§3 "Call credit").

- **Call credit (v2.94; lane s4-call-window-spec-3519, design s4-call-window-design-3516, S1 of 2026-11-27).** A connection sends Calls only against credit the place has granted and backed. Credit counts calls and bytes separately. It replaces the session limit `q` and the slot table completely. A stream still holds one pending stored call (STORE-SPEC §1.8 S7), so a connection runs many calls at once by leasing many streams of its Caller. Evidence: every figure below is an **ESTIMATE** from READ constants (`DONE_MAX`, `wire_outcome_reserve`, STORE-SPEC §1.8 retention), and nothing here is built until the held implementation lane lands (§8 marks the laws **PLANNED**). **Policy and guarantee.** W, S_u, S_P, the session ceiling and the reclaim delay are the place operator's configuration. The values below are what an unconfigured place does, not promises. What Hirsel guarantees is the mechanism: no Call within its grant is refused for memory or wire room (Store room and Store rows are not part of a grant), no stale `Credit` adds credit, and the peak bound below holds for whatever values are configured.
  - **Counters.** For each connection c the owner keeps three cumulative pairs (calls, bytes), each a u64 that never goes down: G_c, the credit granted; A_c, the credit the client has spent, counted from the Call items that arrived plus any forfeit (below); and R_c, the credit returned because its backing was freed. Outstanding credit is O_c = G_c − R_c. The client keeps its own spent pair S_c. It raises S_c before it sends a Call, and it may send a Call only if S_c after the Call is within G_c in both components.
  - **What one Call item costs.** The item costs 1 call and b bytes, where b is the item's encoded length inside the `Call` list: stream 16, p 8, slot 4, `deadline_ms` 4, the arg length 4 and the arg, so b = 36 + |arg|. A `LocalItem` costs 1 call and b = 20 + |arg| bytes (id 8, slot 4, `deadline_ms` 4, the arg length 4 and the arg). The client computes b from what it sends, and the frame's 12 B header (tag and the two counts) costs nothing. A `LocalDone` row is 16 B shorter than a `Done` row, so ρ covers it. The item's backing is σ₀ + 2·round8(b), and since round8(b) ≤ b + 7 that is at most σ₀ + 14 + 2·b. The byte credit counts b unpadded. The rounding is covered by the fixed term σ = σ₀ + 16, so the owner reserves ρ(1, b) = σ + 2·b in the shared pool P for that item, and a grant's ρ(n, B) covers every item that fits it. σ is fixed:
    - `META` 64 B: the per-call record (stream 16, p 8, request digest 32, deadline 4, slot 4). The Caller is per session.
    - `R_reply` 8,228 B: the largest Done row, `DONE_MAX` 8,192 plus its stream, position and outcome prefix (`wire_outcome_reserve` per call).
    - `R_refusal` 48 B: the largest V1 refusal row (§3 "Where an outcome row lives").
    - `κ_fixed`: the fixed part of the proved decode bound. The 2·round8(b) term covers the encoded item and its typed decode, one `Bytes` allocation for the arg. The implementation proves `κ_fixed` from the `Call` codec and reports it. This text takes 64 B, so σ₀ = 8,404 B.
    - 16 B of rounding: two round8 paddings of at most 7 B each, rounded up. So σ = 8,420 B (ESTIMATE).
    - A grant of (n, B) reserves ρ(n, B) = n·σ + 2·B. The reservation is taken when the credit is granted, never when the Call arrives, so a granted Call is never refused for memory. Socket and control scratch (the receive loan, assembly growth, control frames and the Close timer) stay in the owner floor `F` and are separately bounded, as today.
  - **The place window W.** W is configured. Unconfigured, it is (4,096 calls, 786,432 B = 768 KiB), S1's value (DECISIONS 2026-11-27 17:43), and the figures below use that value. The sum over every live connection of O_c stays within W, idle connections' grants included. So the input the front can hold is at most W.bytes + W.calls·`META` at any number of sessions, which is 786,432 + 262,144 = 1 MiB unconfigured. Reply, refusal and decode reservations come on top of that, at most ρ(W) = 4,096·8,420 + 2·786,432 = 36,061,184 B (34.4 MiB) of P's 384 MiB.
  - **The credit formula.** The owner raises G_c only while all four conditions hold after the raise (componentwise in calls and bytes):
    1. **Target.** O_c ≤ T_c = min(want_c, share_c). want_c is the window the client last asked for (`Hello.want`, then `Window`). share_c is c's fair share (below).
    2. **Place window.** Σ_c O_c ≤ W.
    3. **Recovery rows.** Σ over u's connections of O_c.calls ≤ S_u, the Caller's configured stream quota (256 unconfigured, STORE-SPEC §1.8 "Retention"), and Σ_c O_c.calls ≤ S_P, the place's configured stream cap (unconfigured, one stream per 4 KiB of the sized resident allowance). One credit covers one pending stored call, and each pending call needs its own busy stream row, so these bounds keep the grants within the rows Store could hold. **A grant reserves wire backing only, not Store row admission.** Store §1.8 decides each new row at admit time. At the Caller's quota it replaces only the oldest row, and a busy oldest victim gives `StreamOverloaded`, even when the Caller holds fewer credits than S_u (STORE-SPEC §1.8). That answer is V3 `Refused(Overloaded)` with nothing run, and the SDK keeps the call and retries it on a stream it already holds. An unrecovered call uses no stream row. The bounds still count it, because a grant does not know which kind of call the client will send. The 4 KiB density covers a row's retained reply. Store's resident admission still applies (`NoRoom` → V3 `Refused(Overloaded)`): credit promises wire admission, not store room.
    4. **Memory.** ρ(O_c) is reserved in P from unreserved room, and it stays within the session's pooled ceiling (16 MiB unconfigured), together with the session's other pooled work.

    In one line: **raise G_c to the largest value with O_c ≤ min(want_c, share_c), Σ O ≤ W, Σ_u O.calls ≤ S_u, Σ O.calls ≤ S_P, and ρ(O_c) reserved in P and within 16 MiB.** The owner recomputes the raise whenever R_c, want_c or share_c changes. Connections below their target are raised in the order they fell below it (FIFO), so a connection that waits for W room is served before any connection that fell below target after it.
  - **Fair share.** How W is divided is the operator's share policy. Any policy must keep Σ share ≤ W and the idle floor below. Unconfigured, share_u is Caller u's max-min fair share of W, computed separately for calls and bytes. Each Caller's demand is the sum of its connections' wants, and the share is taken over the Callers with a live connection that wants credit. share_c splits share_u across u's connections the same way. A connection with no credit outstanding (O_c.calls = 0) has share_c.bytes ≥ min(want_c.bytes, `frame_max`). This **idle floor** is a share, not a reservation: a raise still needs room under Σ O ≤ W. When the idle floors of the waiting connections sum past the free room in W, they are raised in the FIFO order above as credit returns, before any connection that already holds credit. W.bytes ≥ `max_frame` makes sure one floor always fits once enough has returned. Outstanding credit returns as its calls complete, so an idle connection waits on others' completions, never on a timer. It is not promised room on arrival. Shares are recomputed when a want changes or a connection opens or ends. A smaller share takes back no credit. It only stops raises, and the client forfeits the excess (below).
  - **Frames.** Three new frames and one changed frame carry credit, under the subprotocol `hirsel.5` (§3 "The subprotocol"):
    - `Hello.want: Want{calls: u32, bytes: u64}` is the first want. `Welcome` carries `epoch: u64`, the connection's number, unique within the place's run, and `grant: Grant{calls: u64, bytes: u64, share_calls: u32, share_bytes: u64}`, the first cumulative grant G_c and share_c. It also carries `frame_max: u32`, the place's `socket.max_frame`.
    - `Credit{epoch: u64, grant: Grant}` (server → client) carries the new cumulative G_c and the current share_c. The owner keeps at most one pending Credit, holding the latest totals. It is a control unit: it is written before queued data, in the same socket write as the next unit when one is ready, and alone otherwise. It needs no input credit, passes the release check with no load, and is delivered while the client's input credit is zero.
    - `Window{epoch: u64, want: Want, spent_calls: u64, spent_bytes: u64}` (client → server) sets want_c and reports S_c. To forfeit credit, the client first raises S_c by the amount forfeited, then sends `Window`. A connection's frames are ordered, so when `Window` arrives the owner has seen every item the client counted before the forfeit. The owner sets A_c to the reported spent pair and raises R_c by the forfeit, which is the reported spent pair minus the spent credit of the items that arrived. A `Window` whose spent pair is below A_c or above G_c, or whose epoch is not this connection's, closes the connection with 1008.
    - `Call{calls: List[CallItem], local: List[LocalItem]}`, with `CallItem{stream, p, slot, deadline_ms, arg}` and `LocalItem{id, slot, deadline_ms, arg}` (frames table), carries 1 to `CALLS_MAX` = 256 items in all in one frame. Its fixed header is the tag (4 B) and the two list counts (4 B each), 12 B in all, and the `local` count follows the last `CallItem`. So the reader reads the frame length and the first count before it allocates anything. If that count, or the two counts together, exceeds `CALLS_MAX` or the calls remaining in G_c − A_c, or the length less 12 exceeds the bytes remaining, the connection closes with 1008 before any item is decoded. The second count is checked when the reader reaches it, still before any `LocalItem` is decoded. Every item in a list is a separate send, owed its own Outcome (rule 5).
    - **Fencing.** The client applies a `Credit` only if its epoch equals the `Welcome` epoch of the connection it arrived on and its totals are at least the ones it holds. It takes each component as the maximum of the two. A stale, duplicate or reordered `Credit` therefore never adds credit, and a `Credit` from an earlier connection (the in-process transport, §14.11, or a buffered frame after a reconnect) is ignored.
  - **When credit returns.** A call item's credit, (1, b) with its ρ(1, b), returns to R_c at the later of two points. The first is when its Outcome row's unit is `Sent` (writer completion), or the session ends. The second applies only to a send that reached the head: the send that forwarded a position, or a `LocalItem`, whose head answer has reached the owner. So an `Abandoned` send whose position the head still holds keeps its credit until the head answers. A local join (§2.1 V2) is its own Call item and holds its own credit until its own row is `Sent`. A row kept across `Dropped` keeps its credit until the `Reset` or continuation frame that carries it is `Sent` (below). A V1 refusal returns at its row's `Sent`. A forfeit returns when its `Window` arrives. Credit never returns when a row is staged, only when the bytes that back it are freed.
  - **Abuse and forfeit.** A client may hold credit it does not use. Credit above share_c that is still unused for the place's reclaim delay (30 s unconfigured) after the `Credit` that lowered share_c, meaning G_c − A_c above share_c, closes the connection with 1013. Backlog reclamation needs no other timer. A client that sends without reading never learns of new grants. Its spent credit cannot exceed G_c, so it sends nothing beyond that, and its blocked writes end it within the 30 s write deadline (threat table).
  - **Outcomes in bounded frames.** Every row carries its own (stream, p, Outcome), once per owed send, as before. The publisher's update for a durable version v reaches the owner whole (G2 step 4). The owner writes its first frame: the update's groups (or the `Reset` that replaces them, §3 "Frame bounds") and as many of its Outcome rows as fit in `max_frame`. That frame always has room for at least `DONE_FRAME_RESERVE` = 41,200 B of rows (§3 "Frame bounds"). The remaining rows owed at v follow in **continuation** frames: outcome-only `Batch`es at version v, each at most `max_frame`. They are written before any frame of a later update. So a subscription's change at v is applied or invalidated before any reply that reports v is exposed. Continuation frames carry no content, so the release check passes them with no load, and rows kept across `Dropped` are continued the same way after their `Reset`. Every continuation row is inside its call's reservation, so continuing needs no new memory.
  - **Head work.** The owner forwards the items of one `Call` list that pass V1 to the head as one inbox message, in list order. The head admits them in that order, interleaving other sessions' ready work, and closes its group when it finds its inbox empty (§2 H3, unchanged). Store seals at its existing count and byte bounds, on an empty Runner inbox, or at a barrier (STORE-SPEC §3.3). One completed Directory write acknowledges a prefix of tickets. The head's existing newest-ticket gate (H3) releases the group's outcomes in commit order at its durable version. A V3 answer waits behind the Release that holds its `seen` (§2.1). A failed or uncertain write releases nothing (Poison).
- **A lone call never waits on a batch timer (v2.94).** A call submitted when its connection holds credit takes this path, and every step on it is either immediate or an I/O wait:
  - the SDK pump sends at once (docs/spec/sdk.md);
  - the owner forwards on arrival;
  - the head closes its group when its inbox is empty (H3);
  - Store submits on an empty Runner inbox once the writer has returned its buffer, with no gather deadline (STORE-SPEC §3.3; L94's END interval is off the ack path);
  - the publisher's round runs on the Release (G1);
  - the owner writes at `Sent`.

  The only waits are the previous group's write and its sync. A call submitted with no credit waits for credit to return (a completion or a forfeit) or for a larger share, and both of those are events, never timers. Batching comes only from work already queued when a step runs.
- **Peak charged bytes with a stalled reader (v2.94).** Take a client that sends Calls and never reads.
  - **Bound.** For each connection c, its charge is at most **ρ(O_c) + `budget` + round8(`max_frame` + 14) + `F`**, with ρ(O_c) ≤ min(session ceiling, ρ(W)) at all times and O_c ≤ T_c once returns catch up with a lowered target (proof step 2). For N stalled connections the total is at most **ρ(W) + N·(`budget` + round8(`max_frame` + 14) + `F`)**, because Σ O_c ≤ W. At the defaults, ρ(W) = 34.4 MiB is paid once per place, not once per connection.
  - **Proof.**
    1. **Input.** The reader checks every Call frame against G_c − A_c before it allocates (above), so an item outside its credit is never decoded. Each item's encoded bytes, decoded arg, record, reply row and refusal row lie inside its ρ(1, b). Joins, re-sends of an `Abandoned` position and V1 refusals are items and are covered the same way. A frame over credit closes the connection with 1008 and is never charged.
    2. **No growth without return.** O_c rises only at a raise, and every raise checks conditions 1–4.
