So two invariants hold at all times, whatever later happens to T_c: Σ_c O_c ≤ W, and ρ(O_c) ≤ min(session ceiling, ρ(W)). A lowered want or share takes no credit back. It stops raises at once, and O_c falls to the new T_c as credit returns or is forfeited. Credit above the new share that is neither used nor forfeited within the reclaim delay closes the connection (1013, "Abuse and forfeit"). Until O_c ≤ T_c again, the bound uses T_c at the last raise, which is at most the ceiling. When the reader stalls, no unit after the stalled one completes. Only two kinds of return can then happen. A forfeit lowers O_c. The head's late answer to an `Abandoned` position whose row was already `Sent` returns that credit, and the regrant it allows again keeps O_c ≤ T_c. So the outstanding charge stays within ρ(O_c), and so within the bound above, however long the stall lasts, even when the head keeps answering.
    3. **Replies and control at zero input credit.** The owner keeps taking head answers and building rows and control frames when the client has no credit. Rows are inside their calls' reservations (1). Control frames are bounded as before: one pending `Credit` with the latest totals, `Placed` and `Entry` from the Hello, owner-made `Ended` and `Reset` under `budget`, and Close room in `F`. Publication waits under `budget` (slow-reader row). Continuation frames add no rows (above).
    4. **Writer.** The transport holds at most one frame of at most round8(`max_frame` + 14) B (§3 "Every queue").
    5. **Fencing.** Credit returns only through R_c, which is counted on the server. Neither a stale `Credit` at the client nor any client frame can raise G_c, so no message order lets the charge pass the bound above.
- **Crash cuts and replay (v2.94).** A Caller replays only when it is the same Caller after reconnecting, which means a binding (§3 "Anonymous": a new anonymous Caller reports `LocallyUnknown`). In each cut, replay sends the original Caller, stream, p and argument bytes within the new connection's credit.
  - **Cut 1: the place crashes after a commit and before its row is sent.**
    - (1a) The commit's journal frame is in the acknowledged prefix. The stream holds (p, digest, Replied(r)). The replay is admitted as `Duplicate(Stored{r})` (H2), and the head answers r unchanged: the original bytes, and the body does not run.
    - (1b) The frame is outside the acknowledged prefix, so it was lost at the crash. The prefix-ordered journal keeps no part of it, and the gate (H3) released nothing from it, so the client saw nothing. The replay gets `Abandoned` and runs once (the admit was durable, H4), or `Admitted` fresh (it was not). In both, no earlier effect exists.
    - A group straddling the cut splits by its ticket prefix, each call landing in (1a) or (1b). In both, the effect happens once.
  - **Cut 2: the client receives the row and crashes before settling it locally.** The SDK persisted the pending record (stream, p, argument bytes, in-flight) before it sent, and it persists the terminal outcome and the next position in one write after it receives (docs/spec/sdk.md). Before that write, the stream is still at p on both sides. The place keeps p as the latest position until a send of p+1, and the client sends p+1 only after settling. On restart the lane replays p and is answered `Duplicate(Stored{r})`: the original bytes, with the effect once.
    - This holds while the Caller's installations lease at most S_u streams, and the SDK holds its lanes within its granted calls, which stay within S_u. If several installations of one Caller together lease more, an unsettled replied row can be evicted. The replay then gets `HistoryUnavailable` and reports `LocallyUnknown`. That never gives a second effect.
  - **Unrecovered calls.** These rules are for recovered calls. An unrecovered call is never replayed. At cut 1, its effect is durable or absent, and the client cannot tell which. At cut 2, the client did not settle it. In both cases the SDK reports `LocallyUnknown` and sends nothing again.
  - **Under `MemoryStorage`.** The client keeps no state across a process exit, so both cuts are transport cuts there. Cut 1 kills the place, and cut 2 drops the socket after the row arrives and before the settlement write applies, while the client process lives on. A process kill under `MemoryStorage` loses the pending call by design (docs/spec/sdk.md), and no gate cell measures it.
- **Settlement by successor (v2.94).** The client never sends an acknowledgement. A stream carries one pending stored call (STORE-SPEC §1.8 S7). The SDK sends position p+1 on a stream only after p's terminal outcome is durably settled in its own storage. So when p+1 arrives, the place knows p was settled, and Store's existing rule replaces p's row with p+1's. Store §1.8's rows, its retention and the durable format are unchanged. A connection runs many calls only by using many streams.
- **One wire form (v2.94).** A lone call is a `Call` list with one item, and its answer is one Outcome row in a Batch. There is no scalar `Call` frame, no batch mode and no other session limit. Every item is a separate send with its own recovery identity (stream, p), its own Outcome and its own credit, whatever list it arrived in. A Call list is not a transaction. A corrupt envelope runs nothing, because it is checked before dispatch. After dispatch, any subset of the list may commit.
- **Recovery under credit (v2.94).** These rules close the failure cases of research-1128/call-window/moonshot "Where A can fail".
  - **Reconnect and a smaller grant.** Credit belongs to one connection epoch, and it ends with that connection. Unused grants are not carried over, and the new connection starts from its own `Welcome` grant. Pending calls do not depend on the grant. The SDK persists every item of a list before it sends the list. After reconnecting it resends each unsettled item on its own stream, oldest first, with its original bytes. That includes items of a list that may never have been sent. Items are independent, so recovery sends any subset that fits the new credit and waits for credit to return for the rest. It never merges items, splits them or re-encodes them. A grant smaller than the old one only slows recovery. The idle floor (share_c.bytes ≥ min(want_c.bytes, `frame_max`) while O_c.calls = 0) means any single item that was legal before can be sent again once its floor is raised ("Fair share"). An item that fit a `frame_max` frame fits that floor. Settled lanes are not resent. Stale `Credit`, continuation and Outcome frames from the old connection are ignored by epoch (above).
  - **Cold rows and floors at quota.** Recovery rows are created lazily by Store, as today. A credit is not a row: a grant creates no row, and a warm lane reuses its stream's row. Condition 3 keeps a Caller's outstanding calls within S_u, but a grant does not protect Store rows. A fresh row at quota may evict the Caller's oldest idle row, including a replied row another installation has not settled yet (cut 2's caveat), or it may be refused `StreamOverloaded` (condition 3). A resend of an existing (stream, p) needs no new row and is admitted with every row occupied. Near quota, the SDK drains recovery before it leases new streams. Retention and eviction of idle rows stay Store §1.8 operator policy. A grant promises wire admission, not a recovery horizon. An unsettled row that policy evicted answers `HistoryUnavailable` and reports `LocallyUnknown`, never a second effect (cut 2).
  - **Outcomes across frames.** A fence (`Dropped`) keeps every owed unsent row. The `Reset` that follows invalidates content once, before any of those rows. Rows that do not fit its frame follow in ordered continuation Batches at its version (above). A subscription's change at a version is applied or invalidated whole before an outcome that reports that version. Control frames, `Credit` among them, go ahead of queued data and never wait behind continuations.
  - **Fairness.** The share policy, raise order and stream caps above decide whom credit goes to. On the head, a list's items are admitted in order, with other sessions' ready work interleaved, and `CALLS_MAX` bounds one list's work. A list adds no head priority. Its items are admitted as separate requests among other sessions' work. A slow handler still holds the serial head for up to its entry's step bound `s` per run (§2 P1, D5), the same as a single call, and the step bound is the only bound on that. Fairness across humans or tenants who hold several Callers is the operator's admission policy (http.md, the application's binding), not a wire rule.
  - **Budget authority.** Each server has one ledger, its pool P. Every place it serves takes credit reservations from that ledger, so no two ledgers draw on one allocator. For P, for each Caller across its connections, and for each session, the owner that spends a resource checks fixed + used + promised-but-unused ≤ assigned atomically before it grants. Credit and its ρ are transient. Recovery rows are durable and are counted separately, by S_u and S_P. Draining a socket returns credit and never frees a Store row. A connection with no grant asks for credit with `Hello.want` or `Window`. Unused credit shrinks only through a forfeit, which the `Window` frame acknowledges, or when the epoch ends. Forwarded work that has not come back stays charged after a disconnect, until the head answers (when credit returns).
  - **SDK persisted terminals.** The SDK writes each lane's terminal outcome and next position in one durable write per received frame, and only then exposes the result (docs/spec/sdk.md). Its queue of calls not yet issued is application memory outside protocol accounting. Whether it refuses locally or applies backpressure is the application's choice.
- **Stream-lease cost (v2.94).** Each concurrent call leases its own stream. The implementation reports two named numbers per completed call on the matched keynote2 cell:
  - `lease_instr_per_call`: the instructions spent leasing and settling streams. On the client, that is lane lease and release, the stream-ID mint amortised over the calls on a stream, and the lane-to-identity index. On the place, it is the stream-row work for one call: the admit's row read and write, the reply's row write, and quota and expiry checks. It is measured with `instructions:u` over the matched run, divided by completed calls.
  - `lease_bytes_per_call`: the resident stream-row bytes (key, position, digest, state, retained reply), the client's persisted lane-record bytes, and the 24 wire bytes of (stream, p), per pending call.

  If `lease_instr_per_call` exceeds 20% of the place's instructions per completed call, or the stream rows a run holds exceed 25% of the resident allowance, the implementation lane reports it to the lead. That is the trigger for reconsidering a per-position table (3516 "Peer bounds and decision").
- **Acceptance gate for the implementation (v2.94).** Implementation is held until 3515's per-transfer ledger is in and Sam has seen the design (DECISIONS 2026-11-28, "S4 call window (3516)"). The lane lands only with every item below measured with **durable client storage (`NodeStorage`) and with `MemoryStorage`**:
  1. matched off and p40 keynote2 TPS and p99, against the same cells for SpacetimeDB 2.16.3 under METHOD §16.8, with both sides' durability stated;
  2. time queued in the client against time issued (send to settled), per call;
  3. durable writes per completed call, on the place (journal syncs) and on the client (storage commits);
  4. peak charged bytes with N stalled readers, against the bound above (laws L9 and L19);
  5. both crash cuts, each with the replay rule above (laws L1 and L72), and a lone call with the clock held (L73);
  6. `lease_instr_per_call` and `lease_bytes_per_call`.

  The same change moves every client of the wire to `hirsel.5`. That includes the store-bench native load generator and null peer in hirsel-benchmarks, `store-bench/engines/keynote2/layered/native/` and `store-bench/engines/keynote2/layered/null-peer.mjs` (the s4-layered-drivers report names no path yet), along with `bench/wire/`, `sdk/client/`, `core/programs/runner/frame-vectors.json`, the `wire_` tests in `tools/tests/` and `core/std/api/runner.md`. The 2× target stays unverified until item 1 is measured.

| Threat | Limit | Result |
|---|---|---|
| oversized or malformed frame | max frame bytes; strict decode | `Close` |
| call flood | per connection: its cumulative grant G_c (§3 "Call credit"). A send holds its credit until its row is `Sent` or the session ends, and a forwarding send also until the head answers (V2, v2.94). Place-wide: Σ O_c ≤ W | a Call over the grant: `Close` (1008) before decode |
| a client that sends without reading (v2.11, v2.94) | credit returns only when its rows are `Sent`, so it can send no more than its outstanding credit | it stops at its grant; blocked writes end it under the 30 s write deadline |
| owner admission | 10,000 owners including pending, and at most 1,024 pending upgrades | HTTP 503 before upgrade |
| shared allocation shortage | one 384 MiB byte pool and a bounded allocation inbox shared by all owners, and a 16 MiB ceiling per owner | Close 1013 using the owner’s control room, or a commitless `Refused(Overloaded)` if the owner can identify an unforwarded call and has refusal room |
| incomplete message | one absolute 30 s deadline, including interleaved control frames | Close 1001, with the existing 5 s close deadline |
| blocked data write | one absolute 30 s deadline across prefix writes | close within the following 5 s. The sole writer flushes any partial frame before sending Close 1001, or drops the connection at expiry |
| slow reader | `budget` bytes per session, enforced by the publisher | a full owner inbox spends no strike: the subs are repaired (v2.20), with at most one unsent repair per session (v2.22). Content a later `complete` of the same instance replaces is dropped first, so `budget` weighs only what the client will still see (v2.22). Rows over `budget`: `Reset` with Outcomes, one counted per connection; a second counted overrun: `Close` 1008. `Dropped` is exempt |
| subscription flood | max subscriptions and read-set limits per session, plus the per-owner ceiling for pooled keys and arguments | `Refused(NotAllowed)` at the subscription count limit, `Refused(Overloaded)` on lease shortage, and `Ended(sub, Refused(Budget))` when a rerun exceeds read-set room |
| excess sessions and sends | `max_sessions` and `socket.pending_max` bound transport admission. Each anonymous Caller identifies one session, so its share of W is that session's. HTTP calls carry no credit and are bounded by http.md's relay and slot bounds (HTTP-15). | HTTP 503 at transport admission; a WebSocket send waits for credit |

**Every queue, and the account it is charged to (L9).**

| Queue | Bound | Account |
|---|---|---|
| request digests (v2.11.3): 32 B per held position, in the session's held position, in each `Req` and in each `Worker`, kept while any send or worker holds it | one per outstanding credit; one per worker | the send's call credit (session and `Req`); the head's worker record (`Worker`) |
| requests at the head (inbox, worker waiters, stream FIFO), and (v2.23) the owner's post queue for a full head inbox | the connection's outstanding call credit, held until the head answers | the send's call credit (V2, v2.94); mutant `queue_outside_account` CAUGHT (C6). A full head inbox refuses nothing: the owner's next request waits in its post queue (v2.23) |
| owed sends and their deadline timers, in the session | one per outstanding credit, joins included (v2.94) | the send's call credit; mutant `join_outside_account` CAUGHT (C6b) |
| Outcome rows the owner holds, not yet handed to the writer (v2.11, v2.94) | one row per outstanding credit, each ≤ `R_reply` or 48 B | the send's call credit. **PLANNED** with L19 |
| frames waiting for the client: Batches in the owner's inbox and the owner's outgoing queue, control frames included (v2.8) | `budget` | the session; mutant `unbounded_queue` CAUGHT (K6) |
| the publisher's `PubIn` inbox (v2.12): `Released`, `Joined`, `Subscribed`, `Unsubscribed`, and the internal `Left`, `Halt` and `Drained` (v2.13) | the channel's capacity, 64 in the reference server | the head, `connect` and (v2.22) a wire owner's `Joined` wait while it is full; a `Joined` carries its owner's first Subscribe. Otherwise the owner `try_send`s: a full inbox ends a later Subscribe with `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))` (§3, an admission failure, kept in v2.20) and drops a `Drained`. Per session, subscription traffic is bounded by max subscriptions, and `Drained` by one per C takes while that owner's inbox never empties, plus one at a marked session's end (v2.14) |
| retirement marks (v2.14): one `ch(1, ())` per marked session, its `Receiver` held by the publisher | the marked sessions the publisher holds, at most M + 1 for M slots (CHANGES-v2.14 item 6; http.md: `streams_max` + `reads_max` + 1, since a call response no longer joins, v2.19.1), plus one per `Joined` still queued in `PubIn` | the session's scope (http.md HTTP-15 row 10); after its task ends, its spawner's, by MEM-20 hand-over, until the sweep drops the `Receiver` |
| subscription state: the view's CoverSet, read set (per-row lookup Points included, 48 B each, STORE-SPEC J1) and last value bytes (v2.45); never a copy of covered rows (v2.11) | read-set bytes limit, inside `budget` | the publisher's `budget` ledger for **each** subscribing session, in full, though the view is held once (v2.45; v2.11: a send never transfers a charge) |
| client frames in the owner's inbox, not yet decided (v2.8, v2.94) | `C` = 5 (the inbox's capacity); each ≤ max frame bytes on the wire | a `Call` list: its items' call credit, decode included. Other frames: the shared transport pool with a separate typed decode allocation, through the last use at the owner or the head |
| release fences (v2.19, v2.19.1): one `Latest[Fence]` root per joined session, its current `Arc[Fence]`, the next one the publisher publishes, and one a writer may still hold after a publish; one handle at the writer and one at the publisher | one root and three `Arc[Fence]` per session | the root, its first `Arc` and both handles: the session's scope; the next `Arc` and the one a writer holds: reserved at `Joined` on the publisher's `budget` ledger for the session |
| the round register (v2.19, v2.19.1): one `Latest[u64]` root, its current `Arc[u64]`, the next one, the reserved `u64::MAX` one, and one share per writer | one root and three `Arc[u64]` per place, plus one `Arc[u64]` per session | the root and its three `Arc`s: the mailbox's maker; each handle: its session; the `Arc` a writer may hold after a publish: reserved at `Joined` on the publisher's `budget` ledger for the session |
| the frame inside the transport provider | one frame per session: the owner writes a frame without waiting, a writer child finishes a frame the socket would not take (v2.44), and the owner starts the next only after `Sent` or `Dropped`, so the session holds at most one | the shared transport pool, through the writer’s final use. One encoded frame is at most `round8(max_frame + 14)` B |
| the session's Hello keys | a bound on keys derived from the received Hello; the one Registry is the serving task's `n_place` (v2.43) | a bounded key allocation step in the shared pool task: `bound bytes` around `isolate`, so a failure in it drops only that session. Its surviving charges are the pool's when the scope ends |
| the kernel socket buffer | the OS's setting | outside the process |

**WebSocket serving memory.** Both serving paths use `serving_bytes`. The default logical allowance is `H + N × F + P = 1,125,122,048 B`. The supervisor allowance `H` covers the task and channel records outside the owners, bounded allocation and refusal queues, and map growth. (v2.87) It is the supervisor's own allocations, summed in §12.1 "What operate_bytes charges", capped at 64 MiB; at 10,000 owners the sum passes the cap, so the default stays 64 MiB. Each of the 10,000 owners, including pending upgrades, holds one 64 KiB floor `F`. With a wire, each owner also holds the bind term 4096 + `bind_bytes` for its Hello's bind child, apart from `n_granted` (CHANGES-v2.71 B5). The floor includes its task record, child reservations, HTTP and frame scratch, and control and close room. A caller that sets a lower owner limit uses a correspondingly smaller supervisor allowance. The pending upgrade limit is 1,024.

The server shares `P = 384 MiB`. It pays for frame assembly, typed decode, Registry and Hello keys, entry validation, reply backing, publication processing and wire encoding as they allocate. Owners keep their task handles while sending the data needed by an allocation job. The pool holds the charges until the last holder drops them, including requests at the head and part-written output at the writer. A call's reply backing is reserved with its credit and held through `Sent` or session end.

Admission checks a 16 MiB per-session ceiling on pooled work. The bound includes the reservations ρ(O_c) of the connection's outstanding call credit (§3 "Call credit": input, decode, records, replies and refusals), Hello keys, assembly growth, outgoing publication and reply storage, and the active allocation job. Subscription keys, arguments and Callers' subjects (v2.25) also count toward the ceiling while pending in the publisher inbox or retained by a live subscription. A lease returns their room after the payload drops. The 1 MiB publication limit applies to each session’s outgoing queue. Add the head, publisher, ingress and store reservations at the root. Kernel sockets, host allocator overhead and touched task stacks are measured separately.

## 4. Subscriptions

**Design: the query decides, the diff delivers, and read sets invalidate.**

- **G1. Durable release.** The publisher's one inbox is `PubIn` (v2.12, §1.1). It reads only Releases the head pushed after `durable` (V4), plus snapshot-less Releases (V3), together with the sessions' `Joined`, `Subscribed` and `Unsubscribed`, and the internal kinds (§1.1). After each message it sweeps the marked sessions' retirement marks (v2.14). It drains the inbox in order and takes the newest snapshot, so a slow publisher coalesces (built in v2.23: a run of `Released` and `Drained` is taken with `try_receive`, and one round runs at its newest snapshot). Outcomes are never skipped, because they travel in each Release.
- **Pipelining (v2.46, Sam 2026-11-23).** *Permission:* the publisher may run a round's diffs and the hirsel.3 Batch encoding in parallel with the fdatasync and the S3 PUT of the same commit. On `Staged(v, snapshot)` it may run G2 steps 1, 2 and 4 up to encoding at that snapshot: enumerate `changes`, find the hits, re-run the hit views and encode each recipient's Batch. (v2.48) The staged memory is charged to the publisher's own allowance only, never to a session ledger. Everything step 2 would install is held apart with the staged bytes and installed only at the matching Release: each view's new last value, CoverSet and read set, each subscriber's ledger reservation and admission decision, and each `Ended`. The head pushes `Staged` with `try_send` and never waits for it; a refused `Staged` is simply not staged. *Invariant:* the release still waits strictly for `durable`. Nothing staged is visible until the head's `Released` for a durable version d ≥ v arrives: no fence, no R advance, no ledger debit, no `settle` to an owner and no socket flush happens before it, and nothing encoded reaches a socket before `durable` has returned for the level the place requires (local fdatasync, or the S3 PUT on objects@1). When the Release arrives, the publisher re-runs each subscriber's admission against its ledger as it then stands (a refusal ends that subscriber alone, §3 "Admission per subscriber"), installs the held state, runs step 3 (fences, then R = b) and step 4's delivery with the staged encoding, if the Release's snapshot is the one it staged; otherwise it discards the staged work and runs the round as usual. If `durable` fails or the store's health leaves `Ok`, the staged encoding is discarded unsent and the head exits as for poison (§2.1). Staged work changes no state a later round reads, so dropping it is always safe; a coalesced run of `Staged` keeps only the newest.
- **G2. A publisher round** from base snapshot `a` to newest `b`:
  1. `changes(r, a, b)`, through the publisher's Reader (v2.47), gives `Changes{rows, trimmed, bounds}` (STORE-SPEC v2.61 §14.8): each changed data-tree key with its value at `b`, and per trimmed Log one `Trimmed{lo, hi}` span whose keys were all present at `a` and are absent at `b`, and per Log whose (first, next) differs between `a` and `b` one bounds item, the Log's 4-byte id; `changes_page` returns spans clipped to the page's range and bounds items in key order: O(k log n) node visits for k keys written, w ≤ k returned (STORE-SPEC H4). Streams-only commits produce none. (v2.46, astra 1995 #10) The publisher enumerates it in bounded pages (`changes_page`, STORE-SPEC §1.5) and probes the interval index page by page, so a coalesced round over a large backlog holds one page of changes, not the backlog; it marks a view hit once (a per-round mark, as for sessions) and finishes collecting every hit and publishing every fence of the round (step 3) before it offers any update. Cover images (a join, a repair, a new view's first image) and Cover-only diffs are enumerated in pages the same way. Retained group bytes, temporary encodings and per-recipient bookkeeping are charged to the publisher's allowance or the recipient session's ledger before they are allocated. A read error during enumeration is G4 (v2.47): no content of the round is offered from a partial enumeration, and a session whose hits cannot be decided is fenced as hit before R = b.
  2. An interval index holds every **view's** read set (Points and Spans) and CoverSet, all as data-tree key bytes (I1; v2.45: per view, not per subscription). A Change hits when its `tree_key` equals a Point or lies in a Span, by byte compare. (v2.46, W3B-O1) A `Trimmed` hits when its span [lo, hi) overlaps a Point or a Span, a read set's or a CoverSet's. (v2.49, STORE v2.61) A bounds item hits a read set's Point equal to the Log's id, which is what `bounds()` records (STORE §14.4), or a read set's Span that holds it (v2.53). It is matched against read-set entries only, never a CoverSet entry, a tail cover's `Span(id, id + 1)` included, and contributes no slot row. For each view hit:
     - **a changed key in its read set:** re-run the query once with `look(r, b, Some(s), ..)` and project its answer (§4 "Query budgets").
       - `Seen{value: Answer{value, covers}, reads}`: reserve each subscriber's change in charge (§3 "Admission per subscriber"); encode `value`; if the bytes equal the view's last value, the group's `value` is empty (v2.45, value elision), else it is the new bytes and they become the last value. Per slot, send the rows of §3 "Slot images" from the old registration at `a` to the new one at `b` (v2.53; v2.46, astra 1998 #4): the difference by full logical key, removals included, each key once. So a rerun that changes t, turns a tail into a fixed range, drops the slot or moves coverage to another slot sends every removal and insertion, even when no Log was written. A slot's Log never changes (§1.1). Then keep the new CoverSet and read set. A read-set hit fences every subscriber session in step 3 whatever the value and rows are. The publisher keeps no copy of covered rows; it enumerates them from the snapshots it already holds (v2.11, F4).
       - `Failed` or `Refused`: send `Ended(sub, ..)` to every subscriber and end the view. The run's partial read log stays indexed as its ended entry (§3, "Ended entries"). A subscriber whose own ledger refuses the new charge is ended alone (§3 "Admission per subscriber").
       - A `look` Error (`Foreign`): Reset. Each subscription's read set stays indexed as an ended entry. A `Reclaimed`, `Remote` or `Io` Error goes to G4 (v2.47).
     - **otherwise (a Cover-only hit):** send, per hit slot, the rows of §3 "Slot images" from the view's registration at `a` to the same registration at `b`, and an empty `value` (v2.53). For a Table or Cell slot these are the changed keys inside its covers with their values at `b`. A `Trimmed` overlapping a fixed span deletes each key of span ∩ cover that leaves the image, enumerated from `a`. A tail adds its window's symmetric difference. A key in both a fixed span and the window is sent once, and only if it leaves or enters the image or its value changes. `window_changes(a, b, l, T)` gives these rows only on §3's fast path. Neither path reruns anything or retains rows.
  3. **Fences (v2.19, v2.19.1; v2.45: one barrier).** The publisher takes the round's `Arc`s from their reservations, then publishes `Fence{prior: old latest, latest: b}` for each session subscribed to a view with a read-set hit in step 2, a hit on an ended entry included, each session once, and then R = b once (§3 "The release check", "The publisher's order"). A Cover-only hit moves no fence. Before these fences the publisher stages each joined session's room grants with version b, so a changed `Grant` takes effect with R = b (§4 "Rooms"). Nothing of round b is offered before this step ends.
  4. Each session with groups, or with outcomes from the drained Releases, gets one update for the group containing its content `{version: b, complete, groups}` (each group the view's delta or full-image bytes, spliced behind the session's `sub`; none for a due subscriber, §3 "Repair") and its `Told`s in its owner's inbox. The owner builds one Batch or Reset with one outcome row for every send still owed, including local joins and sessions without subscriptions (§3).
- **The Caller (v2.25).** Every run of a `query_as` body, at G2's re-run, G3's Subscribe and a repair, gets the Caller in the subscription's input, which is the subscribing session's. A `query` body gets none. (v2.45) A `query_as` view is shared only by sessions whose Callers' codec bytes are equal, so the Caller a run gets is every subscriber's.
- **G3. Subscribe** (and a v2.20 repair, §3) joins the live view of its view key with no run when the view is validated through the current base (§3 "Joining"), or makes a view and runs the query with `look` at the publisher's base (v2.45, §3 "Shared views"), and sends its full result, value and slot rows, at that version, **whole in one Batch whose `complete` names the sub, an empty result included** (v2.11, F11). No row for that sub is sent before that Batch. When several initial results each fit but do not fit together, they go in separate Batches at the same version. `Failed` or `Refused` answers `Ended` at the base, and the run's partial read log stays indexed as an ended entry (§3).
  - **The base is defined by inbox order (v2.12).** It is the version of the last `Released` the publisher took from `PubIn` before this `Subscribed`. Releases taken after it reach this subscription as G2 rounds.
  - **A room membership (v2.58)** is a G3 Subscribe of the room's admission view with argument A, refused as §4 "Rooms" says. Its `Grant` and slot rows arrive in this Batch, and it joins the roster only through the relay (§4 "Rooms").
  - A Subscribe of a (session, sub) that is still live replaces the old subscription, with no `Ended` for the old one. Its `Subscribed` carries a new instance and is sent only while the writer holds no unit, and the owner discards the old instance's content (§3, v2.19.1).
- **G4. A failed read (v2.47, astra 1990 #9).** A G2 round, a G3 Subscribe and a repair each **hold** what they read (rows, CoverSet, read set, encoded group) privately, and adopt it only when every read of that work succeeded. A partial `changes` is never a diff.
  - **While pipelining.** A read that fails during a pass at `Staged(v, snapshot)` only discards that staged work: no fence, no R and no Reset come from it. The round runs again when its Release arrives, and G4 applies only if a read fails there.
  - **On `Reclaimed`, `Remote` or `Io`** from `changes`, `look`, a row conversion or a cursor, the publisher discards the held work of that read, logs the typed cause, and neither retries inline nor poisons the Db.
  - **Affected sessions.** If the failure was in one view's work, the affected sessions are that view's subscribers. If it was in `changes` or another shared enumeration, which cannot say which views were hit, every indexed session is affected, ended entries included.
  - **Order.** The round goes on with the views it read in full. Step 3 then publishes the fences of every affected session together with the known-hit fences, then R = b once. Step 4 then offers a `Reset` to each affected session, which carries every owed `Told` of the drained Releases exactly once, under the existing instance and outcome rules (§3). Unaffected sessions get their round as usual.
  - **Restart.** The affected views end; their read sets stay indexed as ended entries (§3). The client resubscribes, and the publisher runs G3 at a fresh base, which is the version of the last `Released` it took (inbox order, never a `Staged` snapshot and never a Snapshot taken from the Db); the first content of each new instance is a whole `complete`.
  - **A failure at an unchanged base.** A G3 Subscribe or a repair that fails without a new round retires the failed instance through the owner protocol, because a fence at the same version cannot invalidate content of that version: the publisher offers that session a publisher-made `Reset` carrying the last instance it took and the session's owed outcomes, and the owner records its last instance as retired and `Retire`s it (§3 "Subscription instances", "The owner on `Dropped`"). Content of the failed instance that arrives later is discarded. Its read log stays indexed as an ended entry.
- Every `fetch` records a Point. `lookup` records its index Span and one primary-key Point for each row it yields (J1). `range` and `range_reverse` record each inspected gap, including the row that ends it, before decoding. In a forward scan the inspected prefix ends just after the last row. In a reverse scan it starts at the last row. A probe that returns None records the remaining gap up to the requested bound. Opening a cursor alone records nothing. The read set includes reads that decide the Caller's access (v2.19). A `cover` records no read.
- **Release (ruling 73 as amended).** A content unit a session holds is written only if no read-set hit of that session after the unit's version has taken effect (§3 "The release check"). The client applies a Batch whole, removals included, before it shows any value from it. After a `Reset` it hides its old cache until each fresh `complete` or `Ended`.
- **After a drop and recreate:** only across opens (rule 12). Every subscription is then Reset, because a resume token from an earlier open is unknown (`changes` is Foreign across opens), and Subscribe runs against the new H.

**Cost.**
- **Per commit:** one `changes`, plus O(w log E) to look up E index entries, plus one query run per **view** whose read set is hit (v2.45), plus one splice per subscriber of a view with content.
- **Memory:** each view keeps its CoverSet, read set and last value once, however many sessions subscribe to it (v2.45); each subscriber is charged the full amount on its session's `budget` ledger, so admission is as without sharing. Subscribed memory is flat in the number of covered rows (L20). A per-subscription copy of the full checkbox board would be 273,560 B (2.548 GiB at 10,000 sessions; WIRE-SPEC §3).
- **Checkbox app:** a toggle hits no read set, so it costs a route plus about 21 B per matching viewer (GUESS, TS-SDK's figure).
- **A subscription over a query that looks up 10,000 rows** (ESTIMATE, from STORE-SPEC J1b's RUN parts):
  - Read set: 480 KB (100 B + 10,000 × 48 B), charged to its session's `budget`. The default `budget` must exceed it, or Subscribe is `Refused(NotAllowed)`.
  - Interval index: 10,001 entries for this subscription.
  - **A commit that hits none of them** costs the publisher O(w log E) for its w changes, with log₂ E ≈ 14 compares per change at E ≈ 10⁴. This subscription adds nothing beyond its share of E.
  - **A commit that hits one** (a looked-up row's value changes, or a row enters or leaves the J range) costs a full re-run: about 10,000 × 4.4k ≈ 44 M instructions (index step, primary get, decode, and 400 for recording each Point), roughly 10 ms. On top come removing and re-inserting 10,001 index entries, O(r log E), and diffing old against new results.
  - So one subscription whose rows change on every commit costs about 10 ms of publisher time per commit, and the publisher coalesces.
- **Worst case:** every commit writes a key that every query reads. The cost is then design A's: a re-run per subscriber.

**Query code runs in the publisher.** A query body runs inside `look`'s `isolate`. The publisher projects rows from the `Answer` that `look` returns, after it returns `Seen`. A body has no cell to project into (rule 4, v2.47). A fault or an overrun ends only the subscriptions whose work it stopped (below). The publisher's other work runs at the slice ends of whatever is running meanwhile (D5, v2.7).

**Query budgets (v2.55).** A view's evaluation has two phases. Each phase gets the entry's registered step bound `s`, and both run inside one `bound bytes n`. No step bound is current around either phase. The publisher is spawned under bytes only, and its staged pass runs under `bound bytes` only. The head inherits whatever its own starter had, so `operate`, `platform.serve` and §14.9's actor must start it with no step bound current, on every backing (v2.54 "No bound"; v2.55.1). That is the caller's contract, because nothing can name a bound (MEM-30). The adapter's shape:

```hirsel
bound bytes n {
    let attempt = QueryAttempt(..)   // env: the StaticFn, H, Caller, argument bytes, and the A and R codecs
    match store.look(read_handle, newest, Some(s), attempt, query_attempt) {
        Ok(Seen(seen)) => bound steps s { isolate(fn() { matched_covers(..); rerun_rows(..) or cover_rows(..) }) },
        ..
    }
}
```

- **The run** is `query_attempt`, `look`'s body. It decodes A, calls the query and encodes R inside its `isolate`. So a query's decode and encode steps are the entry's, as for compute and transact (v2.7 item 3).
  - `look` gets `Some(s)` on every store. Each attempt runs in its own bound of `s` (STORE §13.3 "Retry-capable calls are bounded", "Enclosing step bounds").
  - A run that is `Stopped`, a query that never returns included, is `Failed(OutOfTime(s))`. A decode error is `Refused(Malformed(..))`. A raised fault or an encode error is `Failed(Raised(..))`. An allocation failure the body's isolate catches is `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))`.
- **The projection** matches the answer's covers to the slots and enumerates their rows from the Reader. A rerun uses `rerun_rows` from the old registration at `a`, and a first run uses `cover_rows`. A fresh `bound steps s` covers the entire projection. Waiting Reader calls return typed errors. Non-waiting isolates catch failures in cover matching, row conversion and allocation (v2.55.2).
  - A projection stopped at `s` is `Failed(OutOfTime(s))`.
  - An allocation that fails inside `n` gives `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))` through a local isolate or typed Reader error. That covers rows, temporary encodings and the `Log[Row]` growth the scope admits (MEM-29, MEM-19). A `bound bytes` scope catches nothing after entry (MEM-25).
  - A `store.Error` from a Reader read keeps G4 for `Reclaimed`, `Remote` and `Io`. Its `Full(c)` stays `Failed(OutOfBytes(room(c)))`.
  - The `Seen` value, its read set and the encoded R stay live across the projection inside the same `n`. So they and the projected rows fit `n` together.
  - **Byte outcomes by source (v2.55.1):**
    - `n` cannot be granted at entry: `Refused(Budget(room))`, for every subscriber of the view.
    - Exhaustion inside `n`, in the run or in the projection: `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))`.
    - A Reader's `Full(c)`: `Failed(OutOfBytes(room(c)))`.
    - A subscriber's `budget` ledger refusing its charge: `Refused(Budget(room))`, for that subscriber only (§3 "Admission per subscriber").
    "Conversion" in G4 and L28 is this projection.
- **What an overrun ends.**
  - It ends only the subscriptions whose rows were being projected: every live subscriber of the view for a rerun, a Cover-only round or a first run, and only the joiner for a join image or a repair. These are the evaluation failures of §3 "Admission per subscriber", common to the view.
  - Each gets `Ended(sub, Failed(OutOfTime(s)))` or `Ended(sub, Failed(OutOfBytes(room)))` at the round's version.
  - The session's other subscriptions, the other views, the round and the publisher go on.
  - The ended entry is the read set of the run whose answer was being projected (§3 "Ended entries"). For a rerun or a first run, that is the new run's complete read set, which `Seen` returned. For a Cover-only round, a join or a repair, it is the view's current read set. A projection overrun is never answered with no reads indexed. A run stopped at `s` indexes the reads it made before the stop, returned by `Looked.Stopped` (STORE v2.73).
- **A round that reuses a view's CoverSet** runs only the projection. A Cover-only hit (G2) and a join or repair to a live view (§3 "Joining", "Repair") run it under the same `bound bytes n { bound steps s { .. } }` and the same outcomes. So all three paths charge projection one way, and a Cover-only round never charges a run it did not make.
- **Tiered.** On a store with a Tiered collection, the Reader reads a projection makes (`next`, `changes_page`) run attempts. Every one of their attempts is charged to the projection's bound. The attempt count and each attempt's steps follow from the root and the projection, never from the cache (STORE §13.0 "Eviction is never program-visible", §13.3 "Cost"). So on one build, whether a projection stops at `s` does not depend on residency. Across builds and layouts, steps stay per-build fuel (STORE §13.0 Q1).
- **Worst case.** A view evaluation takes at most (a + 1)·s steps: a run attempts plus one projection. With no Tiered collection, a = 1. On a Tiered store, a is bounded by STORE §13.3 "Termination". Before v2.55, s was meant to cover both phases together.
- **Why not one shared budget.** A remainder handoff would need a core operation that names a bound (MEM-30) or a step count in `Looked`. Both would make steps a program-visible value, against STORE §13.0 Q1. Under retry there is also no single remainder to hand over (CHANGES-v2.55).

**Reset** is sent when the base is gone, when coalesced rows cannot fit `budget`, when `look` returns an Error, when a read fails (G4, v2.47), when a resume token is unknown, or when the writer drops a content unit (§3). A token from an earlier open is always unknown. A `Reset` the release check causes never counts toward the protocol-error close (1008).

**Resume, as built (v2.12, R2-i).**
- Rung 1 mints no token, so every `Some(token)` is unknown and is answered with `Reset`. The SDK sends `resume: None` on every Subscribe (sdk/client/README.md).
- On a `Reset` or transport loss, the client clears the public image immediately and issues a data-free invalidation after all affected subscriptions become Pending. It ignores groups for retired ids. A new instance's first `complete` publishes an image from empty (F11). A repair's `complete` carries a full image, and the SDK publishes its difference from the current image.
- A token that names open, version and subscription state, with bounded retention, is a later rung. Its field and its meaning are added together.

**Rooms (v2.58; rewritten in v2.59 as built at 54f8dc37).** A room carries live presence between the sessions that hold a membership in it. Each `Join` member has one typed State S that every member sees and can send addressed Messages M to the whole room or to one peer. S and M belong to the application. The runner knows only their codecs and bounds and names no application type, so the primitive stays general and domain-agnostic (Sam's ruling). Nothing in a room is stored, replayed, recorded (§7) or ordered against commits. Evidence is READ from `core/programs/runner/runner-room-relay.hirsel` and the other `runner-room*.hirsel` files unless a law is named.

- **The entry and its admission view.** `runner.room` (§1.1) registers a `Room` entry: an admission view of `query_as`'s shape, plus the room's codecs and bounds. A membership is a Subscribe to that entry with argument A. The room is named by (entry, A's bytes), so sessions that subscribe with equal argument bytes share one room. The admission view is an ordinary shared view (G2, G3, §3 "Shared views"). Its `Grant` value and slot rows arrive in ordinary Batch groups, its read set fences, and a commit that changes its answer re-grants. Under rule 13 the runner makes no auth decision. It enforces the Grant the view returns, mechanically:

  | Grant | holds a roster place | `Set` | `Send` to the room | `Send` to one peer | receives Parts |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `Denied` | no | dropped | dropped | dropped | none |
  | `Watch` | no | dropped | dropped | dropped | yes |
  | `Join` | yes | yes | yes | yes | yes |

- **When a grant takes effect.** Each round, the publisher stages every joined session's room grants with that round's version b. It does this after G2 step 2 and before step 3's fences. Each session has one grant register, which holds the prior set, the latest set and b. The owner and the relay read the latest set only once R ≥ b, so a grant change takes effect together with the round's fences and R = b. Changes from one round to the next:
  - any grant to `Denied` ends the membership;
  - `Join` to `Watch` frees the roster place and clears the member's state. The watcher receives a fresh complete image without `you`;
  - `Watch` to `Join` waits for a roster place and receives a new peer id on admission;
  - `Denied` to `Watch` or `Join` starts a new membership under that grant.

  A session that ends (`Left`), and a publisher `Halt`, revoke all of a session's grants.
- **Refusals at Subscribe.** A room Subscribe is refused as any Subscribe is (§3: `max_subs`, the subscription quota), and also in these cases:
  - an encoded argument longer than the entry's `arg_limit`: `Ended(sub, Refused(Budget(Room{requested: |A|, available: arg_limit})))`;
  - a ninth live room membership on one session (`ROOMS_PER_SESSION` = 8): `Refused(NotAllowed)`;
  - an owner that cannot allocate its room state: `Refused(Budget(..))`.
- **Admission to the roster.** One relay task per place holds every roster.
  - **Waiting.** A granted membership waits in one FIFO across the place, ordered by when its grant took effect. The relay admits the oldest waiter that fits: a `Join` needs a free roster place (`RoomBounds.peers`), and a `Watch` can use a full roster. Either can start a roster record if it fits the relay's room bytes.
  - **`full`.** A waiter the relay cannot place gets one Part with `full` set, no `you` and `complete` false, once per wait.
  - **Admission.** On admission to a roster place the relay mints the member's peer id. Both kinds of membership receive a first Part with `complete`. A `Join` also receives `you` (law `first_admission_installs_adm`).
  - **Peer ids.** Ids increase from 1 within each incarnation. The pair (incarnation, peer id) identifies an admission. Peer 0 names no member.
  - **Incarnation and revision.** A roster stays live while it has a peer, watcher or waiter. The relay draws a 16-byte incarnation from the place's randomness on creation and before the first admission to a retained roster after its last peer and watcher leave. Each incarnation starts with peer counter 1 and revision 1. Each roster join and removal adds 1 to the revision.
  - **Exhaustion.** A roster whose next id reaches 2³² − 1 admits no more peers. Waiters with `Join` stay `full`.
  - **The waiter guarantee.** A Join can publish only after the owner accepts the Part that carries `you`. So a `Set` or `Send` from a waiter, from a sub not yet live or from an earlier membership is dropped. A value set while waiting never becomes the member's state. The member's first state is its first `Set` after `you` (law `waiter_set_not_installed_after_promotion`).
- **Roster diffs.** For each roster place, a member keeps the peer id it was last told and a changed mark. A join, a leave or a state change marks that place for every member. For each place a Part includes, the Part carries:
  - `joined`: (peer, Caller), for a peer id the receiver has not been told;
  - `states`: (peer, the current state bytes, or `None` for no state);
  - `left`: a told peer id that is gone.

  The receiver applies `joined`, `states`, `msgs` and `left` in that order. The `complete` Part of a membership starts a new roster image of every occupied place. When that image does not fit one Part, the rest follows in later Parts. A state diff carries the current value, not each `Set`, so states coalesce and the newest wins. Every peer's Caller is in `joined`, which is how a receiver knows who sent a message.
- **Messages.**
  - **Message blocks.** Each session owner holds `ROOM_MSG_BLOCKS` = 4 message blocks of the place's largest message bound. A `Send` takes one. With none free, the `Send` is dropped and its sender's `lost` gains 1. The relay returns a block once it has copied the message.
  - **Targets.** The relay copies the message into each target's ring. A `Send` to the room reaches every other peer and every watcher. A `Send` to peer p reaches only the member whose id is p. Peer 0, and an id that is not a member, reach no one (law `direct_destination_survives_full_block`). The relay checks the sender's grant and each receiver's grant at the current round.
  - **The ring.** Each session has one receive ring of `ROOM_RING_BYTES` = 65,536 bytes, shared by all its rooms. Each message in the ring is tagged with the receiver's admission. A message that does not fit adds 1 to that receiver's `gap`. A message tagged with an earlier admission of the receiver is dropped silently.
  - **The sender's `joined` first.** A message is delivered only once the receiver has been told the sender's id, in an earlier Part or in the same one. A message whose sender left before that is dropped, and the receiver's `gap` gains 1.
  - **Order.** Messages from one sender to one receiver arrive in send order. No other order holds: none across senders, and none between `Live` and `Batch`.
- **Delivery under a full block.**
  - **Buffers.** Each session has two Live buffers, each of min(`LIVE_MAX`, `socket.max_frame`) bytes. When a buffer is free, the relay fills it with at most one Part per room that has something to send, round-robin over the session's rooms.
  - **A blocked write.** While a write is blocked, its buffer stays held and the relay fills only the other one. Messages that arrive meanwhile wait in the ring, and overflow becomes `gap`.
  - **What a Part takes first.** A Part first reserves room for the oldest deliverable message and its sender's diff, then fills with other diffs. When a maximum message leaves too little room for the next diff, that diff takes the first reservation in the next Part. So sustained messages never starve roster diffs (laws `message_delivered_under_full_state_churn`, `roster_diffs_served_under_sustained_messages`).
  - **`Live` is not content.** It takes no fence, no `budget` and no `Dropped`. When the owner takes a filled buffer, it drops every Part whose membership the session's grant register, read at R, no longer holds. So a member is sent no Part after the round that revoked it is published.
- **Decode capacity and loss.** The owner checks each `Set` and `Send` against the room's codecs.
  - These close the session with 1002:
    - bytes over the registered `state` or `message` bound;
    - bytes that fail strict decode;
    - a `Set` or `Send` that names a live sub that is not a room;
    - a sub id never named.
  - A decode runs in an isolate under `bound bytes 2·|bytes| + 4096`. A decode that exhausts that bound is dropped, the sender's `lost` gains 1, and the sub stays live (law `room_decode_capacity_reports_loss`).
  - These are dropped silently:
    - a `Set` or `Send` the grant does not permit (the table above);
    - one for a retired sub id;
    - one from a session that is not yet a member;
    - a `Send` to peer 0 or to a non-member.
  - `gap` counts messages to this member that were dropped. `lost` counts messages and values from this member that were dropped. Each is counted since the membership's last Part, is reset when a Part carries it, and saturates at 2³² − 1.
- **Frames.**
  - **Client to server.** `Set(sub, Option[Bytes])` has tag 9 and `Send(sub, Option[u32], Bytes)` has tag 10.
  - **Server to client.** `Live(List[Part])` has tag 11. A `Part` is `{sub: u32, complete: bool, you: Option[u32], incarnation: (u64, u64), revision: u64, snapshot: bool, joined: List[(u32, Caller)], states: List[(u32, Option[Bytes])], msgs: List[(u32, bool, Bytes)], left: List[u32], gap: u32, lost: u32, full: bool}`. Two little-endian words carry the 16 incarnation bytes in order. `snapshot` means all pending roster changes through the given revision have been told after applying this Part. A `full` Part for a waiter carries zero incarnation bytes and revision 0, with `snapshot` false. In `msgs`, the bool marks a direct send.
  - **Size.** A `Live` frame is at most min(`LIVE_MAX`, `socket.max_frame`) bytes. A Part with a room's maximal state and maximal message needs 169 + `state` + `message` bytes. If that does not fit, the place refuses to start with `Exceeds(Bound("socket.max_frame", ..))`.
  - **Vectors.** Law `frame_sizer_matches_vectors` pins the `Set`, `Send` and `Live` encodings against `core/programs/runner/frame-vectors.json`.
- **Session charging (MEM-20, L15).**
  - **The owner.** A place with no room entries reserves zero owner or relay bytes for rooms. For a place that declares rooms, each owner task reserves:
    - the desk with two Live buffers at min(`LIVE_MAX`, `socket.max_frame`) and its ring;
    - four message blocks at the largest declared message size;
    - one decode at the largest declared state or message size;
    - `ROOMS_PER_SESSION` times the largest membership charge of any declared room. Each charge uses that room's `arg_limit`, `state` and `peers`.

    The blocks keep the owner's record when they move to the relay. The start check counts this reservation inside `WIRE_SESSION_CEILING`, and `operate_bytes` counts it in each owner's floor (law `room_session_charge_covers_maximum_session`).
  - **The relay.** `n_relay` must hold the relay's inbox, `ROOM_RELAY_TOKENS` per session, its desk table, and the largest roster charge of any declared room. Each roster's bound uses that room's `arg_limit` and `peers`. It includes a reusable Live selection buffer with one entry per roster slot. The caller declares a positive peer count, which the roster and session reservations must fund. If the budget is too small, the place refuses to start and names `n_relay`. Each live roster is charged against the rest of `n_relay` using its actual argument size and declared peer count. A room whose roster cannot be charged leaves its waiters `full`.
  - **The admission view.** It is charged on the session's `budget` ledger like any subscription.
- **Leaving.** A membership ends on `Unsubscribe`, on a grant of `Denied`, or when the session ends. If it held a roster place, the relay frees it and marks it changed so receivers that saw the peer get `left`. A room sub that is leaving does not block its successor (law `leaving_sub_does_not_block_successor`). Rooms exist only in sessions (§14.4), so `serve` votes idle only when no owner remains. A bound session's expiry ends its memberships with the session (L62; the room case is OPEN there).
- **Kick.** An application removes a member by making its admission view return `Denied`. The kick API is specified in docs/spec/webrtc.md ("Build slices").
- **Constants** (GUESS values; changing one changes `hirsel.5` only where it bounds a frame): `STATE_MAX` 8,192, `MESSAGE_MAX` 32,768, `LIVE_MAX` 65,536, `ROOM_ARGS_MAX` 1,024, `ROOMS_PER_SESSION` 8, `ROOM_MSG_BLOCKS` 4, `ROOM_RING_BYTES` 65,536.
- **The nine room laws** (RUN: green at 54f8dc37 per the hirsel4-cutover-2276 REPORT, which counts nine room laws but names only laws 1–8 below. This spec counts the room frame vector law as the ninth; all in `core/programs/runner/runner_owner_laws.hirsel` except the vector law, which is in `core/programs/runner/runner-wire-protocol-law.hirsel`):
  1. `first_admission_installs_adm`: the first member gets peer 1 and the second peer 2, each in a `complete` Part with `you`. A broadcast reaches the other member once. A third session gets `full`, and on a leave it is promoted as peer 3, whose broadcast then arrives once. The view allowance exceeds the argument limit. A roomless place costs the same with or without `n_relay`. A Watch member receives an image and a broadcast without appearing in the roster or sending a direct message.
  2. `waiter_set_not_installed_after_promotion`: a waiter's `Set` before promotion is never shown. An observer sees the promoted peer's state as `None`, then the value set after `you`.
  3. `direct_destination_survives_full_block`: two direct sends of a maximal message each reach their own peer once and no one else. Sends to peer 0 and to an id that is not a member reach no one.
  4. `message_delivered_under_full_state_churn`: one buffer is held, then eight maximal states and one maximal message arrive. The message is delivered with three state diffs and `gap` 0. The oracle is hand-derived: 65,536 − 42 − 32,777 bytes holds three 8,204-byte diffs.
  5. `roster_diffs_served_under_sustained_messages`: a maximal message is always pending, and all eight pending diffs still arrive within two Parts.
  6. `room_decode_capacity_reports_loss`: a `Set` whose decode exceeds its bound reports `lost` = 1, and the sub stays live.
  7. `room_session_charge_covers_maximum_session`: eight maximal memberships, the desk, the message blocks and one largest decode all fit inside the reservation.
  8. `leaving_sub_does_not_block_successor`: a waiting removal comes before its one charged successor, an excess Subscribe is refused, and an idle removal makes progress. A `Set` or `Send` naming an unnamed id closes with 1002.
  9. `frame_sizer_matches_vectors`: the hand-packed `hirsel.5` vectors, `Set`, `Send`, `Live`, `Window` and `Credit` among them, and the 48-byte Done size.

  `room_admits_300_funded_peers` admits the 300th peer and checks that startup with insufficient relay bytes names `n_relay`.

  The client half is docs/spec/sdk.md "Rooms", checked by `sdk.client:packed_consumer`.

## 5. Exported entries, the generator and non-termination

- **D1 (ruled by Sam 2026-11-16, gavel 2; the server half is built, W4a).** `hirsel sdk ts <pkg>` builds the package, then launches **the built server executable** in describe mode (`--hirsel-describe`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, with `--hirsel-n-place=<bytes>`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, default 16 MiB). Describe is the first branch of `runner.main(a, place, start_server)`, before any store, listener or capability use.
  - It runs `place` under `n_place` and answers `Describe{digest, target, n_place, exported: List[Exported{described, args_fp, result_fp, revision, slots, value}], tables}` (v2.45, Describe v2: `slots` is a query's `List[(name, key schema, value schema)]` and `value` its R schema, both empty for other kinds; `tables` lists each table and index the place declares, from a `Migrate` run of `schema` on an empty memory store, for the generator's diagnostics; it is not part of the release identity).
  - Or it answers `DescribeError = PlaceCapacity(Room) | AlreadyRegistered(Bytes)` and exits with status 2.
  - The generator records the digest, target and `n_place` in the bindings (rule 10). Laws `place_run_bounded`, `identity_stable` and `identity_unavailable` (W4a). RUN p09 does this by hand: 447 B for three entries.
- **Bounding `place`.** The generator runs it in a child process with a wall-clock limit T (default 10 s, GUESS) and the byte limit `n_place`:
  - on timeout it kills the child and reports `PlaceTimedOut`;
  - if `n_place` is exhausted, it reports `PlaceCapacity(Room)`;
  - `AlreadyRegistered` is reported with the identity.
- **Why one executable, target and bound are required.** `place` is capability-free and has no inputs, so its result depends only on the code it runs and its byte bound. RUN p19 mode 1 shows a `place` that branches on Capacity: its list differs between bounds and is equal under one bound. Another build, another target or another bound may give another list.
- **Cost of every task running `place`** (release, LLVM, perf stat `instructions:u`):

  | Entries | Built R0 (RUN at bf7cf64f) | Probe p19 (RUN at 62f15988) | Bytes (smallest bound that fits) |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | 1 | — | 4,123 instr | 896 (p19) |
  | 100 | **5,289 instr per entry** | 5,069 per entry, 0.066 ms | **49,504 (R0)**; 45,888 (p19) |
  | 1,000 | waits on reuse-history-fault-1031 (64 KiB task stack overflow in the identity dispatch) | 6,422 per entry, 0.906 ms | 419,648 (p19) |
  | 3,000, split into 30 functions | — | 9,531 per entry (28.6 M per `place`) | — |
  | 10,000 | waits on 1031 | `run stop: Stack` with 10,000 functions even when only 2 are registered | — |

  The cost per entry grows with the program's function count because generic `code.identity` scans every function. 1031's redesign removes that growth, after which the 1,000- and 10,000-entry rows are re-run on the built R0.

- **A transact body or a query that never returns** is stopped at its entry's step bound (D5 ruled (a), v2.7), and other tasks run at its slice ends until then.

**R13 text (N9):** unchanged from v2.1; the text is in followups.md.

## 6. Upgrades

The release is the place's **release identity** and the encoded `Describe` that carries it (v2.42, Spec Q5; "One identity" below). The runner owns two tables. In `hirsel.runner.activation: Table[u8, Bytes]`, key 0 holds `current` and key 1 holds `previous`. The append-only `hirsel.runner.releases: Table[u64, ReleaseRecord]` maps each activation commit version to `{ release: Bytes, digest: Bytes, prepared: Bytes }`: the release, the executable image digest (a diagnostic since v2.42: no rule compares it), and (v2.35) the attempt witness, the SHA-256 of the validated PREPARED import that a prepared install activated, empty for genesis and a standalone install. The store writes `prepared` from the import it validated and from nothing else (STORE v2.48 §3.5a, §12.7). Its greatest key is the current activation version, even after later application commits.

- **Ordinary open.** The head keeps STORE's `LOCK` while comparing the launch's expected activation version and identity digest with the recovered version and the identity digest of the `Describe` in `current`. A match runs `schema` in `Open` mode without running `on_migrate` or writing activation (v2.45). A mismatch returns `NotCurrent(Active(version, current))` before handles. Empty activation permits genesis at expected version zero.
- **objects@1 reading (v2.34, STORE v2.47 §12.1, §12.3).** Every rule of this section, §12.1 and §14 worded "under `LOCK`" or "the process holding `LOCK`" reads on objects@1 as "holding the claim; the conditional head write decides". The comparisons move into `store.open`, before any write: the runner passes the launch's expected activation as `o.activation`, its 32-byte identity digest as `o.binding` (v2.42, STORE v2.55; `None` for `Install`, which is authorized to replace the active identity), and, on a hosted start, the expected store ID as `o.expect`. A mismatch is `NotCurrent(v)` or `Foreign` from the store before its claim replaces the head, so a rejected identity or store never deposes the serving holder. The runner maps `NotCurrent(v)` to `NotCurrent(Active(v, current))`, reading `current` through a `follower`. That follower is the one capture of §14.3, provisioned as "The capture's service" says (v2.54), so the cause and the witness come from one read. On log@1 the comparison after open under `LOCK` stays as built, and a probe refused there reads its deploy's witness before it releases `LOCK` (§14.3, v2.37).
- **Restart and rebuild (store-restart-upgrade-1797).** A launch selects one of three things. `Current(v)`, from `--hirsel-activation=v`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, expects the store at version `v`, and `Current(0)` expects an empty store. `Install`, from `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, is the explicit install below. `Reopen`, the selection of a launch with neither option, selects whatever the store holds: genesis on an empty store, and otherwise the recovered activation version under `LOCK`. Either way the ordinary open's `Describe` comparison then decides. So a plain restart of the same executable reopens its store at its current activation and writes nothing. A store is bound to the release identity that activated it (v2.42, Spec Q5; "One identity" below), not to an executable. The same code built by Cranelift or LLVM, at another optimisation level, as a recording build, or by another toolchain whose lowering leaves the checked IR alone, has the same identity and opens the store; a launch with other code, another `n_place`, another exported list, other HTTP route bindings or other slot schemas is refused `NotCurrent(Active(version, current))` before handles. The refusal names the stored version and identity digest and this launch's, and, as a diagnostic, both executable digests. There are two upgrade paths. Standalone: stop the old process, then start the new build once with `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, which runs `migrate` and installs the new activation over the old data (below); later starts are plain restarts again. Hosted: `hirsel deploy` (launch.md "Hot deploy") does the prepared cutover. The platform host's serving starts always pass `--hirsel-activation`<!-- doc-ref: external --> for the activation they chose, so a discarded cutover candidate is still refused `NotCurrent` (§14.1).
- **One n_place (store-restart-upgrade-1797; v2.42).** A place has one `n_place`. A launch whose private prefix holds `--hirsel-n-place`<!-- doc-ref: external --> runs at that allowance in every mode: a serving start, a standalone `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external -->, a describe or preview, a prepare. The platform host and launcher always pass it, with the record's recipe allowance. A launch without it runs at `PlaceConfig.n_place` (and a describe at the 16 MiB default, since it has no `PlaceConfig`). The runtime records whether the option was given, so an explicit allowance equal to the default is still explicit. The `session` configuration carries no `n_place` (v2.45, DX Q6: `WireConfig.n_place` and its `Disagrees` check are deleted); each session takes the one value the head runs at. A place running at an explicit allowance takes it for the head's and the serving task's bound and the task's byte budget (`operate_bytes`), and does not read `PlaceConfig.n_place`. That one value bounds `place` in the head, the publisher and the serving task, enters the release identity, and `Describe`, `Status` and Welcome all report it. A generated SDK carries the value it was generated with (`hirsel sdk ts --n-place`), and the client reports both values when Welcome differs. Evaluator and embedded launch configurations state the allowance and whether it is explicit. **Order (v2.41):** the launcher reads private options only from the leading run of recognised private options (launch.md "Describe launch"); the first other argument ends that prefix, and it and every later argument are `Args`. `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> and `--hirsel-activation`<!-- doc-ref: external --> are `Args` (`launch_selection`), so an install at a hosted allowance is `./place --hirsel-n-place=<bytes> --hirsel-install`, and one written with `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> first runs at `PlaceConfig.n_place`, its `launch_selection` skipping the misplaced option. An install given the wrong allowance has another identity, matches no record of its host, and the no-record cause prints each candidate's allowance next to the read's (§14.3 "What serves"), so the mismatch is visible and the argv is copyable. (v2.40's rule that a launch naming an assembly record runs at the launch's allowance is replaced by this one: the allowance is explicit or it is not, whatever else the launch names.)
- **One identity (v2.42, Spec Q5).** The **release identity** is the value `ReleaseIdentity { revision, n_place, exported, bindings, slots }` (v2.65; `runner-identity.hirsel`): the `code.revision` (PROG-9) of the program's entry, which the runtime supplies in the launch metadata (launch.md "Immutable launch identity"); the allowance the place runs at ("One n_place"); the exported list in registration order, each entry as its `described`, `args_fp`, `result_fp` and `revision`; the HTTP route bindings `Describe.bindings` (each an `HttpDescribe`: the binding and its defaults); and, per export in the same order, the query slot schemas `Describe` reports for it. The entry's revision covers the checked IR of every instance the program reaches, so it covers `place`, `on_migrate` and `schema` (v2.45) and, through them, every table name, key and value codec, index extractor, projection and schema they declare: the store schema is in the identity through the revision, with no second fingerprint. `bindings` and `slots` are taken from `Describe` as it reports them, so a change to an HTTP route binding or to a query slot schema is a new identity even when the revision is unchanged. It excludes the backend, the runtime, the target, the optimisation level and recording machinery (PROG-9, CAP-9). The **identity digest** is SHA-256 over `u8 18 ‖ "hirsel.identity.v1" ‖` the value codec's bytes of the `ReleaseIdentity`, 32 bytes. It is the one key every rule that asks "is this the release the store holds" compares: the store open (`o.image`, STORE §12.1), the runner's post-open comparison on log@1, the host's match of a store's activation to an assembly record (§14.3 "What serves", "Leaving no record"), and a granted trigger's revision stamp (§14.6). `release` (`release_digest`, §12.2) stays the digest of the exported list alone, which clients and the status route compare; it is a function of a part of the identity and never decides a store open. The executable digest and target are diagnostics: `Describe`, `ReleaseRecord.digest`, Welcome, `Status` and refusal messages report them, and no rule compares them. The assembly record's name is the host's name for what it launches and is in no launch metadata and no `Describe`: v2.38's `Describe.assembly` and the `--hirsel-assembly`<!-- doc-ref: historical --> option are deleted, never built.
- **Genesis and explicit install.** `--hirsel-install`<!-- doc-ref: external --> authorizes the process holding `LOCK` to install without a serving predecessor. Stop the old writer and its automatic restarts before the first install. On objects@1 an install's claim deposes a writer that was not stopped, which then ends on `Fenced` (§2.1); stopping it first still avoids that churn. A disk install or genesis with an unavailable release identity (the launch metadata carries no entry revision, as in a program assembled without revisions) returns `IdentityUnknown` (rule 10; v2.42: an unavailable executable digest alone no longer makes the identity unavailable). Memory and temporary places may use an unavailable identity. The head runs `place`, then `on_migrate` and `schema` in `Migrate` mode (v2.45), and stages the activation pair and `releases[v+1]`, and (v2.62) one activation rewrite per held trigger row, settled by the install's fates (§14.6 "The activation fate"; an unsettled one refuses `Unsettled` before staging). It then publishes the catalog and level-1 `FENCE` with the rows in one AM2 cut. If no previous activation exists, the previous row is absent. An install-only launch closes the store and exits after the cut. A later serving launch opens that activation.
- **What a crash leaves.** The first install recovers either the old generation or the complete new generation, with its fence, catalog and activation together. From the directory sync after the MANIFEST rename, even a lost ack leaves the new generation. A probe selects only the recovered activation. A publishing or listening failure after install leaves that activation for the next launch. **On objects@1** the point of no return is the activation CHECKPOINT's head write (STORE §12.7). An install that ends `Store(Fenced(e))` or `Store(Undecided)` has an unknown outcome: the process exits 1 and a later launch decides by what it opens, or, when its open is refused, by its read of the attempt's row (§14.3, v2.36), never by assuming the old activation.
- **Prepared deployment.** OLD leases a durable prefix and exports it. NEW opens that export read-only, proves its executable digest against the image key, runs `on_migrate` and `schema` in `Migrate` mode against the staged catalog (v2.45). It syncs the attempt-bound `PREPARED` file. OLD validates the file, reserves node ids for its ranges, and builds the replacements off-root. A cutover names the saved attempt and file digest. The paused head checks the exact exported base, its own installed Describe and the deadline, then publishes NEW's catalog, activation pair, release row and (v2.62) PREPARED's activation rewrites, which settle every held trigger row (§14.6 "The activation fate"), in one STORE cut, at version exported main head + 1, with the file digest as the row's `prepared`. On the checkpoint ack it reports the installed activation version. The host-side pause, retry and retirement protocol is §12.2's deploy path.
- **The gone-key set.** The head computes the keys in `previous ∪ current` minus this registry's keys and gives each session a shareable copy at connect.
- **Gone keys.** A call whose key is in the gone-key set goes to the head as a gone call; the session does not refuse it (V5). The head runs `admit(p)`: `Admitted` or `Abandoned` gives a reply-only commit of `Failed(Gone)`, and `Duplicate(r)` answers `r`. So the stream passes every gone position with a reply, and a re-send returns it. A key in neither list is `Refused(NoEntry)` at the session, with nothing recorded. **Slots (v2.11, F9):** a session slot keeps the requested entry key from its Hello `Want`, not only its `Entry` answer, so a gone key is still known at `Call`. A slot out of range is `Refused(NoEntry)`. A `Subscribe` on a gone slot is answered `Ended(sub, Failed(Gone))` at the session, with nothing recorded. A pending request is never remapped to a new entry. A stale client costs at most one commit per stream before it sees `Placed(Gone)`. RUN: mutant `gone_refused_at_session` (v1's rule) is CAUGHT (C8).
- **Matching an old client:**
  - unchanged: `Ready`;
  - a defaulted field added to A: `Resolve(slot, writer_args)`;
  - a changed R or a changed published row: depends on D2;
  - a renamed or removed entry, a changed type path or a package major: `Placed(Gone)` for that want. There is no `Upgrade` frame (v2.65): the frame grammar has none. The TypeScript SDK client treats one `Placed(Gone)` as `Incompatible` and stops before it sends its pending call; reloading the bindings is the page's or the bundler's job.
- **Rollback.** Under D2-K, older code after a newer schema number cannot open: its `schema` gets `Missing("schema")` from `declare_table` in `Open` (STORE v2.57 §1.2). Rolling back therefore needs `migrate_table` to the older schema, or D2-A.

**D2 (ruled K by Sam 2026-11-16, gavel 3): an old reader against a newer writer's R or row.** Unchanged from v2.1, with one row added.

| | **K: keep SEM-10 strict** (recommended) | **A: skip additive fields, guarded** |
|---|---|---|
| Good case | rename `{enabled}` → `{active}`: the old reader gets `Schema`, then `Gone` + reload | add `{label: String = ""}`: old tabs keep working |
| Bad case | the same additive `label`: every open tab reloads | rename without a tombstone decodes wrongly; guarded by refusing a schema that drops a field without `removed`, with tombstones kept forever |
| Rollback (new in v2.2) | older code cannot open after an additive schema change (RUN p17 step 3) | older code opens and skips the added field |
| /wholehog | clean: one rule | clean only if SEM-10 changes everywhere (store and wire) |

**My recommendation: K now.** Reloads are tolerable in rung 1. A is a pure relaxation that can come later with no data rewritten. The rollback row is K's real cost, and it is what would make A worth deciding before rung 2.

## 7. Recording and re-execution (R5), at STORE-SPEC v2.4

**As built before RS (1e9b2349).** `store.reply_recorded` admits the reply and CALL together. F2 keeps both on an application error. A runner refusal discards the draft. `serve` takes over that admission in RS.

- **Offline second witness.** Stop the writer and run `hirsel tape check DIR --recorded WRITER --release WITNESS`. Build the same Place release with Cranelift and LLVM and pass the executable that wrote the CALL records as WRITER. The driver checks the two entry code revisions, the two replay directory commitments in the images' metadata (launch.md "Serving image and replay directory commitment"), which must be equal, and the compiler backends, which must differ, then starts WITNESS on a private copy taken under the store's shared LOCK. A held LOCK exits 75. The Runner uses WRITER's image digest and WITNESS's selected callbacks to check each CALL's replay identity and bounds before calling `store.reexecute_with`.
- The report is JSON Lines. Its first line identifies both executables by compiler backend and SHA-256, and gives their common code revision. Each `Different` line identifies the version and whether the reply or writes differ. The final line gives counts by verdict and the main branch interval `(after, through]`. Versions at or below `after` were checkpointed and are outside coverage. Versions in the interval without a CALL count as `not_recorded`. Claim admission refusals count as `admission_refused`, and other keyed CALL records count as `effects` until the keyed replay adapter can execute them.
- A completed check exits 1 for `Different` and 0 for a window without differences. `residency` and `admission_refused` count separately as inconclusive. An empty window or one with only inconclusive verdicts contains no matching executions. Each full VOPR night records a fixture check in the receipt's `second_witness` field. Its two executables cover one program and one set of journal inputs. Coverage across other inputs and code variants depends on which CALLs the check reaches.

- **Recording.** A transact call gives its `CallRecord{key, args, stream, code}` to `store.serve`, which attaches it to the same transaction as the reply (v2.17 B4; the public `record` is deleted). The commit's frame then carries a call section. Its position is the stream's op in that frame, and its CallInfo is the frame's.
- **What each outcome leaves, as `tape check` sees it** (STORE-SPEC G1 table, adopted):
