  | Outcome of the position's run | What the store committed | Call record | `tape check` |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | `Committed` | data, reply and call section, in one frame | yes | `Same`, or `Different(Reply)` / `Different(Writes)` |
  | `Kept` (v2.17; was `Aborted(u)`: the adapter always replies) | reply and call section, one streams-only frame; no data | **yes (G1)** | `Same` when the re-run returns the same reply bytes and no writes, whether it is `Kept` or a zero-write `Committed` (v2.34, below); else `Different(Reply)` or `Different(Writes)` |
  | `Failed(p, stop)` (v2.17, v2.18 M6) | nothing from the body; the runner's `conclude` of the fixed fault, with the record and the attempted run's CallInfo and base (STORE AM6) | yes, in the `conclude` commit | `Same` when the re-run, under the recorded CallInfo, faults the same way, else `Different(Reply)`; a step stop on either side that differs is `Unknown(Residency)` first (v2.47, below) |
  | `NoRoom(p, c)` (v2.18 B4) | nothing (a stream); a keyed claim's third refusal is concluded as `Failed(OutOfBytes(c))` with the record, CallInfo and base, `settle` 2 (ATTACK-4 M3) | none, or the `conclude` commit | nothing to check for a stream; `Unknown(AdmissionRefused)` for the conclude row: the body returned, and the refusal came from resident admission, which replay does not reproduce |
  | a gone entry's reply-only `Failed(Gone)` | reply only | **none: the one exception** (below) | `Unknown(NotRecorded)` |
  | a record in a compacted segment, or **any record in a `memory` store** | — | `recorded` is None | `Unknown(StateGone)` |

- **The one exception to "a frame has a call record iff it has a reply written by a run".** The runner's `Failed(Gone)` reply-only commit has a reply and no record, because there is no entry to run. Every other reply the runner writes has a record. STORE-SPEC v2.4's table already gives that row (none, `Unknown(NoEntry)`). But its model law G ("a frame has a call record iff it has a reply written by a run") does not exclude it, although that commit is a `run`. The amended text for law G went to store-spec-1001 in followups.md.
- **Reading.** `store.recorded(db, b, version)` returns the call record with its settle context (ATTACK-4 M3): `settle` 0 gives the frame's branch, version and `at`, the CallInfo the recorded body saw (its seed follows from the store, branch and version); `settle` 1 or 2 gives the attempted run's base, `at` and seed from the record, and the branch the record was written on. It serves live journal segments only. A compacted commit, and every commit in a `memory` store, reads as None, which is `Unknown(StateGone)`. A memory store keeps no record, because nothing would bound it (STORE-SPEC G3).
- **Re-execution:**
  1. fork at the run's base: v-1 for `settle` 0, the recorded base for `settle` 1 (ATTACK-4 M3). `settle` 2 runs nothing and answers `Unknown(AdmissionRefused)`;
  2. run the candidate's `place` under `n_place`, and its `schema` in `Open` mode on the fork (fresh H, current incarnations, I3);
  3. run the recorded key on the recorded args, **with the Caller decoded by the record's control kind (a stream key as `caller_key ‖ u32`, a keyed claim key by K-4's grammar)**. **In recorded-code mode** the code is the writer's, never assumed: the record's `code` bytes (the entry's `n` and `s`, then the recorded replay identity) name the code that wrote it. A launch of the writer's own image (the in-process `reexecute`) compares them with its own selection, as built. `tape check` substitutes WITNESS's selection for WRITER's only under equal revisions and equal replay directory commitments (above). Every other recorded-code run, `hirsel replay`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> and a host's availability check included, takes its code from launch.md "Recorded-code replay", which resolves the record to one `Replay` export of a verified release installed on this host. Its factory's `place`, `migrate` and `schema`, with the resolved record's image digest as the launch digest, must recompute the recorded replay identity, and its registration at the activation's indexed `n_place` must give the recorded revision, `n` and `s`. Otherwise the verdict is `Unknown(RevisionUnknown)` and nothing runs; so is an empty recorded identity. The **replay identity** (the selection digest) is H(launch digest ‖ the identity bytes of the selected `place`, `migrate` and `handles`), framed with u32 lengths, which `place_head` derives when it is given them. The launch digest stays the writer's image hash (launch.md), so two launches of one image with different constructors differ only in the replay identity, and two recipes whose factories differ have different images through the replay directory commitment. The offline resolver never runs a serving image or a granted `main`, and no rule takes the code from an entry revision, or from an image digest that is not the running image's own. **In candidate mode** the candidate runs whatever its `code`, and its differences show as `Different`. The run is a Tx on the fork whose CallInfo is the recorded run's (from `recorded`'s settle context): its id is derived from the run's base and the recorded branch, its `at` is the recorded `at`, and its seed is the recorded one, never from the fork's branch or a fresh clock read. The fork is only where the speculative writes go. So `info(mut tx)` and everything derived from it read the same in the re-run as in the original, and an unchanged entry compares `Same`;
  4. compare the reply bytes and the write ops.
- **The recorded stop decides first (v2.47, STORE-SPEC v2.59 §13.3, astra 1990 #11).** A format-5 record (STORE v2.64) carries the run's seed and its stop kind (`Raised`, `OutOfSteps(bound)`, `OutOfBytes` or `Conflict`; the runner's `OutOfTime(s)` is the store's `OutOfSteps(s)`), and the replay reads the base at the recorded version and seed. Verdicts go in this order: an absent frame, an unreached pack or a `Refused` replay is `Unknown(StateGone)`; a shared GET failure answers a waiting replay `Unknown(StateGone)`; stops that differ, either one `OutOfSteps`, are `Unknown(Residency)`, because per-build fuel may differ across layouts (FOR-SAM M3-F2); otherwise equal stops compare reply and writes as below, and a recorded `Raised` whose replay succeeds is `Different(Reply)`.
- **`Kept` and a zero-write `Committed` are one case (v2.34, reexec-gate-1939).** An inline `Kept` and a `Committed` whose body wrote nothing can persist identical CALL and reply bytes, so re-execution cannot tell them apart and does not try: the outcome label is not observable from the persisted frame. A same-bytes candidate is `Same` when its re-run returns the same reply bytes and the same (empty) write ops, whichever label either run had, and `Different(Reply)` or `Different(Writes)` otherwise. No outcome marker is added to the CALL section, whose bytes stay as they are. The label is used only at run time (`Served.Kept` answers the client without waiting on data), never read back from a frame.
- `Verdict = Same | Different(Reply | Writes) | Unknown(NoEntry | ArgsUnresolved | StateGone | RevisionUnknown | NotRecorded | Effects | AdmissionRefused | Residency)`. v2.18 appends `AdmissionRefused` (ATTACK-4 M3): no existing reason says that the body ran and its commit was refused for room. v2.47 appends `Residency`: a step stop that per-build fuel may have moved.
- Abandoned positions have no call section beyond their committed reply, so they are `NotRecorded`.
- **No runner rule or law depends on a call section.** Re-sent calls are decided by the streams tree, and gone keys by the activation table and the stream state. Checkpoints keep both.

## 8. Laws, models and measurements

Each law sits in the runner's law suite, beside STORE-SPEC §5's. Each runs against a memory store and a file store under the store's crash cuts.

**Status values:**
- **MODEL**: checked by a v2.2 model, with the mutant named;
- **UNTESTED**: no model reaches it yet; the harness and slice that will test it are named;
- **PLANNED** (v2.15): specified with its mutants for the named slice of service-ops-1127/SLICES.md; nothing is built or run.

| Law | Wrong implementation it catches | Oracle | Model check and mutant | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| L1: at every crash cut, re-sending p gives byte-equal bytes and the effect once (v2.94: with p sent inside a `Call` list and its group straddling the cut, cut 1 of §3 "Crash cuts and replay", **PLANNED**); no Outcome reporting a commit is observed before that commit is durable; `HistoryUnavailable` only for a durably replied position (v2.11.4) | answer before durable; answer on poison; admit of latest+1 while latest is unreplied | durable mark read by the harness | C1, C3: `answer_before_durable`, `poison_answers`, `store_v22_admit` CAUGHT | MODEL |
| L2: 10⁴ hostile frames before `admit` leave the store version and the streams row unchanged, and the **session's** peak charge stays below its idle peak plus one hostile decode (v2.5: V1 refuses before the head, so the charge is the session's) | a head that decodes after admit; a session that keeps each refused frame's decode storage | the store version and streams row; the session's owner task (v2.8; the reader's WebSocket framing is inside the reader's own spawn bytes) running under n_session = idle peak + one hostile decode, answering 10⁴ `Malformed` with no Budget fault and no `Close` | C7: `decode_after_admit` CAUGHT (no model has charges) | MODEL for "never admitted"; the peak-charge half is **UNTESTED** until R1's case (named in the Oracle cell) |
| L3: a `Failed` p, re-sent k times, runs the handler once | (i) re-run on Duplicate, then commit; (ii) re-run on Duplicate, then discard the run (fault or roll back), so no version or reply changes | (i) the version and reply bytes, on every backend. (ii) **the CONC-10 event sink** (v2.6): the case's body makes a channel and `try_send`s once per run, then faults; neither the event nor the run rolls back with `Failed`. The harness counts `TRY_SEND` events (kind number from the published header, hirsel_core_rt_host.h). On the evaluator it reads them from the event log. On cranelift and llvm it links a counting sink into `scheduler.event` (hirsel_core_rt_host.h:89) through `hirsel build --cc` with a `-Wl,--wrap` linker wrapper. `core/langarena/layout/arc_events.c` does exactly this, counting events by kind and leaving the scheduler's choice alone, and `core/langarena/layout/service.py` builds it on both cranelift and llvm (llvm with `--release`). Do not use test-event-order.py's recorder.c: it also replaces `choose`. Expected: 1 run of the faulting body for p, hand-derived from the law, which holds because nothing in package `store` calls `try_send` at a56c9a1c (`git grep` over core/programs/store). If a later store or runner change adds one, the harness counts only `TRY_SEND` events whose object is a channel the body made. | C10 (a body run never starts at a position whose reply the store holds): `rerun_on_duplicate` CAUGHT (`same_session_double_send`, `failed_resent`). C1 alone held L3 only by construction (it counts committed effects). | MODEL; an R1 case on all three backends. An "Outcomes only" native run does not count as L3. |
| L4 (`l4_peak_retained`, v2.9; v2.42 Spec Q5): on one build, the runtime's `peak_retained_bytes` after 10⁴ malformed frames equals its value after 10 malformed frames, with no tolerance. The law compares the two runs of one build and pins no figure, so it holds on every engine and optimisation level (MEM-30, SEM-8) | decode storage outside the account; a decoder that keeps each frame (mutant: keep 1 byte per refused frame, which retains about 9,990 more bytes after 10⁴ frames than after 10) | the runtime's `peak_retained_bytes`, read after each flood on the same build | — (no model has memory) | RUN by runner-build-1033 at b02567be under v2.9's form (10⁴ malformed against one valid call): both engines read 51,712. The v2.42 form is a build item |
| L5: each frame's version is ≥ the previous one's, and the rows equal the oracle at that version (v2.45) and each group's `value` decodes to the oracle's R at that version, empty only when the encoded R equals the previous group's for that sub | a Release at its own commit version; Subscribe at the newest snapshot labelled with the base; two readers sharing a stale result; `value_stale` (a group carries the R of an earlier version) | the K1 reference (full query at the version) | C5, K1, K2: `release_at_commit_version`, `subscribe_at_latest`, `shared_cache` CAUGHT | MODEL |

Slots `ev` on Log L (id 7), `ev2` on L2 (id 8), `ev3` on L3 (id 9). **Grow and shrink:** the query reads Cell `n` and covers `cover_log(ev, 0, 5)` and `cover_tail(ev, n)` over L at (0, 10). With the fixed range removed, n from 1 to 3 inserts 7 and 8. **Move between slots:** with Cell `which` = 0 the query covers `cover_tail(ev, L, 3)`, and with 1 `cover_tail(ev2, L2, 3)`. Setting `which` = 1 sends one Batch whose one group has the deletions 7‖2, 7‖3 and 7‖4 in `ev`, the insertions 8‖0 and 8‖1 in `ev2` (W = [0, 2)), and the new value. **Cover of another Log:** `cover_tail(ev, L2, 3)` ends the view with `Ended(sub, Failed(Raised(5)))` and no rows. **Bounds only:** the query reads `bounds(L3)`, returns n − f and covers `cover_tail(ev3, L3, 5)`. L3 is empty at (0, 0). One Tx appends to L3 and then trims before 1, which leaves (1, 1) with no row and no `Trimmed` (STORE §14.8). The view reruns, the session is fenced at b and R = b, the value 0 is elided and both windows are empty, so no group is sent. The same round over a query that covers L3 but reads no bounds moves no fence. ### 8.1 `calls25_model.py` (RUN; `models/evidence/calls25.txt`)

A BFS over sessions, the head, the journal (group commit, prefix-ordered crash cuts), the Release FIFO and the publisher. v2.5 builds it from `calls22_model.py` (kept, with its evidence `calls22.txt`).

**What v2.5 changed in the model, and why.** In calls22 the session gave a head frame only to requests the client still had in flight, and a client held one Outcome per request. So a second Outcome for one send could not be expressed, and no check could fail on it (review 1042; 1033 08:50). Now the session and the client are separate:
- the session keeps its owed sends, one per `Call` frame, each with its own deadline transition;
- it emits wire rows: V1 refusals, `Abandoned` per send, and one row per owed send when the head answers;
- the client only counts, per (session, stream, p), its sends, the rows it received, and its open sends lost when the session ended.

**Checks:**
- C1: an effect at most once;
- C3: every reply-bearing answer equals the durable reply, and `HistoryUnavailable` only for a replied position (v2.11.4, calls2114);
- C4: liveness;
- C5: every frame's version is ≥ the commit version it reports, and a session's frames never go back;
- C6: the head holds at most 1+q requests of a session;
- C6b (v2.5): the session's slots (per forwarded position, max(1, owed sends)) and its owed sends stay ≤ 1+q;
- C7: a malformed request's position is never admitted;
- C8: a gone position is never left admitted without a reply;
- **C9 (v2.5): exactly one Outcome per send.** At every state, rows + lost ≤ sends (at most one). At every terminal state, rows + lost = sends (at least one). A terminal state is one where no step but a crash or poison is enabled and nothing is poisoned.
- C10 (v2.5): a body run never starts at a position whose reply the store holds (L3).

**What C9 can and cannot conclude.**
- The at-most-one half is a safety check: it fails at the first state with an extra row, on any history.
- The at-least-one half is checked only at terminal states. At a non-terminal state, rows + lost < sends proves nothing, because a row may still come. That is why `one_for_joined` with deadlines is caught at the terminal state, not at the delivery.
- The search is finite: bounded sends, one re-send per request, bounded crashes and poisons. Sends, versions and epochs only grow, so the state graph has no cycles, and every maximal history ends in a terminal state. So "a send never answered while the session stays up" is checked on every history the bounds allow. It is not checked for a history the bounds cut off (a third re-send, a second crash where the scenario allows one).
- The model has no clock. Deadlines fire in any order, so a deadline answered later than its own (`latest_deadline`) is not observable. L15's timed R1 case covers it.

| cross_session_crash | two sessions on one position, typed errors, one crash | 35,249,805 | 1,595,017 | 9,591 | 0 |

| refusals | store refusal, fault, a malformed request, q = 0, one crash | 36,282 | 6,070 | 536 | 0 |

`late_completion_resent` passes every v2.2 check: it cannot be expressed in calls22. Controls (RUN): with C9's per-state half removed, it is still caught by C9's terminal equality. With C9 removed entirely, it is NOT CAUGHT in any scenario but cross_session_crash, which the control skipped for its size.

### 8.2 `subs25_model.py` (RUN; `models/evidence/subs25.txt`)

v2.5: `subs22_model.py` plus K8. The v2.2 numbers below are unchanged in the re-run.

Keys `perm`, `row0` and `row1`. Q1 reads `perm` and covers both rows. Qf reads `row1` and faults when `row1 == 2`. Commits are revoke, grant, w0 and call (with an outcome), plus a fork's commit and `adopt`. Budget is 2. Reset carries outcomes, and the client re-subscribes. There is one crash, then reopen with a Foreign resume.

**Checks:**
- K1: the cache equals a from-scratch `query(snapshot)`;
- K2: versions never decrease;
- K3: nothing above the durable version;
- K4: an outcome is in the first frame at ≥ its version;
- K5: liveness;
- K6: queue ≤ budget;
- K7: one query's fault ends only its subscription;
- K8 (v2.5): at most one Outcome per send. The client sends the call once, and again after reopen only when it has no Outcome, so a frame carrying the Outcome when it already has one is a second Outcome. subs22's client absorbed it silently.

| Scenario | States | Crash cuts | Terminal | Failures |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| main_two_sessions (subs (0,Q1), (0,Qf), (1,Q1)) | 635,530 | 0 | 408 | 0 |
| restart_resume | 122,513 | 57,198 | 294 | 0 |
| branch_adopt | 25,346 | 11,734 | 24 | 0 |
| outcome_only (session 0 has no subscription) | 205 | 0 | 3 | 0 |
| **total** | **783,594** | **68,932** | | **0** |

**Mutants, all CAUGHT:** `subscribe_at_latest` (K1), `shared_cache` (K1), `fault_ends_session` (K7), `unbounded_queue` (K6), `reset_drops_outcomes` (K4), `resume_foreign_as_empty` (K1), `release_fork_commits` (K1), `outcome_never` (K4 in main_two_sessions; **K5 only** in outcome_only), `rerun_without_retract` (K1), and (v2.5) `outcome_repeated` (K8 in main_two_sessions; NOT CAUGHT with K8 removed, RUN as a control).

**Checkpoints:** not modelled as a runner transition. A checkpoint changes no version and publishes nothing, and `changes` is not Foreign across one (STORE-SPEC L23).

| Recorded one-row transact commit | 205 B, of which the call section is 69 B | — | READ STORE-SPEC F7 |

**R1 acceptance (named tests and laws):**
- **L1**, **L2** (both halves; the peak is the session's charge), **L3** (`TRY_SEND` events through the CONC-10 sink, on the evaluator, cranelift and llvm; v2.6), **L4** (`l4_peak_retained`: `peak_retained_bytes` after 10⁴ malformed frames equals it after 10, on one build; v2.42), **L6+L7** (eventual liveness and the count form: the first Batch at ≥ v), **L14** (`handles` twice), **L19** (the owner's held rows within the reserve, v2.11), **L15** (one Outcome per send: `drive_one_outcome_per_send`, the timed deadline-order case, the q = 0 join refusal).
- `calls25_model.py`'s scenarios as the runner's model case, except `upgrade_gone` (R4), with its mutants each still CAUGHT (`gone_refused_at_session` moves to R4).
- `L7/deadline_held` on the real head: p+1 sent while p is unsent waits in the FIFO; `Abandoned` at its deadline with the slot kept; then p sent and p+1 re-sent runs once. `L7/deadline_hung` as in §8 (v2.7), once core's `bound steps` is built (exec-budget slices).

**v2.15's slices** (O1–O7: lifecycle, stop source, status route, manifest and preview, fixture, confirmation, and STORE-SPEC v2.25's pages) are in `SLICES.md` from service-ops-1127, with each slice's laws, mutants, dependencies and land order.

**What no slice may do:** give a send a second Outcome or none (C9, L15); answer a commitless outcome outside V1/V3 (mutant `direct_commitless`); release at a commit version (mutant `release_at_commit_version`); hold a request outside the session's slot table (mutant `queue_outside_account`).

## 10. Decisions

| # | Decision | Options | Recommendation | /wholehog |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| D1 | How the generator learns entries | it runs the server executable's `place` (RULED 2026-11-16) / a declared list (LANG-20 refuses one) / a manifest file | it runs `place`, bounded, in the same executable (rule 10) | same: one list, one path |
| D2 | An old reader against a newer R or row | K or A (§6) | **K now**; the rollback row is K's cost | both clean; A must be global |
| D3 | Typed error | one commit / two commits | one commit (ACCEPTED as STORE-SPEC F2) | one commit |
| D4 | Principals and limits | anon only / JWT | anon in rung 1; the numbers are Sam's. **v2.24: superseded by D9.** The runner mints no principal; JWT or any other scheme is an application binding. **v2.60: closed by §15**, the library binding `jwtbind` over `std.jwt` | §15.8 |
| D5 (RULED (a) 2026-11-16, gavel 5; v2.7: MEM-30 `bound steps`, per-entry `s`, `DEFAULT_STEPS`) | A transact body or a query that never returns | (a) core's step bound (MEM-30), `OutOfTime` already declared; (b) ship R1 without it: a body that never yields stops the single worker, so the whole process stops (every session, the head, the publisher) until a watchdog restarts it (v2.4 corrects v2.2, which said sessions keep answering under (b)) | **(a)**; R1 is not blocked, but L7/deadline_hung can be stated and tested only under (a) | (a) is the end state |
| D6 | Where `runner` lives | package `store` | module `runner` in package `store`; since cs-store-split-1935, module `runner` in its own package `runner`, which binds `store` | one package, then split so store users do not compile the runner |
| D8 (v2.15, v2.19) | What decides a Caller's access to rows | (a) one policy per place, run by the runner, body narrowed to its scope (v2.15 to v2.18); (b) a policy per entry; **(c)** each body reads its own tables with the Caller and refuses in code (Convex); (d) a declarative row filter (SpacetimeDB RLS) | **(c)**, RULED by Sam on 2026-11-19 (ruling C). Access checks are ordinary reads, so they are in the transaction or the read set, and the release check (§3) keeps a removed grant from releasing queued content. (d) is a rule language, which ENDSTATE rules out | (c): no second mechanism beside ordinary code and read sets |
| D9 (v2.24, RULED R6 by Sam on 2026-11-21) | How a wire call gets authority, and who authenticates | (a) **a copied `G: plain + send` per call; the application binds the Caller** (v2.67: amended to a fresh `G` per call from the place's `call` mint; a plain copy is one such mint) ; (b) one movable `G` consumed by the first call; (c) a shareable `Arc[G]` or a badge per Caller (DROPPED, D3: never to be proposed again); (d) runner-side token checks or policy (DROPPED) | **(a)**. A movable `G` stays trigger-only and never reaches the wire (v2.67: withdrawn; a movable `G` reaches a wire call only as the value minted for that call; v2.69: only for the granted entries `Grants.call` lists). The runner has no authentication or authorization (rule 13) | (a): authority is a plain value passed like any other argument; access is ordinary application code |
| D7 (new) | Where compute entries run, given that each needs a Registry | **(a)** each session runs `place` and holds its own Registry (495 B per entry, built; 0.9 ms per connection at 1,000 entries, probe); **(b)** a pool of k compute tasks, each with one Registry, shared by all sessions | **(c)** (v2.43): one Registry per serving task, used by every session through the allocation pool, where compute already ran. (a) held 1,000 equal Registries at 1,000 sessions. Before v2.43, (a): the Registry is in the session's account, so memory is bounded per session, and no compute request waits in a queue shared with other sessions. (b) bounds Registry memory by k instead of by connections, at the price of a cross-session queue. v2.4: hang isolation was no argument on the single-worker runtime; v2.7: with D5 (a) built a hung body stops only its own call, so it favours (a). | (a) treats compute like the head treats transact: one account per caller. Revisit only if a place has thousands of entries and thousands of sessions (1,000 × 1,000 ≈ 500 MB). |

**ENDSTATE corrections:** ENDSTATE-TEXT.md in this directory. Its v2.2 section lists each sentence v2.2 changes, with the replacement.

Unchanged from v2.1. v2.2 takes no new mechanism from outside. V1's session timer and V3's ordered channel are consequences of the output gate (ADOPTED below) and the single publisher. The activation table is an ordinary table of the place.

| per-handler adapter | Hirsel dispatch spool | REPLACED by one adapter per kind |
| row-level security filters with `:sender` (v2.15) | SpacetimeDB `client_visibility_filter`, crates/bindings/src/lib.rs:73-109 (marked "not enforced", behind `unstable`); rewritten into each query, crates/expr/src/rls.rs:15-60; the owner bypasses it, crates/lib/src/identity.rs:63-104 | NOT TAKEN. **Win:** one declaration that every query obeys. **Weakness:** it is a second language beside the code, unenforced in the source read, and a change to it disconnects every client (crates/schema/src/auto_migrate.rs:1317-1338) |

| (issuer, subject) identity (v2.15) | SpacetimeDB `Identity::from_claims`, a blake3 hash of "iss\|sub", identity.rs:196-215; Convex `UserIdentifier::construct(issuer, subject)`, keybroker/src/broker.rs:543,615, issuer routing in authentication/src/lib.rs:140-200 | ADAPTED as `Caller{issuer, subject}`. **Win:** a bounded, non-secret, stable name that may be stored. **Weakness:** neither one names an anonymous caller in the same space. Hirsel reserves issuer 1 for anonymous sessions and draws a fresh random subject for each session |
| identity checked in each function (v2.15, v2.19) | Convex demo users-and-clerk-webhooks/convex/users.ts (`getCurrentUser` reads `ctx.auth.getUserIdentity()` and then the users table) | ADOPTED (v2.19, ruling C). **Win:** checks are reads of ordinary tables, so they invalidate like any read. **Weakness:** each function must remember the check |
| identity change re-runs a session's queries (v2.15) | Convex sync worker.rs:948-964, state.rs:257-280 | ADAPTED: a Caller is fixed per session, so only a change to a query's reads re-runs it |
| explicit, not ambient, caller data (v2.15) | Goose: a callee sees only what its caller passes; even a call's source line is passed explicitly, docs/design/call_location.md | NOT TAKEN as a mechanism: Goose has no store, no principal and no row authority. **Win:** what a function may use is in its signature. Hirsel's Caller is likewise an argument, never ambient. **Weakness:** nothing to take for authority |
| ordered shutdown: drain in-flight requests, then stop workers, with a forced exit after a timeout (v2.15) | Convex crates/local_backend/src/main.rs:217-240 (opened) | ADAPTED as §12.1's drain. The weakness: Convex's force-exit duration is separate from its HTTP drain and is measured from the signal. Here one absolute D serves both. Convex's "preempt" path (a fatal error exits at once) is NOT TAKEN: poison already exits |
| health route (v2.15) | SpacetimeDB crates/client-api/src/routes/health.rs:9-42 (opened) | ADAPTED as `GET /hirsel/status`. The weakness: it reports node and control-database reachability, not whether this database has activated, so a 200 does not say which code runs. Here the body carries the launch identity and `release_digest`, and 200 means `Ready` only |
| pre-publish plan (v2.15) | SpacetimeDB crates/cli/src/subcommands/publish.rs:885-970 (opened) | ADAPTED as `hirsel preview`<!-- doc-ref: planned -->. The weakness: the plan is computed by the server against the live database, so planning needs a write-capable endpoint. Here the new executable answers the old entry list with no store, and schema readiness is said to be unknown instead |
| evaluate then finish push (v2.15) | Convex npm-packages/convex/src/cli/lib/deploy2.ts:90-100 (opened) | NOT TAKEN: a server-side evaluation step is a rollout service Hirsel does not run. `confirm` reads the outcome instead |
| test server with explicit Close (v2.15) | Go src/net/http/httptest/server.go:266-290, Close :482 (opened) | ADAPTED as the `fixture` module. The weakness: `Close` is a method the test must remember to call, and a forgotten one leaks the listener. Here dropping the fixture also cancels and joins its task group, and the durable version is part of each answer |
| per-use mint tasks with their own allowance, contained faults (v2.69) | Goose docs/goose_spec.md:4560 `thread_spawn_contained` (opened); Erlang/OTP erts/doc/references/erl_cmd.md:670-687 per-process `max_heap_size`, `+hmaxk` (opened) | ADAPTED: a Goose contained worker's abort ends only that worker, and an OTP process has its own heap limit, so one request's failure or growth stays in its own process. Both isolate per worker, not per request inside a long-lived server. Here the mint task contains each request with `isolate`, which needs a mint that cannot wait, and each use gets its own task and limit, as a bulkhead does. **Weakness kept out:** OTP's `max_heap_size` kills the process, which would end the mint task, so a mint's exhaustion is a `Capacity` value for its request instead |
| test fixtures (v2.15) | Goose docs/testing.md, "Fixture layout" (opened) | NOT TAKEN: Goose's fixtures are compiler fixtures, inputs and expected outputs for the compiler. It has no runtime fixture that starts a served program, so there is nothing to adapt for §12.3 |
| release manifest and preview (v2.15) | Goose docs/packages.md §1 "Running and building" and §4 "Publishing" (opened) | NOT TAKEN: Goose pins package versions for a build and publishes a package by a repository tag. It has no record of a deployed program's entries or grants and no upgrade preview, so §12.2 follows SpacetimeDB's pre-publish plan instead |
| orderly shutdown: send quit messages, then wait (v2.15) | Goose docs/goose_spec.md:4547-4550, `thread_wait` (opened) | ADAPTED as `Close` to every session and the head, then joins. The weakness: Goose's wait has no deadline, so one stuck worker holds the shutdown. Here D bounds it, and cancellation ends the rest |

## 12. Operating a place (new in v2.15)

This section adds what a place needs in order to be run by a supervisor:
- a lifecycle with readiness and a bounded drain (§12.1);
- a release manifest with a read-only preview (§12.2, with docs/spec/launch.md "Release manifest" and "Preview launch");
- a fixture that application tests start a place with (§12.3).

Keyset pages are a store read. They are specified in STORE-SPEC v2.25 §1.5 and in core/std/api/store.md. This section's rules extend §2.1 (V1, V3), §3 and §6, and each extension is marked **extends** where it occurs. No ruling is reopened.

### 12.1 Lifecycle

**One owner.** `runner.operate` owns a place from store open to store close. It replaces the sequence that place programs and the runner's harnesses wire by hand today (`store.open`, `place_head`, `mailbox`, `serve`), so the sequence exists once.

`runner.operate_http` takes an already bound listener and a raw route builder. It opens the Store, prepares the selected activation and starts the head and publisher before serving. The same router serves raw routes and the Place's generated bindings, including managed SSE. The caller supplies Store, Place, HTTP, relay and request budgets, plus the credential verifier and mints for authenticated routes. A stop signal closes admission, drains HTTP within `http.drain_ms` and then closes the Store.

For generated subscriptions, Runner requires the eight-byte-rounded sum of `out_cap` and `state_max` to fit `handler_bytes`. It refuses an incompatible configuration before traffic with `HandoffBudget`, which carries all three configured byte counts.

`BucketBacking.cache: Option[(Dir, u64)]` gives the managed head a host directory and a disk byte limit for `store.objects_cache` (STORE §13.2). Keep the directory across process exits to reuse packs on the next open. The Bucket retains the durable state if the cache directory is deleted.

```hirsel
pub type Step: send = Recovering | Activating | Publishing | Listening(Addr)
pub type Phase: send = Starting(Step) | Ready | Paused | Draining
pub type Status: shareable + send { phase: Phase, digest: Bytes, target: String, n_place: u64, release: Bytes, activation: u64, store_id: (u64, u64), retain_from: u64, addr: Option[Addr], store: store.Attention, upkeep: UpkeepCounts }   // v2.21: activation; v2.31: store_id, retain_from (§14.9); v2.75: store; v2.78: upkeep (§12.5)
pub type Stop: codec + send { lag_ms: u64, grace_ms: u64 }
pub type Backing: send = Memory | Disk(Dir) | Temp(Dir) | Bucket(BucketBacking) | StreamedBucket(BucketBacking, store.JournalSlot) | Sim(SimBacking)
pub type TriggerConfig: send { entries: triggers.Entries, author: Caller }   // v2.67: the grant moved to Grants
pub type Mint[S: send, H: shareable + inert + send, I: send, G: send]: send {   // v2.69: one use's grant source, served by its own mint task
    seed: S,   // owned by the mint task for the place's life; each Mint has its own
    mint: fn(S, H, I) -> Result[G, Capacity],   // never waits; I by view (isolate takes no owned state); runs as isolate around bound steps mint_steps
    mint_bytes: u64,   // this mint's allowance: every live G it made stays charged here (MEM-20); v2.70: `bytes` is reserved (LANG-2)
    mint_steps: u64,   // the step bound of one mint
}
pub type CallMint[G: send, H: shareable + inert + send, S: send]: send {   // v2.69
    mint: Mint[S, H, (), G],
    entries: triggers.Entries,   // the granted entries a wire call may start; matched by path and argument schema
}
pub type Grants[G: send, H: shareable + inert + send, S: send]: send {   // v2.67; v2.69: one Mint per use, each with its own seed of type S
    attempt: Option[Mint[S, H, TriggerContext, G]],   // a granted trigger attempt's G
    call: Option[CallMint[G, H, S]],   // a wire call's G, for the listed granted entries only
    claim: Option[Mint[S, H, (), G]],   // a keyed HTTP claim's G (http.md K-2)
    bind: Option[Mint[S, H, (), G]],   // v2.70: a bind's G (§3 "Binding"), dropped when the bind ends
}
pub type PlaceConfig[G: send, H: send, S: send]: send {   // v2.70: B deleted with wire_bind
    backing: Backing, selection: Selection,
    place: StaticFn[fn(mut Registry[G, H]) -> Result[(), RegisterError]], n_place: u64,
    on_migrate: Option[StaticFn[fn(mut store.Migration) -> Result[(), store.Error] waits]],   // v2.45 (rule 12)
    schema: StaticFn[fn(mut store.Declare) -> Result[H, store.Error]],
    sizes: store.Overrides,   // v2.45 (DX Q6a): all None for the derived sizes
    session: WireConfig, max_sessions: u64, proxies: List[std.ingress_policy.Prefix], n_publisher: u64,
    listen: Addr, net: Net, tasks: Tasks, clock: Clock, random: Random, stop: Stop,
    grants: Option[Grants[G, H, S]],   // v2.67
    triggers: Option[TriggerConfig],
    idle: Option[u64],   // the idle stop (§14.4); a wake launch's idle time replaces it
    wire: Option[Wire[G]],   // None gives every session a fresh anonymous Caller; v2.67: it no longer decides wire calls to Granted entries; v2.70: its G comes from grants.bind
}
pub type StartError: send = Open(OpenError) | Listen(NetError) | Missing(String) | Exceeds(Bound) | Full(Capacity)   // v2.45: Disagrees deleted with WireConfig.n_place, its only use; OpenError is place_head's: Store | Place
pub type Ended: codec + send = Stopped(Option[u64]) | Unstarted | Forced(u64)   // Stopped carries next_due; Unstarted is a stop before Ready (§14.4)

pub fn status_root() -> Result[Latest[Status], Capacity]
pub fn operate[G: send, H: send, S: send](take p: PlaceConfig[G, H, S], take stop: Receiver[()], status: Latest[Status], take watch: Option[Sender[Receiver[Phase]]]) -> Result[Ended, StartError] waits
pub fn release_digest(exported: List[Exported]) -> Result[Bytes, Capacity]
```

- **Place entry.** `platform.serve(take config, take stop, status, take watch)` accepts the `PlaceConfig`, stop receiver, status and optional phase watcher. It calls `runner.operate`, which derives the store share from the executable's `--limit`. Use `store.derived_sizes()` for the default Store options.

- **Store sizes (v2.45, DX Q6a; v2.56).** Before opening the store, `operate` makes `plan = store.planned(kind, share, p.sizes)` and opens with `store.planned_options(plan)`.
  - **Kind.** It is `Memory` for `Memory`, `Directory` for `Disk` and `Temp`, and `Bucket` for an objects@1 authority.
  - **Share.** It is the task's root limit (`runtime.root_limit()`) − `operate_bytes` − the keep, in checked arithmetic. (v2.87) The keep is `PLACE_KEEP` (64 KiB: operate's locals, the phase watcher and the result it returns) plus, when `operate` or `operate_report` registered the place in its own task, the Template it holds there, bounded by `n_place`. A host that passes a made Placing (`operate_placed`, `operate_sharded`) keeps only `PLACE_KEEP`.
  - **What operate_bytes charges (v2.87).** Every term is a declared limit or the `charge.*` of a task or channel the part makes; no round figure stands for a part.
    - The head: `n_place` (its Registry) + the publisher's `n_place` and `n_publisher` + its mints' grants + its own bytes: three 64 KiB wake relays, the 4 KiB claim timer, the 65-slot `HeadInput` inbox, the pause and wall alarms (4 KiB each), 256 KiB of head state, and the largest `n` of a transact or fenced entry with the call reserve's cause cell, since the head runs those bodies. With an `attempt` mint the own bytes are at least 2 MiB: triggered attempts spawn from them and their count is not declared.
    - The serving task: `n_place` (its Registry) + the owners' floors and bind terms + `n_pool` + relay bytes + the supervisor: 64 KiB of state, 6,711 B per owner for its map entries, the `pending_max` token channel, two allocation slots per owner, the serve inbox, the 8 KiB acceptor, 16 refusal tasks of 16 KiB on a Tcp listener, the pool record and `n_service`, capped at H's 64 MiB.
    - Operate itself: 256 KiB.
    - These three reservations describe the v2.87 implementation. PACK-17 requires one registration admission per activation, including on a shared image hit. Each serving task enforces the allowance the entry retained at activation.
  - **The store term** is `store.planned_bytes(plan)`. Store solves the share so that this term fits, and it includes the Bucket capture's X (STORE v2.76 §1.1 "The place plan"), so a limit that passes `place_store` also fits the capture (§14.3 "The capture's service"). Only a `Bucket` backing has `store.planned_capture(plan)`, and the head provisions the capture from it.
  - **Launch modes.** Every launch mode of a backing uses this one share: the serving start, the install and the prepare child alike.
  - **Refusals.** A `Shortage` is `StartError.Full(Capacity{requested: need, available: have})`, and `operate_report` writes its parts to stderr. A `None` sum is `Full(Capacity{requested: u64::MAX, available: share})`.
  - **What Runner computes.** Runner names no Store figure and computes no part of the term. The root limit supplies the budget, and `p.sizes` supplies any per-part overrides.
- `operate` refuses `grace_ms = 0` with `Missing("grace_ms")` before it opens anything. A zero bound is a bound that was not given, as in STORE-SPEC W1. Admission closes at min(t₀ + `lag_ms`, D), so a lag that reaches the grace needs no refusal.
- `Temp(d)` uses `std.dir.create_temp` for a fresh subdirectory of `d`. The head removes a named directory after it closes or drops the store. Cancellation at directory creation's delivery point can leave a directory whose name was never received (the `host.mkdtemp` cancellation prefix). `Disk` takes the store directory itself, already narrowed by the caller (for launch, the `--dir` grant), and removes nothing.
- (v2.67, v2.69) A managed wire call to a `Granted` entry runs only when `PlaceConfig.grants.call` lists it. The session starts the entry's task with its Caller, and the task asks the `call` mint task for a fresh `G` before it runs the body. Otherwise the session refuses it as `NotAllowed` before any reservation or mint. A `call` list element that names no registered granted entry refuses the open with `OpenError.CallNotGranted(path)`. A triggered attempt takes its `G` from the `attempt` mint.
- (v2.69) **Mint tasks.** Each present `Mint` is served by its own mint task, so `attempt`, `call`, `claim` and `bind` never queue behind one another. The task owns the `Mint`'s seed and a share of H, and it answers requests from one inbox in arrival order. An asker `send`s its request with a reply sender, so a full inbox makes only that asker wait, inside its own deadline. Askers are bounded by what already bounds them: live granted entry tasks for `call`, running attempts for `attempt`, admitted HTTP requests (`max_sessions`, `socket.pending_max`) for `claim`, and admitted Hellos and HTTP requests, by the same bounds, for `bind` (v2.70). The task runs each mint as `isolate` around `bound steps mint_steps`, so `OutOfTime` reaches `isolate` after its bound has ended and is caught (FAIL-16). The mint cannot wait (FAIL-16's body cannot), so each request costs at most `mint_steps`.
  - **Outcomes.** `Error(c)` from the mint, or an allocation refused by the mint's `mint_bytes`, fails that request with `Failed(OutOfBytes(room))`. A fault fails it with `Failed(Raised(kind))`, and an exhausted step bound with `Failed(OutOfTime(steps))`. The mint task keeps serving the next request. A wire call answers that `Failed` and never runs its body. An attempt ends as a body that failed with that cause. A keyed claim is http.md K-2's 503 or 500. (v2.70) A bind is not run: a Hello gets Close 1013 for every mint failure, and an HTTP request gets 503 with `Retry-After: 5` for `OutOfBytes` and B-7's 500 for `Raised` or `OutOfTime`, as a claim does. None is 1008 or 401, since no credential was judged.
  - **An asker that is gone.** A call whose deadline or session expiry passes while it waits for its `G` never runs its body: `wire_expired` drops its task, which drops the reply receiver. A request already taken is still minted, at most `mint_steps`, and its `G` is dropped when the reply finds no receiver.
  - **Charging.** A `G` stays charged to its mint's `mint_bytes` after it is sent (MEM-20), until the call, attempt, claim or bind that holds it ends. So held-open calls can exhaust only `call.mint.mint_bytes`, and an attempt's mint never meets them. A bind's `G` ends with the bind, so `bind.mint_bytes` bounds concurrent binds, never sessions.
- The head runs a trigger drive only with an attempt mint. It selects pending rows on branch 0 whose StoreId matches the serving Store. A restored child, including an adopted child (§14.5), leaves inherited trigger rows untouched. With a mint and an empty entry list the head can settle a local pending row as `Gone`. Without a mint it leaves pending rows in place. A nonempty trigger configuration without an attempt mint refuses with `OpenError.TriggerNoGrant(path)` before open. An HTTP Place with declared trigger tables requires a selector and `HttpTriggers` with a drive configuration and attempt mint. Its granted attempts call back into the same head that accepted the HTTP transaction.
- Runner scopes each keyed HTTP operation to the admitting StoreId. The key distinguishes identical deliveries to a parent and its restored child. Startup queues only open claims from the serving Store. A live claim under an unscoped operation refuses open because its origin cannot be established.
- Before an outside step sends, it commits `Started` with the attempt number and the Store transaction's wall time in milliseconds. Reentry returns `InDoubt`. A lookup can resolve it without resending. Explicit resend requires a newer attempt and a provider idempotency horizon strictly greater than the elapsed time since the first start. Resending keeps the first timestamp. If the step body returns with the doubt unresolved, the wrapper parks the trigger with the step index and first start time.
- (v2.29) **What a granted run may read.** A granted run never holds the `Db`. It reads the store only through `trigger_snapshot(ctx) -> Result[(store.Snapshot, store.Reader), TriggerCallError] waits` (v2.47: with a Reader the head makes by `store.reader(db, store.VALUE_LIMIT + 1_048_576)`, dropped with the run, STORE-SPEC v2.59 §13.4; a refused reservation is `Store(e)`), which sends `Snap(attempt)` on the head inbox like `trigger_boundary`. The head answers it after the release gate (it makes the last commit durable first) and after the attempt fence: it takes `store.snapshot(db)`, checks `triggers.boundary` on that snapshot, and replies with that same snapshot only if the attempt is current and has no committed cancel. A fenced-out attempt gets `StaleAttempt`, a cancelled one `Cancelled`, a closing head `Closed`; none of them gets a snapshot. The snapshot is read-only (`store.get`, `store.scan`, `store.look`), and it is the head's newest durable version at the moment of the check, so it includes every commit the run made through `trigger_call` before asking. It never sees a later commit. **Replay.** A crash ends the attempt; the next attempt runs the body from the top and asks again, so it re-reads the store at a newer version rather than replaying a recorded read. A run is deterministic under replay only for rows that cannot change once its fenced calls have sealed them: a body that builds outside input from an immutable range (for example entries a fenced call appended and no transaction rewrites) reads the same bytes at every attempt. Reading mutable rows off the head is allowed but its result is not part of the run's recorded effects. The read costs the head one snapshot and one row lookup; the run does its scan in its own task, off the write lock.

**Status.** `digest`, `target` and `n_place` are the launch identity (launch.md). `release` is `release_digest(exported)`, which is SHA-256 over `u8 17 ‖ "hirsel.release.v1" ‖` the value codec's bytes of this registry's `List[Exported]` in registration order. It is empty until activation has committed. The preview child computes `release` with the same function for the manifest (§12.2). `addr` is the bound address, present from `Listening` on. `store` is the store's `store.attention` answer, empty until the head's first turn and refreshed between the head's turns when `store.attention_mark` moves (CHANGES-v2.75). `Step` and `Phase` are send-only: status and watch stay in-process, and the status route writes its own JSON.

`status` is a `Latest[Status]` root (CONC-13). The caller makes it with `status_root()`, which holds `Starting(Recovering)` with the launch identity, an empty `release` and no `addr`, so a read before `operate` starts sees the first phase. `operate` is the only task that calls `latest.publish` on it: it publishes a new value at each phase change, and at each store section the head reports. Every other holder gets its own handle with `latest.handle`: S passes one to the route, and the fixture keeps one. Readers call `latest.read` and get the newest value. A read never waits and never consumes, so any number of readers see the same newest phase. In one reader's program order, phases never go backwards.

**Waiting for a phase.** A reader that must wait for a phase, rather than sample it, passes a one-use receiver handoff. `operate` creates its own channel of capacity 6 and offers the receiver on the handoff before it publishes a phase. After each publish, `operate` sends the same `Phase` on the channel it created. It publishes at most six phases (four steps, `Ready`, `Draining`), so that channel never fills. A closed handoff or watch is ignored. `runner.main` passes `None`; the fixture takes the receiver and waits on it (§12.3).

**Tasks.** `operate` runs in the caller's task as the place's supervisor. It waits only on its own inbox, as http.md's supervisor does (HTTP-1). It spawns these children:
- **The head (H).** It opens the store, so it owns the `Db` (P2: own, not send). On objects@1, after a refused open it also starts and closes the capture's `FollowerService` (§14.3, v2.54). On a `Bucket` backing `operate` is entered with no step bound current: `runner.main` and `platform.serve` call it from tasks with none. A caller that enters it under `bound steps` breaks that contract (MEM-30). So the head, `operate`'s child, starts that service outside every bound. It runs `schema` and activation, spawns the publisher as its own child, and then serves its inbox, as `place_head` does today. It sends its progress to `operate`: each step, then `Published(Sender[PubIn])`.
- **The serve supervisor (S).** It owns the sessions. It spawns one acceptor child (A) that holds the listener and sends `Accepted` to S. Sessions are S's children, not A's, so A can be cancelled without cancelling a session. This is HTTP-1's split of acceptor and supervisor, applied to `runner.serve_wire`.
- **A stop watcher (W).** It waits on `stop`, then sends `StopRequested` to `operate`.
- **A drain timer (T).** Spawned at the stop, it sends `AdmissionClose` at min(t₀ + `lag_ms`, D) and `Expired` at D, each with `host.sleep_until`. So `operate`'s timers are events on its inbox like every other.
- **Mint tasks (M, v2.69).** One per present `Mint` of `PlaceConfig.grants`, spawned by the head after `schema`, since each holds a share of H (rule 12), whatever the trigger configuration: a place with `call` and no triggers has its `call` task. Each task's `mint_bytes`, record and inbox are a term of `operate_bytes`, so the place's `--limit` counts them. The head keeps the `attempt` task's sender and passes senders to the `call`, `claim` and `bind` tasks to `operate` with `Published`, and `operate` gives them to S. At `Finish` (Draining step 6), when S's sessions and the trigger drive have ended, the head cancels and joins the mint tasks, then halts the publisher and closes the store. So a call or attempt admitted before admission closes always finds its mint. Before `Forced`, no request is answered `Failed(Gone)` for a missing mint.

Each child sends one last message to `operate` before it ends.

**Starting.** The steps run in this order. `operate` publishes `Recovering`, `Activating` and `Publishing` before starting each step. It stays in `Starting(Publishing)` during the bind, and publishes `Starting(Listening(addr))` only after bind succeeds. The `Listening` step always carries the actual bound address. H runs the steps in lockstep with `operate`: after each of its steps, H sends the step's result and waits on its inbox for `Continue` or `Close`. So a stop during `Starting` is taken between steps (below). A failure in any step returns its `StartError` after the earlier steps' resources are released in reverse order. For the head, that means the head ends: it drops the publisher (cancel and join, CONC-8) and then calls `store.close`.

| Step | Run by | What it waits on | Failure | What a crash in the step leaves |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Recovering` | H | `store.open` (recovery, STORE-SPEC §3.4) | `Open(Store(e))` | what §3.6 says for that cut; the next open recovers again |
| `Activating` | H | Select under LOCK, or stage and install at genesis or on explicit request. Run `schema` once against the selected catalog (§6; v2.45). | `Open(NotCurrent(found))`, `Open(IdentityUnknown)`, other `Open(e)` | One complete generation, selected from its activation on the next open |
| `Publishing` | H | the publisher spawned, and its first base taken (the durable version at open) | `Full(c)` | nothing new on disk: activation is already committed, and the next open on the same code writes nothing (§6) |
| `Listening` | `operate` | `host.listen` on `listen` | `Listen(e)` | as `Publishing` |

**Ready.** After `Listening`, S reserves its resources and parks A before its first `accept`. Once S reports that it is prepared, `operate` publishes `Ready` and lets A accept. No session exists before `Ready`, and `Ready` means that activation has committed for this registry's `release`. A probe that connects between the bind and `Ready` waits in the kernel backlog and is answered after `Ready`.

A failure after the activation commit and before `Ready` leaves the new activation rows on disk, because it happens in `Publishing` or `Listening`. The process never reported `Ready`, so a confirmation fails (§12.2). Restart the selected image. Restoring an earlier activation requires an explicit install and a compatible migration (§6).

**A stop during `Starting`.** `operate` takes `StopRequested` in any phase. During `Starting`, it fixes D = t₀ + `grace_ms` as below, publishes `Draining`, spawns T, and answers H's next step result with `Close`. Nothing has been accepted yet, so there is nothing to refuse.
- H ends as a failure in that step ends: it drops the publisher if it spawned one and calls `store.close` if it opened the store. `operate` releases the listener if it bound one, and returns `Ok(Unstarted)`. The head never ran its trigger drive, so it cannot say when its next trigger is due, and `Unstarted` carries nothing.
- A step still running at D (a long recovery) is not waited for: at `Expired`, `operate` cancels H and joins it. H is cancelled at its next cancellation point inside the step, and the `Db`, if it exists, is dropped without `close` (§1.10, §3.7). `operate` returns `Ok(Forced(0))`.
- `Ready` is never published after a stop is taken. If S has already prepared, its acceptor is released without accepting and S is joined.

**The stop input.** `stop` is an ordinary channel. W takes the first `()` on it, or its `Closed`, as the stop request. Who sends it is the caller's choice: `runner.main` connects it to the host stop source (below), and the fixture sends it at its end (§12.3).

**Draining.** `operate` takes `StopRequested` at t₀ (`host.mono_ns`). The drain deadline is D = t₀ + `grace_ms`, absolute, and it is the only deadline of the drain.
1. **Withdraw readiness.** `operate` publishes `Draining` and spawns T. Until admission closes, sessions and connections are served as before, so a proxy that polls readiness stops routing before anything is refused. (v2.27) An idle stop (§14.4) has no such interval: its acceptor has stayed parked since S confirmed, and `operate` sends `Close(D)` to H at the stop, so admission is closed from the confirmation on; the lag does not apply, and D still bounds the drain.
2. **Close admission** when `operate` receives T's `AdmissionClose`, at min(t₀ + `lag_ms`, D):
   - `operate` sends `Close(D)` to S and to H's inbox. Both inboxes are bounded and always drained, so each `send` waits for at most one message's handling.
   - S cancels A and joins it. That drops the listener, and the host then refuses new connections. S then sends `Close(D)` to each session.
   - Under std.http, the same step is http.md HTTP-1's "Stopping" (v2.15) with deadline D. One D serves both transports.
3. **Sessions after `Close(D)` (extends V1).**
   - A send the session does not already hold is answered `Refused(Overloaded)` at once. Nothing is recorded and nothing is forwarded.
   - A send of a position the session holds is a join (V2), as before, and waits for the head's answer.
   - A `Subscribe` is answered `Ended(sub, Refused(Overloaded))`.
   - A compute call in progress in the owner task finishes there under its step bound (P1), and is answered as usual.
   - Every owed send's deadline becomes min(its own, D).
   - `Overloaded` already means "nothing recorded; re-send the same p", which is what a client of a draining place must do, at another instance or after a restart. So no Refusal tag is added, and frame-vectors.json is unchanged.
4. **The head after `Close` (extends V3).**
   - A running worker keeps running. Its group commits, waits on `durable` and is released as H2–H3 say.
   - A request in a stream's FIFO that was never admitted is answered `Refused(Overloaded)` as V3, in a snapshot-less Release.
   - A request taken after `Close` is answered the same way, unless it joins a running worker with an equal request digest (H1). Then it waits for that worker.
   - Nothing that reached `admit` is answered by the drain.
   - When the head holds no worker and no FIFO entry, it sends `Settled` to `operate` and keeps serving its inbox, which can only bring more `Overloaded` answers or joins.
5. **Sessions end.** A session that owes no send ends. A WebSocket session closes with 1001 (going away). Its subscriptions end with it through its one end path (v2.14: `Left` or the mark), and no `Ended` is sent for them; the client re-subscribes elsewhere. S joins each session as it ends, and once it has none it sends `Done(0)` to `operate` and ends.
6. **Finish.** When `operate` holds `Settled` and S's `Done`, it sends `Finish` to H. H sends `Halt` to the publisher, joins it, calls `store.close` and ends. Sessions are gone before `Halt`, so every Release the head made has reached its sessions. `operate` joins W, T, S and H, and returns `Ok(Stopped(next_due))`, where `next_due` is H's last message (§14.4; `Stopped(None)` for a place that runs no trigger drive).

**At D (extends V1).**
- Each send still owed at D is logically `Abandoned`. A session applies this cut before processing any post-D event. The server can attempt to deliver the answers and Close 1001, but the forced path cancels the writer instead of waiting for delivery. A peer that is not reading observes a disconnect and retries under H4. S sends `Done(n)`, where n is the number of sends its sessions settled as `Abandoned` at D, and ends.
- `operate` then cancels H, if H has not ended, and joins it.
  - H is cancelled at its next cancellation point (CONC-7). That is the `durable` wait, an inbox `receive`, or the end of a running body. A running body ends by its step bound `s` (D5), so the join waits at most one body's step bound plus the shielded joins of the store's tasks (STORE-SPEC §1.10).
  - H's cleanup cancels and joins the publisher (CONC-8). It drops the `Db` without `close`: §1.10 "Drop without close" and §3.7 leave the store at the crash cut of that moment, and the next open recovers it (§3.6).
  - An admitted, unreplied position comes back as `Abandoned` and runs again (H4).
- `operate` returns `Ok(Forced(n))`. So a forced exit adds nothing that L1 and H4 do not already cover.

**Poison** is unchanged: if `durable` returns an error, the head answers nothing and the process exits, in any phase. The same holds when the store's health leaves `Ok` (§2.1 "Poison and fence").

**What a crash leaves at each point.** A crash in `Ready`, or in `Draining` before or after `Close`, is the crash L1 already covers: every Outcome a client saw that reports a commit is durable, and every other position is re-sent and answered. The drain writes nothing of its own, so it adds no cut.

**Replay.** The stop request is a channel receive. t₀ and every deadline come from `host.mono_ns`. Both are events at the replay point (CONC-10, CAP-8). The host stop source is a provider result (below). So a replay takes the stop at the same point, reaches the same D and records the same answers.

**The host stop source** uses the existing `Process` capability (§12.4).
- `host.stopped(p: Process) waits` returns once the process has received its first SIGTERM or SIGINT.
- It is a cancellation point at its entry and while it waits. Its completion passes the replay point at issue and at delivery (CAP-8).
- Before entering a program that holds `Process`, the runtime installs the handler. A program without that grant keeps the default action, which ends the process as a crash cut. A replay tool supplies the recorded stop and installs no handler (docs/recording.md).
- A second SIGTERM or SIGINT after the first ends the process at once, also as a crash cut.
- `runner.main` spawns one task that calls `host.stopped` and then drops the stop `Sender`, so W reads `Closed`.
- `main` returns `Ok(())` for `Stopped(_)` and `Unstarted`. For `Forced(n)` and for a `StartError` it writes one stderr line and returns `Error(Exit(status: 1u8))`. The line is `place: forced at the drain deadline, n sends abandoned`, or the error.

**Readiness is read through a status route on the place's own listener.** The place serves `GET /hirsel/status` as a raw route (HTTP-11), beside `GET /hirsel` (http.md §6).
- The handler calls `latest.read(status)`. It answers 200 when the phase is `Ready` or `Paused` and 503 when it is `Draining`.
- The body is one JSON object, with its keys in this order:
  - `phase`: `"ready"`, `"paused"` or `"draining"`;
  - `digest`: lowercase hex, empty when unavailable;
  - `target`;
  - `n_place`;
  - `release`: lowercase hex.
- No connection is accepted before `Ready`, so the route never sees `Starting`. The route records nothing and admits nothing. It is answered before the place's ingress policy, which admits sessions only.

**Why the route, and not a supervisor channel:**
- The readers of readiness are outside the process: the proxy that routes to the place, the supervisor that restarts it, and the release confirmation in §12.2. Each of them already speaks an HTTP probe. A supervisor channel would need a second protocol that reaches outside the process for the same fact: a control socket, so a second listener and a second grant.
- Every served place already has the raw route path, so the status route is a second route of a kind the place already serves.
- A probe that crosses the listener also shows that the listener accepts, which a side channel cannot show.
- The in-process source still exists: it is `status`, the one record of the phase. The route renders it, and the fixture reads it.

**What a reader sees.** Before the bind, a probe sees a refused connection. Between the bind and `Ready`, it waits in the backlog and then gets 200. From `Draining` until admission closes, it gets 503 `"draining"`. After admission closes, it sees a refused connection again. So "not ready" is either a 503 or a refused connection, and only `Ready` is ever a 200.

**Until std.http serves the place,** the place listens through `runner.serve_wire`'s WebSocket accept path, which has no router. In that interval, the lifecycle and its `Latest` are built and tested in process (§12.3), and the route is built with http.md §6's cutover. That is build order, and SLICES.md records it.

### 12.2 Release manifest and preview

**The manifest** is the release's record. `hirsel build --manifest PATH` writes it after it links the executable. It runs the built executable once in the preview launch with an empty old list (launch.md), through the same `describe_child` helper, with the same deadline and bound. So:
- the entry list and `release` come from running `place` inside the executable, and from nowhere else;
- `release` has one implementation, `runner.release_digest`.

That **extends D1** ("it runs `place`") to the manifest: there is no second entry list. The manifest's contents and encoding are in launch.md "Release manifest": the executable's digest and target, `n_place`, the exported entries, `release`, the exact grants and the root limit.

**The preview** is read-only. `hirsel preview --exe NEW NEW.manifest OLD.manifest`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> compares the release to be run with the running one.
- It refuses an executable whose launch digest differs from NEW's (launch.md).
- It runs NEW in the preview launch mode, which opens no store and no listener.
- The child runs `place` and answers each of OLD's exported entries with **the session's own answer to a Hello `Want`**. That code is extracted from the session's `Placed` path (runner.hirsel, the `Placed(take answer)` arm) as `answer_want`, and the session calls it after the change. So the two cannot differ. The answers are:
  - `Ready`: unchanged;
  - `Resolve`: resolvable. When R4 builds `Resolve`, the preview gains it with no change;
  - `Gone`: gone, so a client that holds it is answered `Placed(Gone)` and the SDK client becomes `Incompatible`.
- Entries of NEW whose key matches no entry of OLD are new.
- The preview also reports changed grants (a set difference of the canonical grant strings), and a changed target, root limit or `n_place`.
- It reports **schema readiness: unknown**. Entry compatibility is not schema readiness. Under D2-K, a changed row schema can make the new `schema` fail with `Schema` or `Missing("schema")` at open, and only the target store's catalog can say whether it will. That catalog belongs to the running place, which holds the store. A catalog-aware preview needs a read-only catalog view from another process, which does not exist, so the preview says unknown and never guesses.
- The preview gates nothing (ENDSTATE: "trials and diffs are tools"). It exits 0 whatever it finds. It exits 2 only when it cannot answer: a child error, a timeout or an unreadable manifest.

**"The running release"** is the manifest whose identity the running place confirms. The preview compares two manifest files, and the confirmation below ties OLD.manifest to what is running, so no second remote read is needed.

**Confirmation through readiness.** `hirsel confirm MANIFEST ADDR`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> sends `GET /hirsel/status` to ADDR under an absolute deadline: 10 s by default, set by `--confirm-timeout`<!-- doc-ref: planned -->. The answer is one of these:
- `Confirmed`: the phase is `Ready`, and `digest`, `target`, `n_place` and `release` all equal the manifest's;
- `NotReady(phase)` for a 503, or not listening for a refused connection;
- `Different(fields)`: `Ready`, with at least one available field that differs;
- `Unknown`: `Ready`, with an empty digest on either side (launch.md, "identity unavailable") and the other fields equal. An unavailable identity is never reported as a mismatch.

`confirm` exits 0 only for `Confirmed`. A supervisor publishes a release with its own tools, then runs `confirm` until it gets `Confirmed` or its own deadline passes. Hirsel runs no rollout.

### 12.3 Place fixture for application tests

The module `fixture` (package store, fixture.hirsel) starts a place in a test with the application's real `place` and `schema`, through `operate`. A test therefore runs the same lifecycle, sessions, head and publisher that production runs. It runs them over the wire path on the granted `Net`, never through the in-process seam (DECISIONS 2026-11-17, "W3e waits on W8a").

```hirsel
pub type FixtureError: send = Start(StartError) | Deadline(u64) | Full(Capacity)
pub type CallError: send = Deadline(u64) | Closed | Protocol(String) | Decode(std.codec.DecodeError) | Full(Capacity)
pub type Answer[R] = Returned(R) | Failed(Fault) | Refused(Refusal) | Abandoned
pub type Replied[R] { answer: Answer[R], version: u64 }
pub type Seen { version: u64, rows: List[Row] }

pub opaque type Fixture
pub opaque type Client
pub opaque type Callee[A, R]: shareable + send

pub fn start[G: send, H: send, S: send](take p: PlaceConfig[G, H, S], within_ms: u64) -> Result[Fixture, FixtureError] waits
pub fn compute_entry[A: codec, R: codec](f: StaticFn[fn(take A) -> Result[R, Capacity]]) -> Callee[A, R]
pub fn transact_entry[H, A: codec, R: codec, E: codec](f: StaticFn[fn(mut store.Tx, H, Caller, take A) -> Result[R, E]]) -> Callee[A, Result[R, E]]
pub fn granted_entry[G, A: codec, R: codec](f: StaticFn[fn(G, Caller, take A) -> Result[R, Capacity] waits]) -> Callee[A, R]
pub fn query_entry[H, A: codec, R: codec](f: StaticFn[fn(store.Tx, H, take A) -> Result[Answer[R], Capacity]], take s: Slots) -> Callee[A, R]   // v2.45
pub fn query_as_entry[H, A: codec, R: codec](f: StaticFn[fn(store.Tx, H, Caller, take A) -> Result[Answer[R], Capacity]], take s: Slots) -> Callee[A, R]
pub fn client(f: Fixture, take token: Option[Bytes], within_ms: u64) -> Result[Client, CallError] waits
pub fn client_at(tasks: Tasks, net: Net, clock: Clock, random: Random, keys: List[Bytes], socket: std.wsock.Config, address: Addr, take token: Option[Bytes], resume: Option[wire_client.Resume], within_ms: u64) -> Result[Client, CallError] waits   // v2.30, v2.71
pub fn resume_at(c: Client) -> wire_client.Resume   // v2.71
pub fn entry_keys[G: send, H: send](place: StaticFn[fn(mut runner.Registry[G, H]) -> Result[(), runner.RegisterError]]) -> Result[List[Bytes], runner.StartError]   // v2.30
pub fn call[A: codec, R: codec](mut c: Client, e: Callee[A, R], take a: A, deadline_ms: u32) -> Result[Replied[R], CallError] waits
pub fn subscribe[A: codec](mut c: Client, e: Callee[A, ()], take a: A, within_ms: u64) -> Result[u32, CallError] waits
pub fn seen_at(mut c: Client, sub: u32, version: u64, within_ms: u64) -> Result[Seen, CallError] waits
pub fn close(take c: Client) -> () waits
pub fn status(f: Fixture) -> Status
pub fn end(take f: Fixture, within_ms: u64) -> Result[Ended, FixtureError] waits
```

**Time.** Every bound is relative and measured on the `Clock` in the fixture's `PlaceConfig` (`host.mono_ns`), as the wire `Call`'s `deadline_ms` is. A wait that reaches its bound is `Deadline(bound)`.

**Start.** `start` requires the backing to be `Memory` or `Temp` and `listen` to be 127.0.0.1 port 0. It refuses anything else with `Start(Missing("backing"))` or `Start(Missing("listen"))`.
- It makes the root with `status_root()`, spawns `operate` in its own task group, and takes the `watch` receiver from the one-use handoff. It receives phases until `Ready`. The fixture keeps a handle to the root for `status`.
- A `StartError` is returned as `Start(e)`.
- The place's task group is spawned with `try_spawn`, so a test whose root allowance is too small gets `Full(cap)`. A `Memory` or `Temp` place sizes its store from the test's root limit (`store.sized`, v2.45), so a test's `limit` in hirsel.hcfg is the whole budget and no figure is reserved beyond it. Those backings open no follower, so their X is 0 (§12.1, v2.54).
- If `within_ms` passes first, the result is `Deadline(within_ms)`. It is returned after the fixture has stopped `operate` and released everything it made.

**Typed calls.**
- Fixture entry builders compute the key from the identity and the schemas that registration describes. A transact entry uses the `Result[R, E]` codec. A query entry uses its registered ordered key and current value schemas.
- A `granted_entry` (v2.24) call succeeds only against a fixture whose `PlaceConfig.grants.call` lists the entry (v2.67, v2.69), and is `Refused(NotAllowed)` otherwise.
- `client` connects one wire client to `status.addr`, with a `Hello` that carries `token` and lists the place's entries in registration order, and owns one stream. A place whose `wire` binds a credential needs the token to give the session a Caller.
- `client_at` with `None` mints a fresh stream, which starts at position 0. With `Some(Resume(stream, next))` it continues that stream at `next`, as a reconnecting Caller must; `resume_at` gives a client's stream and next position, to pass to the next connection.
- `call` sends at the stream's next position and advances it only on a reply the head committed: a `Returned` or `Failed` of a transact entry, or `Failed(Gone)` (STORE-SPEC §1.8 P1–P3; §1.3 Positions). It encodes `a` and sends one `Call` with `deadline_ms`. It returns when that send's Outcome row arrives, and V1 makes the place answer by `deadline_ms` (`Abandoned` at the latest). A transact `Returned` is decoded as `Result[R, E]`. A broken connection is `Closed`. A `Callee` whose key the place does not export is answered `Refused(NoEntry)` without a send.
- `subscribe` sends one `Subscribe` and returns once a Batch's `complete` names the new subscription. An `Ended` first gives `Closed`.
- `close` closes the client's connection and joins its reader and writer.

**A durable reply.** `Replied.version` is the version of the Batch that carried the Outcome. By V4, a reply that reports a commit is released only after `durable`, so a `Replied` that `call` returns is already durable at that version. Nothing extra waits.

**The batch at or after a version.** `seen_at(c, sub, v, within_ms)` returns the subscription's full row set as of the first Batch at a version ≥ v. That is L6+L7's count form. It applies Batches in order, as the client cache does. If the last Batch the client applied is already at or after v, the result is as of that Batch. A subscription that is `Ended` gives `Closed`.

**End.** `end` sends the stop and waits for `operate`'s `Ended`. The drain closes every session, so a `Client` used afterwards gets `Closed`, and `operate` removes the `Temp` subdirectory. It returns `Ended`. A `Client` still open at `end` holds its session until the drain's grace deadline, so `Ended` is then `Forced`; close clients first for `Stopped`. If `within_ms` passes, the result is `Deadline(within_ms)`, returned after the group is cancelled and joined. **Dropping a `Fixture` without `end`** cancels and joins its task group (CONC-8). That drops the listener and the `Db` (a crash cut, §1.10), and `operate`'s cleanup removes the `Temp` subdirectory. Every resource the fixture made is released on either path.

**Grants.** A test that uses the fixture needs `Net` (listen and connect on 127.0.0.1), `Tasks`, `Clock` and `Random`, plus `Dir` for `Temp`. It receives them as ordinary capability parameters (PROG-4) and places them in `PlaceConfig`.

**Engines.** Native fixtures run on Cranelift and LLVM over the granted loopback network. Evaluator fixtures will grant W8a's model network, which supplies their controlled network and virtual clock; that path is **unbuilt** until W8a.

**The runner's own harness.** `runner_calls.hirsel`'s `served` and `served_on` drive the head over the wire with `wire_client`, using the production `serve_connection` owner. The evaluator runs those same fixtures on its model network. Subscription-pressure laws additionally hold a joined publisher inbox undrained until their drain step. The in-process client/session seam is deleted.

### 12.4 Calls this section makes

- **The host stop source** is `host.stopped(p: Process)` under the existing `Process` capability, per DECISIONS, 2026-11-18 "service-ops host stop source".
- **The status route's contract** (outward-facing: proxies and supervisors will read it). `GET /hirsel/status` with 200/503 and the JSON fields above. Reversible until the route ships in a release.
- **The new CLI verbs** (outward-facing): `hirsel build --manifest` and `hirsel manifest show` (built); `hirsel preview`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> and `hirsel confirm`<!-- doc-ref: planned --> (unbuilt).
- **Readiness through the route** is decided here, for the reasons above. Reversible: the route is one raw handler over `status`.
- **`Overloaded` for drain refusals** is decided here. A `Draining` refusal would append tag 8 and change frame-vectors.json, and it would ask the client for the same action. Reversible until a client needs to tell the two apart.
- **The manifest from the build** is decided here, and extends D1. Reversible: the manifest is one file that one command writes.

### 12.5 The head's upkeep (v2.76, Store 1.3; sweep v2.77, Store 4.1; retention v2.79, Store 1.6; projectors v2.80, Store 2.3)

The head task owns the Db, so it drives the store's upkeep itself, one bounded slice per turn. No caller of `index_build_step`, `project_step`, `scrub_step` or `verify_step` is needed in a program.

- **When.** On an idle turn the inbox is empty, no group is open, no release is held, no write pause is active, the head is not closed, and its inbox has not ended. The head runs one slice of the first job that has work, then reads the inbox again.
- **Jobs, in priority order.**
  1. **Build.** One `index_build_step(mut db, 1024)` while an index is `Backfilling`. When the step reports nothing left and a chunk committed since the head last released, the head releases its current snapshot, so readers serve the rebuilt index.
  2. **Sweep (Store 4.1).** One `sweep_expired(mut db, Limits(rows: 1024, byte_limit: 1 MiB))` while a row is due (STORE-SPEC §1.2 "Expiry"). Its commit is a data commit: the head seals it as a group with no calls and releases it once its sync covers it, behind any held group, so readers see the rows gone only when they are durable. A sweep that proved its expiry index wrong demoted that index; the head then releases its snapshot, as for a walker's demotion. The sweep reads the head's own clock (`keyed_time`), so under sim@1 a law drives expiry with the simulated clock.
  3. **Retention (v2.79, Store 1.6).** One `retain_step(mut db, 1024)` while a Log holds more entries than its declared `Keep(n)` (STORE-SPEC §14.4a). Its commit is a data commit, sealed and released like a sweep's: as a group with no calls, once its sync covers it, so subscribers and tail covers see the trim only when it is durable.
  4. **Project (v2.80, Store 2.3).** One `project_step(mut db, name, {rows: 1024, bytes: 1 MiB})` of a projector the declaration registered (STORE-SPEC §1.14 "Driving"), round-robin: the turn starts at the projector after the last one that had work and runs the first whose step is not `Idle`. A step that committed releases the head's snapshot, so readers serve its generations. Every projector `Idle` falls through to the next job.
  5. **Scrub.** One `scrub_step` of 1 MiB of the current tour (STORE-SPEC §11.17). A tour starts at the first idle turn at or after its start time, and its end sets the next start time one hour after it on the store clock (`keyed_time`). A store without a directory has nothing to scrub.
  6. **Walk.** One `verify_step(mut db, mut walk, 1024)` (STORE-SPEC §1.2, the index walker), with the same rest of one hour after each finished walk. A slice that demotes an index releases the head's snapshot, so readers stop serving that index.

  For both scrub and walk, an exact sum of `u64::MAX` is due at that time. If the end time plus one hour exceeds `u64::MAX`, the job cannot finish resting in this open.
- **Projection under load (R4).** When 32 main-branch commits have accumulated since the last projector turn or since the head opened, the head gives one registered projector a bounded `project_step` before taking more work. The step runs between calls. The head closes any open group and releases earlier writes once durable before running the step, so the projection's snapshot follows them. The round-robin advances after this step even if it is idle. The commit count starts again at the main-branch version after the turn, including an idle projector turn. A write pause, a closed or ended inbox, or an export lease defers projection.
- **Build and the other upkeep jobs wait for idle turns.** Under steady load a build can stay `Backfilling` and its index answer `NotReady` until the load drops. A build chunk after every 16th group was measured and refused. On a Directory store, 4,000 one-row commits had a p99 of 31 µs alone, and 19.5 ms when a 1,024-row chunk ran after every 16th (each chunk is a checkpoint: p50 14 ms, p99 24 ms). That is far past the 10% the plan allows.
